# The Input (LF): The Large-Field Ingredients of the Young Matched Pairs

Own working note on the input (LF) of `WILSON_IT1_CAUCHY_ESTIMATE_2026_10_27.md` (its (LF) line, §6.2, and §8.6). It edits no other note. Bałaban is cited by printed page and equation, checked on the transcriptions in `Balaban\tex\<paper>\parts`. The notation is the Cauchy note's: δ_j := (g^B_j)^{−2} − (g^A_j)^{−2} is the two runs' coupling difference at the shared scale j, ϑ_j := g_j(A₁/A₀)p₀(g_j) is Bałaban's δ_j (CRE p.265 l.39), and δ′_j = g_jA₁p₁(g_j) is his (LFR I p.183 l.2).

## Open Gaps

- **(LF-A) The large-field integrands.** These are the action A(ζ₁/(g″)², U″_{h+2}) and the form ⟨DH″B, ζDH″B⟩ of LFR II (1.70). They also include the fine-lattice objects of the renormalisation denominators LFR II (1.1)–(1.2) (the minimiser U₀ of (1.3), H_{1,k}, Δ₁(ζ₀), and Im∂U₀), the factors V(Y) of LFR II (1.91), and the boundary terms 𝐁 generated at the steps at which a large-field region existed. By §4(a), U_{𝐁″} of LFR II (1.50) differs between the runs through its own fine lattice and through its data, which contain the block averages of U_0. The classical depth of U_{𝐁″} at equal data is derived in §4(b), in the layer-weighted norm of VAR's contraction, and the depth of U_0 with its propagation through the data in §4(c), under the coupling condition (T-Λ). Both bounds are per point of the history at its birth scale n, the spacing of its finest layer, which is the form of (LF) that the Cauchy note's callers use. §4(d) derives the per-layer form of (10.6) for H″ and H_{1,k}, and through it bounds the forms and V(Y). It compares the denominator d of (1.1) and the sum 𝐂_k of LFR II p.369 l.2–7 as wholes, so the remainders V(ζ₀, ·) and V(ζ, ·), the linear terms of (1.2) and (1.20), and the complementary-weight parts of the forms are not compared separately (*The complementary-weight parts.*). The age factor is Bałaban's for each run's own terms at its own evaluation points (LFR II p.376 l.40–41, accepted). CRE's printed proof needs U = U_j(□₀, M^·(U)) on □₀ (CRE p.283 l.47–48), and Bałaban does not print the proof at his representation configurations. So the age factor for the frozen difference Δ at the decoupled data and for run B's terms at the lifted data there is derived directly (*The age factor.*). It compares the configuration with a member of 𝒮_z that has the same curvature at z, using the vanishing first-order term (global gauge invariance, 𝔤 semisimple) and the Hölder regularity of the configuration at the collar's index h. At D(s) that regularity comes through CMP98 (77)–(92), by which the boundary-layer data are the averages of a gauge transform of exp(iη𝐇″_k(s))U_{1,2}; at the lifted data it comes from the strip sources' responses. The composites are not in this item: the near domain of z, □₀ := □̃⁵ with □ ∈ π_n the cube of z (the Cauchy note's §5), is the cube □₀ = □^{∼5} on which (1.59) forms the composite at the same z (LFR II p.372, M-cubes of the L^{−n}-lattice in both), so the composite is RG I's U_j(□₀) on its near domain, a composite over 𝒮_z at which the Cauchy note's (5.1c𝐀) gives the age factor (derived, not reviewed; the Cauchy note's §5, *The composites over 𝒮_z.*; §4(d), (iv), *The composites over 𝒮_z.*);

At complex points the plaquette, real-part, A′, and current conditions of U_{k,Λ} and U_1 on X ⊂ Z^c and on □₀ ∩ Z, and those of their composites, are derived, not reviewed, through (1.58) and (1.57) about (𝐔, 𝐉) itself, whose conditions are those of the class (CRE (2.34)–(2.39)). ## 1. The Structure of a Young History (Derived)

Let X be an alive component of a young matched pair, born at the scale j₀ > k − ⌊k/2⌋, so that Ω_i = T for i < j₀ (Cauchy note §6.2).

- *The integration variables.* By CRE (2.20)–(2.21) (p.258 l.14–31), the operation 𝐓^{(j)} integrates V_j on Ω^c_{j+1} ∩ X with the constraint δ(V̄_jV^{−1}_{j+1}). On the part of X that is large-field from the scale j₀ on, the variables V_{j₀}, …, V_{k−1} are fixed by the constraints as iterated block averages of V_{j₀−1}. So the integration there is over V_{j₀−1}, with the Haar measure and the constraints, and over the fluctuation variables A_j on Z_{j+1} ∩ Ω_{j+1} ∩ X.
- *The regularity of the data.* The point lies in Ω_{j₀−1} ∖ Ω_{j₀}, so the determining set carries the layer Λ_{j₀−1} there, with data V_{j₀−1}. VAR's hypothesis (7) (p.278 l.35–45) is one bound ε₁ on the data plaquettes of all layers, the mixed plaquettes at a layer's boundary included. It holds on the support of the term with ε₁ := C max_j(ε_j + ϑ_j), from the seven groups of LFR I p.178, as follows.
  - The cubes of (1.3) at the scale j, □ ⊂ (Ω_j^{∼4} ∖ Ω_{j+1}^∼) ∩ Z (LFR I p.178 l.16–19), bound the plaquettes of U_{j,□}(V_j) by ε_j(L^{k−j}η)². With Ω_{j₀−1} = T they cover the large-field region of the scale j₀ outside the collar Ω_{j₀}^∼ ∖ Ω_{j₀} (Ω^∼ is the enlargement, CRE p.250 l.26). The data of U_{j,□} are V_j, so CMP 98 Proposition 2 (p.26, (54)), which needs only the local plaquette bound (52), gives |V_j(∂p′) − 1| < 2ε_j there. This is the remark of VAR p.278 l.46. The group (1.4) gives the same at the scale j + 1.
  - In the collars, (1.7) and (1.8) put V_j, and the lifted fluctuation field, within 2ϑ_j of the reference configuration V^{(j)}_{□′}. This is the scale-j average of a minimiser corrected by exp i𝐇^{(j)}_{□′} (LFR I p.185 l.29), so its plaquettes are bounded by the item above, and those of V_j by that bound plus Cϑ_j.
  - By CRE (2.4) and (2.6) and ϑ_j = g_j(A₁/A₀)p₀(g_j) (CRE p.265 l.39), ε₁ ≤ Cḡp₀(ḡ) ≤ a₁ for ḡ small, with ε₁ independent of k, K, and ε.
- *The configurations.* The fine configuration of the term is a minimiser with these data: U_k(V) for the action (CRE p.258 l.44–47), and U″_k = U_{𝐁″_k}(·) for the R-operation (LFR II (1.50), p.370 l.4). VAR Theorem 1 (p.279 l.8–28) treats determining sets 𝔅_k = ∪_jΛ_j, layer by layer. Its hypothesis (7) holds with the ε₁ of the previous item. So the regularity (9)–(10), |A| < B₃Mε₁(L^jη)^{−1} and its companions, holds on every cube of VAR's class at the spacing L^jη of its layer (p.279 l.17–27), and every layer of X lies at a scale ≥ j₀ − 1 ≥ k/2 − 1.

The coarse block averages of V_{j₀−1} are not regular at the coarse scales, and the fine configuration depends on them only through the layers. This is why the depth of the young pairs is first order in the ratio of the fine spacing to the layer's spacing, L^{−(j₀−1)}: the same order as for the all-small-field pair at the scale j₀ − 1.

## 2. The Run-Dependent Ingredients, Sorted (Derived)

The ingredients of a young term at the scales i ≥ j₀ are listed in the Cauchy note's §6.2. The selectors are the seven groups of LFR I p.178 l.15–49 and the test (2.17).

(a) *The ingredients of (α).* The small-field action, the covariance, and the function (1.6) are derived in the Cauchy note's §6.2.

(b) *The coupling shells: (1.5) and (1.9).*
- (1.5), χ(|V_j(y,x) − 1| < ε_j), acts on the variable V_j(y,x) of the lattice T_{L^{−j}}, which both runs share. The threshold ε_j = g_jp₀(g_j) depends on the run only through the coupling.
- (1.9), χ(sup|A_j(b)| < g_j^{−1}ϑ_j), acts on the fluctuation variable. Its threshold also depends on the run only through the coupling.
- The mechanism is D6a's cutoff part (`Balaban\DIFFERENCES_PROOFS.md` l.61–82; CRE's cutoff r(g) in `WILSON_CAUCHY_LIP1A_2026_10_21.md` §2, l.66–70). D6a telescopes the two products of indicators and evaluates RG2's bounds on the mixed products, using the room in RG2's constants. Bałaban's bounds are not stated for mixed thresholds, so the split here keeps each piece on one run's own support. With Π_X the product of run X's indicators and ρ_X the rest of its integrand,
  Π_Aρ_A − Π_Bρ_B = Π_AΠ_B(ρ_A − ρ_B) + Π_A(1 − Π_B)ρ_A − Π_B(1 − Π_A)ρ_B.
  The first piece lies on the common support and belongs to (α) and (d). In the second, 1 − Π_B ≤ Σ_b(1 − χ_{B,b}), and on the support of Π_A the factor 1 − χ_{B,b} is the indicator of the shell between the two thresholds at b. The third piece is the same with A and B exchanged.
- So each shell term is run A's own term (or run B's) with one shell indicator inserted. Bałaban's bound (1.79) of 𝐓′1 is proved by estimating the group-valued integrals, with their normalised weights (1.6), by 1, and the A_j-integrals by the positivity of their quadratic forms (LFR II p.383 l.30–34). The shell indicator is ≤ 1 and is kept inside the corresponding integral, so the shell term is at most Bałaban's bound times the mass of the shell under that normalised weight.
- That mass is ≤ C|t′ − t|t′^{d_G−1} times the maximal density on the shell, in the variable X = g_j^{−1}log V_j(y,x) for (1.5) and A_j(b) for (1.9). The thresholds are t = ε_j/g_j = p₀(g_j) and t = g_j^{−1}ϑ_j = (A₁/A₀)p₀(g_j), and |t′ − t| ≤ Ct|g_j − g′_j|/g_j ≤ Ctg_j²|x_j − x′_j| (LIP1A l.68; D6a step 3), with x_j := g_j^{−2} and the primes for the other run.
  - For (1.5), the density of the normalised weight (1.6) in X is ≤ C, uniformly in g_j small, since z ≥ cg_j^{d_G}. On the shell it is ≤ Ce^{−cp₀(g_j)²}, since 1 − Re tr V ≥ c|log V|² near 1.
  - For (1.9), the A_j-Gaussian has the precision Q = C*Δ^{(j)}C − C*Δ^{(j)}CC^{(j)}C*Δ^{(j)}C of CRE (2.21), and Q ≤ C*Δ^{(j)}C since C^{(j)} ≥ 0. The marginal variance of A_j(b) is ((Q^{−1})_{bb}) ≥ (Q_{bb})^{−1} ≥ ‖C*Δ^{(j)}C‖^{−1}, so the marginal density is ≤ (‖C*Δ^{(j)}C‖/2π)^{d_G/2}, a lattice constant. No Gaussian tail is used.
  - So the per-variable relative shell factor is ≤ Cp₀(g_j)^{d_G}g_j²|x_j − x′_j|. The variables are counted per point of the history as the variables of (1.6) are in (α) (Cauchy note §6.2).
- The complement χ^{(j−1)c}(R_j) of LFR II p.380 l.42–46 has the shell of (1.9), since the difference of 1 − χ is minus that of χ. Bałaban bounds its integral by the same positivity of the quadratic form (p.380 l.31), so the same bound holds.
- The radial cutoffs of the R-operation are bounded in the Cauchy note's §8.6, except the numerators' factor χ̄(Λ)(V′_k), whose shell is bounded in §4(d). The denominator's χ({|B′| < M₀g_k^{−1}ε_k}) of LFR II (1.2) is the soft test χ̄(Λ) of LFR I (1.101) (§3(d)), and its shell is bounded per bond by the Lipschitz bound of χ̄. The numerators' χ′ (|B| < g_k^{−1}δ′_k) of (1.70) is LFR I's sharp (1.82), and its shell is bounded by the density device above.
- The bound is relative to Bałaban's bound of the term, not to the term itself. So the majorant of the Cauchy note's (LF) is to be read as M_τ built from Bałaban's bound (1.79) of sup𝐓′1, which is the bound that §11(c) uses.

(c) *The plaquette shells: (1.3), (1.4), (1.7), (1.8), and (2.17).* These test U_{j,□}(V_j), U_{j+1,□′}(V_{j+1}), or the reference configurations V^{(j)}_{□′} built from them. All are minimisers computed on the run's fine lattice. At equal data the two runs' tested quantities differ by δ^f_jt with δ^f_j := C(ε/a_j)^{1−2ε₀}, by the minimiser distance of the Cauchy note's §9.7 applied to the local problem of the cube (VAR Theorem 1 for 𝐁_j(□^{∼4}), as in the Cauchy note's §9.8). Their thresholds, ε_j = g_jp₀(g_j) and 2ϑ_j = 2g_j(A₁/A₀)p₀(g_j), differ through the coupling by ≤ Ctg_j²|δ_j|, as in (b). This is (LF-P), §3.

(d) *The large-field integrands.* These are (LF-A), §4.

## 3. The Plaquette Shells (Derived)

(a) *The redundant tests (derived).*
- CRE (3.6)–(3.8) (p.265 l.46–47, p.266 l.26–33): for a cube □ ⊂ Ω^∼_{k+1} of the scale-k test (2.17), on the support of the next scale's small-field test (3.2) and of the Q-test (3.3), |U_{k,□}(∂p) − 1| < (2(1 + β₀)L^{−2} + 4/10)ε_kη² < ε_kη², "Thus χ_k(□) = 1 for □ ⊂ Ω^∼_{k+1}". (3.9) gives the same for the axial-gauge functions χ_{Ax}.
- CRE p.268 l.26–30 with (3.19) and p.269 l.2–4: for □′ ⊂ R^c_{k+1}, the complement taken in Ω^{∼−1}_{k+1}, the Q-test of (3.3) is equal to 1 on the support of the fluctuation cutoff (3.16).
- So in each run's term the tests of the scale j that are not equal to 1 on the support of the term's other characteristic functions lie as follows. The plaquette and axial tests lie on cubes not contained in Ω^∼_{j+1}, in the large-field region Ω^c_{j+1} of the next scale. The reference tests lie on cubes of the collar Ω^{∼2}_{j+1} ∖ Ω_{j+1} (LFR I (1.7)) and of S_{j+1} = (Ω_{j+1} ∖ Ω^{∼−1}_{j+1}) ∪ R_{j+1} (CRE (3.20)–(3.21), p.269 l.7–21), and, in LFR I, of (Ω_{j+1} ∖ Λ^∼_{j+1}) ∩ Z (LFR I (1.8)). All of these lie in the large-field region of the term or its collar. The tests of the stopping scale act on every cube of Ω_k. Where a test is equal to 1 in both runs there is no shell.
- The consequences are three.
  - The all-small-field term ℓ₀ carries the stopping-scale test χ_k(T) of (2.18) and the fluctuation cutoffs (3.16), whose shells are the Cauchy note's §8.2(b), and no other test.
  - In a young history Ω_{i+1} = T for i + 1 < j₀, so every test that is not equal to 1 on the support of the other characteristic functions is at a scale ≥ j₀ − 1 ≥ k/2 − 1 on a cube of the history, or on a stopping-scale cube of Ω_k. So δ^f_j ≤ CL^{−(1−2ε₀)(k/2−1)}, and the history cubes are counted by the history volume, with no volume factor.
  - The tests at the fine scales occur only in the old histories, whose terms the Cauchy note's §6.2 measures against each run's own Z_{ℓ₀}, with no comparison of tests.

(b) *The stopping-scale test of the all-small-field term (bounded in (d)).*
- *The shell.* ρ^X_{ℓ₀}(V) = χ^X_k(T)(V)exp A^X_k(V) on G^{bonds(T^{(k)})}, X = A, B, with χ^X_k(T) the product (2.17) over the stopping-scale cubes, at most N_s⁴ of them, built on run X's fine lattice. Write f^X_□ := sup_{p⊂□^∼}|U^X_{k,□}(V, ∂p) − 1|/(η^X)² and t^X := ε^X_k. On either run's support, |f^A_□ − f^B_□| ≤ δ^ft^A with δ^f := C(ε/a)^{1−2ε₀} (§2(c)), and |t^A − t^B| ≤ Ct^Ag_a²|δ_k| (§2(b)). With δ^s := 2δ^f + 4Cg_a²|δ_k|, which is δ^s_k of §3(d) and exceeds the width δ^f + Cg_a²|δ_k| that this split needs, the split of §2(b) puts the shell on one run's own support: run A's term with the indicator of {t(1 − δ^s) ≤ f^A_□ < t}, t := t^A, inserted for one cube □, and the same with A and B exchanged.
- *Lemma SH with a radial family does not work here.* In the complete axial gauge of the torus with the winding holonomies w_μ held fixed, x = 0 is flat only when the w_μ commute; at the plaquettes where the winding bonds of two directions meet it carries [w_μ, w_ν], of size up to 2N_s²ε_k = 2N_s²t on the support, so (M) fails there for every threshold on ḡ.
- *The bound.* The soft tests of (d) bound this shell.

(c) *The young pairs (bounded in (d)).*
- *The form needed.* A bound of each shell term's mass by C(ε/a_j)^{1−2ε₀} times Bałaban's bound of the term, plus a coupling part ≤ |δ_j|, has per cube of the history the form (LF) asks for: s^{LF}_j ≤ C(ε/a_j)^{1−2ε₀} ≤ C_hθ̃^j, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−2ε₀)}, with the coupling part inside s′_K. The soft tests of (d) give it.
- *Lemma SH does not work here.* A radial shrinking of V_j on the cube can switch a failed large-field test of ζ(Ω^c_{j+1}) (CRE (2.21)) to passing, so its hypothesis (R) fails.

(d) *The soft tests (derived).*
- *The device.* F_K = N_K/(κ_KZ_K) (the Cauchy note's §1), with N_K and Z_K the lattice integrals, which do not depend on the decompositions of unity used to expand them. At every K and in both runs, each plaquette and reference test χ({f_□ < t}) is replaced by the soft test χ̄(f_□/t), χ̄(x) := min(1, max(0, (1 + κ_s − x)/κ_s)), and its complement by 1 − χ̄(f_□/t).
  - These are the tests of CRE (1.1), (1.4), (1.8), (2.17), (3.2), and (3.3); of LFR I (1.3)–(1.4), (1.7)–(1.8), (1.22)–(1.24), (1.27), (1.75), and (1.88); and their restatements in LFR II (1.70) and p.380 l.31–46.
  - *The cascade tests.* The tests χ_k^{(n)} of LFR I (1.22)–(1.24) are soft at nested thresholds. There n = j − h counts the integrations, n = 0, …, N with N := k − h, and U^{(N)}_{k,Z} = U″_{k,Z} (p.181 l.4, p.182 l.35). At a cube □, f^{(n)}_□ := sup_{p⊂□}|U^{(n)}_{k,Z}(∂p) − 1|/T_n(p), with T_n(p) the threshold of (1.24) at p, and χ_k^{(n)} is replaced by ∏_□χ̄_c(f^{(n)}_□/(1 + κ_c)^{N−n}), with χ̄_c the function χ̄ with κ_c := κ_s/(N + 1) in place of κ_s. So the test n has the plateau f^{(n)}_□ ≤ (1 + κ_c)^{N−n} and the support f^{(n)}_□ < (1 + κ_c)^{N−n+1}, and every cascade threshold lies between T_n and (1 + κ_c)^{N+1}T_n ≤ e^{κ_s}T_n ≤ (1 + 2κ_s)T_n. The test n = N, at j = k, has the plateau T_N and the width κ_c, and it keeps this form wherever it occurs again.
  - A test keeps its soft form wherever it occurs again: as the stopping test of the next step, in a product of tests on disjoint cube sets (LFR II p.378 l.15–16, where CRE (3.20) makes X_i^{∼−6} and Ω_k^{∼4} disjoint, so no χ̄² = χ̄ is used), and after a change of variables, as in LFR I (1.59).
  - The radial, fluctuation, and gauge-fixing cutoffs stay sharp: CRE (1.5), (1.18), and (3.16); LFR I (1.5), (1.9), (1.55), and (1.82), with (1.5) the restatement of CRE (1.5); and χ′ of LFR II (1.70), which is LFR I's (1.82) (LFR II p.381 l.33).
  - The radial cutoff χ(Λ_i) of the R-operation, LFR I (1.101), is soft, as defined at (1.100)–(1.102) below, and it is the same soft function in LFR II (1.1), (1.2), and (1.70) (p.356 l.19–37, p.378 l.13–14). Its shells are bounded per bond by the Lipschitz bound of χ̄: the shell of χ̄(Λ)(V′_k) in §4(d), and the denominator's in the Cauchy note's §8.6.
  - Every decomposition stays exact, since Σ_P∏_{□∉P}χ̄_□∏_{□∈P}(1 − χ̄_□) = ∏_□(χ̄_□ + 1 − χ̄_□) = 1 for any functions with values in [0, 1]. So N_K and Z_K are unchanged, and F_K is defined at every K by this one construction, both as run A of one pair and as run B of the next.
  - κ_s := β/30 = 1/240, with β = 1/8 (§4(d), *The choice β = 1/8, checked.*), within Bałaban's β ≤ 1/4 of LFR I p.191 l.34–35 and p.198 l.16 (the choice of β below (1.24) allows β ≤ ½, p.182 l.16–17). A passed test of width κ_s enters at the threshold (1 + κ_s)t, and a passed cascade test at a threshold ≤ (1 + 2κ_s)t. The checks below are written with 1 + κ_s and hold with 1 + 2κ_s in its place. The smallest relative margin of the passed-test comparisons is that of LFR I (1.31) (p.184 l.8–9), β/10 − β/(5L²) ≥ 7β/90 at L ≥ 3, and (1 + β/15)(1 − 7β/90) < 1. Every other margin below allows a factor 1 + 1/22, and 2κ_s ≤ 1/60. The removals of the cascade tests use no margin (*The statement (R-s)*, χ_k^{(n)}). The letter κ without the index is Bałaban's decay constant, as in exp(−κd_j(X)).
- *The shell bound (derived).* For a test at □ of the scale j, put x^X := f^X_□/t^X in run X.
  - The tested quantities differ by |f^A − f^B| ≤ δ^f_jt^A on either run's support, with δ^f_j := C(ε/a_j)^{1−2ε₀} (§2(c), with the regularity of the support at the thresholds (1 + 2κ_s)t, which changes C by the factor 1 + 2κ_s). The thresholds differ by |t^A − t^B| ≤ Ct^Ag_j²|δ_j| ≤ ½t^A (§2(b)).
  - LFR I (1.27) tests |V_j(b)(V^{(j)}_Z(b))^{−1} − 1| on the bonds b of (Ω_{j+1}^c ∖ Z″_{j+1})^{(j)*} (p.182 l.53), with V^{(j)}_Z = M^j(U^{(j+1−h)}_{k,Z}) the scale-j average of a cascade minimiser (LFR I (1.26)), at the threshold 2δ′_j, and §2(c)'s δ^f_j is stated for the local minimisers of the cubes at the thresholds ε_j and 2ϑ_j. The cascade minimisers are VAR minimisers of their multiscale determining sets, with the same data in both runs, so the profile (P′) of §5(c) and CMP 98 (159)–(163) for the averages bound the two runs' difference of V^{(j)}_Z by 2c_vρ̄_j, ρ̄_j the bound ρ̄ of (P′) on the layer j.
  - *The shell of (1.27) (derived).* For G-valued W_A, W_B, the runs' V^{(j)}_Z(b), ||V_jW_A^{−1} − 1| − |V_jW_B^{−1} − 1|| ≤ |V_jW_A^{−1} − V_jW_B^{−1}| = |W_A^{−1}(W_B − W_A)W_B^{−1}| = |W_B − W_A|. So the tested quantities differ by ≤ 2c_vρ̄_j, and relative to the threshold 2δ′_j by ≤ c_vρ̄_j/δ′_j.
    - The factors. ρ̄_j = 2C_G(ε₀)c″_K(ε/a_j)^{1−ε₀}ε₁ (§4(b), *The configuration difference.*), δ′_j = g_jA₁p₁(g_j) with p₁ < p₀ (LFR I p.183 l.2), and ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k of (c), with ε_k = g_kA₀p₀(g_k) (CRE (2.4)) and k ≥ j the scale of the configuration. By CRE (2.6), g_k ≤ (1 + β₀)(k − j + 1)^{1/2}g_j and g_j ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k, so log g_k^{−2} ≤ 2log g_j^{−2} for g small, and p₁(g_j) ≥ 1. With R_k ≤ L(log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)), ε₁/δ′_j ≤ C(M)(A₀/A₁)(1 + β₀)2^{p₀+r}L(k − j + 1)^{1/2}(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀+r}.
    - The absorption. The shell bound is used on the histories of the young pairs, born at a scale n > k_s/8. There the test scales satisfy j ≥ n, and k ≤ K < 8n + j(C), so k − j + 1 ≤ (1 + j(C))8j and log g_j^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + 8b′j + b′j(C) + C) (BETA_FUNCTION_WILSON_LOOPS.md Lemma 2.1(i)). Both factors are absorbed by L^{ε₀j} = (a_j/ε)^{ε₀}: c_vρ̄_j/δ′_j ≤ C_{1.27}(ε/a_j)^{1−2ε₀}, with C_{1.27} := 2c_vC_G(ε₀)c″_KC(M)(A₀/A₁)(1 + β₀)2^{p₀+r}L·sup_{j≥1}((1 + j(C))8j)^{1/2}(log(ḡ^{−2} + 8b′j + b′j(C) + C))^{p₀+r}L^{−ε₀j}. C_{1.27} is finite, at most polynomial in j(C), and independent of k, K, ε, and the volume. Where §1's ε₁ is the larger, ε₁/ε_k ≤ C(1 + β₀)(k_s − k + 1)^{1/2}(1 + A₁/A₀) adds a factor polynomial in j, absorbed in the same way.
    - So the shell of (1.27) has the form δ^f_j, with the constant C of δ^f_j taken at least C_{1.27}.
  - So |x^A − x^B| ≤ δ^s_j := 2δ^f_j + 4Cg_j²|δ_j| wherever min(x^A, x^B) < 1 + 2κ_s. Where min(x^A, x^B) ≥ 1 + 2κ_s, both soft tests are 0. Since χ̄ is (1/κ_s)-Lipschitz, |χ̄(x^A) − χ̄(x^B)| ≤ δ^s_j/κ_s everywhere. A cascade test is χ̄_c(x/(1 + κ_c)^{N−n}), which is (1/κ_c)-Lipschitz in x, so for it the bound is δ^s_j/κ_c = (N + 1)δ^s_j/κ_s. The same holds for the complements.
  - With Π_X the product of run X's tests and ρ_X the rest of its integrand, the split of §2(b) becomes Π_Aρ_A − Π_Bρ_B = min(Π_A, Π_B)(ρ_A − ρ_B) + (Π_A − Π_B)₊ρ_A − (Π_B − Π_A)₊ρ_B, which is the same split for sharp tests. The first piece lies on the common support and belongs to (α) and (d).
  - In the second piece, (Π_A − Π_B)₊ ≤ 1{Π_A > 0}Σ_□|χ̄^A_□ − χ̄^B_□| by telescoping, all factors having values in [0, 1], plus the sharp shells of §2(b). On {Π_A > 0} every passed soft test has x^A < 1 + 2κ_s and every failed one x^A > 1, so ρ_A is on run A's support at those thresholds. The third piece is the same with A and B exchanged.
  - So each shell term is at most δ^s_j/κ_s, or (N + 1)δ^s_j/κ_s for a cascade test, times a run's own term with its soft tests replaced by the sharp tests at the thresholds (1 + κ_s)t (passed) and t (failed), and for the cascade tests at (1 + κ_c)^{N−n+1}T_n ≤ (1 + 2κ_s)T_n (passed) and (1 + κ_c)^{N−n}T_n ≥ T_n (failed), whose mass Bałaban's bound of the term bounds by (R-s).
  - The bound is pointwise in the variables, so it holds wherever a test stands: in a term, in the numerator of a renormalisation quotient (the Cauchy note's (LF-2), with the modulus of the integrand), and in the stopping-scale test of ℓ₀. A sharp test of a complex argument is not defined, so on Bałaban's complex domains the tests act on the real integration variables, and the soft tests do as well.
- *The result.* Per test the relative shell factor is δ^s_j/κ_s = (2/κ_s)C(ε/a_j)^{1−2ε₀} + (4/κ_s)Cg_j²|δ_j|, and N + 1 times this for a cascade test.
  - For a young pair the first part is ≤ C_hθ̃^j per cube of the history, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−2ε₀)}: the form of (LF), contributing 2C/κ_s to C_h.
  - For a cascade test, N + 1 ≤ k + 1 ≤ 8j, since N = k − h ≤ k and the history's scales j ≥ n satisfy k < 8n. Since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4}, L^{−(1−2ε₀)j} ≤ θ̃^jL^{−(3−7ε₀)j/4}, and 3 − 7ε₀ > 0 by ε₀ < 2/5 (§5). So the first part is ≤ (16C/κ_s)c_Nθ̃^j per cube of the history, with c_N := sup_{j≥1}jL^{−(3−7ε₀)j/4}, a constant of L and ε₀, and 16Cc_N/κ_s is added to C_h.
  - The second part is ≤ |δ_j| for γ small, for a cascade test as well. By LFR I p.198 (below (1.94)), N ≤ O(1)(log g_k^{−2})^ν, and g_j ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k for j ≤ k by CRE (2.6) (p.255 l.44–47). So (4C/κ_s)(N + 1)g_j² ≤ (4C/κ_s)(1 + β₀)²(1 + O(1)(log g_k^{−2})^ν)g_k² ≤ 1 for γ small. It enters with the radial shells inside the term 10sup_{i>k−m}|δ_i| of s′_K (the Cauchy note's §6.2), as one more ingredient per point in C_M.
  - For ℓ₀, (11.1) of the Cauchy note holds at the thresholds (1 + κ_s)t by (R-s), so Z_{ℓ₀} ≤ e^{C_K}, and Z_{ℓ₀} ≥ e^{−C_K}, since on the ball 𝔅 of §11(b) f_□ < t, where χ̄ = 1.
  - Summed over the ≤ N_s⁴ cubes, the two runs, and the numerator and normalisation of F, the stopping-scale shells change F_{K+1} − F_K by at most b^{(S)}_KB_W, with b^{(S)}_K := 4κ_s^{−1}e^{2C_K}N_s⁴[2C(ε/a)^{1−2ε₀} + 4Cg_a²|δ_k|], ε/a = L^{−k}, k = K − j(C). This is summable in K by the Cauchy note's (8.6), given the form of (LF).
- *The statement (R-s) (derived).* Bałaban's construction and bounds hold with the soft tests, with constants independent of k, K, ε, and the volume. This means four things.
  - (a) Every removal of a redundant test holds on the plateau f < t, given the other soft tests at the thresholds (1 + κ_s)t. A cascade test χ_k^{(n)} is removed on its plateau (1 + κ_c)^{N−n}T_n, given χ_k^{(n+1)} at its support threshold (1 + κ_c)^{N−n}T_{n+1}.
  - (b) Every bound that uses a passed test holds at the threshold (1 + κ_s)t, or (1 + 2κ_s)t for a cascade test, and every bound that uses a failed test at the threshold t.
  - (c) The changes of variables that carry a test into a test of the new variables (CRE (1.22), p.269 l.45–50; LFR I (1.59)) stay exact.
  - (d) VAR's (7) holds with ε₁ enlarged by the factor 1 + κ_s (§1). Nonnegativity, the convergence of the expansions, and the loop bound B_W are not affected, since the soft tests have values in [0, 1].

The bound below is derived by the argument of CRE (3.6)–(3.8), with U_{k,Z} in the place of U_{k+1,□′}. It holds at the actual V_k⌈_Λ, which is one of the extensions of the infimum, and all configurations are real.
      - *The geometry.* On a component X_i of Z, Λ_k ⊂ Ω_k^{∼−2} (CRE (3.20)), so every plaquette p of Ω_k^c ∩ X_i is at distance ≥ 2MR_k from Z^c. The LM₂R_k-cube □ ∋ p lies in Ω_k^{∼4} or in (Ω_k^{∼4})^c ∩ X_i, so on the first class it passes the soft test, from χ_k(Ω_k^{∼4}) of LFR I (1.71) or from the decomposition of p.378 l.6: |U_{k,□}(∂p) − 1| < (1 + 2κ_s)ε_kη². The operation ∼ on □ adds a layer of M₂R_k-cubes, and M is much larger than M₂ > M₁ (CRE p.245 l.34–38), so □^{∼4} lies within (L + 4)M₂R_k of p and inside Z^{∼−2}, where 𝔹_k(Z) consists of k-blocks (CRE p.256 l.58–63).
      - *U_{k,Z} as the background.* On □^{∼4}, the constraints of 𝔹_k(□^{∼4}) fix the k-block averages of 𝔹_k(Z), and all bonds outside its support. So a competitor of the □^{∼4}-problem at the data M^·(U_{k,Z}), extended by U_{k,Z}, is admissible for the Z-problem, with the same change of the action. By the uniqueness of the minimal orbit (CRE p.256 l.43–53), U_{k,Z} is the minimiser of the □^{∼4}-problem at its own data, the property of U_{k+1,□′} that CRE (3.6) uses. As in (3.6), U_{k,□} = (exp(iη𝐇_{k,□}(B))U_{k,Z})^{u^{−1}}, with B := (1/i)log[M^·(Q_k^{s*}V_k)(M^·(U_{k,Z}))^{−1}].
      - *The argument B.* Off the boundary layer of □^{∼4} of width 2M₁, both averages are V_k: the constraint of U_{k,Z}, and M^k(Q_k^{s*}V_k) = V_k as in (3.6). So B = 0 there. On the layer, M^·(Q_k^{s*}V_k) = M^·(U_{k,□}), and U_{k,□} and U_{k,Z} have the same k-block averages and plaquettes below α₀η², with α₀ := O(1)B₃(1 + 2κ_s)ε_k, by |∂V_k − 1| < 2(1 + 2κ_s)ε_k (p.378 l.10–11 with the soft χ_k) and the bound of LFR I p.193 l.20. REG (1.65) at α₁ = 0 gives |B| < 11d²α₀.

      - *The plaquette.* REG (1.43) with U₀ = U_{k,Z} and U₁ = exp(iη𝐇_{k,□}) gives W − 1 = PXQ + XS + X + E₂, with X := U_{k,Z}(∂p) − 1 and W := (U₁U₀)(∂p). The bounds of (3.8), with 1/10 replaced by τ, are |P||Q| + |S| ≤ 2τε_kη and |E₂| ≤ 2τε_kη²(1 + 4τε_k), and |W − 1| = |U_{k,□}(∂p) − 1| by gauge covariance. Solved for X,
        |U_{k,Z}(∂p) − 1| ≤ (1 − 2τε_kη)^{−1}(1 + 2κ_s + 2τ(1 + 4τε_k))ε_kη² < 2ε_kη²
        for κ_s ≤ 1/4 and τ ≤ 1/20. So χ_{k,Λ} = 1 on the first class at the soft thresholds; the room is the factor 2/(1 + 2κ_s) of (1.75).

There the modulus of the integrand is ≤ e^{C(M)}e^{−(κ_Λ/4)‖B′‖²}, with κ_Λ of (1.9) as in (c), by the following inputs: the real part of the form is ≥ (κ_Λ/2)‖B′‖², by (1.7)–(1.9) on the class (p.358 l.27–33); the linear term is ≤ 2 by (1.6) on |B′| < 2t; V is ≤ O(g_k^{1−β})|Λ| on |B′| < 2t (p.357 l.9–10); and σ is bounded. At complex backgrounds the bounds of the linear term and of V are those of p.358 l.27–30, with ε_k replaced by O(α₀ + α₁). On the set, e^{−(κ_Λ/8)‖B′‖²} ≤ e^{−κ_Λt²/8}, and |d(1)| ≥ exp(−O(1)log M|Λ|) times the prefactors of (1.10), by (1.11). So |d(s)/d(1) − 1| ≤ τ := |Λ^{(k)}|e^{C(M)}e^{−κ_Λt²/8}, with |Λ| ≤ O(M⁴) (p.358 l.26) in C(M).
      - Since t → ∞ as g_k → 0, τ ≤ ¼ for γ small, and |log d̄ − log d(1)| ≤ 2τ. So the bounds of d on pp.356–358 and in the Cauchy note's §8.6, which hold for any fixed M₀, hold for d̄ up to the factor e^{2τ}.

With the soft χ_k the bound is (1 + κ_s)O(1)B₅M⁶ε_k, and M₀ := (1 + κ_s)O(1)B₅M⁶, the same in both runs. M₀ enters only the cutoffs of LFR I (1.101) and LFR II (1.2), and the bound of p.357 l.37–38 as a factor of e^{−R_k}. ## 4. The Large-Field Integrands (Derived; the Points of the Open Gaps Not Reviewed)

By §1, U″_{h+2}(exp ig_kB, V″) and U″_k are VAR minimisers with layer data regular at their own scales, all at scales ≥ k/2 − 1.

(a) *The configurations and their data (derived).*
- U″_k = U_{𝐁″_k}(V″⌈_{Λ^c}, exp ig_kBM^·(U^{(AL)}_0)⌈_{Λ∩Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}}, V″⌈_{(Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c}) (LFR II (1.50), p.370 l.3–5) is the minimiser of the Wilson action with the multiscale determining set 𝐁″_k. Its data are the shared block variables V″ and B, except on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, where they contain the block averages of U^{(AL)}_0.
- U_0 is the minimiser of A(U) over U regular on Z with M_{𝐁_k}(U) = M_{𝐁_k(Z)}(Q^{s*}_kV_k) on Z ∖ Λ and no constraint on Λ (LFR II (1.3), p.357 l.10–14, with "the results of Sect. G [15]", VAR).
- So the two runs' U″_k differ in three ways: (A-i) the fine lattice of the problem of U″_k at equal data; (A-ii) the data on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, through U_0, whose own problem is solved on the run's fine lattice; and (A-iii) the coupling in exp ig_kB, which differs relatively by ≤ g_k²|δ_k| and belongs with the coupling terms of (α).

(b) *(A-i), the depth at equal data (derived).* The chain of the Cauchy note's §9 is run for the multiscale problem, with a_j := εL^j the spacing of the layer Λ_j in run A's units and j(x) the layer of the point x. Each step has a multiscale form.
- *Regularity.* VAR Theorem 1 treats determining sets 𝔅_k = ∪_jΛ_j. With (7) on the support (§1), (9.2) holds on the cubes of the layer j with a_j in place of a: |F| ≤ C₀ε₁/a_j², |∇F| ≤ C₀ε₁/a_j³, |A| ≤ C₀ε₁/a_j in the cube gauge. The cube-gauge regularity (3.35)–(3.36) of Ũ := MU^B is §9.8's computation on each cube, and the weights (9.11) of the linearised averaging are local, so both hold layer by layer.
- *The blocking artifact.* b(W) = A_ε(MW) − A_{ε/L}(W) is a sum over the coarse plaquettes of the local Taylor remainders of §9.3, each bounded by the regularity of its cube. So |b(U^B)| ≤ Σ_xC_b(ε/a_{j(x)})ε₁², the sum over the points x of the layers in their own units, and the remainders of (9.5) satisfy |r₁| ≤ C_rεε₁/a_{j(x)}³, |r₀| ≤ C_rεε₁²/a_{j(x)}⁴ at x.
- *The residual.* VAR's contraction (116)–(121) is stated for the multiscale problem, on the ball (115), |A_1| < ε₄(L^jη)^{−1} and |∇A_1| < ε₄(L^jη)^{−2} on Ω_j (VAR p.294 l.59–60), with the weighted norms |·|_{(α)} of PBG (3.41) (p.397 l.20–27), and (9.6)–(9.7) are unchanged. PBG (3.42)–(3.44) (p.397 l.37–p.398 l.25) bound the propagator with the factor L^jη of the layer of x and the decay e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)} in the weighted distance PRT2 (2.46) (p.231 l.9–13). At x in the layer j, a source in the layer j′ enters a_j|𝔊ℒ*D*r₁(x)| with the factor a_j²/a_{j′}³ = (a_j/a_{j′})²a_{j′}^{−1}, and the plain source r₀ enters a_j|𝔊ℒ*r₀(x)| with the factor (a_j/a_{j′})³a_{j′}^{−3}, by the first two entries of PBG (3.42) (p.397 l.42–44) transferred to 𝔊 by Theorem 3.13 as in §9.5, with the constant 2B₀. For j′ ≥ j these factors are ≤ a_{j′}^{−1} and a_{j′}^{−3}. For j′ < j the factors L^{2(j−j′)} and L^{3(j−j′)} are paid by the decay: a contour from y′ to y crosses the layers between them, each of width > RM in its own units by PRT2 (2.2) (p.224 l.10–13), which is (2.57) (p.233) (for CRE's domains the boundaries are ≥ 2MR_j apart, CRE p.256 l.21–23, and 2R_j > R for γ small; for the R-operation's sets 𝐁₁ and 𝐁″_k, which Bałaban uses at LFR II (1.52)–(1.54) (p.370), the top N₀ layers of 𝐁″_k are only M wide and are covered by (J-W) below, and the middle layers h + 1 ≤ j ≤ k − N₀ of 𝐁″_k are at least 3LMR_{j+1} wide in their own units, *The middle layers.* below (derived, not reviewed); at the joins 𝐁₁ and 𝐁₂ of (1.52) the width is only LM₁, and RM is replaced by LM₁ under (J-W), which holds for M₁ large, *The joins.* below), and (2.48) (p.232 l.3–9) bounds d(y,y′) below by the segment lengths in each layer's units. Bałaban's count (2.58) (p.233) then bounds the sum over one layer: Σ_{y′∈Λ_{j′}}e^{−αδ₀d(y,y′)} ≤ 6c₀^d(½α)e^{−¼αδ₀RM·max(|j−j′|−1, 0)} for 0 < α < 1, c₀(α) := Σ_{z∈ℤ}e^{−αδ₀|z|} < 4/(αδ₀), under his condition (2.59), ¼αδ₀RM > 2d log c₀(½α) + 1. This form is the printed intermediate line of (2.58), Σ_{m≥m₀}e^{−½αδ₀mRM}c₀(½α)^{d(2m+1)} with m₀ := max(|j−j′|−1, 0), summed over m with (2.59); the printed final form is 6c₀^d(½α)e^{−|j−j′|}. Summed over all layers, Σ_{y′}e^{−αδ₀d(y,y′)} ≤ c_tot(α) := 26c₀^d(½α): the three layers with m₀ = 0 give 18c₀^d(½α), and the others 12c₀^d(½α)Σ_{m≥1}e^{−m} < 8c₀^d(½α). The same count bounds the sum over y in one layer, since d is symmetric. The gradient bound (3.44) has the Hölder seminorm at the scale ξ′ = L^{−j′} of the source, ≤ 2(a_{j′}/ε)^{ε₀} times its supremum, as in §9.5. So the residual is bounded pointwise: a_{j(x)}|𝔊J(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇𝔊J(x)| ≤ ρ(x) := C_G(ε₀)Σ_{y′}K(y,y′)(ε/a_{j′})^{1−ε₀}ε₁, K(y,y′) := L^{3(j−j′)_+}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}, y the block of x in the layer j and y′ in the layer j′, which covers both sources since L^{2(j−j′)} ≤ L^{3(j−j′)} for j′ < j. With α = ½ in the count, (2.59) at α = ½ and ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1, conditions on Bałaban's R and M, the row sums of K are ≤ c_K := 6c₀^d(¼)(3 + 2L³): the layer of y contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(¼); the n-th layer on the side without the factor L^{3n} contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(¼)e^{−(n−1)}, and the n-th layer on the other side ≤ 6c₀^d(¼)L^{3n}e^{−(4 log L+1)(n−1)} = 6c₀^d(¼)L^{4−n}e^{−(n−1)} ≤ 6c₀^d(¼)L³e^{−(n−1)}; and Σ_{n≥1}e^{−(n−1)} < 1.6. So sup_xρ(x) ≤ C_G(ε₀)c_K(ε/a_n)^{1−ε₀}ε₁, a_n the spacing of the finest layer, the birth scale n of the history (≥ k/2 − 1 in a young history). VAR's contraction runs in the layer-weighted sup norm of (115) and (117)–(121), so (9.6) gives the fixed point in that norm, not pointwise: a_{j(x)}|A₁*(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A₁*(x)| ≤ ρ̄ := 2C_G(ε₀)c_K(ε/a_n)^{1−ε₀}ε₁ at every x. This is the form used below.
- *The joins (derived, not reviewed, including the middle-layer width and the block size M′).* The sets 𝐁₁ = 𝐁″_k ∪ 𝐁_h(Ω″^∼_{h+1}) and 𝐁₂ = 𝐁″_k ∪ 𝐁_h((Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c) of LFR II (1.52) are joins by CRE (2.14), and their layers need not be wider than RM.
  - The collar. On Ω ∖ Ω^{∼−2} a join is 𝐁_h(Ω), whose domains satisfy dist(Ω_n, Ω_{n−1}^c) ≥ L^nξM₁ on T_ξ, ξ = L^{−h} (CRE p.256 l.57–60). So its layer n − 1 is at least LM₁ wide in its own units. This is VAR's (1) for R < L, and it is less than RM.
  - The join. By (2.14) the join is 𝐁 on Ω^{∼−2} and 𝐁_h(Ω) on the strip S := Ω ∖ Ω^{∼−2}, with ∂Ω in the layer h of 𝐁 at a distance ≥ 2M₁ from that layer's boundaries inside Ω (CRE p.257 l.9–15). Distances below are in the units of the scale h. The collar's domains Ω_n satisfy Ω^{∼−2} ⊂ Ω_h (p.256 l.61–62) and dist(Ω_h, Ω_{h−1}^c) ≥ M₁ (l.57–60 at n = h), and Ω″_{h+1}^∼ adds one layer of the MR_{h+1}-cubes of the scale h + 1 (LFR I p.179 l.6–7), so dist(Ω″_{h+1}, (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c) ≥ LMR_{h+1}. For j ≤ h, Ω″_j = Ω_j (LFR I (1.12)), and these layers of 𝐁″_k are wider than 2MR_j in the units of their scale j (CRE p.256 l.21–23). For h + 1 ≤ j ≤ k − N₀ the Ω″_j are R-operation domains, with Ω″_j ∖ Ω″_{j+1} = Ω_j^{∼5} ∖ Ω_{j+1}^{∼5} on Z, which CRE p.256 l.21–23 does not cover. Bałaban takes the functions U_{𝐁₁} and U_{𝐁₂} after (1.52) and expands U^0_k, the configuration of 𝐁″_k, around U_{1,2} by VAR's Sects. D–E at (1.54) (LFR II p.370), without stating the widths of these sets. The width of these middle layers is derived, not reviewed, in *The middle layers.* below, from CRE (3.5).
  - The layers of 𝐁₁. Here Ω = Ω″^∼_{h+1} ⊂ Ω″_h, so the join's domains Ω^J_j equal the collar's Ω_j for j ≤ h, and Ω″_{h+1} ⊂ Ω^{∼−2} for the layers above. The layers j ≤ h − 1 are the collar's, ≥ LM₁ wide in their own units. The layer h runs from ∂Ω_h, within 2M₁ of ∂Ω, to ∂Ω″_{h+1}, so its width is ≥ LMR_{h+1} − 2M₁ ≥ LM₁, since M > M₂ = L²M₁ (CRE p.245 l.35–38). The layers above h are those of 𝐁″_k: the top N₀ are M wide, and the middle ones, h + 1 ≤ j ≤ k − N₀, have the width of *The joins.*, derived, not reviewed.
  - The layers of 𝐁₂. Here Ω = (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c, the join's top layer is h, and it can be thin, since (2.14) gives only dist(∂Ω, ∂Ω″_h) ≥ 2M₁ ≥ dist(Ω_h, Ω^c). A layer j ≤ h − 1 of the join is the collar's layer j on S and 𝐁″_k's layer j on Ω^{∼−2}, and the distance from Ω^J_{j+1} to (Ω^J_j)^c is at least L^{j+1−h}M₁, LM₁ in the units of the scale j, in each of the four cases. Collar to collar it is the collar's width. 𝐁″_k to 𝐁″_k it is 𝐁″_k's width. From Ω″_{j+1} ∩ Ω^{∼−2} to S ∖ Ω_j or to Ω^c it is ≥ dist(Ω_h, Ω_{h−1}^c) ≥ M₁, since Ω^{∼−2} ⊂ Ω_h and Ω_j^c ⊂ Ω_{h−1}^c. From Ω_{j+1} ∩ S to (Ω″_j)^c it is ≥ dist(Ω^c, (Ω″_j)^c) − 2M₁ ≥ 2MR_{h−1}L^{−1}, since a path from Ω^c = Ω″^∼_{h+1} ⊂ Ω″_h to (Ω″_j)^c crosses ∂Ω″_h, at ≥ 2M₁ from ∂Ω, and then 𝐁″_k's layers h − 1, …, j.
  - The top layer of 𝐁₂ is not crossed. In PRT2's decomposition of a contour, each crossing joins two surfaces Σ_{j_l} and Σ_{j_{l+1}} with |j_l − j_{l+1}| = 1 and lies in the layer min{j_l, j_{l+1}} ((2.47), p.231 l.20–36). A crossing in the layer h of 𝐁₂ would join Σ_h with Σ_{h+1}, and 𝐁₂ has no Σ_{h+1}. So (2.57) uses only the widths of the layers below the top, and the thin top layer of 𝐁₂ does not enter the count.
  - The count at a smaller width. Bałaban's count (2.58) uses (2.2) only through (2.57), the length > RM of each crossing between two surfaces Σ_j (PRT2 p.233 l.18–23), and Lemma 2.1's (2.60), the decay e^{−αδ₀RM·max(|j−j′|−1, 0)} (p.234 l.2–6), follows from (2.48) and (2.57). So (2.57)–(2.61) and every count of this section hold with RM replaced by any common lower bound W of the layer widths in their own units, each line unchanged.
  - At the joins W = LM₁. The top N₀ layers of 𝐁″_k, inside the joins, are one layer of M-cubes of their own scale wide (LFR I p.179 l.19–24), so M wide in their own units, and M > M₂ = L²M₁ > LM₁ (CRE p.245 l.35–38, with Bałaban's choice M₂ = L²M₁ and M "much larger than M₂"). So every layer that a crossing traverses is ≥ LM₁ wide in its own units, in 𝐁₁ and in 𝐁₂, and so are the middle layers h + 1 ≤ j ≤ k − N₀ of 𝐁″_k in 𝐁₁, by the next item.
  - The middle layers (derived, not reviewed). For h + 1 ≤ j ≤ k − N₀, Z″_j = (Ω_j^{∼5})^c ∩ Z (LFR I (1.11), p.179), so on Z the layer j of 𝐁″_k is Ω_j^{∼5} ∖ Ω_{j+1}^{∼5} for j ≤ k − N₀ − 1, by (1.12). At j = k − N₀ the next set is Z″_{k−N₀+1} = (Ω^{∼7}_{k−N₀+1})^c ∩ Z (LFR I (1.10), p.179 l.15), so that layer is Ω^{∼5}_{k−N₀} ∖ Ω^{∼7}_{k−N₀+1}. Ω_j^{∼n} adds n layers of the cubes of Ω_j, of the side MR_j in the units a_j of the scale j (LFR I p.179 l.7–8). These Ω_j are the domains of the renormalisation steps j, not domains determined by the R-operation (the two kinds of CRE p.256 l.21–22), as Bałaban states (checked). By the condition (ii) (LFR I p.177 l.27–28), no new large-field region was created in Z in the last N steps, and he draws the consequence for (1.2) at once: the one-step operations 𝕋^{(j)}(Z_{j+1}) "are given by the formula (2.21) [III], in which the functions ζ, χ have the simplest form, namely by the condition (ii) no large field characteristic functions are included in them" (LFR I p.178, below (1.2)). Their ζ(Ω_{j+1}^c ∩ Z_{j+1})χ(Ω_{j+1} ∩ Z_{j+1}) is the product (1.3)–(1.9) of the small-field tests of step j, so Ω_{j+1} on Z is the domain that step j's tests determine, and (1.11)–(1.12) take these Ω_j. A step adds at least ten layers of MR-cubes to the large-field region (LFR I p.177 l.18–19). Directly from CRE (3.5) (p.265), Ω_{j+1} lies three layers of MR_{j+1}a_{j+1}-cubes inside B^{j+1}(P^1_{j+1}). That set lies five further layers inside the complement of Z′_j ⊇ Λ_j^c ⊇ Ω_j^c (CRE p.264 l.52–56, p.265 l.16–22; Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j by CRE p.269 l.9, Λ_{k+1} = Ω_{k+1}^{∼−2} ∩ (R′^∼_{k+1})^c). So dist(Ω_{j+1}, Ω_j^c) ≥ 8MR_{j+1}a_{j+1}. The enlargement ∼5 moves the boundary of Ω_{j+1} outwards by at most 5MR_{j+1}a_{j+1}, and that of Ω_j outwards, which only widens the layer. So dist(Ω_{j+1}^{∼5}, (Ω_j^{∼5})^c) ≥ 3MR_{j+1}a_{j+1} = 3LMR_{j+1}a_j, and the layer j ≤ k − N₀ − 1 is at least 3LMR_{j+1} ≥ 3LM > LM₁ wide in its own units (R_{j+1} ≥ 1, M > M₂ = L²M₁). At j = k − N₀ the enlargement ∼7 moves the boundary by at most 7MR_{j+1}a_{j+1}, so that layer is at least LMR_{j+1} ≥ LM > LM₁ wide. The same bounds give VAR's (1) for this sequence, (L^jη)^{−1}dist(Ω″_j^c, Ω″_{j+1}) > RM₁ on Z, with R and M₁ the parameters of VAR's (1) (REG (1.4)), under LM > RM₁, which holds for M large (CRE p.245 l.35–38). At the joins these parameters are R′ and M′ (*The separation.*), and LM > LM₁ > R′M′. With Bałaban's ten layers the width is 5LMR_{j+1}. Case 4 of 𝐁₂ uses only the layers ≤ h − 1 of 𝐁″_k, where Ω″ = Ω. The conditions that the counts of this section put on RM become the condition (J-W): (2.59) at α = 1/128 and ⅛δ_*LM₁ ≥ 4 log L + 1, at the rate δ_* of *The rate.* below. Their left sides increase with the width and the right side of (2.59) decreases with α, so (J-W) implies the conditions at every α ≥ 1/128 and at every width ≥ LM₁, and it replaces the conditions on R and M wherever a layer that the profile crosses is narrower than RM: at the joins, and at the top N₀ layers of 𝐁″_k, which are M wide.
  - (J-W) holds for M₁ large.
    - The rate. Two rates enter the counts: PRT2's local rate δ₀^{(2.43)}, inside the proofs through Lemma 2.1, and the rate δ₀^{PBG} of PBG Theorem 3.1, whose constants M₁, δ₀, a₀, and B₀ are "dependent on d and L only" and serve "for M ≥ M₁", M the size of the big blocks (PBG p.397 l.37–38). Both depend on d and L only (*RDG's big blocks.* below). The counts are taken at δ_* := min(δ₀^{PBG}, δ₀^{(2.43)}). The left side of (2.59) increases with δ₀ and c₀ decreases with it, so the conditions at δ_* give those at either rate, and no comparison of the two rates is needed. The distance is PRT2 (2.46), in the block units of each layer (p.231 l.9–13).
    - Theorem 3.1 at the joins. PBG proves Theorems 3.1–3.3 by generalised random walk expansions that reduce them to the theorems for propagators without external field proved in PRT2, and states their constants independent of {Ω_j} "if the conditions (2.1), (2.2) are satisfied" (PBG p.399). In PRT2's counts, (2.2) enters through the length > RM of each crossing in (2.57) (p.233): Lemma 2.1 (p.234) and the factors e^{−cδ₀RM·max(|j−j′|−1, 0)} of p.235 and p.237. PRT2's own localisations also use the layer geometry, with cubes of the size 2ML^jη that meet at most B^{j+1}(Λ_{j+1}) besides B^j(Λ_j) (p.230 l.1–2, p.235 l.35, p.238, the last four lines, p.239 l.40–42); the reach below covers them, PRT2's largest cube being □̃³. The choice of Ω₀ at distance ≤ RM from Ω₁ is an example ("e.g.") at PRT2 p.228 l.20 and PBG p.394 l.32; at PBG p.396 l.17 it defines Λ₀ for the regularity class, which holds at R′M′ as well. PBG's own localisation is a further use of the layer geometry: its cubes □̃³ are "either contained in B^j(Λ_j), or intersect also the domain B^{j+1}(Λ_{j+1})" (p.408 l.19–34), with local sequences satisfying "(2.1)–(2.4) of [4]" (p.408 l.2) and "the conditions (2.1), (2.2)" (p.408 l.34). The printed dichotomy names only the inner domain; as an exhaustive dichotomy it assumes that □̃³ does not meet the finer domains, and PBG does not treat that outward case. The reach into B^{j−1}(Λ_{j−1}) occurs at any width; the reach into B^{j−2}(Λ_{j−2}) is the case specific to layers LM₁ wide. PBG's cubes are □̃ⁿ, "a cube of the size (2 + 2n)ML^jη" (p.408 l.23–24), and its localisation uses n ≤ 5 (□̃³ in the dichotomy, Ω_j(□) ⊂ □̃⁴, and Ω₀(□) ⊂ □̃⁵) and n = 8 in Sect. C (p.415 l.9–10). A cube of 𝒟_j has one big block in B^j(Λ_j) (p.408 l.21) and can reach one big block beyond ∂Ω_j, so its □̃⁵ reaches about 6ML^jη outwards, while the finer layer Λ_{j−1}, LM wide in its own units, is only M wide in the units L^jη; so □̃⁵ can meet Λ_{j−2}, for every L. This happens in Bałaban's own sequences as well: CRE's maximal sequences 𝐁 have layers L^nξM₁ deep, LM₁ in their own units (p.256 l.57–62), and CRE applies the theorems of the previous papers to them at the size M₁ (p.245 l.35–37). At the joins the sequences are these sequences 𝐁 of CRE, with M₁-cubes. CRE takes M₁ as "the size of large cubes for which all the theorems of the previous papers, especially those concerning the variational problem, are valid" (p.245 l.35–37). Here, at the joins, the theorems of PBG and VAR are applied instead with a smaller block size, so that the reach into B^{j−2}(Λ_{j−2}) does not occur (derived, not reviewed):
      - *The block size M′.* Let M′ be a power of L with M′ ≥ max(M₁^{PBG}, R₀M₀, R₁M₁^{VAR}), with M₁^{PBG} the threshold M₁(d, L) of PBG Theorems 3.1–3.3 (p.397 l.38, "for M ≥ M₁"), R₀M₀ that of PBG's local sequences, and R₁M₁^{VAR} VAR's product, the last two taken as powers of L, so that M′ is a multiple of each: PBG takes M = KR₀M₀ (p.408 l.28), and VAR's cube parameters are multiples of R₁M₁ (VAR p.279 l.3, p.300 l.29–30). Take CRE's M₁ := KM′ with K a power of L and K ≥ 12d + 1, since PBG identifies the big-block lattice with T^{(j+m)} for M = L^m (p.408 l.20). This covers the reach below (K ≥ 10d + 1), the lift's R_ι (K ≥ 4d + 1), and the W-δ note's condition R ≥ 12dL on its walk problems (its §§7, 11, and 12), which at the joins reads R′ = LK − 1 ≥ 12dL and holds since L(12d + 1) − 1 ≥ 12dL. VAR's own M′ := M/(R₁M₁) (VAR p.300 l.30, p.304 l.21–24) is written M′_V here. R′ is not a power of L; the joins use PRT2's conditions (2.1)–(2.4), whose R is "a big positive integer" (PRT2 (2.2)). PBG's choice of R and M as powers of L (p.408 l.26–28) is read as concerning its own local sequences. If it is read as a condition on the sequences themselves, K, a power of L, replaces R′: the separation is then KM′ = M₁ < LM₁, and (J-W) at the width M₁ needs M₁ ≥ LM_J(d, L) instead of 2M_J(d, L), again a lower bound. This is compatible with CRE's conditions on M₁, which are lower bounds. Every M₁-cube of the scale j is a union of K^d M′-cubes, so the domains of 𝐁 are unions of M′-blocks, PRT2 (2.1); so are those of 𝐁″_k, unions of MR_j-cubes, since M′ divides M₁ and M. M′ also satisfies (1/16)δ₀M′ > 2d log c₀(⅛) + 1, PRT2's (2.59) at α = ¼ with RM = M′, which the count of the strip sources at the composites uses ((iv), *The size of the strip sources.*); it is a lower bound of d, δ₀, and c₀(⅛).
      - *R₁ = 1 (a renaming of the product).* VAR's own constructions read R₁M₁ only as the product: the cube class of Theorem 1 (p.279 l.2–4), the Sect. F sequences (144) and cubes □̃ (p.300 l.33–37), (163), (165), and M(ε₁). With M′ a multiple of VAR's R₁M₁ they are run at the cube parameter M′, which is what R₁ = 1 means here. The results of [3, 5, 6] (REG, PRT2, PBG) that VAR invokes on its local sequences (VAR p.277) are applied at VAR's own block size M₁^{VAR}: a sequence of unions of M′-blocks whose layers are R′M′ wide is a sequence of unions of M₁^{VAR}-blocks whose layers are R′M′/M₁^{VAR} ≥ R₁ blocks wide, so VAR's printed hypotheses hold. It is not the reading "[3, 5, 6] at the block size M′ with R = 1", under which a cube of the index j would cross a layer of the index j − 1. Stated plainly, VAR's block size M₁^{VAR} of p.277 l.12 is kept and its R₁ is raised to M′/M₁^{VAR}, which is admissible since [3, 5, 6] need only lower bounds on R; every condition used is a condition on lengths, which the two statements give alike, and VAR Theorem 1 then applies to cubes whose parameter is any multiple of R₁M₁, M′ included.
      - *The separation.* CRE's layers satisfy dist(Ω_n, Ω_{n−1}^c) ≥ L^nξM₁ = (LK)M′L^{n−1}ξ (p.256 l.57–62), so PRT2 (2.2) and VAR (1) hold at the size M′ with R′ := LK − 1, and ½LM₁ ≤ R′M′ < LM₁, since LK ≥ 2. So (J-W) at the width R′M′ holds once M₁ ≥ 2M_J(d, L) (*The choice of M₁.*). The printed conditions on R, M, R₁, and M₁ are lower bounds of four kinds: on the products RM and R₁M₁, among them VAR's "R₁M₁ sufficiently big, so that B₃e^{−½δ₀R₁M₁} ≤ ½" (p.304 l.2–4); separate ones, R ≥ R₁ (VAR p.277 l.17) and R, M "sufficiently large" (PBG p.408 l.26), of which the latter fixes R₀ and M₀ of PBG's local sequences, which are unchanged here, while R enters PRT2 only through the product RM, in (2.59) and (2.83), and through the layer geometry, so R′ needs no separate threshold; divisibility, M = KR₀M₀ (PBG p.408 l.28) and VAR's cube parameters multiples of R₁M₁ (VAR p.279 l.3, p.300 l.29–30; REG p.098 l.6–8); and ratios, REG's example "L^{−1}RM > 2dR₁M₁", which gives □̃ ⊂ Ω_{k−1} (REG p.098 l.17–18), and the reach below. VAR p.300 l.36–37 states □̃ ⊂ Ω_{j−1} for its Sect. F cubes without a printed ratio; for its cubes of the side 2R₁M₁ the derived condition is R > 4dLR₁ (the W-δ note's §12, *The lift's R_ι (checked).*), which R′ meets at R₁ = 1 for K ≥ 4d + 1, since LK − 1 ≥ 4dL + L − 1. The conditions tied to M from above are smallness conditions on α₀ and ε (PBG p.397 l.38, p.408 l.4; VAR p.304 l.24; REG p.098 l.35–36) and VAR's M ≤ M(ε₁) (p.279 l.28), which hold for γ small. No condition bounds R, M₁, or M′ from above, and with the threshold of *The block size M′.* all are met. The counts of this section read the crossing lengths, which are ≥ LM₁.
      - *The reach.* PBG's cubes at the size M′ are □̃ⁿ of the side (2 + 2n)M′L^jη. In Sect. C, PBG takes G′_{□̃₀²} with the boundary conditions outside □̃₀⁵, which it identifies in the original cubes with the outside of □̃⁸, or of □̃₁⁸ with □₁ ∈ 𝒟_{j+1} (p.415 l.9–10, with p.411 l.22–25 and p.413 l.6–10). Theorems 3.9 and 3.10 state that their terms depend on U restricted to the enlargements X̃⁵ of their localisation domains, among them X = □̃₀² = □̃⁴ itself. Under the convention (2 + 2n), the fivefold enlargement of □̃⁴, of the side 10M′L^jη, has the side 20M′L^jη, that of □̃⁹; so Bałaban's identification □̃₀⁵ = □̃⁸ is not exact, and □̃⁹ bounds both. Every other cube of PBG is □̃ⁿ with n ≤ 5, and PRT2's are at most □̃³ (PRT2 p.238, the last four lines). □̃⁹ reaches at most 10M′L^jη beyond ∂Ω_j. The layer Λ_{j−1} of 𝐁 is M₁L^jη = KM′L^jη wide in these units in the lattice distance, which is the ℓ¹ distance (PRT2 p.223 l.16), and an ℓ∞ reach of 10M′L^jη is an ℓ¹ reach of at most 10dM′L^jη; K ≥ 10d + 1 > 10d. The geometric two-layer conditions, PBG's class cubes in B^j(Λ_j) ∪ B^{j+1}(Λ_{j+1}) (the class of (3.35), p.396, used for □̃⁵ on p.409) and PRT2's torus □̃³ carrying two scales (p.238, the last two lines, and p.239), are covered by the same count; inwards they hold with a large margin, since Λ_{j+1} is L²KM′ wide in the units L^jη. For □₁ ∈ 𝒟_{j+1} the same holds in the units L^{j+1}η against the layer Λ_j, which is KM′ wide in these units. So every localisation domain of PBG meets at most the layers j − 1, j, and j + 1. The reach into j − 1 is the one that occurs at every width, in PBG's own sequences too, and the case specific to layers LM₁ wide does not arise.
      - *The constants.* PBG's constants are then those at M′, which depend on d and L only (p.397 l.38–39). The regularity (3.35) at the cube size M′, |A| < O(1)M′α₀(L^jη)^{−1} and the like (p.396 l.25), scales with the size, so it does not follow from (3.35) at M₁. It follows at the same α₀ from the plaquette regularity in an axial gauge: REG's argument on p.098 gives (3.35) for every cube in Ω_j and not in Ω_{j+1} that is a union of R₁M₁-cubes, under L^{−1}RM > 2dR₁M₁, which holds here. It derives (1.128) from REG's hypotheses (1.7)–(1.9) by Proposition 2 of [3], then the axial-gauge bound (1.129), (M + 4R₁M₁)dL²α₀, linear in the size, and then (3.35) by applying Lemma 1 many times (p.098 l.36–37 and the next page). The hypotheses (1.7)–(1.9) are the plaquette bound α₀η²(L^jη)^{−2} and the bound α₀L^{−2j}(L^jη)^{−1} of D^{η*}_U∂U, which is the bound α₀(L^jη)^{−3} of the current. At real points of the class they are CRE's (2.34) and (2.36), p.261, with α₀ := max_n α_{0,n}, which is small for γ small. (VAR (9) at the cube parameter M′ would also give it, but that needs a matching of VAR's cube class, □̃ ⊂ B^j(Λ_j) ∪ B^{j+1}(Λ_{j+1}) (p.279 l.3–4), with PBG's, which is not used.) On that route the smallness O(1)M′α₀ ≤ a₀ is weaker than at M₁. VAR's constants at M′ are constants of d and L, since M′ = M′(d, L); its smallness thresholds that contain R₁M₁, M′_Vε₀ ≤ a₅ of VAR p.304 l.24 and α₀ ≤ O(1)(R₁M₁)^{−1} of REG p.098 l.35–36, become upper bounds on ε₀ and α₀, which hold for γ small.
      So the joins use PBG's and VAR's theorems at M′ within their printed hypotheses, with no condition on L from p.408. At the top N₀ layers of 𝐁″_k, one M-cube of their own scale wide (LFR I p.179 l.19–24), the block size M with R = 1 would be outside PRT2 (2.2) as printed; the same device covers them, since their domains are unions of M′-blocks and their widths M exceed R′M′ < LM₁ < M, so PBG's localisation is applied there at M′ as well, with the reach above. So Theorems 3.1–3.3 hold with RM replaced by a common width W of the layers, under (J-W) at W = LM₁. Bałaban also uses the joins' functions himself: LFR II takes U_{𝐁₁} and U_{𝐁₂} after (1.52) (p.370).
    - RDG's big blocks. PRT2's δ₀ of (2.43) comes from Lemmas 2.2 and 2.4 and Proposition 2.3 of its reference [3], RDG (p.230 l.40–43, p.250 l.33). RDG's rates are proportional to the inverse size of its own big blocks ((2.22), p.579 l.11–22, and Corollary 2.3, p.580 l.62–p.581 l.11). That size is "a large positive integer defined later in this paper" (RDG p.572 l.11–13), fixed by 3^dc₂O(1)M^{−1} ≤ e^{−1}, so it is a constant of RDG's proof, not the cubes of the domain. So δ₀ does not depend on M₁.
    - The choice of M₁. With c₀(1/256) < 1024/δ_* at the rate δ_*, (J-W) holds once M₁ ≥ M_J(d, L) := max{8(4 log L + 1), 512(2d log(1024/δ_*) + 1)}/(δ_*L). CRE's M₁ is "the size of large cubes for which all the theorems of the previous papers, especially those concerning the variational problem, are valid" (CRE p.245 l.35–37). PBG's theorems hold at every larger size (Theorem 3.1, "for M ≥ M₁"), and VAR's at multiples of its block size by *R₁ = 1 (a renaming of the product).* So M₁ := KM′, with M′ and K as in *The block size M′.*, is taken as a power of L at least 2M_J(d, L), the factor 2 for the width R′M′ ≥ ½LM₁ of *The separation.*, before M₂ = L²M₁ and M (l.35–38).
- *The profile.* For any multiscale determining set satisfying PRT2 (2.1)–(2.2), the coarse-side configuration difference of U^A and MU^B at x is set by the layer of x. A bond b belongs to the layer j when b ∈ Ω_j ∖ Ω_{j+1}, as in PBG (3.41), where Ω also denotes the set of bonds with at least one endpoint in Ω (PRT2 p.224 l.17–19); so a bond that straddles the boundary of Ω_{j+1} belongs to the layer j + 1. The blocks of each layer partition its bonds: Δ(y), for a block y of the layer j, is a set of bonds of the layer j with an endpoint in y, each bond in exactly one block. Put π(x) := (ε/a_{j(x)})^{1−ε₀} ≤ 1. The norms are |f|_π := sup_xπ(x)^{−1}(a_{j(x)}|f(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇f(x)|) for fields, |f|_{π,(−3)} := sup_xπ(x)^{−1}a_{j(x)}³|f(x)| for sources, and VAR's |A|_∞ := max{|A|_{(−1)}, |∇A|_{(−2)}} of (115). For a block y of the layer j, ‖A‖_y := sup_{b∈Δ(y)}(a_j|A(b)| + a_j²|∇A(b)|).
  - *The ratio.* By PRT2 (2.60) (p.234 l.5), d(y,y′) ≥ RM(|j−j′| − 1), so π(y′)/π(y) ≤ L^{(1−ε₀)|j−j′|} ≤ L^{1−ε₀}e^{η_πd(y,y′)}, η_π := (1−ε₀)log L/(RM) ≤ δ₀/32 under ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1. Likewise a_{j′}/a_j ≤ L^{|j−j′|} ≤ L·e^{(log L/(RM))d(y,y′)} ≤ L·e^{(δ₀/32)d(y,y′)}.
  - *The propagator.* The plain-source bound of the residual bullet is a_j|𝔊f(x)| + a_j²|∇𝔊f(x)| ≤ 2B₀Σ_{y′}(a_j/a_{j′})³e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}sup_{Δ(y′)}a_{j′}³|f|. With π(y′)/π(y) = L^{(1−ε₀)(j−j′)}, the n-th layer on the finer side contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(¼)L^{(4−ε₀)n}e^{−(4 log L+1)(n−1)} ≤ 6c₀^d(¼)L⁴e^{−(n−1)} by the count (2.58) at α = ½, and the n-th layer on the coarser side ≤ 6c₀^d(¼)e^{−(n−1)}. So |𝔊f|_π ≤ 2B₀c″_K|f|_{π,(−3)}, c″_K := 6c₀^d(¼)(3 + 2L⁴). The same count bounds the π-weighted row sums of K, whose entries equal those of the propagator kernel on the finer side and are ≤ e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)} on the coarser side: ρ(x) ≤ C_G(ε₀)π(x)ε₁Σ_{y′}K(y,y′)π(y′)/π(y) ≤ C_G(ε₀)c″_Kπ(x)ε₁, so |ρ|_π := sup_xπ(x)^{−1}ρ(x) ≤ C_G(ε₀)c″_Kε₁.
  - *The kernel form of VAR Proposition 4.* Let N := δV/δA′, VAR (80) and (85)–(96). For complex A with |A|_∞ ≤ ε₃ ≤ ¼a₃ and every bond b of the block y, a_{j(y)}³|N(A)(b)| ≤ C_Nε₃Σ_{y″}e^{−⅛δ₀d(y,y″)}‖A‖_{y″}, with C_N a constant of d, L, and Bałaban's constants, under ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1 and (2.59) at α = 1/128. Here d = 4, the case of the note. VAR's (98) is the uniform form of this bound; the kernel form keeps one factor of A at its point. It has three ingredients.
  - (i) *The vertices.* Each vertex of VAR's formulas is a function of the field on the bonds near one block or plaquette. The block functions C_j(·,c) and δC_j/δA (VAR (49), (69), (72), [4] Prop. 5) are analytic on the local polydisc of a fixed radius r_v in the units a_j|·| and are bounded there by M_v, both constants of d and L, by (44), (52)–(54), (72), the range argument of (59)–(62) (p.287), and locality. On the complex line through the local field X, of local size s, Schwarz's lemma, applied to τ ↦ f(τX/s) on |τ| < r_v, bounds a vertex f that vanishes to order m at 0 by M_v(s/r_v)^m. For s ≥ ½r_v the direct bound (72) at the actual field gives |f| ≤ M_v ≤ M_v(2s/r_v)^m, so |f| ≤ M_v(2s/r_v)^m for every s, and the factor 2^m enters C_v. For δC_j/δA, m = 1 by (44) and (56), which gives |a_j(δC_j/δA)(X)(c,·)| ≤ C_va_j^{1−d}s, the local form of (72); for its part beyond the linear one, m = 2, which gives C_va_j^{1−d}(s/r_v)s. The plaquette functions (∂/∂A)V₀′(·,∂p) and the commutator terms of (91)–(96) have explicit bounds in the field on ∂p and its differences (VAR (40), p.291, (94)–(96)), of order ≥ 2, so one factor is the local size and the others are ≤ 2ε₃. A local size at a block c is ≤ L·max ‖·‖_{y′} over the blocks y′ meeting the bonds of the vertex for the vertices that depend on the field alone, and ≤ L²·max ‖·‖_{y′} for the gradient vertices of (91)–(96), since adjacent layers differ by one factor L in a_j and L² in a_j².
  - (ii) *D, HD, and 𝔇₂.* D(0) = 0 and D(A) = ∫₀¹⟨𝔇(tA), A⟩dt, so (73) gives |D(A,c)| ≤ O(1)C₃ε₃Σ_ye^{−½δ₀d(c₋,y)}‖A‖_y, with one factor of A at its point and the other in (73). With (3.133) for H and ∇H in the form of VAR (69), the printed rates δ₁ and ½δ₁ renamed δ₀ on PBG p.423 l.39, ‖HD(A)‖_y ≤ C_Hε₃Σ_{y″}e^{−¼δ₀d(y,y″)}‖A‖_{y″}, and the same bound holds for A^♯ := A − HD(A) with 1 + C_Hε₃ in place of C_Hε₃ (A^♯ is not VAR's A″ of p.292). For 𝔇₂ := 𝔇 − 𝔇^{(1)}, 𝔇^{(1)} the derivative of D^{(2)} = C^{(2)} (VAR p.289 l.37–39), so that 𝔇^{(1)} = 𝒱^{(1)}(A), (70) gives 𝔇₂ = (I+ℜ)^{−1}[𝒱(A^♯) − 𝒱^{(1)}(A^♯)] + (I+ℜ)^{−1}𝒱^{(1)}(A^♯ − A) − (I+ℜ)^{−1}ℜ𝒱^{(1)}(A), with 𝒱(X) := a_j(δC_j/δA)(X) and 𝒱^{(1)} its linear part. The first bracket is of order 2 by (i), A^♯ − A = −HD(A) is bounded above, and |ℜ| ≤ 9C₂B₀ε₃ by (69), so with (71), |𝔇₂(A;c,b)| ≤ C_𝔇ε₃a_j^{1−d}e^{−¼δ₀d(c₋,y)}Σ_{y″}e^{−¼δ₀d(y,y″)}‖A‖_{y″} for b ∈ B^j(y).
  - (iii) *The chains.* Every term of N in (85), (88)–(90), and (91)–(96) is a chain of at most six links. The links are the kernels H, H*, Q, Q*, (QGQ*)^{−1}, a, 𝔇*(A), and 𝔇₂(A), at the bounds (69), (71), (73), and (3.132)–(3.133), with Q and Q* at the bound of a local average (a kernel ≤ 1, supported within one block and its neighbours), together with the vertices and fields of (i)–(ii); Δ_πH enters only through (89), which reduces it to these links. In each term one link carries the local size: D(A) by (ii) in the first line of (88) and in (89); QA in the second line of (88); 𝔇₂ by (ii) in (85); and the vertex by (i) in (90)–(96). Every other link is at its printed bound. The factor ε₃ comes from the bound of the local-size link itself in the first line of (88), the first part of (90), and the direct terms of (91)–(96), and from another link in the other terms; in (85) the current J also supplies ε₁. The variants of (91)–(96) that VAR sketches are chains of the same kinds. The sum over each intermediate block uses the triangle inequality (2.54) and the count c_tot(1/128), which costs δ₀/128 of the decay per link. In d = 4 a pairing of a source with a field over a block is scale-invariant, Σ_{b∈Δ(y)}η^da_j^{−3}a_j^{−1} ≤ d, so in the variables a_j|A|, a_j³|f|, and D the links are dimensionless kernels. Where a printed bound places the factor a^{−1} of a one-form at the other block of its link, as H in (86), the ratio a_{j′}/a_j is ≤ L·e^{(δ₀/32)d} by *The ratio.*, at most once per link. The links decay at the rates r_i: δ₀ for H, H*, (QGQ*)^{−1}, and ℜ (after PBG p.423 l.39), ½δ₀ for (71) and (73), and ¼δ₀ for the bounds of (ii). A ratio costs δ₀/32 of its own link's rate, so the i-th link decays at ρ_i ≥ r_i − δ₀/32, and ρ := min_iρ_i ≥ 7δ₀/32. With d_i the distance spanned by the i-th link, Π_ie^{−ρ_id_i} ≤ e^{−(ρ−δ₀/128)Σ_id_i}Π_ie^{−(δ₀/128)d_i}, with Σ_id_i ≥ d(y,y″) by (2.54). The sums over the at most five intermediate blocks, taken one at a time, each use one factor e^{−(δ₀/128)d_i} and give ≤ c_tot(1/128)⁵ in all. So the chain decays from y to y″ at the rate ≥ ρ − δ₀/128 ≥ 27δ₀/128 > ⅛δ₀, which gives the stated bound.
  - *The a priori inequality.* A₁* lies in VAR's ball (115) with ε₄ = 3B₀C₁B₃ε₁ (Proposition 6, B = 0 at equal data) and is the fixed point A₁* = −𝔊J − 𝔊N(A₁*) of (116). Put u(y) := ‖A₁*‖_y. The propagator bound and the kernel form at ε₃ := ε₄ give u(y) ≤ π(y)|ρ|_π + 2B₀C_Nε₄Σ_{y′}(a_j/a_{j′})³e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}Σ_{y″}e^{−⅛δ₀d(y′,y″)}u(y″). Write π(y″)/π(y) = [π(y″)/π(y′)][π(y′)/π(y)]. The first factor is ≤ L^{1−ε₀}e^{η_πd(y′,y″)} by *The ratio.*, so the y″-sum is ≤ L^{1−ε₀}c_tot(1/128)π(y′)|u|_π, since ⅛δ₀ − η_π ≥ δ₀/128. The y′-sum with the second factor is the count of *The propagator.*, ≤ c″_K. So |u|_π ≤ |ρ|_π + 2B₀C_NLc_tot(1/128)c″_Kε₄|u|_π. The lattice is finite, so |u|_π < ∞, and under (T-π) below |u|_π ≤ (4/3)|ρ|_π. No contraction in |·|_π is needed.
  - *The configuration difference.* VAR (112) with B = 0 gives U^A = exp(iεA′*)MU^B, A′* := A₁* − HD(A₁*), in VAR's gauge RD*A′* = 0 relative to MU^B (VAR (76)). Here A′* is the field of (112), not the variable A′ of N := δV/δA′. By (ii) at ε₃ := ε₄ and *The ratio.*, π(y)^{−1}‖HD(A₁*)‖_y ≤ C_HLc_tot(1/128)ε₄|u|_π, so |A′*|_π ≤ (3/2)|u|_π ≤ 2|ρ|_π. Hence, for X = A₁* and X = A′*, a_{j(x)}|X(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇X(x)| ≤ ρ̄(x) := 2C_G(ε₀)c″_K(ε/a_{j(x)})^{1−ε₀}ε₁ at every x. The condition (T-π) is 8B₀C_NLc_tot(1/128)c″_Kε₄ ≤ 1, 2C_HLc_tot(1/128)ε₄ ≤ 1, ε₄ ≤ ¼a₃ (for the kernel form at ε₃ := ε₄), and VAR's ε₄ = 3B₀C₁B₃ε₁ ≤ a₄ (for Proposition 6 and (115)), smallness conditions on ε₁ uniform in k, K, ε, the volume, and the number of layers.
  - *The result.* On a problem whose finest layer is the history's, j(x) ≥ n, and this is the bound ρ̄ above with c″_K in place of c_K. In the presence of layers finer than the history's, those of older histories, U^A and MU^B at equal data, compared in VAR's gauge relative to MU^B, differ at x by an amount set by the layer of x, not by the finest layer of the set. This is the coarse-side comparison; the fine-side comparison of ι(U^A) with U^B in the Cauchy note's §9.7 is not covered by this bullet.
- *The gap.* Every term of §9.6 is a sum over the lattice of products of A₁*, its gradient, and the local quantities J, F, and Δ₁ at the same point, so the bounds of §9.6 hold point by point with ρ̄ and a_{j(x)}: A_ε(Ũ) − A_ε(U^A) ≤ Σ_x[C_gap(ε₀)(ε/a_{j(x)})ρ̄ε₁ + Cρ̄²], the sum over the points of the layers in their own units. Since ε/a_{j(x)} ≤ ε/a_n, the gap is ≤ C′_gap(ε₀)Σ_x(ε/a_n)^{2−2ε₀}ε₁², C′_gap(ε₀) := 2C_G(ε₀)c_K(C_gap + 2CC_G(ε₀)c_K).
- *The weighted action.* LFR II (1.70) evaluates A(ζ₁/(g″)², ·) at the minimiser of the unweighted action, so for the weights w := ζ₁/(g″)² the first-order term of the gap does not vanish. It is ⟨J_w, A₁*⟩ with |J_w| ≤ C(|w| + a_{j(x)}|∇w|)ε₁/a_{j(x)}³ at x, so it is ≤ Σ_xC sup|w|(ε/a_n)^{1−ε₀}ε₁² per point, by the bound ρ̄ on A₁*.
- *The result.* For the action of (1.70) at equal data, |A(w, U″^B) − A(w, U″^A)| ≤ Σ_xC_w(ε₀)g_{j(x)}^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{1−ε₀}ε₁², the sum over the points of the layers, with C_w(ε₀) a constant of Bałaban's and VAR's constants and of ε₀, and a_n = εL^n the spacing of the history's finest layer. With g_{j(x)}^{−2} ≤ g_a^{−2} + β′k by CRE (2.6) and ε₁ ≤ Cḡp₀(ḡ) by §1, this is ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of the history, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−2ε₀)} and the factor linear in k is absorbed by L^{−ε₀n}, n > k/8, into C_h, a constant of ε₀, ḡ, j(C), and Bałaban's constants. This is the birth-scale form of (LF) (the Cauchy note's (LF) line). Outside the history the layer is the stopping layer, and the per-point bound is that of the single-scale (9.10), with the tails of the history's sources included in the sum.

(c) *(A-ii), the depth of U_0 (derived).* Let a be the spacing of the scale of (1.3) in run A's units, so ε/a ≤ L^{−(k/2−1)} in a young history (§1). Λ is a rectangular parallelepiped in a cube of side 100M, with the graph G₀ fixing the axial gauge in Λ (LFR II p.358 l.5–8). The data of (1.3) and the field U_0 are regular with ε₁ := C(M)R_kε_k, by the regularity of Sect. F (LFR II p.357 l.37–40), with C(M) ≥ C_X(M) of the a priori location below.
- *The nested problem.* For V′ on the bonds of Λ^{(k)} with δ_{G₀}(V′), U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ) is the minimiser with data on all of Z. By LFR I Proposition 1 (p.194 l.37–48), V′ ↦ A(U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ)) has exactly one critical orbit, a minimum, and U_0 = U_{k,Z}(V′₀V_Λ) at its minimiser V′₀ ((1.79), p.195 l.7; LFR II p.357 l.10–14). Here V_Λ denotes the data off Λ; LFR I's V_Λ is V′₀V_Λ, and LFR I's axial gauge in Λ^{(k)} is LFR II's G₀. So V′₀ minimises Φ(V′) := A(U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ)). This is the point around which LFR II expands (p.356 l.29–30, by RG I (2.8)). Each run X has its own Φ^X, built on its own fine lattice, as a function of the same variables V′, with the minimiser V′^X₀. Define X on the bonds of Λ^{(k)} ∖ G₀ by V′^B₀ = e^{iX}V′^A₀, and Δ := ‖X‖₂.
- *The expansion at a minimum.* In the variables V′ = e^{iY}V′^X₀, RG I (2.8) (p.266 l.39–42) with ζ₀ ≡ 1 gives Φ^X(V′) = Φ^X(V′^X₀) + ⟨H_{1,k}Y, J_0⟩ + ½⟨H_{1,k}Y, Δ₁H_{1,k}Y⟩ + W^X(Y), with W^X of at least third order. The linear term vanishes by (1.4) (LFR II p.357 l.15–20). The form is ≥ κ_Λ‖Y‖₂², κ_Λ := γ₀/(2d(100M)⁵), by (1.9) (p.358 l.13–16): the proof (1.7)–(1.8) replaces ζ₀ by 1 as one of its steps.
- *The remainder.* VAR Sect. G ((172)–(175), p.305) writes U_{k,Z}(e^{iY}V′^X₀V_Λ) = e^{iηℋ(Y)}U_0 as an analytic function of Y. Let P := {‖Y‖_∞ < 2C₁ε₁}. For Y ∈ P, H₁Y satisfies the bounds (103) (p.293 l.36–37) with C₁′ := 4C₁, since |H₁Y|_{(−1)} and |∇H₁Y|_{(−2)} are ≤ B₀‖Y‖_∞ < 2B₀C₁ε₁ ≤ 8dLB₀C₁ε₁. So by the complex clause of Proposition 6 (p.295 l.49–p.296 l.2), 𝒜₁ is analytic on P, and so is ℋ, by (174) and the analyticity of HD (VAR Sect. C). For real Y ∈ P, (172) holds with C₁′, since |e^{iY} − 1| < 4C₁ε₁ for ε₁ small, and ℋ(Y) represents U_{k,Z}(e^{iY}V′^X₀V_Λ). Here ℋ = 𝒜₁ + H₁Y − HD(𝒜₁ + H₁Y) and 𝒜₁ is the solution of (175). VAR takes C₁ an absolute constant (p.305 l.44); a constant C₁(M) is used here, with ε₁ small depending on it, which is the condition (T-Λ) below (VAR's B₅ = 6B₁B₃C₁ then depends on M; it is not LFR I's geometric B₅). At the real background U_0, the operators H₁ and H decay at the rate δ₀ (PBG (3.133) and Theorem 3.12, with the rate renamed δ₀ on p.423 l.39–40), and so does 𝔊 (Theorem 3.13, p.426). The extra factor (L^jη)^{−1} or (L^{j′}η)^{−1} that p.422 l.32–33 adds for the H of the variational problem is harmless here, since the data lie on the layer of the scale k, the coarsest one, so a_j ≤ a_k. δV/δA′ in (175) is of at least second order, with the kernel form of (b) (*The kernel form of VAR Proposition 4.*, at complex arguments), and 𝒜₁ = −𝔊(δV/δA′)(𝒜₁ + H₁Y) by (175).
  - *The weighted bound.* Let w := e^{(δ₀/16)d(·,Λ)} ≥ 1, |f|_w := sup_yw(y)‖f‖_y for fields, and |f|_{w,(−3)} := sup_yw(y)sup_{Δ(y)}a_{j(y)}³|f| for sources, with ‖·‖_y of (b). The triangle inequality (2.54) gives w(y)/w(y′) ≤ e^{(δ₀/16)d(y,y′)}. So the plain-source bound of (b) and its count give |𝔊f|_w ≤ 2B₀c_K|f|_{w,(−3)}. The kernel form gives |(δV/δA′)(A)|_{w,(−3)} ≤ C_Nε₃c_tot(1/128)|A|_w, since ⅛δ₀ − δ₀/16 ≥ δ₀/128, with ε₃ := CC₁ε₁ ≥ ε₁, a bound on the radius of 𝒜₁ + H₁Y in |·|_∞ that also covers the J-terms of the kernel form. (3.133) bounds each of a_j|H₁Y| and a_j²|∇H₁Y| by B₀Σ_{b∈Λ}e^{−δ₀d(y,b)}‖Y‖_∞, so ‖H₁Y‖_y ≤ 2B₀Σ_{b∈Λ}e^{−δ₀d(y,b)}‖Y‖_∞; with w(y) ≤ e^{(δ₀/16)d(y,b)} for b ∈ Λ and d bonds of Λ per point of the scale-k layer, |H₁Y|_w ≤ 2dB₀c_tot(1/128)‖Y‖_∞. The lattice is finite, so |𝒜₁|_w < ∞, and |𝒜₁|_w ≤ 2B₀c_KC_Nc_tot(1/128)ε₃(|𝒜₁|_w + |H₁Y|_w) ≤ |H₁Y|_w when 4B₀c_KC_Nc_tot(1/128)ε₃ ≤ 1. With (ii) of (b) for HD, whose kernel keeps e^{−(3/16)δ₀d} after the weight, |ℋ(Y)|_w ≤ (1 + C_Hc_tot(1/128)ε₃)·2|H₁Y|_w ≤ C_ℋ‖Y‖_∞, C_ℋ := 6dB₀c_tot(1/128), when C_Hc_tot(1/128)ε₃ ≤ ½. Hence ‖ℋ(Y)‖_y ≤ C_ℋe^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)}‖Y‖_∞ on every block y, for Y ∈ P, with C_ℋ independent of C₁; the smallness conditions on CC₁ε₁ that this uses are part of (T-Λ).
  - *The bound on P.* The block densities of the action change by ≤ C(ε₁ + |ℋ|)|ℋ| per block, with |ℋ| the maximum of ‖ℋ‖ over the block and its neighbours, since plaquettes cross block boundaries: the first-order term is ⟨J₀, ℋ⟩ with |J₀| ≤ Cε₁a_j^{−3}, and the higher orders are ≤ C|ℋ|² when η|ℋ| is small. The count Σ_ye^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} ≤ Σ_{b∈Λ}Σ_ye^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,b)} ≤ c′_Λ := d(100M)^dc_tot(1/16) is the count of (b) over all layers: 𝐁_k(Z) consists of the layers of CRE's domains in Z, so the widths of (b) hold for it, and (2.59) at α = 1/16 follows from (2.59) at α = 1/128, since the condition strengthens as α decreases. So |Φ^X(e^{iY}V′^X₀) − Φ^X(V′^X₀)| ≤ ½C_Φ(M)C₁²ε₁² on P (C₁ ≥ 1), and the same bound holds for |½⟨H_{1,k}Y, Δ₁H_{1,k}Y⟩| by Cauchy's estimate on the complex line through Y, since the linear term vanishes and the form is the second-order Taylor coefficient, with C_Φ(M) a constant of d, M, δ₀, B₀, and C_ℋ, enlarged by a fixed factor for this estimate. W^X vanishes to third order at 0 and is ≤ C_Φ(M)C₁²ε₁² on P, so Schwarz's lemma on the complex line through Y gives |W^X(Y)| ≤ C_Φ(M)C₁²ε₁²(‖Y‖_∞/(2C₁ε₁))³ ≤ (C_Φ(M)/(8C₁ε₁))‖Y‖_∞‖Y‖₂².
- *The growth.* So Φ^X(e^{iY}V′^X₀) − Φ^X(V′^X₀) ≥ (κ_Λ/2 − (C_Φ(M)/(8C₁ε₁))‖Y‖_∞)‖Y‖₂² ≥ (κ_Λ/4)‖Y‖₂² for real Y with ‖Y‖_∞ ≤ 2C₁κ_Λε₁/C_Φ(M) and ‖Y‖_∞ < 2C₁ε₁.
- *The a priori location.* In the axial gauge of G₀, |V′^X₀(b) − 1| < O(1)B₅M⁶ε_k on Λ^{(k)} in each run, by LFR I (1.83) (p.197 l.2–6, M^k(U_0) = V_Λ there). So ‖X‖_∞ ≤ C_X(M)ε_k, C_X(M) := C·O(1)B₅M⁶, with LFR I's B₅. With C₁ := max(1, C_X(M)C_Φ(M)/κ_Λ), the growth applies at Y = X in run A and at Y = −X in run B, since V′^A₀ = e^{−iX}V′^B₀. The condition (T-Λ) on the coupling window, of the same kind as (T2-v) of the Cauchy note, is: ε₁ is below the smallness that VAR Sect. G needs for this C₁, which depends only on M and Bałaban's constants; the conditions of *The weighted bound.* and *The bound on P.*, 4B₀c_KC_Nc_tot(1/128)ε₃ ≤ 1, C_Hc_tot(1/128)ε₃ ≤ ½, ε₃ ≤ ¼a₃ for the kernel form, and the smallness of η‖ℋ‖ ≤ 2ηC_ℋC₁ε₁ that the block densities need, each of the form C·C₁(M)ε₁ ≤ c with c independent of k, K, ε, and the volume; and the smallness conditions on ε₁ of the alignment of §4(d), listed there (*The intermediate current and the estimate.*), at PBG's α₁ lowered to 16dLα₁ ≤ 1; and those of *The Current at the Multiscale Chain.* in (ii): (T-E), the conditions 2C_U(M)ε₁ ≤ α₁ and 4C₀ε₁ ≤ α₁ of its (I), and the condition on κ_W of its ΔJ_c.
- *The comparison.* Adding the two growth inequalities, (κ_Λ/2)Δ² ≤ [Φ^A(V′^B₀) − Φ^A(V′^A₀)] + [Φ^B(V′^A₀) − Φ^B(V′^B₀)] = D(V′^B₀) − D(V′^A₀), with D := Φ^A − Φ^B. Write D = Σ_yd_y over the scale-k blocks y of Z, d_y := [a_y(U^A) − a_y(MU^B)] + b_y(U^B), U^X := U^X_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ), and b_y the blocking artifact of (b). MU^B has the data of U^A, by the composition of the averagings. At fixed V′ the problem is that of (b), with the layers of the multiscale set 𝐁_k(Z) (LFR I (1.74)), whose finest layer is the history's, of spacing a_n ≤ a. By the bound ρ̄ of (b), |a_y(MU^B) − a_y(U^A)| ≤ Cε₁ρ̄ at first order per block, and |b_y| ≤ C_b(ε/a_n)ε₁², so |d_y| ≤ σ̄ := C_σ(ε/a_n)^{1−ε₀}ε₁², C_σ := C(2C_G(ε₀)c_K + C_b). This holds on the segment V′_t := e^{itX}V′^A₀, whose data are regular with 2ε₁, since ‖X‖_∞ ≤ C_X(M)ε_k ≤ ε₁/4, with ε_k the larger of the two runs' values and C(M) ≥ 4C_X(M). Along it, a data change on Λ moves each of U^A, U^B, and MU^B at y by ≤ B₀Σ_{b∈Λ}e^{−δ₀d(y,b)}|X(b)|, by (3.133) for each run's H at its real regular configuration, with M local; and the block densities have derivatives ≤ Cε₁ per unit field. So |d_y(V′^B₀) − d_y(V′^A₀)| ≤ C_Lε₁K_Λ(y)Δ, with K_Λ(y) := Σ_{b∈Λ}e^{−δ₀d(y,b)}. Since min(u, v) ≤ (uv)^{1/2}, D(V′^B₀) − D(V′^A₀) ≤ Σ_ymin(2σ̄, C_Lε₁K_Λ(y)Δ) ≤ (2σ̄C_Lε₁Δ)^{1/2}c′_Λ, since c′_Λ ≥ Σ_bΣ_ye^{−(δ₀/2)d(y,b)} ≥ Σ_yK_Λ(y)^{1/2}. Hence Δ^{3/2} ≤ (2c′_Λ/κ_Λ)(2σ̄C_Lε₁)^{1/2}, i.e. Δ ≤ C_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, with C_Δ(M) := (2c′_Λ/κ_Λ)^{2/3}(2C_σC_L)^{1/3}.
- *The gauge of the data.* By (1.50) and (1.25) (p.362 l.22–27), the data of U″_k on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} are M^·(U^{(AL)}_0) = (M^·U_0)^{ū₀}: the averages of 𝐁₁ = 𝐁″_k(Z) ∪ 𝐁_h(Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}) (p.361 l.36–38), at the shared scales k and h, transformed by ū₀, which is constant on the blocks and equal at their centres to the u₀ of LFR I (1.86). The expressions of the integral are invariant under the compensating gauge transformations of V_h on ∂⁺(Z′^∼_h)^{(h)} and of V′ (LFR I p.197 l.36–40). At fixed V_k these are changes of variables of the Haar measure, and u₀ and the gauge of U_0 at the shared centres are functions of V_k. So each run's integral is unchanged when both runs use run A's ū₀ and one gauge of U_0 at the shared centres, that of the comparison of the Cauchy note's §9.8. The two runs' data are then at distance |M^·U^A_0 − M^·U^B_0|, since a common gauge transformation preserves |·|. Every run-B quantity evaluated there is a run-B integrand at transformed variables, so Bałaban's run-B bounds and analyticity domains apply to it.
- *The data difference.* On Λ^{(k)}, M^k(U^X_0) = V′^X₀, so the difference is e^{iX}, ≤ C‖X‖_∞ ≤ CΔ. At a scale m of 𝐁″_k(Z) between h and k (Λ ⊃ Z″_k ⊃ ⋯ ⊃ Z″_{h+1}, LFR I p.195 l.42), M^m(U^A_0) − M^m(MU^B_0) = [M^mU^A(V′^A₀) − M^mU^A(V′^B₀)] + [M^mU^A(V′^B₀) − M^mMU^B(V′^B₀)], with M^m(MU^B) = M^m(U^B) by the composition of the averagings. The first bracket is ≤ B₀c_K‖X‖_∞ by (3.133) and the count of (b). The second is ≤ Cρ̄ ≤ 2CC_G(ε₀)c_K(ε/a_n)^{1−ε₀}ε₁ by the bound ρ̄ of (b) at equal data, since a scale-m block average of a field difference ≤ ρ̄/a_m has path length ≤ a_m. So the data difference is ≤ C′_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ per data point of Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, at every scale.
- *The propagation.* The data enter U″_k = U_{𝐁″_k}(·) through the data derivative of the multiscale set 𝐁″_k, the H₁-type operator of (1.54) (H″_{1,k}Q̃(η𝐀₀)), which decays at the rate δ₀ at a regular background (PBG Theorem 3.12). The action A(w, U″_k) of (1.70), w = ζ₁/(g″)², changes at first order by ⟨J_w, H″_{1,k}δD⟩, with |J_w| ≤ C sup|w|ε₁/a_m³ per point as in (b), and at second order by ≤ C sup|w||δD|² per data point. Each data point contributes ≤ C sup|w|ε₁|δD| at its own scale, summed with the decay by the count of (b). The change of A(ζ₀, U_0) in (1.10) at fixed data is (b). Its change from V′^A₀ to V′^B₀ in run A has the linear term (1.5), which is localised by 1 − ζ₀ as in (1.6), plus a quadratic term ≤ C(M)Δ². Both are ≤ C(M)ε₁Δ, with Δ ≤ C(M)ε₁ (the a priori location).
- *The result.* Per data point of Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} at its own scale, and per bond of Λ^{(k)}, the change of these actions is ≤ C_A(M)g_{j}^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁², with a_n = εL^n the history's finest spacing. In a history with n > k/8, K − j ≤ 7k/8 + j(C), so g_j^{−2}ε₁² = C(M)²R_k²p₀(g_k)²g_k²/g_j² ≤ C(M)²(1 + β₀)²(k − j + 1)R_k²p₀(g_k)² by CRE (2.6), with g_k^{−2} ≥ ḡ^{−2}: a power of log(k + j(C) + ḡ^{−2}) times k < 8n, polylogarithmic times linear in n and independent of K. Since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4} (the Cauchy note's §8.7), L^{−(1−ε₀)n/3} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−ε₀)n/12} absorbs it, so the change is ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of the history, with C_h a constant of M, ε₀, ḡ, j(C), and Bałaban's constants. This is the birth-scale form of (LF).

(d) *The forms and the functions of the configurations at the step of the R-operation (derived, with the points (3), (6), and (7) of (LF-A) not reviewed, and with the gauge-fixing part of the Cauchy note's §10.4; the π-part of β with multiscale data, its diagonal value and its source form, and the twist term are derived, not reviewed).* The notation is that of (b): a_j = εL^j, j(x) the layer of x, a_n the history's finest spacing. The Hölder exponent of PBG (3.133) is fixed at γ_d := (1 + ε₀)/2 throughout (d). In (d), k is Bałaban's index of LFR II §1, the step of the R-operation, which the Cauchy note's §8.6 calls j. It is at most the stopping scale, so the bounds k < 8n and n ≥ k/2 − 1, which §1 states for the stopping scale, hold for it as well.
- *The objects.* H″_{1,k,Z} is the linear term of 𝐇″_{k,Z}(g_kB) in (1.19)–(1.20) (LFR II p.360 l.44–50, p.361 l.3–8): the data derivative at B = 0 of the minimiser U″_{k,Z} of the multiscale determining set 𝐁″_k(Z), at the background U^0_{k,Z}. Bałaban writes the quadratic term ½g_k²⟨H″B, Δ₁(ζ)H″B⟩ of (1.20) as the form (1.21), Σ_pη⁴ζ(x(p))|(DH″B)(p)|², which (1.70) keeps, plus a small form bounded by O(1)B₃⁵B₅M^{5+d}A₁²p₁²(g_k)R_k^{d+2}ε_k (p.361 l.14–28). H_{1,k} of (1.2) is the data derivative of U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ) in V′ = exp ig_kB′ on Λ^{(k)}, at the background U_0; the form of (1.2) carries the weight ζ₀ (p.356 l.31–p.357 l.8). On the supports of the cutoffs, |g_kB| < δ′_k by χ′ (p.360 l.41–43) and |B′| < M₀g_k^{−1}ε_k (p.356 l.37). So the fields are ≤ b_k := max(A₁p₁(g_k), M₀p₀(g_k)) per point of 𝐁₀ or of Λ^{(k)}. These are Bałaban's dimensionless data: B on a layer j′ is the variable of the constraint L^{j′}ηQ_{j′}A = B of PBG (3.109)–(3.110), so its lift carries the factor (L^{j′}η)^{−1} = a_{j′}^{−1} relative to (3.133) (PBG p.422 l.32–33). Bałaban's bounds of these forms are sums over 𝐁₀, with |𝐁₀| ≤ (100MR_k)^dN² (p.361 l.11–13 and l.19–20), and (d) compares the two runs per point of 𝐁₀ or of Λ^{(k)}.
- *The lifts and the chain rule.* VAR's constraint (3) (p.278 l.11–13) is the plain average, Ū^j = V on Λ_j, while PBG's lift belongs to the double bar. In each run the data derivative of a VAR minimiser with a multiscale determining set, in VAR's gauge (153), is H₁(1 + Ξ), with PBG's H₁ = G₁Q*(QG₁Q*)^{−1} of (3.129) for that set, Q PBG's linearised multiscale double bar, and Ξ the interface twist of *The interface twist.* below (derived there), each run with its own Ξ at its own data. So the lifts of (1.2) and (1.21) are H₁B̃, with the twisted data B̃ := (1 + Ξ)B, and |B̃| ≤ (1 + 2dL)|B| per point.
