PBG Theorem 3.12 gives it (3.132)–(3.133) between any two layers (p.422 l.17–30, p.423 l.30–36): for x ∈ Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, |∇H₁(x, y′)| ≤ O(1)a_j^{−1}a_{j′}^{−d}e^{−½δ₁d(y,y′)}, and ‖ζ′∇H₁(·, y′)‖_β ≤ O(1)(‖ζ′‖^ξ_β + |ζ′|)a_j^{−1−β}a_{j′}^{−d}e^{−½δ₁d(y,y′)} for ζ′ ∈ C₀^∞(Δ̃(y)). So the Hölder bound is at the scale of the receiving layer; in run B, PBG's L^jη is L^{j+1}(ε/L) = a_j at the shared scale j. The proof of (10.2) uses only the positivity of G₁^{−1} and the surjectivity of Q, so H₁B minimises ⟨A, (Δ₁ + DRD*)A⟩ under QA = B for the multiscale problem too. Every layer of run B's set lies at a shared scale j ≥ k/2 − 1 ≥ 1, where its averaging is d(M^{j+1}) = d(M^j)∘dM. So at aligned backgrounds Q^B = Q^AQ₁ layer by layer, for the double bar by (B4) of the Cauchy note's §10.1 with Q₁ = Q̄₁(U^B), and for the plain averaging by composition with Q₁ = Q₁^{pl} = dM(U^B). Hence (10.4)–(10.5) and the characterisation of E hold with the multiscale double bar Q and H₁. The characterisation identifies E with the Green operator on ker Q^A at run A's pair, G₁^Q := G₁ − G₁Q*(QG₁Q*)^{−1}QG₁ = (I − H₁Q)G₁: for ψ ∈ ker Q^A the double-bar lift ℒ of §10.4 gives ℒψ ∈ ker Q^B and Q₁ℒψ = ψ, so ⟨ψ, G_A^{−1}E⟩ = β(ℒψ, 𝒜^B), and QG₁^Q = 0 and ⟨ψ, G₁^{−1}G₁^Qs⟩ = ⟨ψ, s⟩ for ψ ∈ ker Q give E₀ = G₁^Qs with ⟨ψ, s⟩ := β(ℒψ, 𝒜^B), E₀ := (I − H₁Q)E the part of E in ker Q^A, which is all of E at equal twisted data (*The terms in E.*). The bounds of G₁^Q follow from (3.132) for G₁ and (3.133) for H₁, with Q local, as in the Cauchy note's §10.4. The J-term paragraph of §10.4 holds by *The intermediate current and the estimate.* below.
- *The alignment.* In the common gauge of (c) ("The gauge of the data"), each run's background is compared with the other run's averaged one: U^{0,A}_{k,Z} against MU^{0,B}_{k,Z}, and U^A_0 against MU^B_0. In each pair U^A and U^B minimise the action under the multiscale constraints of their determining sets, each with its own data, and U^A = e^{iεA}Ũ, Ũ := MU^B, with a_{j(x)}|A(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A(x)| ≤ d̄ := 2ρ̄ + 2B₀c_KC′_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ ≤ C_U(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, C_U(M) := 4C_G(ε₀)c″_K + 2B₀c_KC′_Δ(M) + C_T, with C_T the strip constant of (iv), *The strips at ∂□₀.*, used only at the composites and the representation configurations. The first term is (b) at equal data, where ρ̄ bounds the displacement A′* of VAR (112) itself; the second is the data difference of (c), propagated by (3.133) with the count of (b). The factors 2 cover the composition of the two displacements: its Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff term is ≤ (ε/a_j)(a_j|X|)(a_j|Y|)/a_j for displacements X and Y, and the change of background in the covariant gradient is of the same order, so both are at most the sum of the two bounds when C_U(M)ε₁ ≤ 1. Throughout (d), ε₁ is the larger of the ε₁ of §1 and the C(M)R_kε_k of (c). The forms of (1.2) and (1.21) depend on the current through Δ₁ and H₁, and the currents of U^A and Ũ differ by second differences of A, which d̄ does not bound. So the argument of the Cauchy note's §10.3 is run for the multiscale problem, with d = 4 and PBG's common rate δ₀ (p.423 l.39–40).
  - *The pair class.* 𝒟̄ is the set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉) with 𝐔 satisfying PBG (3.37) (p.396), which is stated layer by layer (|A′| < α₁a_j^{−1} and |∇A′| < α₁a_j^{−2} on Ω_j), and |𝐉(x)| < α_Ja_{j(x)}^{−3}, α_J := Mα₀. PBG's series for G and G₁ use the current through |J| in (3.131) (p.421 l.38–42), which is this bound, and through |(H*J)(b)| ≤ O(1)Mα₀(L^jη)^{−3} for b ∈ Λ_j (p.422 l.50) in (3.137), which (3.133) gives from this bound with the cross-layer count of (b): the layer j′ enters H*J at b ∈ Λ_j with the factor (a_{j′}/a_j)^{d−3}e^{−½δ₁d(·,·)}, which the row sums c_K pay. So the pair-class paragraph of the Cauchy note's §10.3 holds for the multiscale problem: Δ_{(𝐔,𝐉)} and its propagators are analytic on 𝒟̄ and obey the multiscale (3.132)–(3.133) and Theorems 3.10–3.12, and PBG's (3.38) is not needed.
  - *The interface twist.* By PRT2 (2.3) (p.224), Λ_j = Ω_j^{(j)}∖Ω_{j+1}^{(j)} "for the sets of sites and the sets of bonds", where Ω also denotes "the set of bonds ∪_{x∈Ω}st(x)". So the bonds of Λ_j include the interface bonds S_j := {⟨x, x′⟩ ∈ Λ_j : x ∉ Ω_j^{(j)}, x′ ∈ Ω_j^{(j)}}, whose outer endpoint x is a site of the finer layer j − 1; this is the case "x ∈ Λ′_{j−1}, x′ ∈ Λ′_j" of VAR (154). PRT1 uses the corner blocks B(x) = {x₁ : x_μ ≤ x₁,μ < x_μ + L} (p.018 l.41), with contours Γ_{x,x₁} inside B(x) (p.018 l.44). Since Ω_j is a union of such blocks (PRT2 (2.2), first line), B(x) ∩ Ω_j = ∅ for x ∉ Ω_j^{(j)}, so the bonds of the contours of B(x) belong to Λ_{j−1}. VAR states, in the gauge (153) (p.301 l.62–64, p.302 l.1–18): "Ū̄^j_1(x,x′) = V″(x,x′) for ⟨x,x′⟩ ∈ Λ′_j, x,x′ ∈ Λ′_j," and "Ū̄^j_1(x,x′) = exp[−iΣ_{x₁∈B(x)}L^{−d}(1/i)log V″(Γ_{x,x₁})]V″(x,x′) for ⟨x,x′⟩ ∈ Λ′_j, x ∈ Λ′_{j−1}, x′ ∈ Λ′_j, (154) by Eqs. (1.31) in [6]. If we denote by V₁ the configuration on the right-hand sides of (154), then V₁ = e^{iB} with |B| < 18d²L³Mε₀, (155) for c₁ sufficiently small, and the equalities (154) can be written as Q_j(ηA) = B on Λ′_j, j = 0, 1, …, k, or simply Q(ηA) = B. (156)" Here V″ is the plain data, V″ = Ū′^j_k on Λ′_j by (149), and Ū̄ is the double bar. The exponent in (154) is the factor of level j, at the site x, of the gauge transformation v of CMP 98 (97) and (160): by (162) it is the mean over x₁ ∈ B(x) of the logarithms of the transported contour holonomies.
    - The twist. The chart is the relative one of PBG and CMP 98 (159)–(162), at the run's own configuration U as the base: plain data V″ = e^{iY}V″(U) on the bonds of the layers. In it the right side of (154) is e^{i(1 + Ξ)Y + O(|Y|²)} times its value at Y = 0, where Ξ, the interface twist, is the derivative at Y = 0 of the level-j factor of (154), Ad of the background transports composed with dexp^{−1} at the background holonomies: (ΞY)(x, x′) := −Σ_{x₁∈B(x)}L^{−d}d[(1/i)log V″(Γ_{x,x₁})][Y] for ⟨x, x′⟩ ∈ S_j, and ΞY := 0 on the other bonds. Along a contour the derivative is Σ_{b′∈Γ}dexp^{−1}_h∘Ad(g_{b′})[Y(b′)], with h the logarithm of the holonomy and g_{b′} the partial holonomy. A contour has at most d(L − 1) bonds, and in the operator norm of a faithful unitary representation |[X, Y]| ≤ 2|X||Y|, so ‖Ad(e^{X₁}⋯e^{X_m})‖ ≤ e^{2Σ|X_i|}. So for 16dLα₁ ≤ 1 and data, real or complex, with |Y| < α₁, Ad(g_{b′}) ≤ e^{2dLα₁} ≤ e^{1/4}; the background factors are unitary, so they add nothing to Ad. The holonomy also contains the background: its exponents are the |Y(b′)| < α₁ and the background angles, ≤ 18dL²Mε₀ per bond by VAR (151) (|V″ − 1| < 9dL²Mε₀), over at most dL bonds, so they add up to ≤ dLα₁ + 18d²L³Mε₀ ≤ 1/16 + 1/36 < 1/4 under 648d²L³Mε₀ ≤ 1 (the condition of *Its size.* below), and |h| ≤ |E|/(1 − |E|) ≤ 0.105 with |E| ≤ e^s − 1, s ≤ 0.0903 the exponent sum. Then ‖ad_h‖ ≤ 0.21, and dexp^{−1}_h = Σ_n(B_n/n!)ad_h^n, B_n the Bernoulli numbers, is ≤ 1 + 0.105 + 0.004 ≤ e^{1/4}. Their product is ≤ e^{1/2} ≤ 2. Hence |(ΞY)(b)| ≤ 2dL max|Y| over the bonds of B(x) for b ∈ S_j at x, and the entries obey |Ξ(b, b′)| ≤ 2L^{−d}N(b′) ≤ 2, with N(b′) ≤ L^d the number of contours of B(x) through b′. A bond b′ of Λ_{j−1} lies in at most one block B(x), and at most 2d bonds of S_j have the endpoint x. On these sets, with |log g| ≤ 0.105, Ad has the Lipschitz constant 2e^{0.21} ≤ 2e^{1/4} in the logarithm of its argument and dexp^{−1} the constant 2, and the logarithms of the partial holonomies and of the holonomy, whose derivative is dexp^{−1}_h∘Ad(g)∘dexp, move by at most e^{0.4}dL max|Y_A − Y_B| between two runs with data Y_A and Y_B. So each term of a contour moves by at most (2e^{1/4} + 2e^{1/2})e^{0.4}dL max|Y_A − Y_B| ≤ 9.2dL max|Y_A − Y_B|, and |Ξ_A(b, b′) − Ξ_B(b, b′)| ≤ 12dLL^{−d}N(b′)max|Y_A − Y_B|, the maximum over the bonds of B(x). These bounds are per bond, hence uniform in the volume.
    - Ξ² = 0. Ξ writes on the bonds of S_j, which lie within a_j of Ω_j^c, and reads the bonds of Λ_{j−1}. At the next interface Ξ reads bonds of Λ_j in the blocks B(z) of the outer endpoints z of S_{j+1}; these lie within (d + 1)a_{j+1} of Ω_{j+1}. So no bond of S_j is read by Ξ if every layer is wider than (d + 1)L + 1 in its own units. For a sequence satisfying PRT2 (2.2), (L^jη)^{−1}dist(Ω_j^c, Ω_{j+1}) > RM, and in d = 4 RM ≥ 12L covers the condition. For 𝐁″_k and the joins, whose layers are at least M or LM₁ wide in their own units (*The joins.* in §9, with its middle layers), the condition holds for M₁ ≥ 6. Two interface corrections therefore cannot chain: Ξ² = 0 and (1 + Ξ)^{−1} = 1 − Ξ.
    - The two averagings. Let 𝐐^{pl}(U) be the linearised plain multiscale averaging of VAR (3), layer by layer, and Q_db(U) PBG's linearised multiscale double bar: on each layer the double bar of (B1) and (B4) of the Cauchy note's §10.1, and on S_j the double bar (154). By (B1) on each layer and (154) on S_j, for every X,

      Q_db(U)X = (1 + Ξ)𝐐^{pl}(U)(X + D_Uω_X), equivalently 𝐐^{pl}(U)(X + D_Uω_X) = (1 − Ξ)Q_db(U)X,

      where ω_X is linear in X and equals at the sites of each layer the linearisation of the factors of v up to that layer's level, CMP 98 (160)–(162). At the outer endpoint x of a bond of S_j the factors up to level j − 1 enter ω_X, and the factor of level j is the twist. ω_X enters only through D_U*J(U) = 0 and the covariance 𝒬_{j′}(U)D_Uω = D_{V_{j′}}(ω restricted to T_{a_{j′}}), so no bound on it is used. Of the identities of §10.1, (B1) and (B2) relate the plain averaging to the double bar, and (B3), (B4), and (B5) concern the double bar alone; all five are single-scale, where Ξ = 0.
    - The right inverses. R₀ := H₀(U) := G₀Q_db*(Q_dbG₀Q_db*)^{−1} is PBG's H of (3.126) at 𝐉 = 0, and Q_dbR₀ = 1. By (3.132)–(3.133) of Theorem 3.12 for the multiscale problem, with Theorem 3.3 for G₀, |R₀e_y| ≤ B₀e^{−½δ₀d(·,y)} and |∇R₀e_y| ≤ B₀a_{j(·)}^{−1}e^{−½δ₀d(·,y)}, uniformly in the volume, at every 𝐔 of 𝒟̄, real or complex, by *The pair class.*. R := R₀(1 + Ξ), followed by the gauge term, Y ↦ RY + D_Uω_{RY}, is a right inverse of 𝐐^{pl}(U) by the display, while Q_dbR = 1 + Ξ. So 𝐐^{pl}(U) is onto.
    - The multiplier representation. A VAR minimiser U is critical under VAR's constraint (3), so J(U) = 𝐐^{pl}(U)*λ with a unique λ. For X ∈ ker Q_db(U) the display gives 𝐐^{pl}(X + D_Uω_X) = 0, so ⟨J, X⟩ = ⟨λ, 𝐐^{pl}(X + D_Uω_X)⟩ − ⟨D_U*J, ω_X⟩ = 0, since D_U*J(U) = 0 by the gauge invariance of the action. So J ⊥ ker Q_db(U), and since Q_db(U) is onto, J = Q_db(U)*ν with the unique ν := R₀*J. Then ⟨λ, Y⟩ = ⟨J, RY + D_Uω_{RY}⟩ = ⟨ν, Q_dbR₀(1 + Ξ)Y⟩ for every Y, so λ = (1 + Ξ)*ν.
  - *The multipliers.* Normalise 𝐐^{pl} and Q_db on each layer as 𝒬 of the Cauchy note's §10.3, with the inner product Σ_{j′}Σ_{c∈Λ_{j′}}a_{j′}^d⟨·, ·⟩ on the data, and write 𝐐 := 𝐐^{pl}. By *The interface twist.*, J(U) = Q_db(U)*ν(U) = 𝐐(U)*λ(U), λ = (1 + Ξ)*ν, for U^A with run A's data and for U^B with run B's, with ν^X = (ν^X_{j′}) and λ^X = (λ^X_{j′}) fields on the bonds of the layers. The gauge covariance of each layer's averaging gives 𝒬_{j′}(U)D_Uω = D_{V_{j′}}(ω restricted to T_{a_{j′}}), V_{j′} the data of the layer j′, so D_U*J(U) = 0 is the weighted divergence condition for λ,

    Σ_{j′}(a_{j′}/η)^d1_{T_{a_{j′}}}D*_{V_{j′}}λ_{j′} = 0 on T_η,

    in each run with its own data. Restricted to the sites of T_ε, run B's condition is the condition of Ũ. Unlike the single-scale case, the divergences of the single layers need not vanish where two layers meet, so ⟨𝐐H₀e_y, λ⟩ ≠ ⟨e_y, λ⟩ there, and the multipliers are computed through ν. With H₀ = R₀ and f_y(U) := a_{j′}^{−d}dA_ε(U)[H₀(U)e_y] of the Cauchy note's §10.3 for the multiscale Q_db, and y ∈ Λ_{j′}, ν = H₀*J gives ⟨e, ν^A_{j′}(y)⟩ = f_y(U^A) exactly, with no divergence condition. In run B, ℒH₀(Ũ) is a right inverse of Q_db^B(U^B) = Q_db(Ũ)Q̄₁(U^B), which holds layer by layer by (B4) of §10.1, with the twists equal since Ũ = MU^B carries run B's data on the layers; here ℒ is the double-bar lift of §10.4, with Q̄₁ℒ = 1 and the constant C_ℒ′ of its *Regularity of the Lifts.*. So ⟨e, ν^B_{j′}(y)⟩ = a_{j′}^{−d}dA_{ε/L}(U^B)[ℒH₀(Ũ)e_y] = f_y(Ũ) − a_{j′}^{−d}db(U^B)[ℒH₀(Ũ)e_y], the second equality by (10.3a) for the multiscale problem. Along U(t) := e^{itεA}Ũ, |t| ≤ α₁/(2d̄), U(t) satisfies (3.37) layer by layer. Its plaquettes are ≤ 2α₁ε²/a_{j(x)}² when 4C₀ε₁ ≤ α₁, by the regularity of (b) for Ũ, and |D_{U(t)}H₀(U(t))e_y| ≤ 3B₀a_{j(x)}^{−1}e^{−½δ₀d(·,y)}. Put c̄_e := sup_ya_{j(y)}^{3−d}Σ_pε^da_{j(p)}^{−3}e^{−½δ₀d(p,y)}. It is finite by the count of (b): the layer j″ enters with the factor (a_{j″}/a_{j(y)})^{d−3} ≤ L^{|j″−j(y)|}, which the row sums c_K pay. So |f_y(U(t))| ≤ 6c_Wc̄_eB₀α₁a_{j′}^{−3} on the disc, and Cauchy's estimate gives |f_y(U^A) − f_y(Ũ)| ≤ 24c_Wc̄_eB₀d̄a_{j′}^{−3} for 4d̄ ≤ α₁. At t = 0, |f_y(Ũ)| ≤ 6c_Wc̄_eC₀B₀ε₁a_{j′}^{−3}. The bounds |r₁| ≤ C_rεε₁/a_{j(x)}³ and |r₀| ≤ C_rεε₁²/a_{j(x)}⁴ of (b) give the artifact term ≤ 4c̄_eC_rC_ℒ′B₀(ε/a_n)ε₁a_{j′}^{−3}. Hence

    a_{j′}³|ν^A_{j′} − ν^B_{j′}| ≤ x̄_ν(d̄ + (ε/a_n)ε₁), a_{j′}³|ν^B_{j′}| ≤ B̄_νε₁, x̄_ν := c̄_eB₀(24c_W + 4C_rC_ℒ′), B̄_ν := c̄_eB₀(6c_WC₀ + 4C_rC_ℒ′).

    The Cauchy estimate above takes d̄ as a bound on the whole volume, which holds when no layer is finer than the history's (*The result.* of (b)). Where older histories have finer layers, the pointwise form a_{j′}³|ν^A_{j′}(y) − ν^B_{j′}(y)| ≤ x̂_νd̄(y), with d̄(y) the coarse-side bound at y, is derived at a fixed right inverse in *The Current at the Multiscale Chain.* of (ii), and it applies to these pairs as well, whose run-B sets factor through M (*The lifts and the chain rule.*). So do the pointwise forms a_{j′}³|λ^A_{j′}(y) − λ^B_{j′}(y)| ≤ x̂_λd̄(y) and a_{j(x)}³|δ𝐉(x)| ≤ x̂_Jd̄(x) of that item, which replace the two displays below there.
    - *The pointwise disc (derived, unreviewed).* The disc R̄ of *The intermediate current and the estimate.* below also takes d̄ on the whole support, and on these global minimisers older histories can have finer layers elsewhere in the volume. There the alignment is applied to the localised terms of each density. The densities are built from the action, the multipliers, and the operators H₀, H₁, and 𝔊, whose random walk expansions for the sequence {Ω_j} (PBG Theorem 3.10, (3.107)–(3.108), pp.415–416, used for that sequence at CRE p.276 l.19–20) write each as a sum over walks ω. The term of ω depends on the configuration only on X_ω := X̃_0^5 ∪ … ∪ X̃_n^5 and is bounded by O(1)O(M^{−1/2})^{|ω|}e^{−½δ₀d(ω,y,y′)} times the scale factors (3.108). It is a finite product of localised operators, each analytic where the conditions of 𝒟̄ hold on its support, and the expansion converges uniformly on 𝒟̄. A walk from the layer j(x) of the density's point to the layer j(x) − m crosses m − 1 layers, each wider than W of (LW) in the metric d of PRT2 (2.46). So on X_ω, d̄ ≤ L^{(1−ε₀)m/3}d̄(x) and x̂_Jd̄ likewise, while d(ω) ≥ W(m − 1). The path of R̄ restricted to X_ω stays in 𝒟̄ on X_ω for |t| ≤ R̄_ω := R̄(x)L^{−(1−ε₀)m/3}, with R̄(x) the R̄ with d̄(x) for d̄. Cauchy's estimate on each walk and the sum over the walks, with ¼δ₀W ≥ (1/3)log L as in (LW), give: every per-point density changes under the alignment by ≤ 2c_ωL^{1/3}/R̄(x) times its bound, c_ω := sup_xΣ_ωO(1)O(M^{−1/2})^{|ω|}e^{−¼δ₀d(ω)}, and 2/R̄(x) ≤ c_al(ε/a_{min(j(x),n)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁. This is the display of c_al below, with the factor c_wL^{1/3}, pointwise in x.

    In the inner product of the data, a_{j−1}^d(Ξ*ν)(b′) = Σ_{b∈S_j}a_j^dΞ(b, b′)ν(b), so the entry bounds of *The interface twist.* give a_{j−1}³|(Ξ*ν)(b′)| ≤ 4dL^{d−3}sup_{S_j}a_j³|ν|. Its Lipschitz bound, with the data difference of (c), which is ≤ d̄, and ε₁ ≤ 1, gives a_{j−1}³|((Ξ_A − Ξ_B)*ν^B)(b′)| ≤ 24d²L^{d−2}B̄_νd̄. Hence, with λ = (1 + Ξ)*ν in each run and c_λ := 1 + 4dL^{d−3},

    a_{j′}³|λ^A_{j′} − λ^B_{j′}| ≤ x̄_λ(d̄ + (ε/a_n)ε₁), a_{j′}³|λ^B_{j′}| ≤ B̄_λε₁, x̄_λ := c_λx̄_ν + 24d²L^{d−2}B̄_ν, B̄_λ := c_λB̄_ν.

  - *The adjoint averaging across layers.* Let U be one of U^A, Ũ, and U^B, on T_η, W_ℓ := M^ℓU on T_{L^ℓη}, and μ = (μ_{j′}) a field satisfying the weighted divergence condition of U. The layer j′ sits at the level ℓ(j′) with L^{ℓ(j′)}η = a_{j′}. Put ν equal to μ_{j′} at the level of the coarsest layer, and ν_ℓ := Q₁(W_ℓ)*ν_{ℓ+1} + μ_{j′} at ℓ = ℓ(j′), ν_ℓ := Q₁(W_ℓ)*ν_{ℓ+1} at the other levels. By the chain rule ν_0 = 𝐐(U)*μ.
    - The divergences. By (i) of the Cauchy note's §10.3 and the condition, at the sites of T_{L^ℓη},

      D*_{W_ℓ}ν_ℓ = L^{−dℓ}Σ_{ℓ(j′)≥ℓ}L^{dℓ(j′)}D*μ_{j′} = −L^{−dℓ}Σ_{ℓ(j′)<ℓ}L^{dℓ(j′)}D*μ_{j′}.

      Both sums vanish except where bonds of a layer at a level ≥ ℓ and of a layer at a level < ℓ meet. Each layer is wider than 4L + 2 in its own units, > RM by PRT2 (2.2) (p.224 l.10–13) for a PRT2 sequence, and at least M or LM₁ for 𝐁″_k and the joins (*The joins.* in §9), which exceed 4L + 2 for M₁ ≥ 6. So these are the sites of ∂Ω_{j+1} at the level ℓ = ℓ(j + 1), where only the layers j and j + 1 meet. There D*_{W_ℓ}ν_ℓ = −L^{−d}D*_{V_j}μ_j, which is ≤ 2dL^{−d}sup|μ_j|/a_j. So the contour term of (ii) at the level ℓ(j) is ≤ L^{d−1}a_{j+1}·2dL^{−d}sup|μ_j|/a_j = 2d sup|μ_j|.
    - The reach. The proof of (ii) bounds each column, so (ii) holds bond by bond: the suprema run over the coarse bonds and the block whose terms meet the bond, and κ_W over the plaquettes of their blocks. Q₁(W)* moves supports by at most one coarse block, so the bounds at a bond b of T_η reach, at the level of spacing a, only bonds within distance 4a of b. Under RM ≥ 12L on Bałaban's R and M, the widths then let at most two consecutive layers, j₁ and j₁ + 1 with j₁ ≥ j(b) − 1, and the interface between them enter the bounds at b.
    - The factors. On the bonds reached, κ_{W_ℓ} ≤ 4C₀ε₁L^{2ℓ}η²/a_{j₁}² for ℓ ≤ ℓ(j₁), by CMP 98 Proposition 2 as in (a) with 2C₀ε₁ ≤ c₂. These sum to ≤ (16/3)C₀ε₁: unlike in (a), the last pullback reaches the plaquettes of the layer j₁. So the product of the factors of (ii) is ≤ 2 under

      (ĀQ) 8c_QL^{d+1}C₀ε₁ ≤ 1.

    - (ā) With m(μ) := sup_{j′}a_{j′}³sup|μ_{j′}|, the recursion gives |ν_0(b)| ≤ 2[sup|μ_{j₁+1}| + (1 + 2d)sup|μ_{j₁}|], so, since a_{j(b)} ≤ La_{j₁},

      a_{j(b)}³|(𝐐(U)*μ)(b)| ≤ 4(1 + d)L³m(μ),

      and |ν_ℓ| ≤ 4(1 + d)m(μ)a_{j₁}^{−3} on the bonds reached.
    - (b̄) Let U′ = e^{iηA}U and U be two of these configurations and μ′, μ fields satisfying the conditions of U′ and U, and put d̄_A := sup_xa_{j(x)}|A(x)|. The recursion of (b) of the Cauchy note's §10.3 for ν′_ℓ − ν_ℓ has two new terms: the difference of the multipliers at the levels of the two layers, and the interface term. The interface term is ≤ 2d sup|μ′_{j₁} − μ_{j₁}| + 4dc_vd̄_A sup|μ_{j₁}|, since D*_{V′_{j₁}}μ′_{j₁} − D*_{V_{j₁}}μ_{j₁} = D*_{V′_{j₁}}(μ′_{j₁} − μ_{j₁}) + (D*_{V′_{j₁}} − D*_{V_{j₁}})μ_{j₁}. The transport terms are ≤ (4d + c_D)c_vL^{d−1}L^{ℓ+1}η(d̄_A/a_{j₁})·4(1 + d)m(μ)a_{j₁}^{−3} at the level ℓ ≤ ℓ(j₁), and Σ_{ℓ≤ℓ(j₁)}L^{ℓ+1}η ≤ 2La_{j₁}. As in (b), the divergence of the difference is computed at each level from the two fields' own conditions, so the defect does not accumulate, and different data enter only through the transports. When d̄_A is below the α₁ of CMP 98 (158),

      a_{j(b)}³|(𝐐(U′)*μ′ − 𝐐(U)*μ)(b)| ≤ 4(1 + d)L³m(μ′ − μ) + C̄′_Qd̄_Am(μ), C̄′_Q := 18(1 + d)(4d + c_D)c_vL^{d+3}.

  - *The intermediate current and the estimate.* Put J′ := 𝐐(Ũ)*λ^B and δ𝐉 := J(U^A) − J′ = 𝐐(U^A)*λ^A − 𝐐(Ũ)*λ^B. By (ā) and (b̄) with U′ = U^A, U = Ũ, d̄_A ≤ d̄, and ε₁ ≤ 1,

    a_{j(x)}³|δ𝐉(x)| ≤ x̄_J(d̄ + (ε/a_n)ε₁), x̄_J := 4(1 + d)L³x̄_λ + C̄′_QB̄_λ, a_{j(x)}³|J′(x)| ≤ B̄_Jε₁, B̄_J := 4(1 + d)L³B̄_λ,

    and a_{j(x)}³|J(U^B)(x)| ≤ B̄_Jε₁ on T_{ε/L}. J′ and J(U^B) are the lifts of the same λ^B through the two runs' averagings, which the J-term paragraph of §10.4 uses. In the double-bar form: for a coarse gauge function ω′ and a fine ω restricting to it, ⟨J′, D_Ũω′⟩ = ⟨J(U^B), D_{U^B}ω⟩ = 0 by the covariance of Q₁^{pl}(U^B), so D_Ũ*J′ = 0, and the display of *The interface twist.* at Ũ gives J′ = Q_db(Ũ)*ν^B. By (B1) of §10.1 at n = 1, Q₁^{pl}(U^B)* = Q̄₁(U^B)* − Θ₁*D_Ũ*, so J(U^B) = Q₁^{pl}(U^B)*J′ = Q̄₁(U^B)*J′. The path (e^{itεA}Ũ, J′ + tδ𝐉) joins (Ũ, J′) at t = 0 to the diagonal point (U^A, J(U^A)) at t = 1. It stays in 𝒟̄ for |t| ≤ R̄ := min(α₁/(2d̄), α_J/(4x̄_J(d̄ + (ε/a_n)ε₁))) when 2B̄_Jε₁ ≤ α_J. When R̄ ≥ 2, Cauchy's estimate gives the following: every per-point density that is analytic on 𝒟̄ changes under the alignment by ≤ 2/R̄ times its bound on 𝒟̄, and 2/R̄ ≤ c_al(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, with c_al := 4C_U(M)/α₁ + 8x̄_J(C_U(M) + 1)/α_J. A density that depends on the background alone needs only the path U(t), with the factor 4d̄/α₁. The conditions used are:
    - 4C_U(M)ε₁ ≤ α₁ and 4C₀ε₁ ≤ α₁;
    - 8x̄_J(C_U(M) + 1)ε₁ ≤ α_J and 2B̄_Jε₁ ≤ α_J;
    - (ĀQ), and 2C₀ε₁ ≤ c₂ of CMP 98 Proposition 2;
    - 2C₀ε₁ ≤ c₃ of CMP 98 Proposition 3, for the logarithms of (B1) used in f_y;
    - C_U(M)ε₁ below the α₁ of CMP 98 (158), C_U(M)ε₁ ≤ 1, and ε₁ ≤ 1;
    - 16dLα₁ ≤ 1, for the twist;
    - RM ≥ 12L on Bałaban's R and M, which covers RM > (d + 1)L + 1 of Ξ² = 0, and the conditions of (b) on R and M, under which c_K, c″_K, c̄_e, and C_U(M) are defined.

    All but the last two are smallness conditions on ε₁ and belong to (T-Λ). The condition 16dLα₁ ≤ 1 lowers PBG's α₁, which PBG's theorems permit, since they hold for every α₁ small enough; the conditions on ε₁ that contain α₁ then use the lowered value.
- *The multiscale gauge-fixing defect.* The terms in E below use the functional form (10.6c) of the Cauchy note's §10.4 for the multiscale problem. Its derivation there runs through the identities (i)–(iii), the sizes (G1), the commutator (G2), the defects (G3), the bounds on μ_A − μ_B and δr, and the sources s₂, s₁, and s₀. It carries over with the following four changes, with the weight 𝔰(x) := B₀a_{j(x)}^{−1}e^{−½δ₁d(x,y′)} of 𝒜 = H₁^Be_{y′} by (3.133) at the receiving layer, and γ_d in place of γ_H.
  - The projection. R_X is the orthogonal projection onto Δ_XN(Q′_X) (PBG (3.21)–(3.22), p.394), with the multiscale Q′_X of (3.18)–(3.19). The formula (3.25) writes it with G′_X := (Δ_X + Q′_X*a^XQ′_X)^{−1}, where the penalty (3.24) carries the coefficient a_i of the layer's index i in the run: i = j′ for the layer j′ in run A and i = j′ + 1 in run B, with a_{i+1} = a_i/(L^{−2} + a_i) and a₀ = a₁ = 1. With a_* := 1 − L^{−2}, a_{i+1} − a_* = L^{−2}(a_i − a_*)/((L^{−2} + a_i)(L^{−2} + a_*)), and the denominator is ≥ 1, so 0 ≤ a_i − a_* ≤ L^{−2i} for i ≥ 1. Hence |a^A_{j′} − a^B_{j′}| ≤ L^{−2j′} = (ε/a_{j′})², which is the layer-dependent penalty defect.
  - The identities. (i) holds layer by layer, Q′_{B,j′} = Q′_{A,j′}Q′₁ by CMP 98 (80) for each layer's composite average, and Q′₁Q′₁* = 1 holds on all of T_ε. (ii) and (iii) concern the one-step averages Q₁ and Q′₁ alone, which are the same in every layer, and hold cell by cell with the transport corrections of the Cauchy note's regularity paragraph. With (i), Q′₁Q′_B*a^BQ′_B = Q′_A*a^BQ′_AQ′₁, so the commutator identity holds with Q′_A*(a^A − a^B)Q′_Bψ in place of (a_A − a_B)ℓ^{−2}Q′_A*Q′_Bψ, the factor (a^A_{j′} − a^B_{j′})a_{j′}^{−2} acting on the layer j′.
  - The transfer of the bounds. PBG's (3.42), (3.43), and (3.48) are stated for the global multiscale operators, with the factor of the layer of the evaluation point (pp.397–398). Let ρ be a source with |ρ| ≤ ρ̄a_{j(z)}^qe^{−½δ₁d(z,y′)} on Δ(z), |q| ≤ 3, and let an operator of the chain obey B₀a_{j(x)}^pe^{−δ₀d(x,z)} for sources in Δ(z). Its value at x is then ≤ c̄_KB₀ρ̄a_{j(x)}^{p+q}e^{−½δ₁d(x,y′)}. Indeed (a_{j(z)}/a_{j(x)})^q ≤ L^{3|j(z)−j(x)|}, and the triangle inequality (2.54) of PRT2 with δ₁ ≤ δ₀ leaves e^{−½δ₀d(x,z)}. The count (2.58) at α = ½ then bounds Σ_zL^{3|j(z)−j(x)|}e^{−½δ₀d(x,z)} by c̄_K := 6c₀^d(¼)(1 + 4L³) under the condition ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1 of (b): the layer of x contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(¼), and the n-th layer on either side ≤ 6c₀^d(¼)L^{3n}e^{−(4 log L+1)(n−1)} ≤ 6c₀^d(¼)L³e^{−(n−1)}. This is PBG's remark on p.398, with the inter-layer factors paid by the count. The sources of the chain have q ∈ {−3, −1, 1}: f_X and r_X have q = −1, Q′_XG′_Xf_X has q = 1, and μ_X and Q′_X*μ_X have q = −3. Each operator of the chain costs one count, so B_G and B_G(γ_H) of the Cauchy note become B̄_G := c̄_KB₀ and B̄_G(γ_d) := c̄_K4^{γ_d}B₀(γ_d), and every constant c_𝔠, c_f, and c_{g1}–c_{g7} becomes its value at B̄_G and B̄_G(γ_d).
  - The relative sizes. Every small factor of the single-scale proof is local: the oscillations of ∇𝒜 and of ∇ψ over one cell, ε|∇φ_X| against |φ_X|, the transports, and the penalty defect. At a point of the layer j′ each is at most its single-scale value with ℓ := a_{j′}, with ν_F := εa_{j′}sup|F| ≤ C₀ε₁(ε/a_{j′}) by |F| ≤ C₀ε₁/a_{j′}² of (b). Since a_{j′} ≥ a_n and (ε/a_{j′})² ≤ (ε/a_n)^{γ_d}, τ̂ is replaced throughout by τ̂_n := (ε/a_n)^{γ_d} + C₀ε₁(ε/a_n).

  So the multiscale (10.6c) holds, β_gf(ℱ, 𝒜) = ⟨D_B*ℱ, s₂⟩ + ⟨∇ℱ, s₁⟩ + ⟨ℱ, s₀⟩, with the bounds of the Cauchy note on s₂, s₁, and s₀ at B̄_G and B̄_G(γ_d), with τ̂_n for τ̂ and a_{j(x)} for ℓ at the point x, and with the decay of 𝔰 about y′. This closes the multiscale form of (10.6c) used by the terms in E.
- *The weighted defect.* The weight w is ζ := ζ₀(1 − ζ₁) for (1.21) and ζ₀ for (1.2). For fields on T_{ε/L}, β_w(𝒜′, 𝒜) := Σ_pε⁴w(p)⟨(DQ₁𝒜′)(p), (DQ₁𝒜)(p)⟩ − Σ_{p′}(ε/L)⁴w(p′)⟨(D𝒜′)(p′), (D𝒜)(p′)⟩ at the aligned backgrounds. Put X := D𝒜 and (DQ₁𝒜)(p) = X̄_p + y_p, with y_p := d(εr_p)[𝒜] as in (10.6a). The column sums Σ_pL⁴w_{p,p′} = 1 of the Cauchy note's (9.11) give
  β_w(𝒜, 𝒜) = −Σ_pε⁴w(p)Var_p(X) − Σ_pε⁴Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(w(p′) − w(p))|X_{p′}|² + Σ_pε⁴w(p)(2⟨X̄_p, y_p⟩ + |y_p|²).
  - Since 0 ≤ w ≤ 1, the first and third sums are bounded as in (10.6a).
  - The second sum is the new term. By LFR I p.201 l.28–29, ζ₀ changes from 0 to 1 near ∂Z^{∼−2} at the scale k; by LFR II p.360 l.34–35, ζ₁ changes on a layer of width 2 at the scale h. Neither page bounds the gradients, so they are a choice: ζ₀ and ζ₁ are fixed smooth functions of the continuum point, the same in both runs, with |∇ζ₀| ≤ C/a_k and |∇ζ₁| ≤ C/a_h. The choice is admissible. The R-operation keeps the integral of the density (LFR I (0.4), p.176), and ζ₀ and ζ₁ enter it only through splits of the action, A(ζ₀, ·) + A(1 − ζ₀, ·) in (1.15) and (1.18), and through the expansions (1.17) and (1.20), which are identities for every such ζ₀ and ζ₁. So F_K does not depend on the choice. The scale h satisfies h + 1 ≥ n: ζ₁ ∈ C₀^∞(Z″_{h+1}) needs Z″_{h+1} ≠ ∅, and a young history has no large-field region below its birth scale n. With k ≥ n, the step being one of the history's, |∇w| ≤ |∇ζ₀| + |∇ζ₁| ≤ C(1 + L)/a_n, so |w(p′) − w(p)| ≤ C_ζ(ε/a_n) for p′ within 2ε of p, C_ζ := 2C(1 + L).
  - For 𝒜 = H₁B̃, (3.133) with PBG's common rate δ₀ and constant B₀ (p.423 l.39–40) and the factor a_{j′}^{−1} of the data gives, at x ∈ Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, a_j|𝒜(x)| + a_j²|∇𝒜(x)| ≤ 2B₀Σ_{y′}(a_j/a_{j′})e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}|B̃(y′)|, the sum over the points y′ of the data, whose measure a_{j′}^d cancels the a_{j′}^{−d} of (3.133). Since a_j/a_{j′} ≤ L^{2(j−j′)_+} and |B̃| ≤ (1 + 2dL)b_k, the row sums c_K of (b) bound this by σ_k := 2B₀c_K(1 + 2dL)b_k. With |D𝒜| ≤ 2|∇𝒜| and d(d − 1)/2 plaquettes at a point, Σ_{x∈Δ(y)}η⁴|D𝒜(x)|² and sup_{Δ(y)}(a_j|𝒜|)² are both ≤ N_k := 2d²σ_k² per point y.
  - The form ⟨H_{1,k}B′, Δ₁(ζ₀)H_{1,k}B′⟩ of (1.2) is the second-order part in B′ of g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ)), since V(ζ₀, ·) is at least of third order (p.357 l.9). In each run write U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ) = e^{iη(g_k𝒜₁ + g_k²𝒜₂ + O(g_k³))}U_0, with 𝒜₁ = H_{1,k}B′ and 𝒜₂ the second-order data derivative. For a weight w let Δ^{act}(w) be the plain second variation of A(w, ·) at U_0 (PBG (3.10) with the weight w(p) on each plaquette) and J(w) its current, ⟨X, J(w)⟩ := dA(w, U_0)[X]. Then the form is ⟨𝒜₁, Δ^{act}(ζ₀)𝒜₁⟩ + 2⟨𝒜₂, J(ζ₀)⟩. The expansion is taken in VAR's gauge (153), in which the double-bar constraint (156) holds with the twisted data, so 𝒜₁ = H₁B̃′ by *The lifts and the chain rule.*; the second-order coefficient does not depend on the gauge, since each plaquette term of A(ζ₀, ·) is gauge invariant. By (1.4) (p.357 l.19–26) the unweighted current J_0 = J(1) is 𝐐*λ_0, with 𝐐 the plain multiscale averaging and λ_0 a field on the data of Z∖Λ, and by *The interface twist.* J_0 = Q*ν_0 with Q := Q_db and ν_0 = (1 − Ξ)*λ_0. Let C^{(2)} be the second-order term of the multiscale double bar (PBG p.421, after (3.127)) and B̃₂ the second-order part in B′ of the twisted data. Then Q𝒜₂ + C^{(2)}[𝒜₁, 𝒜₁] = B̃₂, so ⟨𝒜₂, J_0⟩ = ⟨B̃₂ − C^{(2)}[𝒜₁, 𝒜₁], ν_0⟩. Since A(ζ₀, ·) + A(1 − ζ₀, ·) is the unweighted action, J(ζ₀) = J_0 − J(1 − ζ₀), and

    ⟨H_{1,k}B′, Δ₁(ζ₀)H_{1,k}B′⟩ = f(𝒜₁) + 2⟨B̃₂, ν_0⟩ − 2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩, f(𝒜) := ⟨𝒜, Δ^{act}(ζ₀)𝒜⟩ − 2⟨C^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜], ν_0⟩.

    The bulk form f consists of the action part of the Cauchy note's §10.4, with the weight ζ₀, and its constraint part. The identity is the Taylor expansion itself, so no π-part and no gauge-fixing term enter it. The third term is the part carried by the weight 1 − ζ₀, below. The second, the twist term, vanishes unless Λ meets an interface. Off Λ the plain data is fixed, so B̃₂ vanishes off Λ and off the bonds of S_j whose blocks B(x) meet Λ. On the data of Λ, λ_0 = 0, so ν_0 = −Ξ*λ_0 there, which vanishes off the bonds read by Ξ. So 2⟨B̃₂, ν_0⟩ = 0 when no block B(x) of an outer endpoint of S_j meets Λ. Otherwise its run difference is bounded as follows (derived, not reviewed).
    - The twisted data. By VAR (154), on a bond ⟨x, x′⟩ of S_j the twisted datum is (1/i)log V_1 with V_1 = exp[−iΣ_{x₁∈B(x)}L^{−d}Y_{x₁}]V″(x, x′), Y_{x₁} := (1/i)log V″(Γ_{x,x₁}), each contour having at most dL bonds; on the other bonds it is (1/i)log V″. Here V″ = e^{ig_kB′}V_bg on Λ, with V_bg the background data in the gauge (153). Write x_b := g_kB′(b) and V_bg(b) = e^{iβ_b}. The twisted datum is then an analytic function f(x, β) of the angles of the bonds read, and B̃₂ is the coefficient of g_k² in f(g_kB′, β), that is, the homogeneous part P₂ of degree 2 of f in x, evaluated at x = B′.
    - Its size. In a faithful unitary representation with the operator norm on 𝔤_ℂ, a product of exponentials whose exponents have norms adding up to s ≤ 1/4 is 1 + E with |E| ≤ e^s − 1, and |log(1 + E)| ≤ |E|/(1 − |E|) ≤ 2s. Take r := 1/(36dL), and complex angles with |x_b| ≤ r and |β_b − β_b^0| ≤ r about real angles with |β_b^0| ≤ r. Each bond factor then has exponents of total norm ≤ 3r, so |Y_{x₁}| ≤ 6dLr, the exponents of V_1 add up to ≤ 9dLr = 1/4, and |f| ≤ K := 1/2. By Cauchy's estimate in the variable t of f(tx, β), |P₂(x)| ≤ K(sup|x|/r)², so |B̃₂| ≤ c₂b_k² with c₂ := K/r² = 648d²L², since |B′| ≤ b_k. By Cauchy's estimate in β on the polydisc of radius r, the derivative of P₂ in β along δβ is ≤ c₂b_k²sup|δβ|/r. The premise |β^0| ≤ r holds by VAR (151), |V″ − 1| < 9dL²Mε₀, when 648d²L³Mε₀ ≤ 1, a smallness condition of the kind of VAR's 9dL²Mε₀ ≤ c₁.
    - The pairing. With the normalisation of the Cauchy note's §10.3, a datum B on the layer j′ enters the pairing as a_{j′}^{−1}B, with the measure a_{j′}^d. The two runs share Λ, the layers, S_j, and B′ (*The complex class.*, *The lines.*: the integration variables are fixed and equal in both runs), and their background angles differ by ≤ d̄ on the bonds read (as in *The multipliers.*). ν_0 is the multiplier ν of *The interface twist.* at U_0 = U_{k,Z}(V′₀V_Λ), the VAR minimiser at the data V′₀V_Λ (*The expansion at a minimum.*), whose data are regular, on Λ by LFR I (1.84) (|M^j(U_0) − 1| < O(1)B₃B₅M⁶ε_k, p.197, a closeness to 1 in the axial gauge in k-blocks) and off Λ by the small-field conditions. In the gauge of the twist chart the angles of U_0 on Λ are then ≤ 2·O(1)B₃B₅M⁶ε_k, which is ≤ r/2 = 1/(72dL) for γ small (the bound (1.84) is in the axial gauge in k-blocks; the twist's size bounds use only the logarithms of contour holonomies, which are gauge covariant, so that gauge serves as the chart's gauge, and both runs use the same gauge, so that d̄ bounds the angle difference), and its plaquettes obey LFR I (1.80) (p.195 l.16), |U_0(∂p) − 1| < (2 + O(1)B₃B₅M⁵)ε_kη² on Ω_k, which is within the curvature bound C₀ε₁ under which B̄_ν and x̄_ν are derived, since ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k with C(M) ≥ 2 + O(1)B₃B₅M⁵, and whose run difference is the depth of §4(c), so *The interface twist.* and *The multipliers.* apply to U_0 with its data in place of run B's, and to run A's counterpart with run A's. On the bonds of S_j, ν_0 obeys the bounds of ν. On the data of Λ, ν_0 = −Ξ*λ_0 lies on bonds of the layer j − 1, and the entry and Lipschitz bounds of Ξ* in *The multipliers.* give a_{j−1}³|ν_0^B| ≤ 4dL^{d−3}B̄_λε₁ and a_{j−1}³|ν_0^A − ν_0^B| ≤ x̄_tw(d̄ + (ε/a_n)ε₁), x̄_tw := 4dL^{d−3}x̄_λ + 24d²L^{d−2}B̄_λ, from Ξ_A*(λ_0^A − λ_0^B) + (Ξ_A − Ξ_B)*λ_0^B. Put B̄_0 := max(B̄_ν, 4dL^{d−3}B̄_λ) and x̄_0 := max(x̄_ν, x̄_tw). So, with at most d bonds of the data at a point, a³|ν_0^B| ≤ B̄_0ε₁ and a³|ν_0^A − ν_0^B| ≤ x̄_0(d̄ + (ε/a_n)ε₁), and the Lipschitz bound of B̃₂ integrated along the segment of the angles,
      |2⟨B̃₂^A, ν_0^A⟩ − 2⟨B̃₂^B, ν_0^B⟩| ≤ 2dc₂b_k²[36dLd̄(B̄_0ε₁ + x̄_0(d̄ + (ε/a_n)ε₁)) + x̄_0(d̄ + (ε/a_n)ε₁)]
      per interface point. With d̄ ≤ C_U(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, ε₁ ≤ 1, and N_k = 8d²B₀²c_K²(1 + 2dL)²b_k², this is ≤ C_tw(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁N_k per interface point, with C_tw(M) := c₂[36dLC_U(M)(B̄_0 + x̄_0(C_U(M) + 1)) + x̄_0(C_U(M) + 1)]/(4dB₀²c_K²(1 + 2dL)²). It is supported on the bonds of S_j whose blocks B(x) meet Λ and on the bonds of Λ read by Ξ, so it needs no statement on how LFR II's Λ meets the interfaces. At the pair (Ũ, J′) of the alignment, Ũ = MU^B_0 and J′ = Q_db(Ũ)*ν^B (*The intermediate current and the estimate.*), so run A's bulk form f^A has ⟨C_A^{(2)}[X, X], ν^B⟩ = ⟨H^AC_A^{(2)}[X, X], J′⟩ in its constraint term, for every X, since Q_db(Ũ)H^A = 1. By (B4) of §10.1 on each layer, and on S_j since the twist reads the double bars of the finer layer, the multiscale double bar composes as Q^B_db(W) = Q^A_db(M̄(W)), with M̄ the one-step double bar. So the second-order chain rule C_B^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜] = C_A^{(2)}[Q₁𝒜, Q₁𝒜] + Q^AC₁^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜] holds for the multiscale double bar, with Q₁ := Q̄₁(U^B) and C₁^{(2)} := C̄₁^{(2)}, the quadratic part of CMP 98 (122), throughout (d). The weighted actions are the same under M̄ and M, plaquette by plaquette, since the two differ by the gauge transformation v₁ of (B1). With Q̄₁ = Q₁^{pl} + D̄Θ₁, the term y_p of (10.6a) gains [F̄_p, Θ₁𝒜], with |Θ₁𝒜| ≤ c_vε|𝒜| by CMP 98 (162)–(163), which is of the relative size (ε/a_j)ε₁ of y_p itself and enters C_d. Then the action and constraint parts of §10.4 give, for β_f(𝒜, 𝒜) := f^A(Q₁𝒜) − f^B(𝒜),

    β_f(𝒜, 𝒜) = d²b_{ζ₀}(U^B_0)[𝒜, 𝒜] − 2⟨J^A(ζ₀) − J′, C₁^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜]⟩,

    with J^A(ζ₀) the current of A_ε(ζ₀, ·) at Ũ and b_w(W) := A_ε(w, MW) − A_{ε/L}(w, W) the weighted blocking artifact. By the column sums, b_w is the expansion of (10.6a) with the weight w(p) on each coarse term, plus the weight-gradient sum −½Σ_pε⁴Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(w(p′) − w(p))|F_{p′}|² and the quartic terms with the same weights. Besides the terms of (10.6a) and the new term above, the second variation of the weight-gradient sum and the current term give two pieces.
    - The curvature piece of the weight-gradient sum, −Σ_pε⁴Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(w(p′) − w(p))·2⟨F_{p′}, [𝒜 ∧ 𝒜]_{p′}⟩ up to higher orders, is ≤ 2C_ζ(ε/a_n)·C₀ε₁a_j^{−2}·2|𝒜|² per fine plaquette by (9.2) at the scale j, so ≤ 2d²C₀C_ζ(ε/a_n)ε₁N_k on the fine cells of Δ(y).
    - The current term. J^A(ζ₀)(b) = ε^{−1}Σ_{p∋b}±ζ₀(p)F_p, transported, and the 2(d − 1) plaquettes at b pair into differences at the spacing ε. So |J^A(ζ₀)| ≤ 2(d − 1)(|∇F| + |∇ζ₀||F|) ≤ c_wε₁a_j^{−3}, c_w := 4(d − 1)(1 + C)C₀, by |F| ≤ 2C₀ε₁/a_j² and |∇F| ≤ 2C₀ε₁/a_j³ for Ũ (§9.3) and |∇ζ₀| ≤ C/a_k ≤ C/a_j. The alignment gives a_j³|J′| ≤ B̄_Jε₁. With |C₁^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜]| ≤ C₂ε|𝒜|² (VAR (44)), the current term is ≤ 2d(c_w + B̄_J)C₂ε₁(ε/a_j)N_k on the fine cells of Δ(y).
  - With (3.133) at the layer of p and the exponent γ_d, the part of |β_w(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B)| on the fine cells of Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, and the same part of |β_f(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B)|, are ≤ C_d[(ε/a_j)^{γ_d} + C_ζ(ε/a_n) + ε₁(ε/a_j)]N_k. Here ε₁ ≤ 1 by (T-Λ), and C_d := C_β(γ_d) of (10.6a) plus 2d²C₀ plus 2d(c_w + B̄_J)C₂ plus the constants of the Cauchy note's (10.6c) at γ_d, so that C_d also covers the two pieces above and bounds the gauge-fixing sources below.
  - The part carried by the weight 1 − ζ₀, −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩, is not of the form of §10.4: J(1 − ζ₀) need not annihilate ker Q, so the constraint does not remove 𝒜₂. The main proof does not compare it separately: the form of (1.2) enters only through the denominator d of (1.1), which *The complementary-weight parts.* below compares as a whole (derived).
- *The terms in E.* Put 𝒜^X := H^X₁B̃^X, with each run's twisted data B̃^X := (1 + Ξ_X)B at the common B, E := Q₁𝒜^B − 𝒜^A with Q₁ = Q̄₁(U^B), and F_w^X(𝒜) := Σ_pη_X⁴w(p)|(D𝒜)(p)|². Then
  F_w^A(𝒜^A) − F_w^B(𝒜^B) = β_w(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B) − 2F_w^A(E, Q₁𝒜^B) + F_w^A(E), and likewise f^A(𝒜^A) − f^B(𝒜^B) = β_f(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B) − 2f^A(E, Q₁𝒜^B) + f^A(E).
  - Unlike in (10.5), the terms in E are of first order, since H₁ minimises the unweighted form.
  - Q^AE = B̃^B − B̃^A = (Ξ_B − Ξ_A)B, so E = E₀ + E_Ξ with E₀ := (I − H^A₁Q^A)E ∈ ker Q^A and E_Ξ := H^A₁(Ξ_B − Ξ_A)B. By the entry bounds of *The interface twist.*, |((Ξ_B − Ξ_A)B)(b)| ≤ 12d²L²d̄ max|B| on S_j, so (3.133) with the count of (b) gives a_{j(x)}|E_Ξ(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇E_Ξ(x)| ≤ 12d²L²d̄σ_k ≤ 12d²L²C_U(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁σ_k. By §10.4 and *The lifts and the chain rule.*, ⟨ψ, G_A^{−1}E⟩ = β(ℒψ, 𝒜^B) for ψ ∈ ker Q^A, with β the unweighted defect, and ⟨ψ, G₁^{−1}H^A₁Q^AE⟩ = 0. So E₀ = G₁^Qℒ*(D*s₁ + s₀), with G₁^Q := (I − H₁Q)G₁ at run A's pair, where β(ℱ, 𝒜^B) = ⟨Dℱ, s₁⟩ + ⟨ℱ, s₀⟩; PBG's 𝔊 of (3.153) is not used, as in the Cauchy note's §10.4. The variance term gives s₁(p′) := Σ_pL⁴w_{p,p′}(X_{p′} − X̄_p), a probability average with |s₁(p′)| ≤ the oscillation of X over the 2L × 2L fine squares meeting p′, a set within 4ε of p′. The r-terms give pieces of s₁ of the size of y_p and of that oscillation. The curvature couplings and the current term give s₀. The gauge-fixing part gives, by the Cauchy note's (10.6c), a piece of s₀ and the sources D s₂ and ∇*s₁^{gf}, with |s₂| and |s₁^{gf}| of the relative size of s₁; the bounds of G₁^Q of the Cauchy note's §10.4, those of (3.42) for G₁ entry by entry with B₀ + 2c_δ′B₀² in place of B₀, here at the multiscale (3.132)–(3.133) of *The pair class.* with the transfer of the bounds above, treat them as they treat D*s₁. The diagonal value β_π(𝒜, 𝒜) of the π-part of β for multiscale data is derived (not reviewed) in *The π-part of β for multiscale data.* below. Its contribution to E₀ enters the pairings of the next bullets through *The π-part in the pairings.* there, the π–π term of F_w^A(E) included (derived, not reviewed). The gauge-fixing sources use the multiscale form of (10.6c) of the bullet above, with a_{j(x)} for ℓ at the point x and τ̂_n for τ̂.
  - This is the residual of (b) with s₁ and s₀ in place of r₁ and r₀. A source at a point of the layer j′ has relative size ≤ C_d(ε/a_{j′})^{γ_d}, in place of C_r(ε/a_{j′})ε₁, and the gradient bound (3.44) costs the Hölder factor 2(a_{j′}/ε)^{ε₀}. So, for these sources, the π-part E₀^π excluded, a_{j(x)}|(E₀ − E₀^π)(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇(E₀ − E₀^π)(x)| ≤ x_E(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/2}σ_k, with σ_k the layer-weighted supremum of 𝒜^B above (the sources at y′ are sized by 𝒜^B near y′), x_E := C_G^Q(ε₀)c_KC_d, and γ_d − ε₀ = (1 − ε₀)/2. Here C_G^Q(ε₀) is the Cauchy note's C_G(ε₀) = 4c_𝒦C_r(B₀ + B₀′(ε₀)) with B₀ + 2c_δ′B₀² in place of B₀; its factor C_r is not needed here, and x_E is taken with max(C_r, 1) in place of C_r, so that it is an upper bound in either case. With E_Ξ, ε₁ ≤ 1, and (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/2} ≤ (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}, a_{j(x)}|E′(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇E′(x)| ≤ x̂_E(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}σ_k for E′ := E − E₀^π, x̂_E := x_E + 12d²L²C_U(M).
  - By Cauchy–Schwarz on each Δ(y), |F_w^A(E′, Q₁𝒜^B)| ≤ x̂_E(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}N_k per point, and F_w^A(E′) ≤ x̂_E²(ε/a_n)^{2(1−ε₀)/3}N_k. By *The π-part in the pairings.*, F_w^A(E₀^π, Q₁𝒜^B) and F_w^A(E₀^π, E′) add at most 2d²C_ψC_π(1 + x̂_E)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/2}ε₁N_k per point, with the decay of its sum over the blocks taken by c_K. The term F_w^A(E₀^π, E₀^π) of F_w^A(E) is at most C_ππ(ε/a_n)^{1/3}ε₁²N_k per point (*The π–π term.* there).
- *The π-part of β for multiscale data (derived, not reviewed).* Notation as in the Cauchy note's *The π-Part of β.* (its l.840–844) and *The Gauge-Fixing Part of β.* (l.774–818), at d = 4, with ν := ν_π = 𝒬′*λ^B, ℓ := a_j at an interface site of the layer j, τ̂_n for τ̂, and 𝔰 := σ_ka_j^{−2}, as for the gauge-fixing sources above.
  - Each run's remainder. In run X, the bracket of (3.120) and the term Q_Xc_X of (B5) cancel as in the Cauchy note's two bullets, and the term −c̄(Q′_Xμ_A, Q_XA) of (B5) vanishes by Q′_Xμ_A = 0. The term D̄q_X no longer drops out, since D̄*ν ≠ 0 at the sites of ∂Ω_{j+1} where two layers meet. So π_X(A, A) = −⟨q_X(μ_A, A) + q_X(μ_A, P_XA), D̄*ν⟩, and β_π := π_A(Q₁𝒜, Q₁𝒜) − π_B(𝒜, 𝒜) = ⟨Θ, D̄*ν⟩ with Θ := [q_B(μ^B, 𝒜) + q_B(μ^B, P_B𝒜)] − [q_A(μ^A, Q₁𝒜) + q_A(μ^A, P_AQ₁𝒜)], μ^B := μ_𝒜 in run B and μ^A := μ_{Q₁𝒜} in run A.
  - The composition. The gauge factor w of (B5) composes as the double bar does ((B4), CMP 98 (90)–(92)): run B's w is run A's w about MU^B, applied to the gauge parameter (1/i)log w₁ = Q′₁λ + q₁(λ, 𝒜) + … of the one-step factor w₁ and to the field Q₁𝒜. Its bilinear part gives q_B(λ, 𝒜) = q_A(Q′₁λ, Q₁𝒜) + Q′_Aq₁(λ, 𝒜). The first order of (B5) in λ gives Q₁D_Bλ = D_AQ′₁λ, so Q₁P_B𝒜 = Q₁𝒜 − D_AQ′₁μ^B. With δμ := Q′₁μ^B − μ^A and bilinearity,
    Θ = 2q_A(δμ, Q₁𝒜) − q_A(δμ, D_Aμ^A) − q_A(Q′₁μ^B, D_Aδμ) + Q′_A[q₁(μ^B, 𝒜) + q₁(μ^B, P_B𝒜)].
  - The bound of q. The one-step factor is v₁(U₁^u)^{−1}·u(y)·v₁(U₁) with v₁(U₁)(y) = (R_{0,y}U₁)‾^{−1} of CMP 98 (87) and (89) at one step: the average over x ∈ B(y) of the logarithms of the holonomies h(x) of U₁U₀^{−1} along Γ_{y,x}, transported by the real background, each path having at most dL fine bonds of length η = ε/L. Under u = e^{iλ}, h(x) becomes u(y)h(x)u_x^{−1}, with u_x the transported u(x). The averages are linear in the logarithms, so the bilinear part of (1/i)log w₁ in (λ, A) consists only of the second-order Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff terms ½[X, Y] of the compositions: the two products u(y)h(x)u_x^{−1} and the product of the three factors of w₁. Each such term pairs one value of λ on B(y) with a sum of at most dL values of ηA on the paths, and |[X, Y]| ≤ c_G|X||Y|. At most four such terms occur, so |q₁(λ, A)(y)| ≤ c_qε sup_{B(y)}|λ| sup|A|, c_q := 4dc_G, the second supremum over the bonds of the paths. Iterating the composition over the scales m < j, with |Q′| ≤ 1 and |Q₁A| ≤ sup|A| (averages), gives |q_A(λ, A)| ≤ Σ_{m<j}c_qL^mε sup|λ| sup|A| ≤ 2c_qa_j sup|λ| sup|A|, the suprema over the block of the scale j.
  - δμ. Here μ^X = G′_Xr_X, with r_X := R_Xf_X of the gauge-fixing paragraph (not its multiplier μ_X). So δμ = −𝔠(r_B) − G′_Aδr, with 𝔠 of *The Commutator.* and δr := r_A − Q′₁r_B of *The Defects.* By (G1), (G2), and l.818, |δμ| ≤ c_μτ̂_na_j²𝔰 = c_μτ̂_nσ_k, c_μ := B_G(c_𝔠c_{g2} + c_{g6}). For ∇δμ, (3.42) gives |∇G′_Aδr| ≤ B_Ga_jc_{g6}τ̂_n𝔰, and ∇𝔠(r_B) is the entry ∇G′D* of (3.44) applied to the bracket of l.802, whose Hölder seminorm at the scale of ε is at most 3(a_j/ε)^{β′} times its supremum, as in the W-δ note's §11 (a). So |∇δμ| ≤ c′_μ(β′)(a_j/ε)^{β′}τ̂_nσ_ka_j^{−1}, c′_μ(β′) := 3B₀′(β′)c_δ′(c_𝔠c_{g2} + c_{g6}) + B_Gc_{g6}.
  - The other factors. |μ^B| ≤ B_Gc_{g2}σ_k by (G1) and (3.42); r_A = Q′₁r_B + δr with τ̂_n ≤ 2 gives |D_Aμ^A| ≤ B_G(c_{g2} + 2c_{g6})σ_ka_j^{−1}; |𝒜|, |Q₁𝒜| ≤ σ_ka_j^{−1}; and |P_B𝒜| ≤ (1 + B_Gc_{g2})σ_ka_j^{−1}.
  - Θ. The four terms are at most 4c_qc_μτ̂_nσ_k², 2c_qc_μB_G(c_{g2} + 2c_{g6})τ̂_nσ_k², 2c_qB_Gc_{g2}c′_μ(β′)(a_j/ε)^{β′}τ̂_nσ_k², and c_qB_Gc_{g2}(2 + B_Gc_{g2})(ε/a_j)σ_k², the last with the one-step bound. So |Θ| ≤ C_Θ(β′)(a_j/ε)^{β′}(τ̂_n + ε/a_j)σ_k², with C_Θ(β′) the sum of the four constants.
  - The pairing. The multiplier is PBG's double-bar one, Q_db*ν = 𝐉, so ν = R₀*J = (1 − Ξ)*λ (*The two averagings.*). Its plain part λ is the recursion of *The adjoint averaging across layers.*, so D̄*λ vanishes off the interface sites. At a coarse interface site the weighted condition is a_{j−1}^dD*λ_{j−1} + a_j^dD*λ_j = 0, so |D*λ_j| ≤ L^{−d}·2d|λ_{j−1}|/a_{j−1} ≤ 2dL^{4−d}B̄_λε₁a_j^{−4}, and the fine layer's own divergence D*λ_{j−1}, paired with its measure, contributes the same amount per coarse site; together 4dL^{4−d}B̄_λε₁a_j^{−4}. The twist part Ξ*λ lives on the bonds of Λ_{j−1} in the blocks B(x) of the outer endpoints x of S_j, with a_{j−1}³|Ξ*λ| ≤ 4dL^{d−3}B̄_λε₁ by the entry bound of Ξ* (*The multipliers.*), that is a_j³|Ξ*λ| ≤ 4dL^dB̄_λε₁, and the divergence on the layer j − 1 costs 2d/a_{j−1} = 2dL/a_j. So |D̄*ν| ≤ c_νB̄_λε₁a_j^{−4}, c_ν := 4dL^{4−d} + 8d²L^{d+1}, as a density per coarse cell within a_j of the interface (pointwise the fine divergence at a coarse interface site can be larger, and only the mass of |D̄*ν| per cell enters below), and D̄*ν vanishes elsewhere. On the layer j − 1 the bound of Θ holds with ε/a_{j−1} = Lε/a_j, which costs at most the factor L. Against the measure a_j⁴ of a coarse site, |β_π| ≤ Lc_νB̄_λC_Θ(β′)ε₁(a_j/ε)^{β′}(τ̂_n + ε/a_j)σ_k² per interface point. Since j ≤ k < 8n + j(C) (the Cauchy note's l.406; k < 8n for k ≤ k_s), (a_j/ε)^{β′} ≤ L^{j(C)β′}(a_n/ε)^{8β′}, and ε/a_j ≤ ε/a_n. At β′ := ε₀/16, with N_k = 2d²σ_k², this is ≤ C_π(ε/a_n)^{1/2}ε₁N_k per interface point, C_π := L^{1+j(C)ε₀/16}c_νB̄_λC_Θ(ε₀/16)(2 + C₀ε₁)/(2d²), and (ε/a_n)^{1/2} ≤ (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}. C_π depends on the history through j(C), the offset of the Cauchy note's l.406 (k < 8n + j(C)); it is a constant where j(C) is bounded, and otherwise the factor L^{j(C)ε₀/16} is carried with j(C) as in that note. PBG's (3.114)–(3.115) at U₁ = 1 (p.418) and the Cauchy note's single-scale cancellation enter only through (B5) and the two bullets cited.
  - *The π-part in the pairings (derived, not reviewed).* π_A and π_B are symmetric bilinear forms, and μ_A, P_XA, and δμ are linear in the field, so β_π(ℱ, 𝒜) := π_A(Q₁ℱ, Q₁𝒜) − π_B(ℱ, 𝒜) = ⟨Θ(ℱ, 𝒜), D̄*ν⟩ with Θ(ℱ, 𝒜) the polarisation of Θ, a sum of terms q_A(X, Y) and Q′_Aq₁(X, Y) in which one argument is linear in ℱ and the other in 𝒜 := 𝒜^B.
    - The polarised bound. The bound of q is sup to sup, and (G1), (G2), l.818, (3.42), and (3.44) bound μ_ℱ, δμ_ℱ, D_Aμ_ℱ, and P_Bℱ for any field ℱ as they bound them for 𝒜. So the four bounds of *Θ.* and the bound of *The pairing.* hold with σ_k(ℱ)σ_k(𝒜) in place of σ_k², σ_k(ℱ) the decay-weighted, layer-weighted supremum Σ_ye^{−½δ₀d(·,y)}sup_{Δ(y)}(a|ℱ| + a²|∇ℱ|) about the interface site, since μ_ℱ = G′r_ℱ is nonlocal (the row sums c_K take the sum): |β_π(ℱ, 𝒜)| ≤ 2d²C_π(ε/a_n)^{1/2}ε₁σ_k(ℱ)σ_k(𝒜) per interface point.
    - The pairings. The π-part E₀^π of E₀ is the solution in ker Q^A of ⟨ψ, G₁^{−1}E₀^π⟩ = β_π(ℒψ, 𝒜) for ψ ∈ ker Q^A. For a field X let ψ_X ∈ ker Q^A solve ⟨φ, G₁^{−1}ψ_X⟩ = ⟨φ, WX⟩ for φ ∈ ker Q^A, with W the operator of F_w^A, so ψ_X = G₁^Q(WX). Then F_w^A(E₀^π, X) = ⟨E₀^π, WX⟩ = ⟨ψ_X, G₁^{−1}E₀^π⟩ = β_π(ℒψ_X, 𝒜), with no transposition of β_π. For F_w^A, W = D*wD, so ψ_X = G₁^QD*(wDX). The entry G₁^QD* of (3.42), with B₀ + 2c_δ′B₀² in place of B₀, bounds a_j|ψ_X|. The gradient a_j²|∇ψ_X| needs the entry ∇G₁^QD*, which (3.42) does not list; (3.44) gives it with the Hölder norm of wDX, bounded by the lattice Hölder bound 3(a_j/ε)^{β′} times its supremum, as for ∇δμ. With the row sums c_K and (3.133) for ℒ this gives σ_k(ℒψ_X) ≤ C_ψ(β′)(a_j/ε)^{β′}Σ_ye^{−½δ₀d(·,y)}‖X‖_y, C_ψ(β′) a constant of B₀, B₀′(β′), c_δ′, c_K, C₀, and C_ζ. At β′ = ε₀/16 the factor (a_j/ε)^{β′} ≤ L^{j(C)ε₀/16}(a_n/ε)^{ε₀/2} is absorbed as in *The pairing.*, with C_ψ := L^{j(C)ε₀/16}C_ψ(ε₀/16). So |F_w^A(E₀^π, X)| ≤ 2d²C_ψC_π(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/2}ε₁σ_k times the decay-weighted supremum of X, per interface point, for X = Q₁𝒜^B and for the parts E_Ξ and E₀ − E₀^π of E, which are bounded pointwise above.
    - *The π–π term (derived, not reviewed).* The pairing route bounds F_w^A(E₀^π, E₀^π) once E₀^π has a pointwise bound with any constant: X = E₀^π in the last display. That bound comes from the source form of β_π. The source is spread at the scale of the cells, because the composition dilutes the weight of each block centre.
      - The source. β_π(ℱ, 𝒜) is linear in ℱ, so β_π(ℱ, 𝒜) = ⟨s_π, ℱ⟩_B for a field s_π of run B, and E₀^π = G₁^Qℒ*s_π, since ⟨ψ, G₁^{−1}E₀^π⟩ = ⟨ℒψ, s_π⟩_B for ψ ∈ ker Q^A and G₁^Q = G₁ − G₁Q*(QG₁Q*)^{−1}QG₁ is the symmetric Green operator on ker Q^A. Each term of Θ(ℱ, 𝒜) reads ℱ in one slot of q_A or q₁: in the field slot raw, through Q₁ℱ, or through P_Bℱ = ℱ − D_Bμ_ℱ, or in the parameter slot through μ_ℱ, δμ_ℱ, or Q′₁μ_ℱ, with the other slot a field of 𝒜, at most O(1)σ_ka_j^{−1}, or a parameter of 𝒜, at most O(1)σ_k (*The other factors.*, *δμ.*).
      - The weights. In the composition of *The bound of q.*, the step of the scale m reads the m-fold averages Q′^{(m)}λ and Q^{(m)}A at the sites and bonds of the scale m, and its output is carried to the scale j by the averages Q′ of the scales m + 1, …, j − 1. So each of the (a_j/a_m)⁴ blocks of the scale m in a cell of the scale j carries the weight (a_m/a_j)⁴. Within one block, q₁ pairs the parameter at the centre and at the points x of the block with the transported path sums of at most dL bonds of the scale m, and a bond lies on at most all L^d paths of its block, so its coefficient is at most a_m/L times the parameter, while the mean over the dL^d bonds of the block is a_mL^{−d} times it. The coefficient of a bond therefore exceeds the block mean by at most L^{d−1}. A Q^{(m)} average or a Q′^{(m)} average is an average over a block of the scale m with bounded weights, so its transpose spreads a coefficient over that block. With the measure a_j⁴ of the interface site and |D̄*ν| ≤ c_νB̄_λε₁a_j^{−4} per coarse cell (*The pairing.*), the field-slot part of s_π therefore has, on every bond of the cell, the density at most c_qL^{d−1}c_νB̄_λε₁Σ_{m<j}a_m a_j^{−4}|λ| ≤ 2c_qL^{d−1}c_νB̄_λε₁a_j^{−3}|λ|, |λ| the parameter in the other slot. The parameter-slot part is a transpose T*n of an operator T ∈ {μ_·, δμ_·, Q′₁μ_·} applied to a scalar density n with |n| ≤ 2c_qL^dc_νB̄_λε₁a_j^{−4}σ_k (the parameter is read at the run-A points of the cell, a sublattice of run B's points, whence L^d in place of L^{d−1}). On the layer j − 1 the same holds with the factor L of *The pairing.*.
      - The gradient terms. The field-slot entries D_Bμ_ℱ, D_Aμ_ℱ, and D_Aδμ_ℱ are covariant differences of parameters. At the scale m, Q^{(m)}D_A = D_{(m)}Q′^{(m)} by the first order of (B5) iterated (Q₁D_Bλ = D_AQ′₁λ), and a transported path sum of a covariant difference telescopes to the difference of the transported end values. So these entries are parameter-slot reads at the two ends of each path, at most two per path, without a derivative: the step of the scale m contributes at most 2c_q|λ| times the end values, with no geometric factor a_m/a_j, and the sum over m < j gives the factor j. Their part of s_π is T*n with |n| ≤ 4c_qjL^dc_νB̄_λε₁a_j^{−4}σ_k.
      - The transposes. μ_ℱ = G′_Br_B(ℱ) and δμ_ℱ = −𝔠(r_B(ℱ)) − G′_Aδr(ℱ) are products of G′_X, R_X = 1 − P_X, Q′_X, Q′_X*, D_X, D_X*, Q₁, and the averages of 𝔠 and *The Defects.* G′_X and R_X = 1 − G′_XQ′_X*K_X^{−1}Q′_XG′_X (the Cauchy note's l.778) are symmetric, and, once R_X is expanded, every derivative in these products stands next to a G′ (f_X = D_X*ℱ, and the forms G′_AD_A* and D_AG′_A of 𝔠). So T* is a product of the same factors in the reverse order, with D and D*, Q′ and Q′* exchanged, and its sup bound is a product of the entries G′D* and DG′ of (3.42) and of the averages' bounds, |T*n| ≤ c_Ta_j sup|n|, with c_T a polynomial in B_G, d, and C_tr. The factor τ̂ of (G1)–(G3) comes from the regularity of the argument and is not used here.
      - The bound. So |s_π(x)| ≤ c_s(1 + j)ε₁a_j^{−3}σ_k e^{−½δ₀d(x, I)}, I the interface, with the layer factors of (3.42), c_s := 4c_qL^{d+1}c_νB̄_λ(B_G(c_𝔠c_{g2} + 2c_{g6}) + c_T) times the multiplicities of the terms, with c_𝔠 of *The Commutator.* for the 𝔠-part of δμ, as in c_μ, and with c_T including c_𝔠; the tail comes from G′ and its transpose in T*n and in the telescoped entries, and the row sums c_K below absorb it. ℒ* = I* + (1 − Q₁I)*(Q₁Q₁*)^{−1}Q₁ is sup-bounded with the exponential tail at the rate δ_Q, by c_ℒ*. The entries G₁^Q and ∇G₁^Q of (3.42), with B₀ + 2c_δ′B₀² in place of B₀, and the row sums c_K give a_{j(x)}|E₀^π(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇E₀^π(x)| ≤ x_π(1 + j)ε₁σ_k, x_π := 2(B₀ + 2c_δ′B₀²)c_Kc_ℒ*c_s, at the points x within the decay of the interface, the layer factors a_{j(x)}/a_j taken by (3.132)–(3.133) as in *The pairings.*
      - The π–π term. The last display of *The pairings.* with X = E₀^π gives |F_w^A(E₀^π, E₀^π)| ≤ 2d²C_ψC_πx_π(1 + j)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/2}ε₁²σ_k² per interface point. Since j ≤ k < 8n + j(C) and nL^{−n(1−3ε₀)/6} ≤ 6/(e(1 − 3ε₀)ln L), 1 + j ≤ 8n + j(C), and with X_L := 6/(e(1 − 3ε₀)ln L), (1 + j)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/2} ≤ (8X_L + j(C))(ε/a_n)^{1/3}, so, with σ_k² = N_k/(2d²), F_w^A(E₀^π, E₀^π) ≤ C_ππ(ε/a_n)^{1/3}ε₁²N_k per interface point, C_ππ := C_ψC_πx_π(8X_L + j(C)), within the (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} of the other interface pieces.
  - For f^A two further terms are of relative size ε₁. The curvature terms of PBG (3.10), with |F| ≤ 2C₀ε₁/a_j² at Ũ, add at most 2(1 + d²)C₀ε₁ times these bounds. The constraint term is ⟨C_A^{(2)}[X, Y], ν^B⟩ = ⟨H^AC_A^{(2)}[X, Y], J′⟩, by J′ = Q_db(Ũ)*ν^B, with |C_A^{(2)}[X, Y]| ≤ C₂a_{j′}|X||Y| at a point of the layer j′ (VAR (44) at the block scale), H^A bounded by (3.133) as for 𝒜 above, and a_j³|J′| ≤ B̄_Jε₁. With the row sums c_K it adds at most 4dB₀c_KC₂B̄_Jε₁ times these bounds. So the bounds hold for f^A with the factor 1 + c_bfε₁, c_bf := 2(1 + d²)C₀ + 4dB₀c_KC₂B̄_J; the name c_bf keeps it apart from the Cauchy note's c_f.
- *The complementary-weight parts.* The parts −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩ of the form of (1.2) and −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ)⟩ of the small form of (1.20) need no comparison of their own. The form of (1.2) enters the main proof only through the denominator d of (1.1), and the small form of (1.20) only through the sum 𝐂_k of LFR II p.369 l.2–7. Both are compared below through the weighted actions of (b) and (c), with no use of 𝒜₂, E₂, or the remainders V(ζ₀, ·) and V(ζ, ·), so bounds of 𝒜₂ and E₂ are not needed. The comparison of d and of 𝐂_k as a whole is derived. LFR II then localises 𝐂_k and re-sums its pieces into V(X^∼) and V(Y) (p.373 l.10–17, (1.59)–(1.64), (1.68)), and the comparison of these pieces, *The Localised Pieces of 𝐂_k* below, is derived, not reviewed. The comparisons are at real configurations and at equal shared variables and couplings, as in (b)–(d), except in the item on the couplings.
  - *The denominator.* By (1.1) (p.356 l.19–20), d = ∫δ_{G₀}(V′)χ exp[−g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ))] dV′|_Λ, where Bałaban's V_Λ carries on Λ the data of U_0 (LFR I (1.79), p.195). So V′ is the deviation from this data, V′ = 1 gives U_0, and the cutoff χ̄, the soft form of χ(|(1/i)log V′(b)| < M₀ε_k) (LFR I (1.101), p.201; §3(d)), is centred there. In the notation of (c), run X's integrand at V′ is exp[−g_k^{−2}A_X(ζ₀, U^X_{k,Z}(V′V′^X₀V_Λ))], with V_Λ the shared data off Λ. At equal V′ the two runs' data on Λ^{(k)} differs by e^{iX}, since V′V′^B₀ = V′e^{iX}V′^A₀ and the norm is bi-invariant. On the support of χ̄, |V′ − 1| < (1 + κ_s)M₀ε_k. With C(M) ≥ 8(1 + κ_s)max(M₀, C_X(M)), V′ and e^{itX} each move the data by at most ε₁/8 per bond, so the data V′e^{itX}V′^A₀V_Λ, 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, is regular with 2ε₁, as in (c), "The comparison". At each V′ of the support the two runs' exponents therefore differ by at most the sum of two bounds.
    - The equal-data difference of g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(·)) at the data V′V′^A₀V_Λ. As for A(ζ₀, U_0) in (c), "The propagation", (b) bounds it by Σ_xC_w(ε₀)g_k^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{1−ε₀}ε₁² over the points x of the layers of 𝐁_k(Z). The weight has a_{j(x)}|∇ζ₀| ≤ C, since |∇ζ₀| ≤ C/a_k (*The weighted defect.*) and a_{j(x)} ≤ a_k.
    - The change of run B's g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(·)) from the data V′V′^A₀V_Λ to V′V′^B₀V_Λ. (c), "The propagation" and "The result", bounds it by C_A(M)g_k^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁² per bond of Λ^{(k)}. That bound uses only that each data point contributes ≤ Cε₁|δD| at first order and ≤ C|δD|² at second order at regular data, with |δD| ≤ CΔ, so it holds at every V′ of the support and not only at V′ = 1.

    The Haar measure, δ_{G₀}, and χ̄ are the same in both runs at equal couplings, and the integrands are positive. So the Lemma of the Cauchy note's §8.6 (real backgrounds) gives |log d^B − log d^A| ≤ φ_d, with φ_d the sum of the two bounds. By the results of (b) and (c), φ_d is ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of the layers of 𝐁_k(Z) plus C_hθ̃^n per bond of Λ^{(k)}. The decomposition (1.10), the bulk form f, the part −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩, the linear term of (1.2), and V(ζ₀, ·) enter d only through the action A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ)), so none of them is compared separately. On the complex class the complex version of the Lemma uses the lower bound of |d| by e^{−c(M)} times the integral of the modulus of its integrand, with e^{−c(M)} := exp(−O(1)|Λ|) = exp(−O(M⁴)) (p.358 l.24–30 with (1.10)–(1.11)). For a pointwise bound φ of the two runs' difference of the exponents it needs (e^φ − 1)e^{c(M)} ≤ ½, and it gives |log d^B − log d^A| ≤ 2(e^φ − 1)e^{c(M)} ≤ 4e^{c(M)}φ. The value D(1) at V′ = 1 of that difference does not depend on V′ and factors out of both integrals exactly, so φ is only the oscillation φ′_d := sup_{V′}|D(V′) − D(1)| over the support of χ̄. The data at V′ differs from that at V′ = 1 on Λ only, so by the kernel form of VAR Proposition 4 in (b) (rate ⅛δ₀) both runs' changes of U_{k,Z}, and their run difference, decay from Λ, and φ′_d ≤ C_d(M)C_hθ̃^n, with C_d(M) a constant of M that counts the points of the layers within the decay length of Λ, |Λ| = O(M⁴). The second bound above is already on the bonds of Λ^{(k)}. So (e^{φ′_d} − 1)e^{c(M)} ≤ ½ for n ≥ n_d(M), and then |log d^B − log d^A − D(1)| ≤ 4e^{c(M)}C_d(M)C_hθ̃^n, with |D(1)| ≤ φ_d. For n < n_d(M), each run's log d is bounded by Bałaban's bounds, and the factor θ̃^{−n_d} is absorbed in C_h. This uses the bounds of (b) and (c) on the class, which *The complex class.* derives under (T-c).
  - *The sum 𝐂_k.* LFR II collects the small terms of all the expansions of the Wilson actions, together with I(U_0) of (1.10), into 𝐂_k (p.369 l.2–7). 𝐂_k enters (1.49) as one term, and the expressions V(Y) of (1.68)–(1.69) through the re-summation of p.377 l.2–9. Among these small terms are the linear term of (1.20), the small form of (1.20) with its part −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ)⟩, V(ζ, ·), the last three terms of (1.24), the pieces of (1.26)–(1.29), and A(ζ₁, U_0) of (1.31) (p.361 l.10–28, p.362 l.29–40, p.363 l.11–45, p.364 l.1–16). Since (1.49) is an equality for (1.1), 𝐂_k is given exactly by (1.37) (p.365 l.43–50, with the second term of its right-hand side included in O(1) by p.366 l.12), the localisation of p.367 l.50–p.368 l.4 with (1.48) (p.368 l.32–36), and (1.10). In the notation of (c), with (g″)^{−2} := 1/(g″_k(·))², and with the weights split as ζ₀ + (1 − ζ₀),

    𝐂_k = W_ext + g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_k) − A((g″)^{−2}ζ₀, U″_k) − g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(V′_kV′₀V_Λ)) + A((g″)^{−2}ζ₁, U″_{h+2}(exp ig_kB, V″)) + ½Σ_{p∈T_η}η⁴ζ(x(p))|(DH″_{1,k,Z}B)(p)|² − log d + E_k(Λ),

    W_ext := A(w_e, U_k) − A(w_e, U″_k), w_e := (g″)^{−2}(1 − ζ₀).

    Here (g_k(·))^{−2} is g_k^{−2} on Z and (g″)^{−2} off Z (p.365 l.19), and the support of ζ₀ lies in Z (ζ_i ∈ C₀^∞(X_i), LFR I p.201 l.28, as p.365 l.33–40 uses it), so (g_k(·))^{−2}ζ₀ = g_k^{−2}ζ₀. On Z, (g″)^{−2} − g_k^{−2} is localised in Λ (p.359 l.36–39), and the support of 1 − ζ₀ is localised away from Λ, at a distance larger than 8MR_k from the support of B (p.363 l.43–44; the distance dist(Ω_k, Λ) of (1.6), p.357 l.33–38), so (g_k(·))^{−2}(1 − ζ₀) = w_e. Bałaban uses this identity himself: he combines the weights ((g″)^{−2} − g_k^{−2})(Z_k ∖ Z) and g_k^{−2}(1 − ζ₀) into (g″)^{−2}(1 − ζ₀) (p.364 l.36–45) and expands the result around U_k with the weight (g_k(·))^{−2}(1 − ζ₀) (p.365 l.29–34). The A(ζ₀, U_0) of (1.37) cancels against that of the denominator.
    - The part 𝐂_k − W_ext. At equal couplings E_k(Λ) is the same in both runs. Each of the four actions is a sum over the plaquettes in the support of its weight, which lies in Z, and its weight is of the kind treated in (b): g_k^{−2}ζ₀ as for d above, and (g″)^{−2}ζ₀ and (g″)^{−2}ζ₁ as the weight ζ₁/(g″)² of (1.70). The configurations are VAR minimisers with multiscale data. The shared variable is V_k, as in *The lines.*; then V′_k = V_k(V^X_Λ)^{−1} (p.378 l.12) depends on the run. U_k and U_{k,Z}(V′_kV′₀V_Λ) carry on Λ the data V′_kV′^X₀ (p.365 l.10–13), compared at equal V_k and regular as for d above, since χ̄ restricts V′_k in (1.1). U″_k carries the data exp(ig_kB)M^·(U^{(AL)}_0) of (1.50), and U″_{h+2}(exp ig_kB, V″) carries exp(ig_kB) and V″ only; both are regular as for the action of (1.70). So each action differs between the runs by ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of the layers of its configuration in Z, by (b), plus ≤ C_hθ̃^n per data point of Λ, by (c). The form differs by ≤ ½r̄_nN_k ≤ ½C_hθ̃^n per point of 𝐁₀, by *The result for the forms.*, and log d by ≤ φ_d. Hence the two runs' values of 𝐂_k − W_ext differ by ≤ 11dC_hθ̃^n per point of the component's history: a point of a coarser layer of Z, a point of 𝐁₀, a data point, and a bond of Λ^{(k)} are each counted at a point of the history that contains it, and the factor d covers the bonds at a point. This uses the Cauchy note's (10.6c) through the form, the form's interface pieces, the point (6) of (LF-A), and *The complex class.* with (T-c) on the class, derived, not reviewed. It is derived at real configurations, and on the class by *The complex class.*, under (T-c).
    - *The shell of χ̄(Λ)(V′_k) (derived).* The first factor χ̄(Λ)(V′_k) of (1.1) depends on the run through V^X_Λ. It stands on the components of the second class only (p.378 l.10–16), and it is soft (§3(d), LFR I (1.101)).
      - At equal V_k the two runs' arguments f^X_b := |(1/i)log V′^X_k(b)| differ by at most the data difference s_n := C_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ per bond of (c), by the triangle inequality of the bi-invariant distance, since V′^B_k = V′^A_k·V^A_Λ(V^B_Λ)^{−1}.
      - The thresholds are t^X := M₀ε^X_k, with M₀ the same in both runs (§3(d), p.378 l.10–14) and |t^A − t^B| ≤ Ct^Ag_k²|δ_k| ≤ ½t^A (§2(b)).
      - Everywhere, χ̄(f/t) is Lipschitz in f with the constant 1/(κ_st), and |∂_tχ̄(f/t)| ≤ (1 + κ_s)/(κ_st), since the derivative vanishes where f ≥ (1 + κ_s)t. So per bond |χ̄(f^A_b/t^A) − χ̄(f^B_b/t^B)| ≤ δ^χ_n + δ^χ_c, with δ^χ_n := s_n/(κ_st^A) the data part and δ^χ_c := (1 + κ_s)|t^A − t^B|/(κ_s min(t^A, t^B)) ≤ 4Cg_k²|δ_k|/κ_s the coupling part.
      - The factor enters the split of §2(b) with the other tests of Π_X (§3(d), *The shell bound.*). Since (Π_A − Π_B)₊ ≤ 1{Π_A > 0}|Π_A − Π_B|, with every factor in [0, 1], and 1{Π_A > 0} is the product of run A's sharp tests at the thresholds (1 + κ_s)t^A for the passed tests, t^A for the failed ones, and the cascade thresholds of §3(d) (*The shell bound.*), telescoping over the bonds gives (Π_A − Π_B)₊ρ_A ≤ |Λ^{(k)}|(δ^χ_n + δ^χ_c) times run A's own term with these sharp tests, which (R-s) covers (§3(d)), and the same with A and B exchanged. So the shell is bounded as the shells of §3(d): by δ^χ_n + δ^χ_c per bond of Λ^{(k)}, times a run's own term with Bałaban's bounds. The coupling part is carried with |δ_k|, as in (b).
      - δ^χ_n ≤ C_χ(M)θ̃^n. Here ε₁/ε_k = C(M)R_k, with R_k ≤ L(log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)), log g_k^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) (BETA_FUNCTION_WILSON_LOOPS.md Lemma 2.1(i)), and K − k < 8n + j(C) (*The Ratio.* in (d)), so R_k is at most a power of log(ḡ^{−2} + 8bn + bj(C) + C). And (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} = L^{−(1−ε₀)n/4}L^{−(1−ε₀)n/12} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−ε₀)n/12}, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4} ((c), the Cauchy note's §8.7). The slack L^{−(1−ε₀)n/12} absorbs R_k, and also the factor (k_s − k + 1)^{1/2} of ε₁/ε_k when §1's ε₁ is the larger one. So C_χ(M) depends on M, j(C), ḡ, L, ε₀, r, b, κ_s, and Bałaban's constants, and not on k, K, ε, or the volume.
    - *The linear term of (1.20).* g_k⟨DH″_{1,k,Z}B, ζη^{−2}Im∂U^0_{k,Z}⟩ is not compared term by term in this section. It enters only through 𝐂_k, so its run difference is inside the bound of 𝐂_k − W_ext above (derived), and a bound of its own, of the type of C_ℓ(M), is not needed. The same holds for the small form of (1.20) and V(ζ, ·). So, of *The result for the forms.*, the main proof uses only the bound of (1.21), which (1.49) and (1.70) keep: the bulk form f, the small form of (1.20), and the linear term of (1.2) lie inside d or 𝐂_k, and so do the remainders V(ζ₀, ·) and V(ζ, ·).
    - The couplings. Their differences enter 𝐂_k − W_ext in four ways. Through the weights they enter as the coupling enters the action in the Cauchy note's §8.4, ≤ Cε₁²(|δ_{j(x)}| + |δ_k|) per point. Through the data exp(ig_kB) at fixed B they enter by ≤ C(1 + β₀)²(k − j + 1)ε₁δ′_k|δ_k| per data point of the scale j, by (c), "The propagation", since |g^A_k − g^B_k||B| ≤ g_k²|δ_k|δ′_k on the support of χ′ and g_k²/g_j² ≤ (1 + β₀)²(k − j + 1) by CRE (2.6). Through log d and E_k(Λ) they enter as in the coupling items of the Cauchy note's §8.6. The form ½⟨DH″B, ζDH″B⟩ depends on them through its background U^0_{k,Z}, whose data contain exp(ig_kB); this is a data derivative of the same kind as the second, and it is bounded in the same way, per data point of the scale j.
    - *The Localised Pieces of 𝐂_k (derived, not reviewed).* LFR II localises 𝐂_k with the old terms: the terms of the first part of (1.49), except the first two, are treated by (1.59) or (1.60) and the decoupling of σ₀, then by the representation (1.61)–(1.64) (p.373 l.10–17), and re-summed into V(X^∼) and V(Y) ((1.68), pp.376–377). So the comparison of each piece is that of *The functions of the configurations.* with the localised terms 𝐂(X) of 𝐂_k (p.367 l.50–p.368 l.4, (1.48)) in place of 𝐓(X), in three parts. (i) Their own run differences at a common configuration: the action pieces are sums over the plaquettes of X, bounded per point by (b) and (c) as in *The part 𝐂_k − W_ext.*, and the piece of −log d is the single term I(U₀) of (1.10), with |I(U₀)| < O(1)log M|Λ| on the analytically extended class ((1.11), p.358), which 𝐂_k contains ("including the term I(U₀) from the denominator", p.369 l.7) and which is not a polymer expansion. It is localised as the other terms are, through the representation of its background (1.61)–(1.64), so its pieces are I at the representation configurations, which lie in the class (*(1.65) at complex decoupling parameters*). Its run difference at a common configuration is that of *The denominator.* above for log d (the complex version of the Lemma of the Cauchy note's §8.6, with the lower bound e^{−c(M)} of (1.10)–(1.11)), which holds at every configuration of the class under (T-c) (derived, not reviewed). (ii) The variation along the joint line of (ii) of that item, term by term with Bałaban's class bounds of 𝐂(X); these terms are of the generation k, so no age factor and no generation sum enter. (iii) The decoupled propagators, which need (W-δ). The fine side and the current of the joint line, the inputs of (ii), are derived, not reviewed (*The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.*, *The Current at the Multiscale Chain.*). (iii) is the relative walk-term bound (W-δ) for the decoupled propagators of the pieces, applied as in *The functions of the configurations.* (the (W-δ) note, derived, not reviewed).
    - *The exterior part W_ext (derived).* U_k and U″_k have the same determining set and data off Λ (p.370 l.7–9), so W_ext is one weighted action at two configurations whose determining sets and data differ only on Λ (by (1.33)). Each of its two actions is a sum over the whole lattice, so (b) and (c) alone bound its run difference only by a sum over all points. The data on Λ is not close either. On the deep part Λ_1 := Λ ∖ Λ_0, Λ_0 := Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} (LFR I p.195 l.27–29), U″_k carries the large-field data V″ of (1.50), and U_k carries the averages of a configuration that is small inside Λ. So no short segment of data joins the two. The comparison below localises each run's change near Λ through Bałaban's expansion of U″_k around U_k (p.370 l.10–p.371 l.49), sizes it by the per-block depth of (b), and combines the two bounds by min(u, v) ≤ u^{1/3}v^{2/3}. The decay budget is the following: the chain's fields decay at the rate δ₀/16 of the weight of (c), *The weighted bound.*; the power v^{2/3} keeps the rate δ₀/24; and the whole rate δ₀/24 goes into the count (2.58) at α = 1/24, which needs (2.59) at α = 1/24. It is at equal couplings, except in *The couplings of W_ext.* below, and in the common gauge of (c), "The gauge of the data", with U_0 in the AL-gauge from the beginning (LFR I p.197 l.42). Here ε₁ is the larger of the constants of §1 and (c), ε̂ := ε₁ + δ′_k, and w̄ := sup|w_e| ≤ g_k^{−2} + O(1)k (p.364 l.35–36). A point of Λ is a block of a layer of 𝐁″_k ∩ Λ or a bond of Λ^{(k)}; it is a point of the component's history at a layer ≥ n. A point of the history carries at most one block and d bonds, so a bound per point of Λ is at most d + 1 times the same bound per point of the history, a factor absorbed in C_h.
      - *The weight.* The weighted blocking artifact and the current of A(w_e, ·) need a_{j(x)}|∇w_e(x)| ≤ Cw̄, as in *The weighted defect.* For ζ₀ this is the choice made there, with a_{j(x)} ≤ a_k. At a point x of the layer m, (g″)^{−2}(x) − g_k^{−2} = β_m(1 − φ_m(x)) + Σ_{m<l≤k}β_l, as in the coupling-term item of (e), *The step map.*, with the layers of the sequence of domains of g″_k(·), whose bound O(1)(k − j) is p.364 l.35–36. The form of g″_k(·) through the functions φ_m is that of CRE (2.24). Here φ_m ∈ C_0^∞(Λ_m), φ_m = 1 on Λ_m^{∼−1}, as in CRE (2.24) (p.259 l.17–21). So only φ_m varies at x. Bałaban allows any such φ_m. The collar Λ_m ∖ Λ_m^{∼−1} is one layer of the cubes of which Λ_m is a union (CRE p.245 l.40–43), so its width is ≥ a_m, and φ_m is chosen, as ζ₀ and ζ₁ are, as a fixed smooth function with |∇φ_m| ≤ C/a_m, the same in both runs. F_K does not depend on the choice. By (2.24), −A(g_k^{−2}(·), U) − Σ_{j≤k}β_j(g_{j−1})A(φ_j, U) = −g_0^{−2}A(U) for every U, so φ_j only moves terms between the Wilson term of (2.23) and the counterterms of (2.25) (CRE p.253 l.25–37, p.258 l.49–51, p.259 l.13–27).
      - *The chain.* The domains of (1.50)–(1.55) are taken within Z, the union of the class of components (LFR I p.177 l.33, (1.1)); this is Bałaban's convention (R-Z), which three printed statements force. There (Ω″_{h+1})^c, (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c, and (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c stand for their intersections with Z, so U_2 and θ live in Z, and (1.53) puts U_1 on Ω″^∼_{h+1} ∪ Z^c and U_2 on (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Z. The same holds for the domains of the join in (1.52): Ω″^∼_{h+1} stands for Ω″^∼_{h+1} ∪ Z^c in 𝐁₁ and its complement for (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Z in 𝐁₂. It is Bałaban's convention for the section: "For simplicity we denote intersections of the regions Z_j with Z by Z_j also … We write the intersections explicitly only if it may lead to a misunderstanding" (LFR I p.177 l.33–35), and the operation 𝕋_k(Z_k ∩ Z^c), which the section does not change, is omitted from the formulas ((1.1), l.41–42). Three printed statements force it. By LFR I (1.12) (p.179 l.35) the literal (Ω″_{h+1})^c is Z″_{h+1} ∪ (Ω^c_{h+1} ∩ Z^c), and its enlargements' complements reach Z^c where Ω^c_{h+1} ∩ Z^c is deeper than one or two of their layers. So (1) and (3) fail in the literal reading whenever (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c ∩ Z^c ≠ ∅, and (2) whenever (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Z^c ≠ ∅. Such terms occur: the section does not restrict 𝕋_k(Z_k ∩ Z^c), and the components outside Z may be old and large. (1) U^0_k "considered on Z^c depends weakly on the field V″⌈(Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c" (p.370 l.14–15), while on Z^c ⊂ Λ^c the field V″ is itself an argument of (1.50). (2) U_{1,2} "restricted to Z^c is equal to U_1" (p.371 l.26–27), while (1.53) puts U_2 on (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c. (3) Λ ⊃ Z″_k ⊃ ⋯ ⊃ Z″_{h+1} ⊃ (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c (LFR I p.195 l.42), while Λ = (Ω^{∼4}_{k₀+1})^c ∩ Z ⊂ Z (LFR I (1.73), p.192 l.43), and already Z″_{h+1} ⊂ Z by (1.11). The convention leaves 𝐁″_k, U^0_k, U″_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_k unchanged everywhere, since in (1.50) the literal overlap of Λ^c and (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c carries the same V″, so the data is the same with and without the convention. It changes the auxiliary objects 𝐁₁, 𝐁₂, U_1, U_2, U_{1,2}, and θ of (1.52)–(1.55) in Z^c. Off Z″_{h+1}, which contains Λ^c, U_1 carries the data V″⌈Λ^c of U^0_k and 𝐁₁ coincides with 𝐁″_k, since the boundary layer of the join (2.14) [III] (CRE p.257 l.15) lies where θ = 1, in Z″_{h+1} ⊂ Λ (p.371 l.2–4). So on supp w_e, U_{1,2} = U_1: on Z^c by (2), and in Z on Ω″^∼_{h+1} by (1.53) (p.370 l.26–33), whose complement in Z lies in (Ω″_{h+1})^c ∩ Z = Z″_{h+1} ⊂ Λ (LFR I p.179 l.35, p.195 l.42). The support of 1 − ζ₀ in Z is localised away from Λ, at the distance dist(Ω_k, Λ) of (1.6) (*The sum 𝐂_k.*), and Bałaban places it at a distance larger than 8MR_k from the support of B (p.363 l.43–44). So on supp w_e the four formulas (1.51), (1.54), (1.57), and (1.58) give U″_k from U_k in four links, each of the form U′ = (exp iη𝐇(Ψ)U)^{u^{−1}} (p.371 l.48–49). The arguments are the following.
        - (1.51): g_kB on 𝐁₀ ⊂ Λ, with |g_kB| < δ′_k by χ′ (p.360 l.41–43).
        - (1.54): 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2}, written by (1.55) (p.371 l.20–23) with the sources θJ_{1,2} and Q*_{𝐁₁}θ₁H*_{𝐁₁}J_1, which lie in Z″_{h+2} ⊂ Λ. Here θ ∈ C_0^∞((Ω″_{h+1})^c) (p.371 l.3), so by (R-Z) supp θ ⊂ (Ω″_{h+1})^c ∩ Z = Z″_{h+1} and θ = 0 on Z^c. θ₁, which is 1 where θ ≠ 0, 1 and decreases to 0 on a neighbourhood of that set (p.371 l.24), is chosen with support in Z″_{h+1} as well; Bałaban's conditions fix θ₁ only on that set and its neighbourhood, so this is an admissible choice. For (1.55) to be an identity, θ₁ = 1 on the block bonds c whose blocks B(c_±) meet {0 < θ < 1}, so the neighbourhood is at least one block of the scale ≤ h wide. It fits in Z″_{h+1}: θ is taken with its transition a few h-blocks inside (Ω″_{h+1})^c, which the strip between the layer of width 2M₁ and ∂Ω″_{h+1}, of width (LMR_{h+1} − 2M₁)L^{−(k−h)}, allows (M₁ < M, CRE p.245 l.35–37). The blocks of 𝐁₁ that meet Z″_{h+1} are of the scales ≤ h, by LFR I (1.13) and (2.14) [III], and Q* sends the datum of a block bond c into the two blocks B(c_−) and B(c_+) that it joins (CMP 98 (14), p.19 l.38–42); the multiscale Q_{𝐁₁} is the composition of these one-step averages, whose contours nest inside the two blocks. So Q*_{𝐁₁}θ₁H*_{𝐁₁}J_1 lies within 2√dL^{−(k−h)} of Z″_{h+1}. For h + 2 ≤ k − N₀, which holds since N ≫ N₀, the sets are (Ω^{∼5}_j)^c ∩ Z by (1.11), and since {Ω″_j} is an admissible sequence (LFR I p.179 l.40), Z″_{h+1} and Z″^c_{h+2} are separated by more than RM in the units of the scale h + 1 (PRT2 (2.2), p.224 l.9–14), the pattern of the widths of p.179 l.19–24. This exceeds 2√dL^{−(k−h)}, since 2√d < LRM for M large (CRE p.245 l.34–37). So this source lies in Z″_{h+2}, and Z″_{h+2} ⊂ Λ by p.195 l.42. The currents obey a_{j(x)}³|J_{1,2}(x)| ≤ 4(d − 1)C₀ε₁, since U_{1,2} is regular at its layers (p.370 l.35–38) and |J| ≤ 2(d − 1)|∇F| as in the current term above. The second argument is H″_{1,k}Q̃(η𝐀₀) on Z″_k ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, with |𝐀₀| < O(1)B₃²B₅M⁶ε_k by LFR I (1.87) (p.197 l.33–35).
        - (1.57): −(1/i)log M^·(U_{k,Λ}) on Z″_k, bounded by LFR I (1.84) (p.371 l.32–33).
        - (1.58): −(1/i)log V′_k on Λ, < (1 + κ_s)M₀ε_k on the support of the soft χ̄(Λ) (LFR I (1.101)).

        With C(M) ≥ O(1)B₃²B₅M⁶ + M₀, every argument is ≤ C_Ψε̂ in the dimensionless norm of its layer. All of them lie in Λ except 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2}, whose sources lie in Λ and which decays from it: 𝐆″_k is the operator 𝔊 of PBG (3.148) for 𝐁″_k (p.370 l.48–49), whose kernel has the bounds (3.42) with the decay e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)} by Theorem 3.13 (PBG p.426 l.29–32, p.397 l.37–p.398 l.25), so |𝐆″_kJ_{1,2}| ≤ C_Ψε̂e^{−δ₀d(·,Λ)}, faster than the weight e^{(δ₀/16)d(·,Λ)} below. Here C_Ψ is a constant of Bałaban's constants, for 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2} through the bounds and the localisation of the kernel of 𝐆″_k, which Bałaban uses for (1.55) (p.370 l.46–49, p.371 l.24–25). Also ε̂ ≤ 2ε₁, since O(1)δ′_k < ε_k with δ′_k = g_kA₁p₁(g_k) (LFR I p.183 l.2, p.184 l.18). Each 𝐇 is the function of VAR Sects. D, E for its determining set (p.370 l.38–39), analytic with the derivative bounds VAR (190) (p.308 l.37–47), which Bałaban uses for it (p.360 l.20–22). Its defining contraction closes in the weighted norm with the weight e^{(δ₀/16)d(·,Λ)}, by the kernel form of VAR Proposition 4 of (b), as in (c), *The weighted bound.* So a_{j(x)}|𝐇(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐇(x)| ≤ C_𝐇ε̂e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} at x ∈ Δ(y), with C_𝐇 a constant of M and Bałaban's constants, given by C_Ψ and the constants of the bounds of 𝔊 and ℋ in (c), *The weighted bound.* (C_𝐇 is not the C_ℋ of (c)). The configurations of the chain lie in the regular class ((1.65), p.375 l.17–35), and the gauge transformations u leave the plaquette actions unchanged.
      - *Run B's chain at run A's data.* The four formulas are identities of each run's minimisation maps. They use U_0 only through its averages and the bounds (1.84) and (1.87). So they hold, with the same bounds, for run B's maps at the data built from run A's U^A_0. Let U^X_k[Y] be run X's U_{𝐁_k} at the data V′_kV_Λ, with V_Λ = M^k(U^Y_0) on Λ^{(k)}. Let U″^X_k[Y] be run X's U_{𝐁″_k} at the data exp(ig_kB)M^·(U^Y_0) on Λ_0 and the shared data elsewhere. Let W^X[Y] be the corresponding exterior part. Then W^B_ext − W^A_ext = (W^B[B] − W^B[A]) + (W^B[A] − W^A[A]).
      - *The data part W^B[B] − W^B[A].* In both actions the two runs' data differ only on Λ. For U_k the difference is e^{iX} on Λ^{(k)}, with ‖X‖_∞ ≤ Δ ≤ C_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ by (c). For U″_k it is the data difference of (c), ≤ C′_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ per data point of Λ_0. On Λ_1 both carry V″. Along the segments of (c), "The comparison", the data stays regular with 2ε₁. The data derivative of run B's A(w_e, ·) at a unit datum e_z is ⟨H^B_1e_z, J^B(w_e)⟩. By *The weight.*, |J^B(w_e)| ≤ 2c_ww̄ε₁a_j^{−3} on the segments, as for J^A(ζ₀) in the current term, with the factor 2 for the regularity 2ε₁. The lift obeys a_j|H^B_1e_z| ≤ 2B₀K(y, z) on Δ(y), as for 𝒜_z in the linear term of (1.2), and the column sums of K are ≤ c_K. So the derivative is ≤ 4dB₀c_Kc_ww̄ε₁ per unit datum, and the fundamental theorem of calculus along the segments gives |W^B[B] − W^B[A]| ≤ 4dB₀c_Kc_w(C_Δ(M) + C′_Δ(M))w̄ε₁²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} per point of Λ.
      - *The equal-data part W^B[A] − W^A[A].* It is Σ_yω_y over the blocks y of the layers that meet supp w_e. Here ω_y := [a^B_y(U^B_k[A]) − a^A_y(U^A_k[A])] − [a^B_y(U″^B_k[A]) − a^A_y(U″^A_k[A])], with a^X_y run X's weighted plaquette action of the block (run B's through the weights w_{p,p′} of §9.3). It has two bounds.
        - (u) Each bracket is the per-block run difference at equal data of (b): b_{w_e}(U^B) at y plus a_y(w_e, MU^B) − a_y(w_e, U^A). The block density has first derivative ≤ Cw̄ε₁ per unit dimensionless field, so with the profile ρ̄(y) of (b) and the weighted blocking artifact of *The weighted defect.*, |ω_y| ≤ u_y := C_ew̄(ε/a_{j(y)})^{1−ε₀}ε₁², with C_e := C(2C_G(ε₀)c″_K + C_b + C_ζ).
