        - (v) Grouped by runs instead, |ω_y| ≤ Σ_X|a^X_y(U^X_k[A]) − a^X_y(U″^X_k[A])|. Each link of the chain changes the block density by ≤ C_Lw̄(2ε₁ + |𝐇|)|𝐇|, as in (c), "The comparison". With two runs and four links, |ω_y| ≤ v_y := 8C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇w̄ε̂²e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)}.

        Hence |ω_y| ≤ u_y^{1/3}v_y^{2/3} ≤ C′w̄ε̂²(ε/a_{j(y)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}Σ_{z∈Λ}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)}, with C′ := C_e^{1/3}(8C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇)^{2/3}, since ε₁ ≤ ε̂ and e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,Λ)} ≤ Σ_{z∈Λ}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)}. For a point z of Λ, the count (2.58) at α = 1/24 bounds Σ_y(ε/a_{j(y)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)} by c‴(ε/a_{j(z)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}, with c‴ := 6c₀^d(1/48)(14 + 13L^{1/3}). This uses Bałaban's condition (2.59) at α = 1/24, (1/96)δ₀RM > 2d log c₀(1/48) + 1, and ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1 of (b), which gives (1/96)δ₀RM ≥ (1/3)log L + 1/12. By (2.58) a layer r steps from the layer of z contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48)e^{−(1/96)δ₀RM(r−1)_+} times the ratio of the factors (ε/a_j)^{(1−ε₀)/3}.
        - The layer of z contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48).
        - A layer m steps finer carries the ratio ≤ L^{m/3} and contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48)L^{m/3}e^{−((1/3)log L + 1/12)(m−1)} = 6c₀^d(1/48)L^{1/3}e^{−(m−1)/12}. This pays for the layers of other components older than n.
        - A layer m steps coarser contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48)e^{−(m−1)/12}.
        - Σ_{m≥1}e^{−(m−1)/12} = (1 − e^{−1/12})^{−1} < 13.

        Since j(z) ≥ n, the equal-data part is ≤ c‴C′w̄ε̂²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} per point of Λ.
      - *The result.* At equal couplings, |W^B_ext − W^A_ext| ≤ C_E(M)w̄ε̂²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} per point of Λ, with C_E(M) := 4dB₀c_Kc_w(C_Δ(M) + C′_Δ(M)) + c‴C′. Here w̄ ≤ (1 + O(1)k)g_k^{−2} and ε̂ ≤ 2ε₁. If (c)'s constant is the larger, ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k and w̄ε̂² ≤ 4(1 + O(1)k)C(M)²R_k²p₀(g_k)², by ε_k = g_kp₀(g_k) (CRE (2.4), p.255 l.33). If §1's constant is the larger, ε₁/ε_k is at most C(1 + β₀)(k_s − k + 1)^{1/2}(1 + A₁/A₀) by CRE (2.6), and k_s − k < 7n, so ε₁² gains at most a factor linear in n. As in the result of (c), this is a power of log(k + j(C) + ḡ^{−2}) times a polynomial in n (k < 8n + j(C), the Cauchy note's §8.6), independent of K, and L^{−(1−ε₀)n/3} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−ε₀)n/12} absorbs it. So the change is ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of Λ, with C_h a constant of M, ε₀, ḡ, j(C), and Bałaban's constants, taken at least as large as C_E(M) requires. The points of Λ are points of the component's history, so this is the form (LF-2) of the Cauchy note's §8.6. With *The part 𝐂_k − W_ext.*, the two runs' values of 𝐂_k differ by ≤ (11d + 1)C_hθ̃^n per point of the component's history. The bound uses neither 𝒜₂ nor a remainder. It is derived at real configurations, and on the class in the last item.
      - *The couplings of W_ext.* With the two runs' couplings, W^B_ext − W^A_ext = [A(w^B_e − w^A_e, U^B_k) − A(w^B_e − w^A_e, U″^B_k)] + [W^B_ext(w^A_e) − W^A_ext(w^A_e)], with W^B_ext(w^A_e) run B's exterior part with run A's weight. The second bracket is the comparison above, except that run B's data contains exp(ig^B_kB) on 𝐁₀ ⊂ Λ_0. That is a data difference of ≤ g_k²|δ_k|δ′_k per bond, as in *The couplings.* above, and the data part bounds it by 4dB₀c_Kc_ww̄g_k²ε₁δ′_k|δ_k| per point of Λ, with w̄g_k² ≤ 1 + O(1)kg_k² (p.364 l.35–36). This is of the form of the data term of *The couplings.* The first bracket keeps the weight difference under the sum over the blocks.
        - At a block y of the layer m, w^B_e − w^A_e = (φ_mδ_m + (1 − φ_m)δ_{m−1})(1 − ζ₀) by *The weight.*, with δ_m the two runs' difference of g_m^{−2} of the Cauchy note's §8.3. So |w^B_e − w^A_e| ≤ ω_m := |δ_m| + |δ_{m−1}| and a_m|∇(w^B_e − w^A_e)| ≤ Cω_m on Δ(y), with the C of *The weight.* So (v), for one run and with ω_m in place of w̄, bounds the block's part by 4C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇ω_mε̂²e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)}.
        - With e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} ≤ Σ_{z∈Λ}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)} and the count (2.58) at α = 1/24 of the equal-data part, a layer r steps from the layer of z contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48)q^{(r−1)_+} times its ω_m, with q := L^{−1/3}e^{−1/12}.
        - The near layers m ≥ n contribute ≤ 2·6c₀^d(1/48)(1 + 2·13)max_{n−1≤l≤k}|δ_l|, since Σ_{r≥1}q^{r−1} < 13.
        - The far layers m ≤ n − 1 lie r ≥ n − m steps from z, since j(z) ≥ n. There |δ_m| and |δ_{m−1}| are ≤ |δ_{n−1}| + C_βΣ_{i=m}^{n−1}d_i, since δ_{m−1} − δ_{n−1} = Σ_{i=m}^{n−1}Δβ_i and |Δβ_i| ≤ C_βd_i (the Cauchy note's §8.3). With Σ_{m≤i}q^{n−1−m} ≤ 13q^{n−1−i}, they contribute ≤ 2·78c₀^d(1/48)(|δ_{n−1}| + C_βD^♭_{n−1}), with D^♭_l := Σ_{i≤l}q^{l−i}d_i.

        Hence the first bracket is ≤ C_wcε̂²[max_{n−1≤l≤k}|δ_l| + C_βD^♭_{n−1}] per point of Λ, with C_wc := 1920C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇c₀^d(1/48), and C_wcε̂² ≤ 1 for γ small, since ε̂ ≤ 2ε₁. The Cauchy note's recursion accepts this form. Each |δ_l| with n − 1 ≤ l ≤ k is the coupling part of 𝒟_l, and 𝒟_l ≤ e^{½C_P(k−l)}𝒟_{[k]} with k − l + 1 ≤ P_k(Z), as for the exponent differences on a component in the Cauchy note's §8.6. D^♭_{n−1} is D_{n−1} with the rate q in place of L^{−β′}, and D_l ≤ D^♭_l since β′ > 1/3. In the weighted norm of the Cauchy note's §8.7, Σ_{i≤l}q^{l−i}m_i ≤ (1 − L^{−1/3}/θ̃)^{−1}m_l, since q < L^{−1/3} < L^{−(1−ε₀)/4} ≤ θ̃; this is the bound that §8.7 uses for D_l[u], with (1 − L^{−1/3}/θ̃)^{−1} in place of (1 − L^{−β′}/θ̃)^{−1}. A bound of the far layers at the rate L^{−β′} does not work, since the weight's rate δ₀/16 gives only q per layer. The bound is derived at real configurations, and the class item below carries it to the class.
      - *The complex class of W_ext.* The bound holds on the class, through the Lemma (Two Constants) on the lines of *The complex class.*, under (T-c).
        - At the real points of a line the configurations are real, with data regular with ε₁′, and the shared variables B, V″, and V_k are fixed. The chain's arguments move with the line by at most the moves of *The real points.*, which ε′_k contains. So the derivation above holds with ε₁′ for ε₁ and ε̂′ := ε₁′ + δ′_k for ε̂, and the run difference of W_ext is ≤ 𝔪 := C_E(M)w̄ε̂′²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} per point of Λ.
        - On the disc |z| < 25 the line lies in the analyticity domains of VAR Sect. G (*The disc.*). So the four formulas, identities between analytic functions at the real points, hold there. Their arguments are ≤ 26C_Ψε̂′, so the weighted contraction of *The chain.*, which holds at the complex arguments of these domains, gives a_{j(x)}|𝐇(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐇(x)| ≤ 26C_𝐇ε̂′e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} at x ∈ Δ(y). The plaquettes are ≤ 52C₀ε₁′/a_j², the regularity 2ε₁ of (v) with 26ε₁′ for ε₁. So each link changes the block density by ≤ C_Lw̄(52ε₁′ + 26C_𝐇ε̂′)26C_𝐇ε̂′e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} ≤ 26²C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇w̄ε̂′²e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)}, which is (v) for one run at its own data with the factor 26². With e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} ≤ Σ_{z∈Λ}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)} and Σ_ye^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)} ≤ c_tot(1/24) (the count of (b), under (2.59) at α = 1/24), each run's W_ext, over four links, is ≤ ½𝔐 per point of Λ, with 𝔐 := 8·26²C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇c_tot(1/24)w̄ε̂′².
        - The Lemma, applied to the run difference along the line divided by the number of points of Λ, gives at z = i the bound 𝔪^{15/16}𝔐^{1/16} ≤ max(C_E(M), 8·26²C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇c_tot(1/24))w̄ε̂′²(ε/a_n)^{5(1−ε₀)/16} per point of Λ. Since ε̂′ ≤ 2ε₁′, w̄ε̂′² carries the factors of g_{j(x)}^{−2}ε₁′², and "The absorption" of *The results.* gives ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of Λ on the class, since 5/16 exceeds 1/4.
        - The couplings enter as in *The couplings of W_ext.* The first bracket there is linear in the weight difference, and z = i lies in the disc, so the disc bound of the preceding sub-item, with ω_m in place of w̄ and one run, bounds it on the class by 26²C_wcε̂′²[max_{n−1≤l≤k}|δ_l| + C_βD^♭_{n−1}] per point of Λ, with the same count. The data term exp(ig_kB) is bounded by Cauchy's estimate on the data disc of (c), "The remainder".
- *The result for the forms.* With the alignment, per point of 𝐁₀ (for (1.21)) and of Λ^{(k)} (for the bulk form f of (1.2)), the two runs' forms differ by ≤ r̄_nN_k. Here r̄_n := C_F(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} and C_F(M) := C_d(2 + C_ζ) + (1 + c_bf)(2x̂_E + x̂_E²) + c_4c_alε₁, with c_4 := 1 + 2(1 + d²)(C₀ + 2α₁) + 4dB₀c_KC₂α_J. On the alignment's disc the lifts obey (3.133) (PBG Theorems 3.4, p.400, and 3.12), |F| ≤ (2C₀ε₁ + 4α₁)/a_j², and |𝐉| ≤ α_Ja_j^{−3}, so the forms are ≤ c_4N_k per point there. The bound holds since a_j ≥ a_n, ε₁ ≤ 1, and γ_d, 1, and 2(1 − ε₀)/3 all are at least (1 − ε₀)/3. The k-dependent ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k enters C_F(M) only through ε₁ ≤ 1 and the conditions of (T-Λ). The terms in E use the Cauchy note's (10.6c) in its multiscale form. The π-part of β for multiscale data, its diagonal value and its pairings with E₀ and E_z (*The π-part of β for multiscale data.* and its *The π-part in the pairings.*, with the π–π term of F_w^A(E)), and, for the bulk form f of (1.2), the twist term 2⟨B̃₂, ν_0⟩ where Λ meets an interface (*The weighted defect.*) are derived, not reviewed. So the bound is derived at real configurations, and on the class *The complex class.* gives it with max(C_F(M), 2c_4)(ε/a_n)^{5(1−ε₀)/16} in place of r̄_n. The other parts of the exponents of (1.2) and (1.20) are as follows.
  - The small form of (1.20) is the quadratic form of (1.20) less (1.21). The identity of the (1.2)-form bullet holds with the weight ζ, the set 𝐁″_k(Z), and its multiplier λ″: U^0_{k,Z} is critical for the unweighted problem with all of its data, so J_0 = 𝐐″*λ″ = Q″*ν″, with Q″ the multiscale double bar and ν″ = (1 − Ξ)*λ″ by *The interface twist.*. In VAR's gauge Q″𝒜₂ + C″^{(2)}[𝒜₁, 𝒜₁] = 0 everywhere, since the twisted data is a function of the plain data and is fixed with it, so no twist term enters. So the small form is ⟨𝒜₁, Δ′(ζ)𝒜₁⟩ − 2⟨C″^{(2)}[𝒜₁, 𝒜₁], ν″⟩ − 2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ)⟩, with Δ′(ζ) := Δ^{act}(ζ) − D*ζD the curvature terms of PBG (3.10). The first two terms are f with ζ in place of ζ₀, less (1.21). Their defect is β_f − β_w, which consists of the curvature couplings, the curvature piece of the weight-gradient sum, and the current term, all within the per-cell bound above, and their terms in E are the curvature and constraint terms of f^A, bounded with c_bf. So they differ between the runs by ≤ r̄_nN_k per point of 𝐁₀, with the π-part's contribution to E through *The π-part in the pairings.*, its π–π term included (*The π–π term.*, derived, not reviewed). The third term is the part carried by the weight 1 − ζ, as for (1.2). The small form enters the main proof only through 𝐂_k, compared as a whole in *The complementary-weight parts.*, so the third term is not compared separately.
  - The third-order remainders V(ζ, ·) and V(ζ₀, ·) enter the main proof only through 𝐂_k and d, compared as wholes in *The complementary-weight parts.*, so they are not bounded separately.
  - The linear term of (1.2). In each run it equals (1/g_k)⟨𝒜₁, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩, by (1.4)–(1.5). It is linear in B′, and for a unit datum e_z at z ∈ Λ^{(k)} the lift 𝒜_z := H₁(1 + Ξ)e_z has a_j|𝒜_z| + a_j²|∇𝒜_z| ≤ 2B₀K(y, z) on Δ(y), as in the bound of 𝒜 above, with B₀ standing in this bullet for c_zB₀, c_z := 1 + 4de^{(d+1)δ₀}: (1 + Ξ)e_z has at most 2d + 1 nonzero entries, each ≤ 2, within (d + 1)a_j of z, by *The interface twist.*. The alignment moves run A's term to the pair (Ũ, J′). In divergence form (the Cauchy note's §10.3), with the plaquettes of the alignment's disc ≤ 2α₁η²/a_j² and |D𝒜_z| ≤ 6B₀K(y, z)a_j^{−2}, the term is ≤ 6d²c_Wα₁B₀c_K/g_k per unit datum on the disc, so the alignment costs at most c_al(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ times this. At the pair, with H^Ae_z = Q₁𝒜_z − E_z, the difference is (1/g_k)(db_{1−ζ₀}(U^B_0)[𝒜_z] − ⟨E_z, J^A(1 − ζ₀)⟩).
    - The first piece is the Cauchy note's (9.5) at the scale j, with the weight 1 − ζ₀ on each coarse term, plus the first variation −Σ_pε⁴Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(w(p′) − w(p))⟨F_{p′}, (D𝒜_z)(p′)⟩ of the weight-gradient sum. On Δ(y) it is ≤ d²[C_r(ε/a_j) + C₀C_ζ(ε/a_n)]ε₁·2B₀K(y, z).
    - The second piece is ≤ dc_wε₁a_j|E_z| on Δ(y), by |J^A(1 − ζ₀)| ≤ c_wε₁a_j^{−3} as for J^A(ζ₀). The sources of E_z are sized by 𝒜_z, so a_j|E_z − E_z^π| ≤ (C_G^Q(ε₀)C_d + 12d²L²C_U(M))(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}Σ_{y′}K(y, y′)2B₀K(y′, z), as in the bound of E′ above. The π-part E_z^π enters only through the pairing ⟨E_z^π, J^A(1 − ζ₀)⟩ = β_π(ℒψ_f, 𝒜_z), ψ_f := G₁^Q(J^A(1 − ζ₀)), by *The π-part in the pairings.* with the source f := J^A(1 − ζ₀) in place of WX. So it is ≤ 2d²C_ψC_π(ε/a_n)^{1/2}ε₁ times dc_wε₁ and the decay-weighted size of 𝒜_z, per interface point, with no pointwise bound of E_z^π.
    - The sum over y uses the column sums of K, which the count of (b) bounds by c_K as it bounds the row sums, the factor L^{2n} falling on the other side. Per unit datum the difference is ≤ (2B₀c_Kε₁/g_k)[d²(C_r + C₀C_ζ) + dc_wx̂_E + 3d²c_Wα₁c_al](ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}. With |B′| ≤ b_k and ε₁/g_k = C(M)R_kp₀(g_k) (CRE (2.4)), the linear term changes per point of Λ^{(k)} by ≤ C_ℓ(M)R_kp₀(g_k)b_k(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}, with C_ℓ(M) := 2B₀c_KC(M)[d²(C_r + C₀C_ζ) + dc_wx̂_E + 3d²c_Wα₁c_al]. The E-piece uses the Cauchy note's (10.6c) in its multiscale form, with the π-part as in *The terms in E.*, derived, not reviewed.

  Per point, r̄_nN_k = 2d²(2B₀c_K(1 + 2dL))²C_F(M)b_k²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}. Since b_k, R_k, and p₀(g_k) are powers of log g_k^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) (BETA_FUNCTION_WILSON_LOOPS.md Lemma 2.1(i)), up to the factor L in R_k (CRE (2.5)), and k, K − k < 8n + j(C), the changes of the forms, of the bulk part of the small form, and of the linear term are ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4}, exactly as in the result of (c). Bałaban's bounds of the parts not compared separately are O(1)B₃⁵B₅M⁵A₁²p₁²(g_k)ε_k|𝐁₀| for the small form (p.361 l.20), O(1)|𝐁₀|δ′_k³/g_k² for V(ζ, ·) (p.361 l.11–13), and O(g_k^{1−β})|Λ| for V(ζ₀, ·) (p.357 l.9–10).
- *The functions of the configurations.* V(Y) of (1.68)–(1.69) re-sums the old terms 𝐓 ∈ {𝐄^{(j)}, 𝐑^{(j)}, 𝐁^{(j)}} at the configurations U″_k, U^0_k, U_{1,2}, U_1, U_{k,Λ}, and U_k. These configurations are linked by (1.51), (1.54), (1.57), (1.58), and (1.61)–(1.64) (pp.370–375). The re-summation uses the identities (1.59)–(1.60) and the decoupling expansion of RG II (1.9)–(1.10), which is the fundamental theorem of calculus in the parameters s(Δ) (RG II p.4 l.2–35). LFR II cites RG II's §§1–2 as "Sects. 6, 7 [I]", and its (1.9)–(1.10) and (1.23)–(1.29) as "(6.9)–(6.10), (6.24)–(6.29) [I]" (p.372 l.16–19, p.376 l.42); RG I has §§0–5 only. Each term of (1.10) is the Cauchy integral (1.23) of one old term and is bounded through that term's bound alone (RG II (1.24)–(1.29), pp.7–8). Here 𝐓 stands for the renormalised combinations, 𝐄^{(j)}(Λ_j, ·, z) − 𝐄^{(j)}(Λ_j, 1, z) − β_jA(h_z, ·) at the points z and 𝐑^{(j)}(X, ·) − 𝐑^{(j)}(X, 1) (CRE (2.43)–(2.44)), since for these terms the re-summation "includes renormalization in the form described in the proof of Theorem 2" (p.376 l.40–41). The completions that p.377 l.22–26 adds and subtracts are single terms of the new action at U_k, so the split below applies to them at that one configuration. With them, Σ_YV(Y) is Σ_X[𝐓(X, U″_k) − 𝐓(X, U_k)] re-expanded. The re-summation is linear in the old terms, but the operator that re-sums them depends on the run: each term of (1.10) evaluates an old term at the run's decoupled fluctuation field, built from that run's propagators H(s), G̃(s), and H₀(s) at complex s (RG II p.3 l.30–37, (1.23)). So the two runs' V(Y), which (1.68) (p.377 l.10–14) bounds with the weight exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), differ by the parts (i) and (ii) below at the chain's configurations and by the part (iii) of the decoupled propagators. The contour of the parameter t_□ in (1.23) is the circle 1/|t_□| = 8B₀C₁e^{16κ₁}α₂^{−1}g_k|B| of RG II (1.22) (p.7). It is a function of g_k|B| and Bałaban's constants, so at equal B and g_k the two runs use the same circle. The derivative at t_□ = 0 that (1.23) represents does not depend on the radius, so where the runs' g_k differ the smaller radius serves both, with the larger 1/|t_□| in (1.24).
  - (i) The term's own difference, at run A's configurations. Let G(X, ·) := 𝐓^B(X, (ι_X(·), Q₁^{pl}(ι_X·)*J(·))) be run B's term at the pair lift of the Cauchy note's §3, with J(·) run A's current (I.1.8), Q₁^{pl}(W) := dM at W, and ι_X the localised lift of the Cauchy note's §9.8 on the localisation domain X of the term. G is a functional of configurations on T_ε, and for 𝐓 = 𝐄^{(j)}, G − 𝐓^A is the frozen difference Δ_j of (α).
    - For the renormalised combinations G is renormalised with its own coefficient Π^G_{22,11} and its plane-resolved part N_j, as in the Cauchy note's §3, so G − 𝐓^A is irr(Δ_j) with Π^Δ_{22,11} = Π^G_{22,11} − β^A_j.
    - The comparison (ii) evaluates all terms of V(Y) at one lift, ι_Y, the localised lift of the Cauchy note's §9.8 with Y^∼ in place of X^∼ (*The Lift ι_Y.* in (ii), with its tail and moment defect). The counterterm of a term at z is the weighted action A(h_z, ·), and supp h_z lies in the scale-j cubes at z and its neighbours, so inside X^∼ ⊂ Y^∼. Since Mι_YU = U on Y^∼ and ι_Y1 = 1, run B's renormalised term at the pair lift through ι_Y differs from the renormalised G by
      r_z := (Π^G_{22,11} − β^B_{j+1})A_ε(h_z, c^A) + N_j(c^A) + β^B_{j+1}b_{h_z}(ι_Yc^A) + ℓ_X(c^A).
      Here b_{h_z}(W) := A_ε(h_z, MW) − A_{ε/L}(h_z, W) is the weighted blocking artifact, and ℓ_X(U) := 𝐓^B(X, (ι_YU, Q₁^{pl}(ι_YU)*J(U))) − G(X, U) is the lift change, which vanishes at U = 1 and is governed by the tail of ι_X and ι_Y on X (the Cauchy note's §9.8). The first three parts are per point z and ℓ_X is per domain X, so as a per-X identity it needs the assignment of the counterterm discussed below. For the 𝐑-terms, which carry no counterterm, r_z = ℓ_X(c^A).
    - At the composites of (1.59) and at the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63), r_z is part of the variation groups (iv) and (α) below, which take run B's own minimisers as the partner: (iv) = [renB(U^B) − renB(ι_YU^A)] + r_z, and it is bounded with them by the geometric-mean bound of the Cauchy note's (5.1v). The completions of p.377 l.22–26, single terms at U_k, are terms of V(Y), and U_k is a member of 𝒞, so V^B(Y)[c] reads them at the entry ι_YU^A_k, not at run B's own U^B_k. Their r_z at U_k is covered in the next bullet (derived, not reviewed).
    - r_z at the members of 𝒞 read inside V(Y) (derived, unreviewed, away from the near domains). By the rule of *The entries.* in (ii), run B's renormalised combination at the entry ι_Yc^A reads U^B_{j+1}(□₀, D), run B's own local minimiser at its averages D of ι_Yc^A at its own blocks (l.659: run A's data M^·(c^A) on the shared blocks, the fine bonds of ι_Yc^A on run B's finest strip S_□), and run A's reads U^A_j(□₀, M^·(c^A)) = c^A. So the partner of renG(X, c^A) is a run-B minimiser, as at the composites, and with D each run's averages of the entry, as in l.659, the difference renB(X, U^B_{j+1}(□₀, D)) − renA(X, U^A_j(□₀, D)) is (iv) at the data D plus (i) at c^A, for the generations j > n/2 at z ∈ Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1}, the only ones at which (iv) and (i) are formed; the older generations are compared directly (*The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer*, *The old generations.*). r_z sits inside (iv), (iv) = [renB(U^B) − renB(ι_YU^A)] + r_z, and is bounded with it by the geometric-mean bound of the Cauchy note's (5.1v), with the status of (iv). The completions of p.377 l.22–26 at U_k are covered in the same way. The obstacles of a reading at the entry itself, no age bound at the non-minimiser ι_Yc^A and the weight-change part of the blocking artifact per Y, do not arise, since no renormalised combination reads ι_Yc^A. The counterterm A(h_z, ·) belongs to the renormalised sum over the domains X ∋ z, which Bałaban re-sums into the V(Y) (p.376 l.39–46), so its assignment among the Y is his. At the near domains at the boundaries of minimisation domains, where the representation U = U_{j,□₀}(M^·(U)) is not available, r_z is part of the near-domain item.
    - r^s_z at the decoupled configurations of (1.60) (derived, unreviewed): r^s_z = 0. The brackets of (1.60) are differences of single terms with X ⊄ □^{∼2}, 𝐄^{(j)}(X, exp iη𝐇″_k(g_kB)U^0_k, z) − 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U^0_k, z) (p.372 l.20–24), and these terms are read at the fluctuated configuration itself, not through a local minimiser. RG I's form (3.28) is that of the near domains X ⊂ □̃² (RG I p.273 l.25–36), while the far domains are written directly (RG I p.271 l.5–11 and p.273 l.21–24, CRE p.280 l.1–8). The counterterm A(h_z, ·) and the subtraction at 1 of the renormalised combination at z are not in these brackets: Bałaban's proof of Theorem 2 bounds the far domains on their own by (3.48) (CRE p.280 l.3–6), and the renormalisation acts on the near domains, which LFR II treats through (1.59) at the composites, where r_z is part of (iv). Two passages refer the far terms further. CRE p.280 l.6–8 ("For the complete analysis we apply the formulas (3.3), (3.6)–(3.13) [I]", or "the simpler representation 𝐄^{(j)}(X, exp iη𝐇U_{k+1}, z)") points to RG I's analytic representation through the t_□-derivative, which bounds the far terms by (3.17) (RG I p.273 l.14–16) with no counterterm, and to the representation that (1.60) itself is. LFR II p.372 l.29–31 controls the resummation over Y₀ "by the renormalization procedure for 𝐄-terms", the bounds (2.31) [III] for 𝐑-terms, and (2.42) [III] for 𝐁-terms; for the 𝐄-terms this is the renormalisation of the near domains of (1.59), already in place, and it adds no counterterm to the brackets of (1.60). So the run difference of a bracket of (1.60) at W is (i) of its far terms at W, (iii), and (δ).
    - G is analytic, with Bałaban's bound of run B's term, where the lifted pair lies in run B's domain of the term. At the configurations where (i) evaluates it, this is the lift condition of the Cauchy note's §5 (*The lift condition*). It is derived at the single-scale real minimisers through the Cauchy note's §9.7, given the localised lift of its §9.8, with the current of the pair lift (the Cauchy note's *The lift condition*). The members of 𝒞 in a young history (§1) are multiscale minimisers. At the real members U″_k, U^0_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_k it is derived, for ι_X and for ι_Y, by *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.* in (ii), *The lift condition of (i).* (unreviewed). At the composites of (1.59) it is an input of (iv). At U_1 it is derived in *The lift of U_1.* below (not reviewed), and U_2 is not read by the terms. At complex configurations it is derived, not reviewed, at the members (*The lift condition at complex points.*) and at the decoupled configurations of (1.60) (*The lift half at W.*). RG I (1.17) does not give it, since it concerns one lattice.
    - Let 𝒞 := {U″_k, U^0_k, U_1, U_2, U_{k,Λ}, U_k} be the minimisers of the chain, with U_1 = U_{𝐁₁} and U_2 = U_{𝐁₂} the functions of the two determining sets of (1.52)–(1.53) (p.370 l.22–33). Bałaban uses 𝐁₁ for two sets: 𝐁″_k(Z) ∪ 𝐁_h(Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}) of §4(c) (p.361 l.36), and 𝐁″_k ∪ 𝐁_h(Ω″^∼_{h+1}) of (1.52) (p.370 l.19). From here on 𝐁₁ is the second. For c ∈ 𝒞, c^A and c^B are the two runs' values at equal shared variables (B, V″, V_k), and the 𝐁-terms read them with the same fluctuation fields A (*The fluctuation fields A* in (ii)). Then (i) := G(X, c^A) − 𝐓^A(X, c^A) at each configuration c^A at which run A evaluates the term, for the terms of a generation j > m/2 at their points of a layer m; the older generations are compared directly, without G ((iv), *The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer*, *The old generations.*). These are the following.
      - U^0_k, through its representation (1.61)–(1.64) as a localised function of U_k, in which U_{1,2}, U_1, and U_{k,Λ} are expansion points (p.374 l.31–34).
      - The representation configurations of that expansion, U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), D(s)), U(𝐁_1(Y_2), ·), and U(𝐁_k(Y_3), ·), whose data D(s) are decoupled, at complex s(Δ) with |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ₁}, the radius that Bałaban's bounds on p.376 l.11–19 presuppose through exp(−(κ₁ − 1)M^{−d}|Y′_i|). They are not the real U^0_k, but values of the extended function of (1.65).
      - The two composites U_j(𝐁_j(□₀), exp iQ(η𝐇″_k(g_kB))M^·(U^0_k)) and U_j(𝐁_j(□₀), M^·(U^0_k)) of the bracket of (1.59) (p.372 l.5–11), which are not in 𝒞.
      - For X ⊂ Z^c, the configurations of the later stages, at which (1.59)–(1.60) are repeated around U_{1,2}, then around U_1 by (1.57), and around U_{k,Λ} by (1.58) (p.373 l.2–6): the old terms at U_{1,2}, U_1, and U_{k,Λ}, and the composites with the data M^·(U_{1,2}), M^·(U_1), and M^·(U_{k,Λ}). Their new terms with the domains Y_0 are re-summed into V(Y) (p.372 l.27–33, p.373 l.10–11).
      - The decoupled configurations of the brackets of (1.60) (*(i) at the decoupled configurations*, below, derived, not reviewed).
      - U_k, for the completions of p.377 l.22–26.
    - At real c^A, (i) is bounded by the class norm d_j, the lifted pair lying in run B's space by the lift condition. c^A is a local minimiser of its own data. So at the near domains inside its minimisation domain that satisfy the condition (N) of the Cauchy note's §5 it lies in the regular subclass 𝒮_z, and the class bound (5.1c) gives the age factor: (i) ≤ d_jE₁(α)L⁴L^{−β′(m−j)} per point of the layer m, β′ the age exponent of that section. At the near domains that fail (N), whose □₀ meets a finer layer, the bound is given by (R-Δ^c-T) (the Cauchy note's §5, derived, not reviewed). The near domains that meet the boundary of the minimisation domain are a separate case, derived, not reviewed, in (iv), *The near domains at ∂Y_i.*: ∂Y_1, ∂Y_2, and ∂Y_3 for the representation configurations U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), ·), U(𝐁_1(Y_2), ·), and U(𝐁_k(Y_3), ·), whose determining sets are localised in these domains. The composites are not among them, since their □₀ is the near domain of z itself (Open Gaps (3), the near-domain item). Since Y_i = Y_{i−1} ∪ Y_i′, parts of ∂Y_1 can be parts of ∂Y_2, so the cases overlap. There c^A is not the local minimiser of its own data on □₀, so it is not in 𝒮_z (Open Gaps (3), the near domains at the boundaries of minimisation domains).
    - **(i) and r_z at U_{1,2} (reduced to the member U_1, the reduction derived, not reviewed; r_z there with the status of (iv)); the background entry of U_{1,2} (derived near the cut, not reviewed, in (ii)); (i) at the decoupled configurations (derived, not reviewed).** U_{1,2} is U_1 on Ω″^∼_{h+1} and U_2 on its complement (LFR II (1.53), p.370 l.30), and in it "the arguments are separated completely across the boundary" ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1} (l.35). On a near domain that meets ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1}, U_{1,2} is not the local minimiser of its own data, so it is not in 𝒮_z, and the patch (1.53) is not among the link maps of (ii). Neither is needed for (i) and r_z, since no term reads U_{1,2} near the cut; the patch enters (ii) as the background of 𝐇″_k (below).
      - The terms read at U_{1,2} are the first-stage terms, with X ⊂ Z^c (p.372 l.1–3, p.373 l.2–6). A term of the form (1.60) reads X ⊂ Z^c, where U_{1,2} = U_1 (p.371 l.26–27). A term of the form (1.59) reads □₀, which meets Z only inside Ω_{k₀+1}, away from Ω^c_{h+1} ∩ Z ⊃ (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Z and from the cut, so that there too U_{1,2} = U_1 (§4(d), *The cubes □₀ at Z.*). Off □₀ and X the brackets read the configuration only through 𝐇″_k, 𝐇_{𝐁₁}, and 𝐇_k: the composites U_j(𝐁_j(□₀), ·) read their data on the blocks of □₀, a term of the form (1.60) reads X, and p.372 l.33–37 states the analyticity domain of the brackets accordingly.
      - So (i) and r_z at U_{1,2} reduce to (i) and r_z at the member U_1, on near domains inside its minimisation domain, where 𝐁₁ = 𝐁″_k and U_1 is the local minimiser of its own data. The items above at the members of 𝒞 cover them with their statuses: (i) at U_1 is derived, not reviewed, and r_z at the members has the status of (iv), derived, not reviewed. For the per-term lift ι_X, which reads only X^∼ and does not reach the cut, the reduction is an identity. At the readings V^B(Y)[c] with ι_Y the entry is ι_YU^A_1, not ι_YU^A_{1,2}: on X and □₀ the two agree up to the tail of ι_Y, which is of the size of the tail term of *The per-term lift ι_X.* and enters (i) and r_z as that term does, and U_1 is the configuration read there. (5.1c) is not evaluated at the patch on a near domain that meets the cut. The patch is not removed from (ii): run B's value at U_{1,2} is the background of 𝐇″_k in (1.54), (1.55), and (1.61), and the joint line moves it from U^B_{1,2} to the background ι_YU^A_{1,2} at which (iii) compares ((ii), *The background entry U_{1,2}.*, derived, not reviewed). U_2 enters only through U_{1,2} as that background and through 𝐆″_kθJ_{1,2} of (1.55).
      - The lift of U_1. ι_Y reads U_1 on Y^∼, which can reach the cut; X^∼ cannot, since X ⊂ Z^c lies at a distance ≥ 8MR_ka_k from it (§4(d), *The cubes □₀ at Z.*). U_1 is extended by 1 off its support Ω″^∼_{h+1} ∪ Z^c (p.370 l.22, with (R-Z)). Its finest strip at the cut is 1 (the W-δ note's §12, *The band regularity of U_{1,2}.*, *The cut.*), at least LM₁ bonds wide, so the extended U_1 is ≡ 1 on a neighbourhood of the cut. p.370 l.22–25 introduces U_{𝐁₁} and U_{𝐁₂} "to break the configuration U^0_k into two independent parts by the boundary conditions" and does not state the boundary condition of the minimisation. Both choices give the same minimiser (derived, not reviewed): the strip bonds have the data 1 in every configuration of the space, so 𝐁₁'s action is the same whether the configuration outside its support is fixed to 1 or left free (the next sentences). The strip data 1 are those of the W-δ note's §12 and of (R-Z) (derived, not reviewed). The extended U_1 is the minimal configuration of the determining set 𝐁₁′ obtained from 𝐁₁ by adding the bonds off the support to its layer 0, with the data 1 there. Every configuration of 𝐁₁′'s space restricts to one of 𝐁₁'s, and a plaquette with a bond off the support has its other bonds off the support or in the strip, all fixed to 1, so it is flat. So both problems have the same minimum, attained at the extended U_1, and VAR Theorem 1 at 𝐁₁′, whose Ω₀ is the whole lattice, gives VAR (9)–(10) on the cubes of its class that cross the cut, each in the gauge that VAR (9) provides on it (VAR p.279 l.20–27). So the lift condition at U_1 is derived as at the other real members (*The lift condition of (i).*), at the block size M′ of the joins (derived, not reviewed).
      - The decoupled configurations of (iii) are not minimisers.
      - Where U_2 and the cut are read. "This configuration restricted to Z^c is equal to U_1" (p.371 l.26–27), so the terms with X ⊂ Z^c read at U_{1,2} are terms at U_1. U_2 and the cut enter through the expansion (1.54) of U^0_k around U_{1,2} (p.370 l.39–42); (1.51) expands U″_k around U^0_k in B, and the two together give the expansion of U″_k around U_{1,2} (p.371 l.25–26). Through the 𝐇″_k of (1.54), U_{1,2} is also the background of the second set of expressions (p.373 l.2–3) and of the localisation of (1.61), restricted to Y_2 (p.373 l.34–35). In them J_{1,2} = J_1 on Ω″^∼_{h+1} (p.370 l.49–50), and (1.55) writes 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2} = 𝐆″_k(1 − θ)Q*_{𝐁₁}θ₁H*_{𝐁₁}J_1 + 𝐆″_kθJ_{1,2}, with θ = 1 on (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c and on a boundary layer of width 2M₁ (p.371 l.2–5, l.19–24), so the current of U_2 enters only through 𝐆″_kθJ_{1,2}.
      - *(i) at the decoupled configurations (derived, not reviewed).* (iii) compares run B's operators and contains no difference G − 𝐓^A, so (i) at run A's decoupled configuration is a separate item; it is taken at the configuration W := e^{iξ𝐀^A}c^A of RG I (3.13), 𝐀^A the scale-j field of 𝐇^A(s) ((δ) below). (1.60) is applied only to the domains X with X ⊄ □^{∼2} and to the boundary terms of the second group (p.372 l.20–22); the near domains X ⊂ □^{∼2} go through (1.59), whose composites are local minimisers. Since z ∈ X (CRE p.279 l.45), these X are the far domains of CRE p.279 l.43–p.280 l.6 (RG I (3.5), p.271), which Bałaban bounds through (I.1.18) alone, with no regularity beyond the class: |𝐄^{(j)}(X, z)| ≤ E₀exp(−κ(L^jL^{−n})^{−1})exp(−½κd_j(X)) (CRE (3.48)). The same step applies to Δ under the hypothesis of the Cauchy note's §5 *Statement.*, the bound dE₀exp(−κd_j(X)) of Δ on the norm class 𝒫_j(X; r), which is the bound of the old terms of the generation j that the induction (LF-2) carries. So the sum of |Δ(X, W, z)| over these X is ≤ O(1)dE₀exp(−κL^{n−j}) ≤ O(1)dE₀(L^{j−n})⁵ (CRE p.280 l.3–5), within the factor (L^{j−n})^{4+β′} of (5.1c), as the Cauchy note's §5 bounds its far domains. So (i) at W needs no age bound off 𝒮_z, only W ∈ 𝒫_j(X; r). The terms of (1.60) read run A's current argument of RG I (3.13), J(W) + (L^{j−1}η)^{−3}D_Aφ_A with φ_A := R^A_jD_A*𝐀^A, R^A_j the projector of the Landau gauge of run A's scale-j functions (p.272 l.21–22), the factor (L^{j−1}η)³ of (3.12) common to both runs being taken out (below, D_Aφ_A and D_Bφ_B include the factor (L^{j−1}η)^{−3}), and (i) is taken there, with run B's term at the image Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*(J(W) + D_Aφ_A). Since this step uses only the classes, the gauge-fixing term enters only through the current conditions, where |D_Aφ_A| ≤ C_φ(S_j + α₂)a_j^{−3} on the circle |t_□| = r̄ of (3.15), with the constant C_φ = O(1)(1 + B₀)B₀ of d, L, and B₀ ((δ), *The current part.*, *The gauge-fixing terms.*, the pointwise entry). Its S_j part is an addition of the size of the Laplace-type entries of 𝐀^A(0) in *The part at W.*, proportional to the ε_m as they are. Its α₂ part, that of t_□δ𝐇_j, is of the order c₂α_{0,j}, a fixed fraction of the radius, and the lift half's *The conditions.* counts it in the stage-2 condition on c₂. Run A's half of that is Bałaban's: the brackets are analytic, with his bounds, at the analytically extended configurations (p.372 l.33–37), at the reduced radii by *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*. The 𝐑- and 𝐁-terms of these brackets are bounded in the same way by their class norms, the carried differences of §8.4 and of (e); the boundary terms of the second group are 𝐁-terms, bounded through (2.42) [III] (LFR II p.372 l.30–31), not through (3.48). The lift half, ι_XW in run B's U^c_{j+1}(X, r_{0,j}, r_{1,j}) on X, is the class condition at the decoupled configurations that (δ) also uses for W and ι_XW. W is neither a member of 𝒞 nor a composite, so neither *The lift condition of (i).* nor *The composites and the representation configurations* covers it; it is derived below.

The items below are written for the factor e^{iη𝐇″_k(s)} and read ξ𝐀^A for η𝐇″_k(s): 𝐇_j is B₀-bounded in its data with the decay e^{−δ₀d^{(ξ)}} on the ξ-scale, its first covariant derivatives and Laplace-type entries included (VAR's Proposition 9 and (190)), Q_j is a block average, and on the layers m ≥ j PBG's weighted distance d (p.397 l.29, (2.36) of [4]) measures in units L^mη ≥ ξ, so d ≤ d^{(ξ)} there and the weights w of *The nonlinear part.* sum against e^{−δ₀d^{(ξ)}} as in *The linear operators.* So ξ𝐀^A has the weighted bounds of *The fixed points.*, the derivative entries included, with O(1)B₀ for 1, and t_□δ𝐇_j adds the factor exp(−½δ₀M(L^jη)^{−1}) of (3.9) times the radius of the circle (3.15); the constants C_fac, C′_H, and M_H below include this factor B₀. ι_XW is reached from ι(c^A) by a second stage of the flow of *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.* and *The Fine Side at Complex Points.*, along that factor.
          - The factor. 𝐇″_k(s) is the 𝐇″_k of (1.51), VAR's field of LFR II (1.19) (p.360), whose datum g_kB is supported in Λ ((1.50), p.370), with the propagators of the localisation expansion (6.9)–(6.10) [I] at the parameters s, |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ₁} (p.372 l.15–20). RG II continues the section numbering of RG I, so these are RG II (1.9)–(1.10). RG II (1.12)–(1.21) runs the fixed points of the same field, H₀B′ + 𝐀₀ − HD, at the parameters s for RG I's datum (I.3.2); the bounds below use only that the datum is local to Λ and of size O(g_k|B|), which the datum of (1.50) is. On the support of the integrand |g_kB| ≤ g_kb_k ≤ D_Bε_k, D_B := max(A₁, M₀)/A₀, by b_k of (b), p₁ < p₀, and ε_k = g_kA₀p₀(g_k). RG II bounds H(s(Y₀))X on this polydisc by B₀e^{16κ₁}|X| "in all norms of Theorems 3.1–3.10 [13]" (p.5 l.18–19), among them the second-order entry Δ_UG′ of PBG (3.42), and it gives the fixed point of (1.12) with |A| ≤ 2ε₂ and the statements of VAR's Sect. C at complex s (p.5 l.32–40). In (1.11) a walk ω that joins x to the support of a source in Λ has d(ω) ≥ d(x, Λ). Keeping half of e^{−δ₀d(ω)}, the bound e^{mκ₁}e^{−½δ₀d(ω)} ≤ 1 for m > 2⁴ needs δ₁M ≥ 2κ₁, twice Bałaban's δ₁M ≥ κ₁ (p.5 l.14–16) and a lower bound on M of the same kind, and the factor e^{−½δ₀d(x,Λ)} survives. This holds for the linear operators H(s), H₀(s), and G̃(s), in all the norms of Theorems 3.1–3.10 [13]. RG II's bounds (1.14)–(1.18) of the nonlinear parts are global, and their decay from Λ follows by running their proofs in a weighted norm (*The nonlinear part.* below). So η𝐇″_k(s)(g_kB) and its first covariant derivative obey the bound of LFR I (1.45) of *The plaquettes.*, and its Laplace-type entries D*D and Δ obey it with one more inverse power of the scale (VAR (190) at s = 1, and the entries of the outer operator at complex s, *The fixed points.* below) (the item on (1.64)–(1.69) above), with O(1)B_Ve^{16κ₁} for B_V, D_B for D_W, and δ_H := min(δ_V, δ₀/32, δ_f/4) for δ_V, at real and at complex s. As there, on X^∼, which lies in Z^c up to M₁-cubes of its scale, the factor (k − m + 1)^{1/2} of ε_k/ε_m is paid by (N₀ + 1)^{1/2} on the layers m ≥ k₀ + 1 and by the decay from Λ to ∂Z on the others. So the size of the factor at a point x of the layer m, in the units of that layer, is σ_H(x) ≤ σ̄_H(m) := C_fac(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}ε_m, C_fac := O(1)B₀B_Ve^{16κ₁}D_B(1 + β₀)c_H, with c_H the c_V of that item at δ_H. Its global size is M_H := O(1)B₀B_Ve^{16κ₁}D_Bε_k.
          - The nonlinear part (derived, not reviewed). Let w(b) := e^{δ_wd(b,Λ)}, with δ_w := δ₀/32 and d the weighted distance of PBG p.397, PRT2 (2.46) (PBG cites it as (2.36) of [4]). Let |A|_{(α),w} be PBG's norm (3.41) with |A(b)| replaced by w(b)|A(b)|, and the same for |∇A|_{(α),w}. Three facts carry RG II's chain (1.12)–(1.21) to these norms.
            - The linear operators. The local bounds of H(s), H₀(s), and G̃(s) carry e^{−½δ₀d(y,y′)} at |s| ≤ e^{κ₁} (*The factor.*), and w(y) ≤ w(y′)e^{δ_wd(y,y′)}. Summing e^{−(½δ₀−δ_w)d(y,y′)} over y′ by Lemma 2.1 of [4], as PBG derives (3.47) from (3.42) (p.398), gives their global bounds in the weighted norms, the derivative entries included, with O(1)B₀e^{16κ₁} for B₀e^{16κ₁}.
            - The nonlinear functions. D is the fixed point of the sup-norm contraction X ↦ C_j(L^jη(A′ − HX)) of VAR (50)–(55), with C_j local at a block and quadratic, so w(c)|C_j(a)(c)| ≤ C₂|a|w(c)|a| on the block of c, and with the weighted bound of H, |T(X)|_w ≤ O(1)C₂(|A′|_{(−1)} + B|X|)(|A′|_{(−1),w} + B|X|_w); with |D| ≤ 4C₂|A′|²_{(−1)} (VAR (55)) this gives |D(A′)|_w ≤ O(1)C₂|A′|_{(−1)}|A′|_{(−1),w}. The bounds of 𝔇 (VAR (70)–(73)) and of δV/δA′ (VAR (85)–(96), Proposition 4) are compositions of kernels with a factor e^{−δ₀d} or e^{−½δ₀d} ((86)) and local factors: C_j at a block, whose expansion begins at the second order (VAR (56)); V₀′ and its derivative at a plaquette ((90), with (40)); and the pointwise commutators (91)–(96). Two of the local factors give the two powers of ε₃ (of ε₂ in (1.13)), as sup norms of A′ and ∇A′. The weight is placed on a factor next to the output point b: in 𝔇 the local derivative (δC/δA)(c₂, b) has b in the block of c₂; in (85)–(86) the current J sits at b′ and carries no weight, and the sums over b′ and c₁ are paid unweighted by the decay of the kernels; where A′ enters through HD(A′), the weight sits on D, carried by |HD|_w ≤ O(1)B|D|_w; (91)–(96) are pointwise products. No weight crosses the Neumann kernel (71). This gives |(δV/δA′)(A′)|_{(−3),w} ≤ O(1)C₄ε₃max{|A′|_{(−1),w}, |∇A′|_{(−2),w}}. The differences at two arguments in the sup ball obey the same bounds with the weighted norms of the difference, by the same bilinear argument with the weight on the factor of the difference; the Cauchy formula along the segment would leave a term |A|_w|Δ|. The fixed-point bounds below do not use the differences. At complex s the propagators in D and V are those of the previous item.
            - The fixed points. Each of D in (1.12), 𝐀₀ in (1.4), and D̃ in (1.19) solves X = T(X) with |T(X)|_w ≤ θ(|Y|_w + |X|_w), where Y is the datum (A′, H₀(s)B′, or g_kCB) and θ ≤ ¼ once ε₂, ε₃, and ε₁ are below absolute constants an O(1) factor smaller than Bałaban's, which RG II leaves free (p.7 l.8). On the finite lattice |X|_w is finite, so |X|_w ≤ ½|Y|_w, a bound free of the volume. Since g_kB is supported in Λ, where w = 1, and C is local, (1.17) and (1.19)–(1.21) give |𝐇″_k(s)(g_kB)|_{(−1),w} ≤ O(1)B₀C₁e^{16κ₁}g_k|B|. This is the decay e^{−δ₀d(x,Λ)/32} used in *The factor.*, with the δ₀/32 of δ_H.

With α₂ = c₂α_{0,j} and r_{0,j} ≥ α_{0,j}/(8B₃), these are at most (8/3)B₃(4C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3} + C_{Q*}(1 + 3C_φ))c₂ times the radius, at most 1/16 of it under c₂ ≤ (43B₃(4C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3} + C_{Q*}(1 + 3C_φ)))^{−1} (W-δ §8, stage 2; C_δ(ε₀), C_{Q*} := 2(1 + (2d + 1)L^{d−1}) of the Cauchy note's l.112, and C_φ are constants of stage 2 that do not involve c₂). That is half of the room ⅛, and the other half covers the parts above for γ small. So ι_XW lies in run B's U^c_{j+1}(X, r_{0,j}, r_{1,j}) on X for γ small, at real and at complex points and parameters s. For n < n₀′ at complex points the difference is bounded as a whole, as in *Small n.*
    - For 𝐑^{(j)} (i) is the carried R-difference of §8.4 at run A's configurations, and for 𝐁^{(j)} the carried boundary-term difference of (e). Its size is of the type of the Cauchy note's carried differences 𝒟_m, not C_hθ̃^n; for the 𝐁-terms it is the bound of (e).
  - (ii) The variation of the chain (derived, not reviewed). Each run's V(Y) evaluates that run's old terms at that run's chain.
    - *The re-summation as a function of the chain.* The regions that the terms of V(Y) read, the cubes □₀ of (1.59) and the domains Y_4 of (1.61)–(1.65), lie in Y by the nesting Y = Y_4 (LFR II p.376 l.39), Y_4 ⊇ Y_1 ⊇ X (p.373 l.15–17, p.374 l.33), and X ⊇ Y_0 ⊇ □₀ for the second-stage terms (p.372 l.17, p.373 l.10–15). V(Y) depends on U_k restricted to Y and on the fields A, B, and V″ localised in Y (p.377 l.7–8), and its bounds (1.68)–(1.69) are on Ũ^c_k(Y, α̃₀, α̃₁) (p.377 l.8–19). The shared fields A, B, and V″ are equal in the two runs (for A, the next item).
      - *The fluctuation fields A (derived, unreviewed).* Bałaban's A of p.377 l.7 is "the system of the fluctuation fields {A_{j−1}}" (CRE (2.23), p.258 l.47–48), the second argument of the boundary terms 𝐁_k(U″_k, A) + 𝐁″_k(U″_k, A) of (1.49) (p.369 l.14), which are re-summed into V(Y) (p.372 l.21, l.30–31, p.373 l.10). The 𝐁-terms depend on U_k and A restricted to X and are invariant under the gauge transformations of 𝐔, 𝐉, and A, with 𝐉 and A transformed in the adjoint (CRE (2.41), p.261 l.58–62). The 𝐄- and 𝐑-terms do not depend on A.
        - On the component Z of the R-operation the A_j with h ≤ j ≤ k − 1 are integrated inside the R-operation, by LFR I's preliminary integrations (LFR I (1.25), p.182 l.25–33; (1.60)–(1.62), p.188 l.50–p.189 l.6; (1.71), p.192 l.28–35), before V(Y) is formed; that integration produces 𝐁″_k. Bałaban keeps the notation A, since A″_k "has the form (2.23) [III], with some new boundary terms only" (LFR I p.182 l.23–25). So the A of V(Y) is the remaining system. Its A_j are integrated by the postponed operations, 𝕋_h(Z_h) in (1.71) and the 𝐓^{(j)} on Z_{j+1} ∩ Ω_{j+1} of the other regions (CRE (2.20)–(2.21), p.258 l.19–31), outside V(Y). N₀, h, Z″_j, and Ω″_j of LFR I (1.10)–(1.12) are the same in both runs, since R_j = R̄(K − j) in both ((M) of the Cauchy note's §2), so the remaining system is the same set of variables in both runs. Each A_j is a variable on the scale-j lattice, which at the shared scale j is the same lattice in both runs. The operator C that removes the δ-functions is determined by the sequence {Ω_j} (CRE p.267 l.40–44), and a matched pair has the same sequences of domains at the shared scales.
        - Run B's extra step has Ω_1 = Λ_1 = T in a matched pair ((M) of the Cauchy note's §2), so it postpones no fluctuation variable.
        - So the two runs' systems A are the same variables on the same sets with the same Lebesgue measure, as in the Cauchy note's §6.2, and their matching is the identity. V^B(Y)[c] below takes A as a further argument, with the same real value in both runs, like B and V″.
        - The matched variable is Bałaban's scaled field A_j, after A′_j = g_jA_j (CRE p.267 l.42). The scaling's run dependence sits in the integrands, through the factors g_j of the expansions (as in B′ = g_kCB − h𝐃̃(g_kCB), RG I (3.2)), which are coupling ingredients of the Cauchy note's §6.2. Its Jacobian is a constant, which cancels in the ratio of the Wilson loop expectation.
        - Both runs' 𝐁-terms are read at real A in the intersection of the two runs' supports of the restrictions |A_j| < g_j^{−1}δ_j (LFR I (1.8)–(1.9)). The rest of either support is a difference of the χ-functions, a run-dependent ingredient of the Cauchy note's §6.2.
        - The gauge is consistent: under a gauge transformation v of the entries ι_Y transforms by an extension ṽ equal to v on T_ε (the Cauchy note's §9.8), and the scale-j lattices are sublattices of T_ε, so v acts on A in the same way in both runs.
        - The run differences of the carried terms 𝐁^{(i)} at equal (U, A) are the class norms b_i of (e). That of 𝐁″_k, the boundary terms of LFR I's preliminary integrations, together with those integrations' own run differences, is the creation difference of Open Gaps (10), derived, not reviewed. The run differences of the postponed operations that integrate the remaining A, through Δ^{(j)}, C^{(j)}, χ, and z^{(j)}, are those of the Cauchy note's §6.2 and §10.
      - *The field 𝐀₀ (derived, unreviewed).* 𝐀₀ := 𝐇_{k,Λ}((1/i)log M^·(U_0(𝐔))) is the analytic extension (p.367 l.46–49) of the field 𝔸₀ of LFR I (1.86)–(1.87), which represents U_0 inside Λ in the mixed axial and Landau gauge, U_0^{(AL)} = exp iη𝔸₀ (LFR I p.197 l.25–38). It is not the fixed point 𝐀₀ of RG II (1.3)–(1.4) in (Wδ-3). It is a function of the background 𝐔 through U_0(𝐔) of (1.43) (p.367 l.26–28), so Bałaban's dependence of V(Y) on U_k covers it, and it is not a separate variable.
      - Inside V(Y), 𝐀₀ enters as Q̃_·(η𝐀₀), with exp iQ̃_·(η𝐀₀) = M^·(U_0^{(AL)}) (p.367 l.48; (1.50), p.370 l.4 and l.15–16; l.41 and l.45, (1.54), through 𝐇″_k and the gauge transformation u^0_k, p.373 l.29–30), and as M^·(U_0^{(AL)}) in (1.52) for U_1 (p.370 l.28), in (1.61) (p.373 l.21), in (1.62) (p.373 l.40), and at p.375 l.9. These are the data of U^0_k, the configuration (1.50) at B = 0 (p.370 l.14), on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, and with exp ig_kB those of U″_k; U_1's data on Λ ∩ Ω″_k are their scale-k averages, read through U_1's own entry. The occurrences at p.368 l.5–11 and (1.46) are in the data of U″_{h+2}, of the action term A(ζ₁/(g″_k)², U″_{h+2}) of (1.70), outside V(Y).
      - V^B(Y)[c] reads 𝐀₀ through the entry of U^0_k, as the link image M^·(c_{U^0_k}) on these layers. The link-image rule does not force this choice: Bałaban's map 𝐔 ↦ 𝐀₀(𝐔) at the entry ι_YU^A_k would give run B's 𝐀₀ at that entry. Both choices agree at run B's chain, so either defines V^B(Y)[c], and the bound of f on the disc (*The bound of f on the disc*, below, derived, not reviewed) covers the choice made.
      - At ι_Yc^A it is run A's 𝐀₀, since Mι_YU = U on Y^∼ and the averagings compose, in the common gauge of §4(c) (*The gauge of the data.*), in which ι_Y is covariant (the Cauchy note's §9.8). At c^B it is run B's 𝐀₀ in that gauge, in which both runs use run A's ū₀; in run B's own AL gauge the two differ by the block-constant ū₀^A(ū₀^B)^{−1}, which §4(c) removes by the compensating change of variables.
      - Its two runs' difference is the data difference of §4(c) through U_0, ≤ C′_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ per data point (*The data difference.*), which the coarse side d̄ of the fine-side item below contains, with the constant C_U(M) ≥ C′_Δ(M). No lift other than ι_Y enters it.
      - *The entries.* For an entry c = (c_i)_{c∈𝒞}, one configuration on Y^∼ per member, write V^B(Y)[c] for run B's re-summation with the following variables. This is well defined for U″_k, U^0_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_k. For U_1 and U_2 it is not: they "are supported in the domains Ω″^∼_{h+1}, (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c correspondingly" (p.370 l.23), so one configuration on Y^∼ per member fails where Y meets both sides, and ι_Y, a function of a configuration on Y^∼, cannot be applied to them there. So U_1 is taken extended by 1 off its support ((i), *The lift of U_1.*), and U_2 enters only through the background entry U_{1,2} (*The background entry U_{1,2}.*, derived, not reviewed).
      - Where a term of V(Y) reads a member of 𝒞 as such, it reads the entry of that member. A renormalised combination, a term together with its counterterm, reads the entry c only through its data on the cube □₀ of its localisation: both read U_j(□₀, M^·(c)), Bałaban's form (3.28) of RG I (p.276) for the term and the representation U_k = U_{j,□₀}(M^·(U_k)) of the second proof of Theorem 2 (CRE p.283) for the counterterm A(h_z, U_k), which (3.66) analyses "in the same way". So each combination has one configuration, a local minimiser. At run B's chain this is the term's value where the member equals U_{j,□₀}(M^·(·)) on □₀, which p.376 l.39–46 uses in its renormalisation "in the form described in the proof of Theorem 2"; at the near domains at the boundary of a member's minimisation domain, where the member is not the local minimiser of its data on □₀, the representation is not available, and these positions are the near-domain item (Open Gaps (3)).
      - Every other configuration that the re-summation reads is a link image: the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63), the composites of (1.59) and of the later stages, the decoupled configurations of (1.60), and the fluctuation fields with their propagators. Each is run B's map ((1.51), (1.54), (1.57)–(1.64)) or run B's local minimisation at the entries from which it is built.
      - So the free variables are the entries. Run B's V(Y) is V^B(Y)[c^B], and at run B's chain the entries and the link images agree. At other entries they need not: run B's (1.61) at the entries ι_Yc^A gives U^B(𝐁″_k(Y_1), M^·(c^A)), which is not the entry ι_YU^{0,A}.
    - Put (ii) := V^B(Y)[c^B] − V^B(Y)[ι_Yc^A], with ι_Yc^A := (ι_Yc^A_i)_{c∈𝒞} and each current the pair-lift current Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A_i)*J(c^A_i). Then V^B(Y)[ι_Yc^A] − V^A(Y)[c^A] is the re-summation of the following pieces. They are telescoped in this order.
      - For the brackets of (1.59), at the members and at the representation configurations, (iii) and (β) come first, at the fixed configurations ι_Yc^A and c^A, and replace run A's propagators, fixed points, and gauge transformations by run B's. (i), (iv), and (α) are then differences under run B's re-summation operator, with run B's propagated data.
      - For the brackets of (1.60), (i) comes first, at run A's decoupled configuration e^{iη𝐇^A(s)}c^A, read by the term through its scale-j argument of RG I (3.13), the configuration W = e^{iξ𝐀^A}c^A with run A's current argument ((δ)), where it replaces 𝐓^A by G. The brackets of (1.60) carry no counterterm, so no renormalisation remainder enters at that configuration (*r^s_z at the decoupled configurations of (1.60)*, r^s_z = 0). Then (iii) and (δ) compare run B's term at the lift of that argument with run B's term at run B's decoupled field. The other order fails, since after (iii) 𝐓^A would be read at run B's decoupled field, which lives on T_{ε/L}.
      - So 𝐓^B(X, e^{iη𝐇^B(s)}ι_Yc^A) − 𝐓^A(X, e^{iη𝐇^A(s)}c^A) is (iii) and (δ), plus (i) at the decoupled configuration. Each bracket's difference is a sum of pieces from the list below. (β), (iv), and (α) occur only for (1.59), and (δ) only for (1.60). (i) is evaluated at the composites with run B's data for (1.59) and at run A's decoupled configuration for (1.60).

      The pieces are the following.
      - (i) with its remainder r_z, where a term reads a member of 𝒞.
      - (iv) below, the variation group at the composites of (1.59) and of the later stages for X ⊂ Z^c.
      - (iii), the two runs' propagators at the configurations ι_Yc^A and c^A, through the transfer map of (iii).
      - (α) The variation group at the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63): run B's U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), ·), U(𝐁_1(Y_2), ·), and U(𝐁_k(Y_3), ·) at the data M^·(ι_Yc^A) = M^·(c^A) against run A's at the same data, with the gauge transformations u^0_k, u_1, and u_{k,Λ}, in the form of (iv), with (i) at run A's representation configurations (with the status of (iv), its inputs derived, not reviewed, away from the near domains that meet ∂Y_i, by *The representation configurations.* in (iv), and at those near domains by *The near domains at ∂Y_i.* in (iv), derived, not reviewed).
      - (β) The run differences of the functions of the localisation expansions of (1.61)–(1.64), 𝐇″_k with σ̃₀, 𝐇_{𝐁₁}, and 𝐇_k (p.373 l.31–34, p.374 l.8–10 and l.26–29), and of the gauge transformations u^0_k, u_1, and u_{k,Λ} (p.373 l.29–30) (derived, not reviewed: §12 of `WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md` reduces them to (W-δ)). Their propagator parts are of the type of (iii); they also contain the run difference of the fixed points D and 𝐀₀, of the type of (Wδ-3), so (β) is not purely a propagator difference.
      - (δ) At the decoupled fine configurations of (1.60), the comparison of the scale-j arguments of the terms. At complex s a term of (1.60) is RG I's (3.15) of the function (3.13): 𝐄^{(j)} at the configuration exp iξ𝐀𝐔 and the current argument (L^{j−1}η)³𝐉 + Δ_𝐔𝐀 − P₁𝐀 + 𝐅₁(𝐔, 𝐀), with 𝐀 = 𝐇_j(B(t)) + t_□δ𝐇_j the decoupled scale-j field at the data B_□ = Q_j(η(t + t_□ζ_□)𝐇_k) (RG I p.271 l.16, p.272 l.30–35, p.273 l.1–3; RG II p.8 l.36–38). By (3.10)–(3.12) and p.272 l.21–22, with 𝐅₁ = 𝐅 + ℓ𝐀, at 𝐉 = J(𝐔) this current argument is (L^{j−1}η)³ times the configuration's own current J(exp iξ𝐀𝐔) plus the gauge-fixing term D_𝐔R_jD_𝐔*𝐀, R_j the projector of the Landau gauge RD*𝐇_j = 0 of the scale-j functions, not zero at complex s, since the decoupled 𝐇_j need not satisfy that gauge. Run A's term is this with run A's functions at 𝐔 = c^A and 𝐇_k = 𝐇^A(s); 𝐀^A denotes its scale-j field and W := e^{iξ𝐀^A}c^A. Run B's term at the u = 0 end of *The segment.* below is this with run B's functions of the matched generation, j + 1 in run B's numbering and written 𝐇^B_j, Q^B_j as in (iii), whose data lie on run A's blocks of the scale j (the (W-δ) note's §11: Λ^A_j = Λ^B_{j+1}, and ξ_B = L^{j+1}η_B = ξ), at 𝐔 = ι_Yc^A and 𝐇_k = ℒ𝐇^A(s); 𝐀^B_0 := 𝐀_0 of (iii) denotes its scale-j field. (i) is taken at run A's argument, W with run A's current argument (*(i) at the decoupled configurations.* above), and (δ) compares run B's term at the lifted argument, ι_XW with Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)* of run A's current argument, with run B's term at the u = 0 end. It has three parts: the nonlinearity of the lift, ι_XW against e^{iξℒ𝐀^A}ι_Yc^A, with the currents of the configurations (below and *The current part.*); the run difference of the gauge-fixing terms, Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*D_AR^A_jD_A*𝐀^A against D_BR^B_jD_B*ℒ𝐀^A (*The gauge-fixing terms.*); and the scale-j field difference Δ_j := 𝐀^B_0 − ℒ𝐀^A at the fixed background ι_Yc^A (*The scale-j segment.*). In the first part the pair-lift current is Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*J(W), and at e^{iξℒ𝐀^A}ι_Yc^A the current is the RG I (3.11) current of run B's link maps. The entries of the field of W: 𝐀^A = 𝐇_j(B(t)) + t_□δ𝐇_j, where 𝐇_j is B₀-bounded in its data with the decay e^{−δ₀d} on the ξ-scale, its first covariant derivatives and its Laplace-type entries included (VAR's Proposition 9 and (190), RG I p.271 l.43–44), and the data B(t) = Q_j(ηt𝐇^A(s)), 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, is on a j-block of the layer m at most a_j times the field there, ≤ O(1)σ̄_H(m)a_j/a_m in angle units, at the layers m ≥ j of X (*The part at W.*). Summing the decay e^{−δ₀d^{(ξ)}} over the blocks, |𝐀^A|, a_j|∇𝐀^A|, and a_j²|Δ𝐀^A| are at most O(1)B₀S_ja_j^{−1}, S_j := sup_{m≥j} σ̄_H(m)a_j/a_m, and so are a_j²|P₁𝐀^A|, since P₁ is of second order with the kernel bound of DPD* (PBG (3.49)), and a_j²|D*D𝐀^A|, a Laplace-type entry; these are the entries of 𝐀^A(0) := 𝐇_j(B(t)), the field at t_□ = 0. On the circle |t_□| = r̄ of (3.15) the part t_□δ𝐇_j has its four entries |·|, a_j²|P₁·|, a_j|∇·|, and a_j²|Δ·| at most ⅓α₂a_j^{−1}, since r̄ ≤ r, Bałaban's radius, fixed by r times the largest of them being ⅓α₂ (RG I p.273 l.9–11; r̄ is defined by r̄N′e^{−E} = ⅓α₂ in the t_□ bullet of (iii)), and (3.9) holds for the derivatives and the second-order operators (p.271 l.47), in the case dist^{(ξ)}(X, □) ≥ M(L^jη)^{−1} of (3.9) (p.271 l.39), the case of (3.15)–(3.17). r̄ ≤ r holds for run A since N′, built with B_T, B_T′ ≥ B₀ and e^{2·6^dκ₁} ≥ e^{16κ₁}, bounds run A's (3.9) bound of δ𝐇_j as well, and a_j²|D*D·| is covered with the second-order operators of (3.9) at the common bound N′e^{−E}. Here α₂ := c₂α_{0,j} for the term on U_j^c(X, α_{0,j}, α_{1,j}) (CRE p.259 l.42–47; the table's c₂α̃_{0,k} at the term's scale) is a choice of RG I's radius of (3.14): RG I states only that "there exists a constant α₂, depending on α₀ and on some absolute constants" (p.272 l.38–39), and the linear form with c₂ of W-δ §8, stage 2, within the rooms of (3.13), is admissible. This part is not small against r_{0,j}: the bounds below that read the entries of 𝐀^A through S_j are bounds of 𝐀^A(0), and each piece that uses them bounds t_□δ𝐇_j separately (*The part at W.*, the lift half's *The conditions.*, and *The gauge-fixing terms.*). Relative to the radius: r_{0,j} ≥ α_{0,j}/(8B₃) (*The conditions.* of (i)), α_{0,n} = C₀g_n(log g_n^{−2})^{q₀} and ε_n = g_nA₀(log g_n^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.28), (2.4)), and for m ≥ j CRE (2.6) gives g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)max(1, (m − j)^{1/2})g_j and g_j ≤ (1 + β₀)g_m (at m = j trivially), so log g_m^{−2} ≤ 2log g_j^{−2} for g small; with max(1, (m − j)^{1/2})L^{−(m−j)} ≤ 1 this gives S_j/r_{0,j} ≤ ŝ_j := 8B₃C_fac(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}(A₀/C₀)(1 + β₀)2^{p₀}(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀−q₀}, which tends to 0 with γ since q₀ > p₀ and N₀ = O(log R_k/log L), as in *The conditions.* of (i). So the entries of 𝐀^A(0) in the units a_j^{−1}, a_j^{−2}, and a_j^{−3} of the scale j are at most O(1)B₀ŝ_j times r_{0,j}, in place of the ratio σ̄_H(m)/r_{0,m} of the k-field's entries. The bounds below that use the field of W read these entries, the second-order ones included, in place of those of η𝐇^A(s), and in the template *The divergence part.* of (iii) S_j stands for σ̄_H(m) and a_j for a_m on X ∩ Ω_j, a_j being the unit of the term's layer there, where CRE's class conditions hold uniformly at n = j ((2.34)–(2.39), p.261 l.24), while on a finer layer m < j of X a_m stays, read layer by layer as in *Where the current enters.*, with S_ja_m/a_j for σ̄_H(m), the entries of the field of W in the units a_m: the template uses σ̄_H(m) only through σ̄_H(m) ≤ α_{0,m} at the layer of its threshold θ_m (*The current along the segment.*), which S_j satisfies on X ∩ Ω_j, S_j ≤ ŝ_jr_{0,j} ≤ α_{0,j} for γ small, and S_ja_m/a_j on a finer layer m < j, S_jL^{−(j−m)} ≤ ŝ_j(1 + β₀)²(j − m)^{1/2}L^{−(j−m)}α_{0,m} ≤ α_{0,m} (CRE (2.6) and the first row of (2.7), as in *The induction.*), and a_m only through a_m ≤ a_k. For γ small the same bounds give (3.14) with ⅓α₂ for 𝐀^A(0), which is what RG I assumes of 𝐇_j(B(t)) (p.273 l.13), since O(1)B₀ŝ_jr_{0,j} ≤ ⅓c₂α_{0,j}; so 𝐀^A satisfies (3.14) with ⅔α₂ on the circle (3.15), and W is in the domain of run A's (3.13). (δ) is taken at the transformed pair lift ((ι_XW)^{u(W)}, R(u)Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*J(W), R(u)B) of the (W-δ) note's 10.5 (*The gauge choice.*), with u(W) := exp(−γ), which removes the gauge term from the lift's derivative. Its value is the same, by the invariance of the terms under RG I (3.29) for G^c-valued u near G (RG II p.011 l.27–28). In this form (δ) contains three further terms: the commutator −[γ, δJ] with the pair-lift current defect δJ := Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*J(W) − J^B(ι_XW); the rotation −[γ_c, B] of the fluctuation argument, of second order; and the gauge factor u(W) beyond first order in the configuration. Since (δ) compares the configurations themselves, it also contains the nonlinear part of the run difference of the fixed points D. By the (W-δ) note's §11, D^B at run B's linear coordinate equals D^A at run A's coordinate of the one-step average plus the one-step remainder, and the two coordinates differ by the kernel term R₁ := (1 − H^AQ̂^A)ε^{−1}C^B_1 plus a propagator term of the type of (iii). R₁ is quadratic in the field and carries no factor δ_w. It does carry the factor L^{−j(x)} of the layer: (L^jε)|ε^{−1}C^B_1| ≤ C₂L^{−j(x)}|A|²_{(−1)} (the (W-δ) note's §11, *The size of the remainder terms.*). On the young histories j ≥ k/2 − 1, so its part of (δ) is O(C₂L^{1−k/2}) relative to |A|², summable in k, and it is bounded directly rather than matched against the kernel part of ι_X's fluctuation. The bound of (δ) is derived, not reviewed, in the pieces below.
        - *The nonlinearity of the lift (derived, not reviewed).* Put h := (ξ/η)𝐀^A, so that W = e^{iηh}c^A, and U := ι_X(c^A). ι_X is run B's one-step local minimiser with the data of its argument, so ι_X(W) = e^{iη(ℒ^Xh + N(h))}U, ℒ^X := dι_X at c^A, where N is the nonlinear part of VAR's one-step fixed point at the base U (VAR at j = 1, with Λ₀ unaveraged, as in the (W-δ) note's bridge): N(h) = −H₁ε^{−1}C₁(εℒ^Xh) plus G̃₁ applied to the cubic and higher vertices of the one-step action, H₁ and G̃₁ the one-step operators at U, C₁ acting on the fine linear part ℒ^Xh. The same fixed point defines ι_X at the complex W, and it agrees with the real lift at real W by the uniqueness of the fixed point in VAR's ball. Its constraint is VAR's double-bar form (156); at real W it gives Mι_X(W) = W through the gauge relation (B1)/(B3) of the Cauchy note's §10.1, and both sides are analytic in W, so Mι_X(W) = W holds at the complex W by the identity theorem. The conversion from the plain data to the double-bar data is the gauge factor v₁ of (B1), CMP 98 (160) at k = 1, v₁(x) = the block average of the contour logarithms of U′ = e^{iεh} over B(x). By (162) at j = 0 (CMP 98 p.42), θ(th) := (1/i)log v₁ at the field th is analytic for |t|sup|h| ≤ α₁, with |θ| < O(1)α₁Lε there; here h and ε are read in CMP 98's block units, in which the coarse block has size 1 and |A′| < α₁ (p.42 l.17–21), as VAR (44) reads |C_j(L^jηA)| ≤ C₂(L^jη)²|A|² (VAR p.285 l.16). Cauchy's estimate in t on that disc bounds the part of θ(h) beyond first order by O(1)(L/α₁)ε(sup|h|)², for sup|h| ≤ α₁/2, the supremum over the bonds of the paths of the block, in CMP 98's block units (L^kη = 1, (163)). In the units of this note, where |h|_{(−1)} ≤ α₂ is applied (below), the condition reads the same for the dimensionless angles ηh, and it holds for γ small since α₂ = c₂α_{0,j} → 0, and the form "length × field²" of the bound is scale covariant. The conjugation of the data by v₁ adds a second-order part ≤ 2|θ|²e^{2|θ|}|X| beyond its first order [θ, X]. Both are local to the paths of a block and of the form of VAR (44), with C₂ replaced by C₂ + O(1)(L/α₁ + L²), so they are bounded with C₁ below. VAR (44) at j = 1 is a sup-norm bound, |ε^{−1}C₁(εf)| ≤ C₂ε|f|², and C₁ is local to the paths of a block, so at x it holds with the maximum of |f|² over the bonds of the paths of the block of x. With |ℒ^Xh| ≤ O(1)B₀ sup|h| on these bonds, exactly as for R₁, L^{j(x)}ε|H₁ε^{−1}C₁(εℒ^Xh)(x)| ≤ O(1)B₀³C₂L²L^{−j(x)}|h|²_{(−1)}. The vertices carry the coupling η = ε/L once more than the quadratic term: the cubic vertex η[Φ, ∇Φ] at a field of size |Φ|_{(−1)} ≤ O(1)B₀α₂ (Φ = ℒ^Xh + N(h), with |ℒ^Xh| ≤ O(1)B₀ sup|h| as above) against G̃₁ of size O(1)B₀ε² gives at most O(1)B₀³(ε/a_j)²α₂² in |·|_{(−1)}. Since ηh = ξ𝐀^A, the angles of h are those of 𝐀^A, and on the circle (3.15) |h|_{(−1)} ≤ ⅔α₂ ≤ α₂ by (3.14) with ⅔α₂ ((δ), the entries of the field of W, the t_□ part included); with it the nonlinearity carries the factor (ε/a_j) of the layer, ≤ L^{1−k/2} on the young histories, and is bounded directly, as R₁ is.
        - *The gauge terms of the transformed pair lift (derived, not reviewed).* With sup|γ| ≤ 2(O(1) + 2d)εC_ι sup|ℒ_ιv| (the (W-δ) note's 10.5), the rotation −[γ_c, B] is at most O(1)C_ι(ε/a_j)|ℒ_ιv|_{(−1)}|B| and the gauge factor beyond first order is O(|γ|²), so both carry (ε/a_j). The commutator −[γ, δJ] is at most 2 sup|γ| times |δJ|, and |δJ| ≤ C_{Q*}|J(W)| + |J^B(ι_XW)|.
          - The part at W (derived, not reviewed). J(W) is J(c^A), bounded by the current condition of run A's class (RG I (1.14) with γ₀ = α₀ for the 𝐄-terms, CRE (2.36) for the 𝐁-terms; the two agree at n = j), at most r_{0,m}a_m^{−3} on the layer m, plus the local 𝐅(U, 𝐀) of RG I (3.11), a local operator in U, ∂U, 𝐀, and ∇𝐀 that vanishes at 𝐀 = 0 (p.272 l.15–19), so at most O(1)(α_{0,j} + B₀S_j + α₂)(B₀S_j + α₂)a_j^{−3} (this product form is derived, not printed: RG I obtains (3.11) by expanding the current in 𝐀 with the formulas (1.43)–(1.54) of [14] (p.272 l.12), and the part of the current of exp iξ𝐀U linear in 𝐀 differs from D_U*D_U𝐀 only by terms carrying ∂U − 1, so 𝐅 is of second order in (𝐀, ∇𝐀) or of first order times the plaquette deviation, at most α_{0,j} by the class), which is ≤ ½r_{0,j}a_j^{−3} for γ small and is taken with the entries of 𝐀^A(0) below, plus the Laplace-type entries of the field of W: those of 𝐀^A(0), at most O(1)B₀ŝ_jr_{0,j}a_j^{−3} ((i), *The lift half at W.*; (δ), the entries of the field of W), which is ≤ ½r_{0,j}a_j^{−3} for γ small, as in *The conditions.* of (i), and those of t_□δ𝐇_j, at most ⅓α₂a_j^{−3} on the circle (3.15) (the same item). Since r_{0,j} ≥ r_{0,m}L^{j−m} and a_m = L^{m−j}a_j for m ≥ j, r_{0,m}a_m^{−3} ≤ r_{0,j}a_j^{−3}. So |J(W)| ≤ ř_ja_j^{−3} with ř_j := 2r_{0,j} + ⅓α₂, C_{Q*}|J(W)| ≤ C_{Q*}ř_ja_j^{−3}, and its part of the commutator carries (ε/a_j).
          - The part at ι_XW (derived, not reviewed). J^B(ι_XW) is the own current of ι_XW (RG I (1.8)), not the 𝐉-entry Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*J(W) of the pair in run B's class, which *The conditions.* bounds; a one-step bound through VAR (10) or the Laplace entries of VAR (190) at the scale ε reads the smooth data at that scale and loses (a_j/ε)². The bound goes through the constraint instead.
            - The identity. ι_X(W) is critical for run B's action under the constraint Mι_X(W) = W (*The nonlinearity of the lift.*), so J^B(ι_XW) = Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*λ(W) with the multiplier λ. Differentiating the constraint, dM·dι_X = id, gives the envelope identity λ(W) = ∇_W[A^B∘ι_X](W) = J(W) + ∇_WΔ(W), with Δ(W) := A^B(ι_X(W)) − A^A(W) and J(W) the own current of W for run A's action. Up to its coupling part, δ_j times the current, ∇_WΔ = −∇b₁, the first variation of the blocking artifact at ι_XW pulled back by dι_X = ℒ_ι (the (W-δ) note's §11, l.733, and §12's κ). Both sides are analytic in W, so the identity holds at complex W by the identity theorem.
            - Real points. At real (𝐔, s, t_□), W is real, and ∇b₁ = ∇_A*K + s₁ with |K| ≤ O(1)osc_pX (the (W-δ) note's §11, l.733), the transposed curl costing η^{−1} = L/ε as a pointwise source. osc_pX at ι_XW is bounded as in the (W-δ) note's §12, *§11's oscillation at U_{1,2}.*: the base ι_X(c^A) is the lift of a member, a minimal configuration of its data, with (9.2) and *The base at the layer j.* there, and the factor e^{iξ𝐀^A} is the path c_τ = e^{iτξ𝐀^A}c^A, 0 ≤ τ ≤ 1, with the Hölder entries of 𝐀^A at real s: 𝐇_j(B(t)) is the scale-j minimiser of its real data, whose Hölder entry follows by integrating the third entry ‖ζ∇(δℋ/δB)‖_β of VAR (190) (p.308 l.36–44) along the data sB(t), 0 ≤ s ≤ 1 (the linear part H has the same entry by PBG (3.133), p.422 l.23–29), and t_□δ𝐇_j has its Hölder entry from the same entry of (190) applied to the data vector of (3.8). The entry of (190) has no small factor, so the first gives O(1)B₀S_j, by the data bound of (δ)'s entries of the field of W; RG I's δ₀ is the decay rate of (190) (RG I p.271 l.33), so for dist^{(ξ)}(X, □) ≥ M(L^jη)^{−1} the second decays at least at the rate E of (3.9), and r̄ times it is at most O(1)α₂ for |t_□| ≤ r̄, as r̄ times (3.9) is ⅓α₂. So osc_pX ≤ O(1)C_δ′(ε/a_j)^{β_o}a_j^{−2}, β_o := min(β, 1 − 2ε₀), with C_δ′ := C₀^cε₁″ + B₀S_j + α₂, the first term the base's, there at V₀'s data parameter ε₁ = O(1)B₃²B₅M⁶ε_k, here at the parameter ε₁″ = C(M)ε′_k of the real points of the class at the full radii (*The complex class.*, *The real points.*, l.782), with the constant C₀^c, the bootstrap's C₀′ (the (W-δ) note's l.169) read with the target's and the base's parameters both at most O(1)ε₁″ here, in place of the terms O(1)B₃³B₅M⁶ε_k and C₁ε₁ of ε₂'s bound ε₂ ≤ B₁(O(1)B₃³B₅M⁶ε_k + C₁ε₁) there, so a constant of M and the stages, since the bootstrap bounds its ε₂ linearly by these parameters (the (W-δ) note's l.169, l.938, and l.980), and it and VAR's statements read the configuration and the data only through VAR's classes, which are local and gauge invariant (VAR (2) and (7)), not through the axial-gauge representation that carries the R_k of ε₁′, under O(1)C₀^cε₁″ ≤ 1 (the (W-δ) note's §8, stage 6) and VAR's hypotheses at ε₁″, which those of (T-c) at ε₁′ ≥ ε₁″ imply, and |∇b₁| ≤ O(1)LC_δ′(a_j/ε)^{1−β_o}a_j^{−3}, s₁ and the coupling part being smaller. With |J(W)| ≤ ř_ja_j^{−3} (the part at W), |J^B(ι_XW)| ≤ O(1)C_{Q*}(ř_j + LC_δ′(a_j/ε)^{1−β_o})a_j^{−3} at real points.
            - Complex points. Let P = P_r + iP_i be a point of the complex variables (the class variable A′, the decoupling parameters s, and t_□, in real coordinates), with P in the class at a fifth of the radii, |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ₁}/5, and |t_□| ≤ r̄/5, and f(t) := J^B(ι_XW) at P_r + tP_i, |t| ≤ 4. For real t the point is real, so |f| ≤ m := O(1)C_{Q*}(ř_j + LC_δ′(a_j/ε)^{1−β_o})a_j^{−3} on the real diameter. On the disc the point lies in the class at the full radii, in the polydisc |s| ≤ e^{κ₁}, and in |t_□| ≤ r̄, where ι_XW satisfies the plaquette condition of run B's class (*The conditions.*), so |f| ≤ 4d(L/ε)sup|F(ι_XW)| ≤ M_c := O(1)Lr_{0,j}(a_j/ε)a_j^{−3}. The harmonic measure of the circle at t = i, relative to the disc cut along the real diameter, is ≤ (4/π)arctan(1/4) < 1/3 (the Cauchy note's l.899), so the two-constants theorem gives |f(i)| ≤ m^{2/3}M_c^{1/3} ≤ O(1)C_J″(a_j/ε)^{1−2β_o/3}a_j^{−3}, C_J″ := C_{Q*}^{2/3}(ř_j + LC_δ′)^{2/3}(Lr_{0,j})^{1/3}.
            - The commutator. So 2 sup|γ||J^B(ι_XW)| ≤ O(1)C_ιC_J″(ε/a_j)^{2β_o/3}|ℒ_ιv|_{(−1)}a_j^{−3}, a positive power of ε/a_j, ≤ L^{−(2β_o/3)(k/2−1)} on the young histories and summable in k, as the other pieces of (δ). The bound holds on the class at a fifth of the radii, on the polydisc of radius e^{κ₁}/5, and for |t_□| ≤ r̄/5: the factor 5 joins the values of μ of *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, the Cauchy extraction in s on the smaller polydisc costs e^{log 5} per localisation, paid by κ₁ larger by log 5, a lower bound of the same kind, and the Cauchy integral (3.15) of the pieces bounded through *Complex points.* is taken on |t_□| = r̄/5, which multiplies their bound (3.17) by 5.
        - *The current part (derived, not reviewed).* The pair-lift current Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*J(W) against the current of run B's link maps at e^{iξℒ𝐀^A}ι_Yc^A is the current along the pair line at the decoupled configurations, as in (iv). ι_X(W) is critical for run B's action under the constraint Mι_X(W) = W, so J^B(ι_XW) = M*λ(W) with the multiplier λ, and by the envelope identity λ(W) is the W-gradient of A^B(ι_X(W)); hence δJ = −Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*∇Δ(W) with Δ(W) := A^B(ι_X(W)) − A^A(W), the one-step classical remainder, which extends to the complex W by the identity theorem. Up to its coupling part, which is δ_j times the current, Δ(W) = −b(ι_X(W)) with b the blocking artifact of §4(b) (*The blocking artifact.*), so the lattice part of δJ is Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*∇_W[b∘ι_X](W). At the real backgrounds this is the first variation ∇b₁ of the (W-δ) note's §11 (*The term G̃Δ^{(2)}H₀B.*, second bullet), bounded there in divergence form, ∇b₁ = ∇_A*K + s with |K| ≤ O(1)(ε/a_j)C₀ε₁a_j^{−2}, through the covariance identity of *The artifact through covariances.* *The gauge-fixing terms (derived, not reviewed).* With the currents of RG I (3.13), the pair-lift current is Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*(J(W) + D_Aφ_A), and run B's current at e^{iξℒ𝐀^A}ι_Yc^A is its (3.11) current plus D_Bφ_B, with φ_A := R^A_jD_A*𝐀^A at c^A, φ_B := R^B_jD_B*ℒ𝐀^A at ι_Yc^A, and R^X_j = 1 − P^X, P^X := G′Q′*(Q′G′²Q′*)^{−1}Q′G′, PBG's R of the scale-j functions at run X's background, for run B those of its generation j + 1, whose blocks are run A's j-blocks, as in the (W-δ) note's §11 (*The Sects. D–E form (111).*, the 𝔊 bullet). So δJ gains 𝔍 := Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*D_Aφ_A − D_Bφ_B = 𝔍₁ + 𝔍₂ + 𝔍₃, with Q₁ := Q̄₁(ι_Yc^A), the linear part of the one-step double bar at U^B = ι_Yc^A (that note's §3(b)), 𝔍₁ := (Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW) − Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A))*D_Aφ_A, 𝔍₂ := Q₁*D_Aφ_A − D_Bφ_B, and 𝔍₃ := (Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A) − Q₁)*D_Aφ_A. The pointwise entry: D_AR^A_jD_A* = D_AD_A* − D_AP^AD_A*, and D_A*D_A + D_AD_A* is Δ up to a local curvature term (p.272 l.21–22), so |D_Aφ_A| is bounded by the Laplace-type entries D*D and Δ of 𝐀^A, the curvature term, and the kernel bound of DPD* in PBG (3.49) (p.399 l.5–11) at the gradient entry, in all by C_φ(S_j + α₂)a_j^{−3}, C_φ := O(1)(1 + B₀)B₀ with the O(1) of PBG (3.49) and of the curvature term, a constant of d, L, and B₀, on the circle (3.15) ((δ), the entries of the field of W: S_j for 𝐀^A(0), ⅓α₂ for t_□δ𝐇_j). So the commutator −[γ, ·] of the transformed pair lift, which also acts on Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*D_Aφ_A, is at most 2sup|γ|C_{Q*} times that bound and carries (ε/a_j) through sup|γ|, as the part at W does. 𝔍₁: the plain linearised average reads its background through the transports along the paths of a block, so |𝔍₁| is at most O(1)C_{Q*} times the angle difference of ι_XW and ι_Yc^A on these paths times |D_Aφ_A| there. On X, ι_XW = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒′_H}ι_Xc^A with a_{j(x)}|𝐒′_H(x)| ≤ 4C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3}(σ̄_H(m(x)) + d_□(x)) + 4C^c_tε₁′e^{−½δ₀M₁L^j} (*The lift half at W.*, with the part 4C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3}d_□ of t_□δ𝐇_j added in its *The conditions.*), and ι_Xc^A agrees with ι_Yc^A on X up to the tail term, in the same field units (*The per-term lift change.*), so the angle difference is at most (ε/a_j)(4C_δ(ε₀)L^{4/3}(σ̄_H + ⅓α₂) + O(1)C^c_tε₁′), d_□ being ⅓α₂ in the units a_j of the term's layer on X ∩ Ω_j, and 𝔍₁ is a pointwise current term with the factor (ε/a_j), bounded directly, as the commutator is. 𝔍₃: by the Cauchy note's identity (ii), Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A) = Q₁ + D_Aθ up to the corrections of its *Transports.*, with θ_ℱ(y) := −(ε/L)L^{−d}Σ_{x∈B(y)}ℱ(Γ_{y,x}), so 𝔍₃ = θ*D_A*D_Aφ_A and ⟨ℱ, 𝔍₃⟩ = ⟨D_Aθ_ℱ, D_Aφ_A⟩_A. The coarse difference of θ_ℱ compares line sums along paths of at most dL fine bonds translated by one coarse spacing, so |D_Aθ_ℱ| ≤ dε sup|∇ℱ| over the paths of the two blocks, up to the transports; so 𝔍₃ is a divergence ∇*s₃^g with |s₃^g| ≤ O(1)dε|D_Aφ_A|, which carries (ε/a_j) by the pointwise entry and joins s₁^g below. 𝔍₂: by the adjoint of D_A*Q₁ = Q′₁D_B* + e_f (the Cauchy note's identity (iii) and *Functional Form.*, used in the (W-δ) note's 10.4, *DRD*: the identity.*, here at the global operators, with no cut terms of χ_A) and Q′₁Q′₁* = 1 (the Cauchy note's identity (i)), 𝔍₂ = D_Bρ_g + e_f*φ_A, with ρ_g := Q′₁*φ_A − φ_B = Q′₁*δr_g − (1 − Q′₁*Q′₁)φ_B and δr_g := φ_A − Q′₁φ_B, the algebra of β_gf there. Paired with a field ℱ of run B, ⟨ℱ, e_f*φ_A⟩ = ⟨e_f(ℱ), φ_A⟩ is a divergence ∇*s₁^g plus s₀^g, with |s₁^g| ≤ C_ld sup_{cell}ε|D_Aφ_A| + C_{5L}C_trε²|F||φ_A| and |s₀^g| ≤ C_{5L}C_trε|F||φ_A| (there), which carry (ε/a_j) by the pointwise entry. With τ_g := D_A*𝐀^A, I′ the smooth interpolation of the (W-δ) note's §11 (its l.797), and ℒ′ = Q′₁* the scalar lift, Q′₁ℒ′ = 1 (its l.356), δr_g = (Q′₁P^Bℒ′ − P^A)τ_g − Q′₁R^B[(I′ − ℒ′)τ_g + ι_τ], ι_τ := (D_B*ℒ − I′D_A*)𝐀^A. The first term is the P part of the 𝔊 bullet's defect: through the walk expansions (3.90) and (3.98) it reduces to the coarse-side defects of G′_□, C_□, and Q′ of that note's §4(d), 10.1, and 10.3, telescoped as in its §7, per walk term and pointwise, at the fixed backgrounds c^A and ι_Yc^A, so it carries 𝔡_H ≤ C_𝔡L^{−γ_Hj_min} (*The field difference.* in (iii)) times sup|τ_g|, at real and at complex s. The other terms of δr_g and (1 − Q′₁*Q′₁)φ_B are oscillations: ι_τ is the D*-intertwining of the lift, of relative size O(L)(ε/a_j)^β on a C^{1,β} field (the 𝔊 bullet); (I′ − ℒ′)τ_g and (1 − Q′₁*Q′₁)R^B(I′τ_g + ι_τ) are bounded by the covariant oscillations of τ_g and ι_τ over the range of I′ and over a block, and by that of P^B(I′τ_g + ι_τ), whose gradient PBG (3.49) bounds by O(1)B₀a_j^{−1} times its argument. At real (𝐔, s, t_□) the Hölder entries of 𝐀^A(0) and of t_□δ𝐇_j (*Real points.* above) bound these terms by O(1)L(1 + B₀)²(Lε/a_j)^{β_o} times the gradient entry O(1)B₀(S_j + α₂)a_j^{−2} ((δ), the entries of the field of W); at the complex points of *Complex points.* above, on the disc there, they are at most O(1)L(1 + B₀)² times it, and the two-constants theorem gives O(1)L(1 + B₀)²(Lε/a_j)^{2β_o/3}. So |ρ_g| ≤ O(1)L(1 + B₀)³(𝔡_H + L(ε/a_j)^{2β_o/3})(S_j + α₂)a_j^{−2}, and sup|τ_g| ≤ O(1)B₀(S_j + α₂)a_j^{−2}, for |t_□| ≤ r̄/5. The bound: 𝔍₂ + 𝔍₃ = D_Bρ_g + ∇*(s₁^g + s₃^g) + s₀^g has the form of δ in *The divergence part.* of (iii), a gradient and a divergence of fields with pointwise bounds plus a pointwise part, in its form with S_j + α₂ for σ̄_H(m) and a_j for a_m ((δ), the entries of the field of W); the template uses σ̄_H(m) only through σ̄_H(m) ≤ α_{0,m}, and S_j + α₂ ≤ C_αα_{0,j} on X ∩ Ω_j and (S_j + α₂)a_m/a_j ≤ C_αα_{0,m} on a finer layer m < j, with C_α of *The scale-j segment.*, so it applies with the move multiplied by C_α, with the relative factor ϱ := ϑ_gf := O(1)L(1 + B₀)³(𝔡_H + L(ε/a_j)^{2β_o/3} + (C_ld + C_{5L}C_tr)(ε/a_j)), the term C_{5L}C_tr(ε/a_j) from s₀^g and the second part of s₁^g, with |F| ≤ α_{0,j}a_j^{−2} ≤ a_j^{−2} by the class condition (CRE (2.34)). The functionals of 𝔍₂ + 𝔍₃ of the three kinds are bounded as in that template, the first kind with D_B and ∇ moved onto H through PBG (3.133), the second and the gradients of the third with the loss L^{β′}L^{β′k}, which ϑ̂_H absorbs. The bounds of D: the data of the two runs agree up to the one-step remainder c₁ (§11's composition, as in *The data segment.* of (iii), Q₁ℒ = 1), and at equal data the two runs' scale-j fields are the field difference of *The field difference.* of (iii) at the level j in place of k, with the operators of the generation j of each run (run B's generation j + 1, on the same blocks), whose forms (c) and fixed points carry 𝔡_H and c₁ the factor L^{−j(x)} ≤ L^{−j_min}.
