The n-th term of the telescoped series is a sum of 2n + 1 terms, each with one factor replaced by its defect and the others taken from either run, so it is at most (2n + 1)(1 + 𝔡_H)^{2n}𝔡_H times the n-th term of Bałaban's decay series, which keeps the walk decay. *The nonlinear part of 𝐇_j.* 𝐇_j is not linear: by VAR (179)–(180) and (184)–(188) (p.306, p.308) its derivative carries the solution 𝔄₀ of the linear equation (184). Its run difference Δ₀′ solves the τ-derivative of the identity of the (W-δ) note's *The fixed point 𝐀₀.*, Δ₀′ = −K_BΔ₀′ + S₁ + ⋯ + S₅, with K_B := G̃^B(δ²V^B/δA′²)(A′^B), small by VAR (187), and with the sources S₁, the form (c) for H₀ applied to the data vector; S₂, the third derivative of V^B along the field difference A′^B − ℒA′^A applied to run A's derivative; S₃, the form (c) for G̃; and S₄ := −ℒG̃^Aσ′, σ′ the Hessian of V^B∘ℒ − V^A in the direction of run A's derivative. On |τ| = R with R|b| = ε_d the data stay below twice the data parameter, where the conditions of *The field difference.* hold for γ small, so |D(τ)| ≤ C_H⁰ϑ_H·O(1)B₀·2ε_d there, and Cauchy's estimate gives |D′(0)| and a_j|∇D′(0)| ≤ O(1)B₀C_H⁰ϑ_H|b| pointwise. *Without the factor ϑ_H.* Each run's derivative has the decay of VAR (190) (p.308 l.44) from the support of b. The pointwise minimum of the two bounds is at most their geometric mean, ϑ_H^{1/2} times a bound with half the decay of (190), which is not Bałaban's rate E, so the bound goes through weights instead. *The weighted rerun.* In the case dist^{(ξ)}(X, □) ≥ M(L^jη)^{−1} of (3.15)–(3.17) (in the second case of RG I (3.5) E = 0, and *Without decay.* gives the bound), the τ-derivative problem is linear in b with fixed backgrounds, and it is rerun in a norm weighted from □, as Bałaban obtains (190) from (189) through the Neumann series (188) (VAR p.308 l.23–44). Its summation over X, □′, j, and □ is RG II's (p.9 l.16–26) at the rate ½δ_w in place of ½δ₀: the sum over □′ costs O(1)δ_w^{−d} in place of O(1)δ₀^{−d}, and e^{−½δ_wM(L^jη)^{−1}} ≤ (2/(eδ_wM))L^jη gives the factor L^jη that controls the sum over j (p.9 l.20–21), with 2/(eδ_wM) in place of 2/(eδ₀M), so the sum is at most K_w times (1.68), K_w := O(1)(δ₀/δ_w)^{d+1}, a constant of d, L, and δ_min. So the scale-j segment contributes at most C_Hϑ̂_H times Bałaban's bound, after the summation, a positive power of L^{−j_min}, summable in k, as the other pieces of (δ).
          - The pointwise size. δJ has no small factor pointwise: the coefficients of the covariance identity carry the fixed pattern of the fine plaquettes inside a coarse one, so ∇_A*K costs ε^{−1} and |δJ| is not below the size ř_ja_j^{−3} of the current itself: at real points it is at most O(1)C_{Q*}(ř_j + LC_δ′(a_j/ε)^{1−β_o})a_j^{−3} (*Real points.* above), and on the disc of *Complex points.* at most M_δ (*Where the current enters.* below). A Cauchy estimate in the pointwise domain of CRE (2.36) gives no decay in the layer, and a shift of the data that makes Q₁^{pl}*J(W) the own current of a nearby lift doesn't work either, since it moves the own current by the same pointwise amount. The bound uses the divergence form at the level of the terms.
          - Where the current enters. By RG I p.261 l.37–41 the current "appear[s] in expansions of the action, and in expressions defining propagators". In PBG the regularity condition (3.36) is used only through |H*J| ≤ O(1)Mα₀(L^jη)^{−3} in the coefficient of Δ^{(2)} ((3.136), p.422 l.47–51) and through the smallness of the J-pairing in the almost-invariance (3.117) of the quadratic form (p.419 l.8–13); both pair J with a field, H in the first and i[λ(b_−), A(b)] − i[A(b), R_bλ(b_+)] − i[λ(b_−), (Dλ)(b)] in the second. Moving the derivative of ∇_A*K onto the field needs ∇H, which PBG (3.133) bounds, and in (3.117) the first differences of A and the second differences of λ. PBG uses (3.117) at λ = G′RD*A ((3.119)–(3.120), p.419 l.24–36), and the J-pairings of p.421 (l.9, l.19, and l.21, with HC^{(2)}, and l.41) are of the same two kinds. So is the pairing ⟨H₁D̃^{(2)}(B), J⟩ in the covariance (3.155) (p.427), of the first kind, H₁*J against a quadratic polynomial in B. In the composite walks the first differences of A land on the neighbouring G factors through the entry ∇G of (3.42), and the second differences of G′RD*A are bounded by the crude lattice Hölder bound at the exponent β′, as for ∇D_Aω^A in the (W-δ) note's §11 (*The term G̃Δ^{(2)}H₀B.*), so the (3.117) pairing carries (ε/a_j)^{1−β′}. At the real decoupled W, which is not a local minimiser, K is bounded through the oscillation, |K| ≤ O(1)osc_pX ≤ O(1)C_δ′(ε/a_j)^{β_o}a_j^{−2} (*Real points.* above), and ∇b₁ paired with a field carries the factor of K (the (W-δ) note's §11, *The term G̃Δ^{(2)}H₀B.*). So in the first pairing |H*δJ| ≤ O(1)B₀C_{Q*}C_δ′(ε/a_j)^{β_o}a_j^{−3}, and the (3.117) pairing carries (ε/a_j)^{β_o−β″}, with the crude lattice Hölder exponent β″ ∈ (0, 1/20] of the second differences (W-δ §8, stage 0), at which the crude lattice Hölder bound holds as at every exponent in (0, 1). The third kind, the images 𝔊δJ, G̃δJ, and GP₀*δJ in |·|_{(−1)} and |∇·|_{(−2)}, is bounded in the same way: the values pair K and ∇b₁ with the first differences of the kernels, which PBG's Theorem 3.13 bounds with B₀ (as VAR (117) uses it), so they are at most O(1)B₀C_{Q*}C_δ′(ε/a_j)^{β_o}, and the gradients pair them with the second differences, through the crude lattice Hölder bound, with the factor (ε/a_j)^{β_o−β″}. These real bounds hold at every point of the real diameter of *Complex points.* above, real class points at the full radii, real s with |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ₁}, and real t_□ with |t_□| ≤ r̄, since *Real points.* reads s and t_□ only through the bounds of VAR (190), which Proposition 9 gives on the whole polydisc, and the class point only through the class conditions at the full radii. At complex points all three are analytic on the disc of *Complex points.*, and there they are bounded through the pointwise size of δJ = Q₁^{pl}(ι_XW)*J(W) − J^B(ι_XW). On the disc |J(W)| ≤ ř_ja_j^{−3}, by the class at W and the entries of 𝐀^A on the circle (3.15), as in *The part at W.*, and |J^B(ι_XW)| ≤ M_c (*Complex points.*). So |δJ| ≤ M_δ := C_{Q*}ř_ja_j^{−3} + M_c ≤ O(1)C_{Q*}Lř_j(a_j/ε)a_j^{−3}. The first kind is then at most O(1)B₀M_δ, by the kernel bound (3.133) of H summed by Lemma 2.1 of [4]. The second is at most M_δ times the field norms with which PBG bounds the (3.117) pairing, linearly in the bound Mα₀ of the pointwise current (PBG (3.131), p.422 l.3–10). The third is at most B₀a_j³M_δ in |·|_{(−1)} and |∇·|_{(−2)}, by Theorem 3.13 of PBG as VAR (117) uses it. These disc bounds lose a_j/ε, against the gains (ε/a_j)^{β_o} and (ε/a_j)^{β_o−β″} of the real bounds. With the harmonic measure < 1/3 as there, the two-constants theorem gives m^{2/3}M^{1/3}. For the first kind and the values of the third this is at most O(1)B₀C_{Q*}C_J‴(ε/a_j)^{(2β_o−1)/3}, in the units a_j^{−3} for the first kind, with C_J‴ := C_δ′^{2/3}(Lř_j)^{1/3}. For the second kind and the gradients of the third it is the same with the factor (ε/a_j)^{(2(β_o−β″)−1)/3}. Both exponents are positive, since β_o − β″ > ½ by the choice of the exponents (W-δ §8, stage 0): β ≥ 3/4 gives β_o = min(β, 1 − 2ε₀) ≥ 3/4, since stage 0's ε₀ < 1/125 gives 1 − 2ε₀ > 3/4, β″ ≤ 1/20, and so (2(β_o − β″) − 1)/3 ≥ 2/15. So no analytic continuation of the covariance identity is needed for the smoothed current. Layer by layer: these bounds are those of the points of X ∩ Ω_j, the term's layer of the scale j, on which CRE's class conditions hold uniformly at n = j ((2.34)–(2.39), p.261 l.24), in its units a_j. At a point of a finer layer m < j of X they hold with a_m for a_j: there J(c^A) is at most r_{0,m}a_m^{−3} ≤ (1 + β₀)r_{0,j}a_m^{−3}, by r_{0,n} = α_{0,n}/(8B₃) (the Cauchy note's l.329) and α_{0,m} ≤ (1 + β₀)α_{0,j} (*The quadratic form.* below), and the entries of the field of W are at most their bounds above in the units a_m, since a_m < a_j; the base's oscillation is (ε/a_m)^{β_o} at the layer of the point, its bounds being weighted layer by layer (the (W-δ) note's *The base at the layer j.*, its l.947); and the plaquette condition of run B's class at the layer m gives M_c with r_{0,m} and a_m. So |J(W)| ≤ 2ř_ja_m^{−3}, and the two-constants bounds hold with (ε/a_m)^{c_J} in the units a_m^{−3}, with 2ř_j for ř_j, absorbed in the O(1). A term of a finer scale j_T < j reads δJ at a point of the history's layer m in its own layer ℓ_T := min(m, j_T), with its kernels at the scale ℓ_T ≤ ℓ := min(m, j); in the units a_{ℓ_T} its real bounds carry at least the factor (a_{ℓ_T}/a_ℓ)^{2−β″} (the derivatives moved onto its kernels cost a_{ℓ_T}^{−1}, and the crude lattice Hölder bound loses (a_ℓ/a_{ℓ_T})^{β″}), and its disc bounds the factor (a_{ℓ_T}/a_ℓ)³ of the conversion of units, so the bounds at ℓ hold for it in its units a_{ℓ_T}^{−3}. Terms of a coarser scale do not read δJ: it enters only the scale-j term's current argument, so it is read by that term, by its old terms through (3.13), and by the large-field integrals of the steps k′ ≤ j inside them.
          - *The enlarged domain (derived, not reviewed).* The terms are analytic in the pointwise current, and the next step re-reads them with the current argument (L^{j−1}η)³𝐉 + Δ_𝐔A − P₁A + 𝐅₁(𝐔, A) (RG I (3.13), p.272).
            - Where Bałaban puts 𝐉. The second-order derivative "appears only in the functions" (1.8), which "appear in expansions of the action, and in expressions defining propagators", and 𝐉 replaces them (RG I p.261 l.34–46). The operators of VAR (179)–(180) take (𝐔, 𝐉) in place of (U_{k+1}, J_{k+1}) (RG I p.273 l.1–3). CRE's 𝐑- and 𝐁-terms take (𝐔, 𝐉) "in the same way" (CRE p.260 l.25, l.54), and CRE's class reads 𝐉 only through (2.36); its current conditions (2.37) concern the currents 𝐉_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)) of the local minimisers of the data, which do not read 𝐉 (p.261 l.11–22). LFR I introduces no further dependence: the transcription has no occurrence of (𝐔, 𝐉) outside these. In LFR II "only the quadratic form in B and the term V(X^∼) depend on (𝐔, 𝐉)" in (1.71) (p.379 l.28), and the extended configurations of (1.61)–(1.63) read (𝐔, 𝐉) through the localised 𝐇_k (p.374 l.38–42, p.375 l.12–21). The expansions of the action pair 𝐉 with fields built by H, as −2⟨H_{1,j}hC̃^{(2)}(B), J⟩ in RG I (1.6) (p.261 l.7), and RG I's extension replaces J_{k+1} "in all propagators and operators by the variable 𝐉" (p.269 l.43–p.270 l.2). Every occurrence of the current in PBG pp.417–428 and in VAR is one of three kinds. The first kind pairs it with a field built by H or H₁: ⟨HC^{(2)}(A), J⟩ = ⟨C^{(2)}(A), H*J⟩ (PBG p.421 l.9, l.19, l.21; p.422 l.37, l.47, bounded through |H*J| ≤ O(1)Mα₀(L^jη)^{−3}, p.422 l.50), ⟨H₁D̃^{(2)}(B), J⟩ (p.427 l.33; p.428 l.4, l.38), and VAR's ⟨HD(A′), J⟩ and ⟨H(δ/δA′)D₃(A′), J⟩ (VAR p.289–291, where p.291 l.10 sums |J(b′)H(b′, c₁)|, a bound of (H*J)(c₁)). The second kind is the (3.117) pairing (PBG p.419 l.5–7, l.33; p.421 l.41; VAR p.299 l.5). The third kind is the image of the current under VAR's propagators in its fixed-point equations, 𝔊J = G₁𝔓*J (VAR p.294 l.21–27), GP₀*J, and G̃J (VAR p.298–300), in the norms |·|_{(−1)} and |∇·|_{(−2)}, which VAR bounds by B₀|J|_{(−3)} through Theorem 3.13 of PBG (VAR (117), p.295 l.12); at the critical backgrounds of p.305–306 𝔓*J = P₀*J = 0. The J of PBG pp.424–426 is a source variable of the Gaussian identities, not the current. Three further occurrences are identities or values. First, PBG's identities (3.117) (p.419 l.5–7) follow from gauge invariance and hold for the current J = D*η^{−2}Im ∂U of a configuration (p.419 l.2, l.10). They are used once, to pass from the representation (3.119) of ⟨A, Δ_πA⟩, which contains no current, to (3.120) (p.419 l.28), and the only other D*J of pp.417–428 is that of the source variable (pp.425–426). At a current variable the two representations differ by −⟨A, Δ_π′(𝐉 − J(𝐔))A⟩. Read with (3.119), Δ_π does not depend on the current; read with (3.120), as RG I's replacement "in all propagators and operators" gives it, it depends on 𝐉 linearly through pairings of the second kind. In either reading δ enters Δ_π only through the second kind, and D*J = 0 is not applied to the current variable. Second, VAR's first-order term ⟨A′, J⟩ ((74), (81), (84); p.289 l.47, p.290 l.11, l.36, l.51) enters the fixed-point equation as 𝔊J, the third kind ((110)–(111), p.294 l.21–27). Its value at the solution is read by no term. In RG I's expansion (2.6) of the action at exp iη𝐇_k(B′)U_{k+1}, the pairing ⟨𝐀₁, J⟩ of the fixed point with the current is dropped, "By Eq. (171) [15] we have ⟨𝐀₁, J⟩ = 0" (RG I p.266 l.32), since VAR (171) is 𝔓*(U_k)J = 0 at the critical U_k (VAR p.305 l.26–28) and 𝐀₁ = 𝔓𝐀₁ lies in the range of the projection 𝔓, the projection onto the configurations with QA′ = 0 and RD*A′ = 0, orthogonal for the scalar product defined by Δ₁ + D*RD + aQ*Q (VAR p.293 l.24–26), since QA₁ = 0 and RD*A₁ = 0 by (102) (l.30). The extension is made in the resulting expression (2.8) (p.266 l.41), "replacing the function J_{k+1} in all propagators and operators by the variable 𝐉" (p.269 l.43), and (2.8) contains the current only through ⟨H₁B′, J⟩, of the first kind, through Δ₁ = Δ_π − Δ_π^{(2)} (VAR (79), p.290 l.19), whose current enters Δ_π^{(2)} through ⟨HC^{(2)}(A′), J⟩, of the first kind, and Δ_π through the second kind (the first item above), and through V(H₁B′), whose current enters through −⟨HD₃, J⟩, of the first kind (VAR (80), p.290 l.27), and through the fixed point 𝐀₁, of the third kind. So the extended terms are defined by (2.8) and contain neither ⟨𝐀₁, 𝐉⟩ nor a part quadratic in the current through 𝔊. Third, the criticality (141) (VAR p.299 l.30–35), ⟨δA′, J⟩ = 0 at the critical U_k, with ⟨A′, J⟩ = 0 and (142), shows that the real critical configuration is a minimum. The fixed point and its bounds come from (111) and (116)–(118), which read J only through 𝔊J, and at 𝐉₀ + δ the fixed point is that of (111) with the perturbed current. So every term is a function of 𝐔, of finitely many smoothed functionals of 𝐉 of these three kinds at its own step, and of the current arguments (3.13) of its old terms, and Bałaban's estimates use the pointwise bounds of the current only to bound these functionals.
            - The induction. For a constant c ≤ ½ let 𝒟^c be the set of pairs (𝐔, 𝐉₀ + δ) with (𝐔, 𝐉₀) in the class and δ a current on the lattice of 𝐉₀ whose smoothed functionals, at every point, for the term and, through (3.13), for its old terms, each read in its own layer ℓ_T := min(m, j_T) at a point of the history's layer m, j_T the reading term's scale (CRE's class conditions (2.34)–(2.39) are stated on X ∩ (Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1}) for n < j_T and on X ∩ Ω_{j_T} at n = j_T, p.261 l.24), are at most cθ_{ℓ_T}, θ_n := C_θα_{0,n}, in the units a_{ℓ_T}^{−3} for the first and second kinds and in |·|_{(−1)} and |∇·|_{(−2)} for the third. Here θ_n is the bound of the same functional of the class current on the layer n, at most α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3} there (CRE (2.36), p.261 l.19, the multiscale form of RG I's |𝐉| < γ₀ of (1.14), p.262 l.37, with CRE's α_{0,n}, not PBG's α₀): C_θ := O(1)(1 + B₀) covers the kernel bound (3.133) of H summed by Lemma 2.1 of [4] for the first kind, the field norms of the (3.117) pairing, which is linear in the pointwise bound of the current (PBG (3.131), p.422 l.3–10), for the second, and B₀|·|_{(−3)} of VAR (117) (p.295 l.12–13) for the third. So cθ_n is c times the bound that the class gives each functional on the layer n, and every bound of these functionals that Bałaban's proofs use is at least θ_n: CRE's and LFR II's through α_{0,n} itself (CRE (2.36); LFR II p.379 l.31), PBG's O(1)Mα₀ (p.422 l.50 for the first kind, (3.131) for the second), since C_θα̂₀ ≤ Mα₀ for γ small, α̂₀ = max_{m≤k}α_{0,m} → 0 while Mα₀ = α_J is fixed (W-δ §8, stage 4), and the current part B₀|𝐉|_{(−3)} ≤ B₀α̂₀ of VAR (117) for the third. Since α_{0,n} → 0 with γ, these thresholds are not fixed constants; what the transfer below uses is only that they are at most c times the class bounds of the same functionals, layer by layer. The constant c is chosen after α_J (W-δ §8, stage 4) and before c_R, C_gf, and C_H, which depend on it. 𝒟^c contains pairs outside Bałaban's spaces, since |𝐉₀ + δ| < γ₀ (RG I p.262 l.37) need not hold, so his statements do not apply to them as printed; what transfers is his proof, which reads the current only through these functionals (*Where Bałaban puts 𝐉.*). On 𝒟^c each functional of 𝐉₀ + δ is at most 1 + c ≤ 3/2 times its bound on the class, so each use of a bound of the current in his proof holds with that bound multiplied by 1 + c: PBG's, through H*J and the (3.117) pairing, under his smallness of Mα₀ (PBG p.396 l.32, "We will need α₀ so small that O(1)Mα₀ is still a sufficiently small number", for the class (3.35)–(3.36), whose (3.36) bounds |∂*∂A| by O(1)Mα₀(L^jη)^{−3}; used in Theorem 3.12, p.423 l.34, and at p.428 l.28) read at (1 + c)Mα₀ ≤ 2Mα₀ (W-δ §8, stage 4); VAR's, through (117)–(121), with (B₀ + cC_θ)α̂₀ ≤ ½ε₄ at ε₄ = 3B₀C₁^cB₃ε₁′ (§4(b), (T-π)), the third-kind functional of 𝐉₀ + δ being at most B₀α̂₀ + cC_θα̂₀, which holds for γ small since α̂₀/ε₁′ ≤ (C(M)R_kC_c)^{−1} → 0 (*The real points.*), which is what (118) uses of the current, and ε₄ ≤ a₄ is (T-c)'s threshold of Proposition 6, so the third kind is analytic up to VAR's radius a₄; RG I's and CRE's, through VAR's and PBG's operators (RG I p.269 l.43–p.270 l.2, CRE p.260 l.25, l.54) and through the old terms; and LFR II's large-field integrals, through σ (*The large-field integral.* below). The statement is joint analyticity: a term is analytic, with Bałaban's bound of the term, along every family (𝐔(z), 𝐉₀ + δ(z)) that depends analytically on complex parameters z and lies in 𝒟^c, so the configuration and the perturbation may move together and δ(z) need not be linear in z. If the old terms have this property, so does the new term. At the first step there are no old terms, and the step's own constructions read the current only through the functionals of *Where Bałaban puts 𝐉.*, so the property holds there. Under (3.13) the perturbation passes linearly, as (L^{j−1}η)³δ, while Δ_𝐔A stays in the pointwise part of the argument. No transport between lattices enters: all terms of a run, old and new, are functions on the run's one fine lattice, which RG I reads at the scale j as T_ξ, ξ = L^{−j}, "the continuous space T scaled properly" (RG I p.257 l.2–3), the term of the scale j reads the current (1.8) of its configuration on that lattice (p.261 l.37), and (3.12) identifies the current of U_{k+1} on the old term's lattice with (L^{j−1}η)³J_{k+1} (p.272 l.26–30), an identity of currents of one configuration on one lattice, so the factor (L^{j−1}η)³ is the conversion of the current's units between the two scales. So the old terms see the perturbation itself, at the same points and in the same divergence form, in their units, against the thresholds of their own layer ℓ_T, since CRE's class bounds are per layer ((2.34)–(2.39)) and its thresholds α_{0,n}(L^nξ)^{−3} (CRE (2.36)) are the bounds of the current in the term's units, which the same conversion relates. Bałaban's estimates use the pointwise bound (2.36) only to bound these functionals: PBG's and VAR's as listed in *Where Bałaban puts 𝐉.*; RG I's for the old current arguments through (3.13), covered by the induction; and RG I's Lemma 4 reads 𝐉 only through 𝐇_k(B′) (the Cauchy note's §5, *The near domain.*). Nothing compounds over the steps: the hypothesis on δ is the same at every step, its functionals per layer at most cθ_n, and the conclusion is Bałaban's bound of the term with his constants (RG I p.263 l.29–32), the factor 1 + c being taken by the smallness conditions above and not by the bound. The terms are analytic on 𝒟^c since they are given by Bałaban's constructions, fixed points, Neumann series, and convergent expansions, whose estimates hold uniformly there, so they are locally uniform limits of analytic functions. So they hold on 𝒟^c, at fixed and at moving configurations of the class alike, since the class conditions on the configuration and on the current are separate (RG I p.262), and the proof uses of δ nothing but the bounds of its functionals. For (δ), δ = uδJ: at a point of the history's layer m its smoothed functionals, read by a term of scale j_T ≤ j in its layer ℓ_T, are at most |u|·O(1)B₀C_{Q*}C_J‴(ε/a_ℓ)^{c_J}, ℓ := min(m, j), in the units a_{ℓ_T}^{−3} for the first two kinds and in |·|_{(−1)} and |∇·|_{(−2)} for the third, c_J := (2(β_o − β″) − 1)/3 ≥ 2/15, the smaller of the two exponents, by the two-constants bounds of *Where the current enters.* and their layer-by-layer reading there, so the pairs meet the thresholds cθ_{ℓ_T} for |u| ≤ R_ℓ := cθ_ℓ(O(1)(1 + β₀)²B₀C_{Q*}C_J‴)^{−1}(a_ℓ/ε)^{c_J}: for ℓ_T < ℓ, θ_{ℓ_T} ≥ θ_ℓ/((1 + β₀)²(ℓ − ℓ_T)^{1/2}), since g_ℓ ≤ (1 + β₀)(ℓ − ℓ_T)^{1/2}g_{ℓ_T} and (log g_ℓ^{−2})^{q₀} ≤ (1 + β₀)(log g_{ℓ_T}^{−2})^{q₀} (CRE (2.6) and the first row of (2.7), p.255 l.45 and l.55), and the finer reader's gain, at least (a_{ℓ_T}/a_ℓ) = L^{−(ℓ−ℓ_T)} in both bounds (*Where the current enters.*), covers (ℓ − ℓ_T)^{1/2}; with C_J‴ = C_δ′^{2/3}(Lř_j)^{1/3} of *Where the current enters.* and C_δ′ of *Real points.* (*The factor.* bounds them), and (δ) uses 𝒟_R := {(𝐔, 𝐉₀ + uδJ): |u| ≤ R} ⊂ 𝒟^c, with R the least of the R_ℓ over the points of the term's domain.
            - The factor. The history's layers of the term's domain and the term's scale j are ≥ j_min, so ℓ = min(m, j) ≥ j_min, with j_min ≥ k/2 − 1 ≥ n/2 − 1 on the young histories (*The condition C_Hϑ̂_H ≤ 1/6.* of (iii), k ≥ n). For R ≥ 2, Cauchy's estimate on |u| ≤ R bounds the current part, the difference of the term at u = 1 and u = 0, by 2(bound)/(R − 1) ≤ 4(bound)/R. Here C_J‴ = C_δ′^{2/3}(Lř_j)^{1/3} ≤ K_Jα_{0,k} with K_J := (1 + β₀)^{1/3}K_δ^{2/3}L^{1/3}, since ř_j = 2r_{0,j} + ⅓α₂ ≤ α_{0,j}, by r_{0,j} = α_{0,j}/(8B₃) (the Cauchy note's §5, its l.329) and α₂ = c₂α_{0,j} ≤ ½α_{0,j}, and α_{0,j} ≤ (1 + β₀)α_{0,k} for j ≤ k, since g ↦ g(log g^{−2})^{q₀} is increasing for g small and g_j ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k (CRE (2.6), p.255 l.46). And C_δ′ = C₀^cε₁″ + B₀S_j + α₂ ≤ K_δα_{0,k} with K_δ := (1 + β₀)(C₀^cC(M)C_ε + B₀ + 1): C₀^c is a constant of M and the stages (*Real points.*, from W-δ l.169 with C₀′ε₁ := K_ℬ(C₀ + C(ε₀))ε₁ + C_ℬC(β₂)ε₂ of W-δ l.980, whose C₀ is the Cauchy note's curvature constant and whose C₁ = L³, not CRE's prefactors); ε₁″ = C(M)ε′_k ≤ C(M)C_εα_{0,k} with C_ε := 2C_c(1 + β₀)(1 + C₁^{CRE}/C₀^{CRE}) for γ small, since α̂₀ ≤ (1 + β₀)α_{0,k} and α̂₁ ≤ (1 + β₀)α_{1,k} = (1 + β₀)(C₁^{CRE}/C₀^{CRE})α_{0,k} (*The real points.*; CRE (2.28) with the choice q₁ = q₀ of l.700; here C₀^{CRE} and C₁^{CRE} are CRE's prefactors C₀, C₁ of (2.28) (p.259 l.47–50), fixed with the classes, before c, c_R, n_R, and n₀′, as in W-δ l.1101), and ε_k + 25g_kb_k ≤ (1 + 25D_B)ε_k (l.446) with ε_k/α_{0,k} = (A₀/C₀^{CRE})(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀−q₀} → 0 (CRE (2.4), (2.28)); S_j ≤ ŝ_jr_{0,j} ≤ α_{0,j} for γ small ((δ), the entries of the field of W); and α₂ = c₂α_{0,j} ≤ α_{0,j}. The threshold of the layer ℓ ≤ k is θ_ℓ = C_θα_{0,ℓ} ≥ C_θα_{0,k}/((1 + β₀)²(k + 1)^{1/2}), by CRE (2.6) and the first row of (2.7) as in *The induction.* With ℓ ≥ j_min ≥ k/2 − 1, (a_ℓ/ε)^{c_J} = L^{c_Jℓ} ≥ L^{c_J(k/2−1)}, and (k + 1)^{1/2} ≤ C_½L^{c_Jk/4}, C_½ := sup_k(k + 1)^{1/2}L^{−c_Jk/4}, a constant of c_J and L, every R_ℓ, and so R, is at least c_RL^{c_J(k/4−1)}, c_R := cC_θ(O(1)(1 + β₀)⁴B₀C_{Q*}K_JC_½)^{−1}, a constant of the stages, independent of k, γ, and the volume: the factor R_k of ε₁′ does not enter, since C_δ′ reads the base at ε₁″. Let n_R be the least integer ≥ 2 with c_RL^{c_J(n_R/4−1)} ≥ 2, a constant of the stages, not of k, ε, or the volume. For n ≥ n_R, R ≥ 2, since k ≥ n, and the current part is at most 4c_R^{−1}L^{−c_J(k/4−1)} times the bound of the term, summable in k, as the other pieces of (δ). For n < n_R the difference of V(Y) is bounded as a whole, as in *Small n.*, and n₀′ is taken ≥ n_R, as it is ≥ n_H.
          - The large-field integral. LFR II introduces (𝐔, 𝐉) "in the usual way" (p.374 l.38, p.375 l.12–13), and its one further use is in the normalised large-field integral: the quadratic form in B and V(X^∼) are expanded to first order in A′ and 𝐉, and σ, the difference of the exponent at (𝐔, 𝐉) from its value at the regular configuration (U, 0), "the small term of the first order in A′, 𝐉" (𝐓′_k(X, (𝐔, 𝐉)) = 𝐓′_k(X, (U, 0))e^σ, p.380 l.5 and l.17), enters through e^{3 sup|σ|}, the supremum over the integration variables (p.379 l.28–34, p.380 l.6–22). Its printed bound is O(1)B₃²A₁²p₁²(g_k)|𝐁₀|(α_{0,k} + α_{1,k}), a pointwise bound of 𝐉 times the volume, and the enlarged domain needs sup|σ| ≤ O(1) on 𝒟^c. Bałaban states where 𝐉 enters σ: "For all these terms the expansion in A′, 𝐉 creates a connection between their localization domains and the set 𝐁₀ ∪ (Γ″_k ∩ X^∼), a connection with a proper exponential decay" (p.379 l.32–34). Here 𝐁₀ is the set of bonds of the large-field fluctuation variables B of (1.81) [IV] (p.360 l.41–42), |𝐁₀| ≤ (100MR_k)^dN² (p.361 l.12), and Γ″_k is the coarsest layer of the determining set 𝐁″_k = {Γ″_j}, whose layers (1.33) uses (p.365). The young pairs lie in the large-field region from the scale j₀ on (§1, *The integration variables.*), where the bonds 𝐁₀ lie, so δJ does reach that set, with its pointwise size of *The pointwise size.* above. σ has two parts (derived, not reviewed).
            - The quadratic form. It is −½⟨DH″_{1,k,X}B, ζDH″_{1,k,X}B⟩ (p.378, the definition of 𝐓′_k(X)), so 𝐉 is not paired with B; it enters through PBG's multiscale H″_{1,k,X} and through the background U^0_{k,X}(𝐔, 𝐉) of the form and D at it. The background reads 𝐉 only through the operators of the localised 𝐇_k, VAR (179)–(180) with (𝐔, 𝐉) (RG I p.273 l.1–3; LFR II p.374 l.38–42), so through the same two uses, and the source 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2} of (1.54) reads the current only through 𝐆″_k, a smoothed functional of the first kind, 𝐆″_k∇_A*K = (∇_A𝐆″_k)*K up to transport. Through H″_{1,k,X}, 𝐉 is read by the two uses of (3.36) above, the coefficient H*𝐉 of Δ^{(2)} and the pairing of (3.117), which carry (ε/a_ℓ)^{c_J} at the term's layer ℓ, as above. Bałaban's bound of this part of σ, O(1)B₃²A₁²p₁²(g_k)|𝐁₀|(α_{0,k} + α_{1,k}) (p.379 l.28–31), reads the current only through these functionals, linearly in their class bounds, α_{0,k} being the threshold of 𝐉 in CRE (2.36) (p.261 l.19) on the coarsest layer, the one that the term α_{0,k} of the printed bound carries; the thresholds of the finer layers are at most (1 + β₀)α_{0,k}, since by CRE (2.4) and (2.28) α_{0,n} = C₀g_n(log g_n^{−2})^{q₀}, g ↦ g(log g^{−2})^{q₀} is increasing for small g, and g_n ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k for n ≤ k (CRE (2.6)). On 𝒟^c each such functional of 𝐉₀ + δ is at most its class bound plus cθ_n, so at most 1 + c times the class bound (*The induction.*), and H″_{1,k,X} and the operators of 𝐇_k are PBG's and VAR's operators at (𝐔, 𝐉₀ + δ), analytic in the first and second kinds up to PBG's O(1)Mα₀ and in the third up to VAR's a₄ (*The induction.*). So Bałaban's bound of this part holds on 𝒟^c multiplied by (1 + c)(1 + o(1)), the factor 1 + o(1) since the background U^0_{k,X}(𝐔, 𝐉) moves with δ as U_k⁰ does (*V(X^∼).* below), at real and complex points alike, since it is read from the bounds of the functionals, through derivative bounds of the operators over PBG's and VAR's fixed radii, and not from a Cauchy estimate over the thresholds cθ_n. Since σ is the whole difference from (U, 0), the bound runs along the path t(𝐉₀ + δ), 0 ≤ t ≤ 1 (with A′ scaled alike), on which t𝐉₀ is a class current and tδ has its functionals at most cθ_n, so the derivative bounds hold along it. Here |𝐁₀| ≤ O(1)M^dR_k^{d+2} (p.361 l.12–13, with N ≤ R_k), and the same holds at every step k′ ≤ k of the run, for the large-field integrals inside the old terms, with g_{k′}, R_{k′}, 𝐁₀ of the step k′, and the thresholds θ_n of the layers that the step k′ reads, each functional read in its layer (*The induction.*).
            - V(X^∼). Its terms are old terms 𝐄(X, U_k⁰), read at the configuration U_k⁰(𝐔, 𝐉) of (1.61)–(1.63), which maps Ũ^c_k(Y₄, α̃₀, α̃₁) into Ũ″^c_k(Y₁, α̃₀, α̃₁), a space inside the analyticity domains of all the old terms ((1.65), p.375 l.16–35). U_k⁰ reads 𝐉 only through the 𝐇-functions 𝐇_k″, 𝐇_{𝐁₁}, and 𝐇_k of (1.61)–(1.64) and the gauge transformations given by them ((106) [12], p.373 l.29–30). (𝐔, 𝐉) are introduced "in the usual way into the localized function 𝐇_k in (1.63)", while M^·(U_k), V″, and V_Λ^{(A)} become the functions M^·(𝐔) and V_Λ^{(A)}(M^k(𝐔)) of 𝐔 (p.374 l.37–41). "The remaining fields in (1.62) are replaced by functions of 𝐔 in the usual way" (p.375 l.6–7). The "other expressions" of (1.61) (p.375 l.11) take the variables in the usual way, the current in the propagators and operators (RG I p.269 l.43–p.270 l.2), here those of 𝐇_k″. The backgrounds U(𝐁, M^·(𝐔)) of (1.61)–(1.63) are minimisers for data that are functions of 𝐔, and their currents 𝐉_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)) are functions of 𝐔 (CRE (2.37), p.261 l.10–22). So the current component of U_k⁰, which RG I (3.11) gives at these backgrounds as their current plus D*D𝐀 + 𝐅(U, 𝐀) for the perturbations 𝐀 of the 𝐇-functions (RG I p.272 l.14–24), reads 𝐉 only through the 𝐇-functions too (inferred from U_k⁰ = U(𝐁″_k(Y₁), M^·(U_k⁰)), a minimiser of data built from M^·(U₁), V″, M^·(U₀^{(AL)}), and the 𝐇-factors (p.373 l.20), from p.374 l.37–41, and from the usual way of RG I p.269 l.43; Bałaban does not print this component, and the alternative of RG I (3.12)–(3.13), where the old terms read 𝐉 directly, is excluded by Bałaban's explicit replacement instructions for (1.61)–(1.63) (p.374 l.37–41, p.375 l.6–7), under which no current of the backgrounds is left to replace except inside the operators, not by a printed formula; under that alternative the old terms would read (L^{j−1}η)³(𝐉₀ + δ) directly, which the induction covers through (3.13) (*The induction.*), so the conclusion does not depend on the exclusion). The 𝐇-functions are VAR's (179)–(180) with (𝐔, 𝐉) in their operators (RG I p.273 l.1–3), so they read 𝐉 through the smoothed functionals of *The induction.*, analytically up to PBG's O(1)Mα₀ and VAR's a₄, the fixed radii of the quadratic form's part. Bałaban's bound of V's part of σ, "the same bound as above, with a different constant O(1)" (p.379 l.35–36), is read from these functionals linearly in their class bounds (a reconstruction of p.379 l.32–36, which prints no derivation, with the structure of the quadratic form's part), through the exponentially decaying connections to 𝐁₀ ∪ (Γ″_k ∩ X^∼) (l.32–35) and the bounds of the old terms on Ũ″^c_k, as the quadratic form's part is. Two facts carry it to 𝒟^c (derived, not reviewed). First, the points U_k⁰(𝐔, t(𝐉₀ + δ)), 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, on the path of the quadratic form's part, lie in Bałaban's own space Ũ″^c_k(Y₁, α̃₀, α̃₁), so the old terms are read inside their printed domains, with their printed bounds (p.375 l.29–31); the descending β-factors of Bałaban's spaces (LFR I (1.64)–(1.69), p.190 l.25, p.191 l.32–43, with LFR I's β ≤ ¼ of p.198 l.16, not the Hölder exponent β of stage 0) are fixed fractions of α_{0,m}, which his own descending construction uses (p.191 l.37–42); the argument below uses no room from them. Let K_𝐇 be the bound of an 𝐇-function, pointwise with its exponential decay, B₀ times its datum times e^{−δ₀d}, on the currents whose functionals are below the fixed radii r := min(PBG's O(1)Mα₀, VAR's a₄) (*The induction.*), the form of the bounds that Bałaban's proofs use, at most a fixed constant times them. Let θ̂ := max_nθ_n ≤ C_θα̂₀, with θ̂ < r/2 for γ small (θ̂ → 0 while r is fixed). Then on the line t𝐉₀ + ztδ, |z| ≤ R := r/(2cθ̂), the functionals stay below θ̂ + r/2 < r, and the Cauchy estimate over this fixed radius bounds the difference of the 𝐇-function at t(𝐉₀ + δ) from its value at the class point (𝐔, t𝐉₀) by K_𝐇/(R − 1) = 2K_𝐇cθ̂/(r − 2cθ̂) ≤ 4K_𝐇cθ̂/r for R ≥ 2, pointwise with the same decay, a relative O(cθ̂/r) that tends to 0 with γ; the same holds for the current component D*D𝐀 + 𝐅(U, 𝐀), through VAR's second representation (180) of the 𝐇-functions (RG I p.272 l.19–24). The 𝐇-functions are nested (𝐇″_k reads 𝐉 also through its background, U₁ → 𝐇_{𝐁₁} → U_{k,Λ} → 𝐇_k); on the disc the inner ones move by at most 2K_𝐇, fields of the size of their bounds, O(ε_k) by the bounds of LFR I p.199 l.36–50, inside the "large domain of analyticity" of the extended functions (p.374 l.40–41), so the composites are analytic on the disc and the estimate applies to them, with K_𝐇 the composite's bound. The proof of (1.65) is not printed ("we do not repeat them here", p.375 l.24–27); by its model, the proof of (1.89) [IV] with ε_j replaced by α_{0,j}, it reads the 𝐇-functions only through their bounds (a reconstruction): the budgets αε_h on the cubes with fixed α (α = 1/12 in (1.91), (1.94), (1.95), p.199 l.16; α ≤ 1/8 at p.200 l.21), the size of 𝐇″_k on Λ^∼, where the regularity is "similar to the properties of the configuration U″_k" (p.375 l.13–14), and the smallness on the boundary layers, "very small, because of the localization of the argument, and the exponential decay" (p.374 l.41–p.375 l.4, l.7–10). The slack is fixed: the bounds proved for the 𝐇-functions carry the vanishing factors exp(−R_h) and (A₁/A₀)p₁(g_j)/p₀(g_j) against the budget αε_j (LFR I p.199 l.36–50), and (1.95) gives ½(1 + L^{−h}αε_h) + α + 8α²ε_h = 7/12 + o(1) < ¾ at α = 1/12 (p.199 l.3–7, with (1.96) at l.13). The conditions of Ũ″^c_k on the currents 𝐉 and J_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)), on A′ and ∇A′, and on Λ^∼ (LFR I (1.64)(i), p.190 l.21–27) receive the 𝐇-contributions through budgets of the same kind, fixed fractions of their thresholds (a reconstruction), against which the 𝐇-bounds carry the same vanishing factors. So each budget holds at t(𝐉₀ + δ), where the 𝐇-functions change by the relative O(cθ̂/r), for γ small, and the points U_k⁰(𝐔, t(𝐉₀ + δ)) lie in Ũ″^c_k. Second, along the path the functionals of t(𝐉₀ + δ) are at most 1 + c times their class bounds (*The induction.*). So V's part of σ is at most (1 + c)(1 + o(1)) times Bałaban's bound of it on 𝒟^c, at real and complex points alike, the factor 1 + o(1) since its coefficients are evaluated at the moved points, by the derivative bounds of the operators over the fixed radii and the old terms' printed bounds on Ũ″^c_k. No Cauchy estimate over the thresholds cθ_n enters, so their size, which tends to 0 with γ, plays no role, and the bound O(1)Σ_j|Γ_j^0 ∩ Y| of (1.69), which is not small, is not used.
          So on 𝒟^c sup|σ| is at most (1 + c)(1 + o(1)) ≤ 2 times its printed bound, the factor 1 + o(1) from the coefficients at the moved points U_k⁰(𝐔, t(𝐉₀ + δ)) (*V(X^∼).*). The printed bound is small (p.379 l.31) and tends to 0 with γ uniformly in the step k′ ≤ k: it is O(1)B₃²A₁²C₀^{CRE}M^dR_{k′}^{d+2}p₁²(g_{k′})q₀(g_{k′})g_{k′}, which is g_{k′} times a power of log g_{k′}^{−2} by CRE (2.5) and p₁(g) = (log g^{−2})^{p₁} (LFR I p.183 l.2), and g_{k′} ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k (CRE (2.6)). So |σ| ≤ 1, Bałaban's estimate of it in (1.92) (p.388 l.24), holds on 𝒟^c for γ small, e^{3 sup|σ|} keeps Bałaban's bound, and the normalised large-field integral is analytic on 𝒟^c with Bałaban's bound, jointly in the configuration and δ as in *The induction.*
        - The per-term lift change. U = ι_X(c^A) against ι_Yc^A, with ℒ^X against ℒ_ι, is the lift change of (i): the two lifts agree on X up to the tail of ι_Y, of the size of the tail term of *The per-term lift ι_X.* (derived, not reviewed), with the status given there.
      - r^s_z = 0: the brackets of (1.60) are differences of single far terms and carry no counterterm ((i), *r^s_z at the decoupled configurations of (1.60)*).
    - So (ii) and these pieces make up the two runs' difference of V(Y) exactly, with the background of 𝐇″_k as a further entry of the joint line (*The background entry U_{1,2}.* below, derived near the cut, not reviewed). The bounds of (i), r_z, (iii), (iv), (α), (β), and (δ) have the statuses listed.
    - V^B∘ι_Y, the single function of the background, alone does not give (i) + (iii): at run A's background it evaluates run B's chain at the lifted background, not at the lifts ι_Yc^A of run A's chain, and it leaves an equal-data variation of the type of (ii).
    - Replacing the bound of f by Bałaban's one-background (1.68)–(1.69) along a line of single backgrounds doesn't work: ι_Yc^A has the data of c^A, and run B's member at those data is c^B, so no background gives ι_Yc^A.
    - The variation term by term doesn't work: Bałaban's bound of an old term on its domain has no age factor, the renormalised terms enter (1.68) through the proof of Theorem 2 (p.376 l.40–41), whose sum over the generations needs the age decay, and the geometric-mean bound of the Cauchy note's (5.1v) gives only the power θ_V of the distance.
    - *The joint line.* For c ∈ {U″_k, U^0_k, U_{k,Λ}, U_k}, run B's constraints factor through M, every layer of the member's determining set being at a shared scale ≥ 1. So run B's constraint set contains {W : MW = Mc^B}, which contains c^B, and c^B, minimal over the larger set, minimises ι's one-step problem at the data Mc^B. That problem's minimiser is unique up to gauge (VAR Theorem 1 at one step), so ι(Mc^B) = c^B up to gauge, as for U^B (*The intermediate current and the estimate.*). For U_1 and U_2, minimisers with boundary conditions at ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1} (p.370 l.22–25), the identity is needed only for U_1, and only where the terms read U_1, since U_2 enters V^B(Y) only through the background entry of U_{1,2} (*The background entry U_{1,2}.* below): on X ⊂ Z^c and on the cubes □₀, at a distance ≥ 2MR_ka_k from the cut ((i), *The lift of U_1.*; §4(d), *The cubes □₀ at Z.*). Extended by 1 off its support, U^B_1 is critical for every variation that preserves its ε-block averages, except at the bonds of its finest strip next to its layer 1, since its current vanishes where the configuration is ≡ 1 and its other constraints are averages of the ε-blocks or coarser (derived, not reviewed). There the current is that of the layer 1, which the band regularity bounds at the index h (the W-δ note's §12, *The band regularity of U_{1,2}.*). So ι(MU^B_1) = U^B_1 up to gauge, up to the response of the one-step problem to that current (derived, not reviewed). Let J_S be the current of U^B_1 on the strip bonds S next to its layer 1, and W_τ, τ ∈ [0, 1], the critical point of run B's action minus τ⟨J_S, ·⟩, the pairing taken with the logarithm of W(U^B_1)^{−1}, under MW = MU^B_1, continued from W_1 = U^B_1; then W_0 = ι(MU^B_1). ∂_τW_τ = G_τJ_S, with G_τ the Green's function of ι's one-step problem at W_τ under its constraint (PBG Theorems 3.1–3.3 at k = 1, as in the W-δ note's 10.5), which decays as e^{−δ₀d/ε} in the distance d while W_τ stays in PBG's class, the decay that gives the tail of ι_Y its factor e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} over the distance M₁a_k, and the bound below keeps it there for γ small, by continuation in τ as in *The flow.* At a point x of the regions read, d(x, S) ≥ 2MR_ka_k = 2MR_kL^kε, |J_S| ≤ O(1)ε₁a_h^{−3} by the band regularity, and the sum over the (d − 1)-dimensional edge S is taken by the exponential decay, so it is volume-free. So the response at x is ≤ O(1)ε₁P(L^k)e^{−2δ₀MR_kL^k} in the units of the layer j(x), P a polynomial from the constants of G at the scale ε, the conversion of units, and the edge sum over S, which gives a polynomial in d(x, S)/ε absorbed by a part of the rate. Since R_k ≥ 2 and MR_k ≥ M₁, so that 2MR_k − ½M₁ ≥ 3M, this is ≤ C_rε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k}, C_r := O(1)sup_{x≥1}P(x)e^{−δ₀Mx} a constant of d, L, and M, so it is of the size of the tail of ι_Y and is absorbed in C_t. Write ι_Yc^A = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B on Y, as in the Cauchy note's §5 (*The pair line.*). At c = U_k, 𝐒_c is the field of the Cauchy note's §9.7 with the localised lift ι_Y, of the size σ̂_{k,j} with the tail of ι_Y, which is smaller, in place of that of ι_X; in a young history U_k is multiscale, and its size there is the bound σ_c of *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.* below. The joint line moves all entries together with one parameter, c(t) := (e^{it(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B)_{c∈𝒞}, t ∈ ℂ, with the currents frozen at run B's, and f(t) := V^B(Y)[c(t)]. The currents then move at the fixed entries ι_Yc^A, as in the frozen-current split of the Cauchy note's *The pair line.*, and (ii) is f(0) − f(1) plus that current part (*The current of the line*, below). For r(Y) ≤ ½, Cauchy's estimate gives |f(1) − f(0)| ≤ sup_{|t|≤1}|f′(t)| ≤ 2r(Y)sup|f| for f analytic for |t| < 1/r(Y). Here r(Y) := max_c sup_x σ_c(x)/ϱ(x), the maximum over the entries c of V^B(Y)[c] (the members of 𝒞 other than U_2, and the background entry of U_{1,2}) and the supremum over the points x of Y at which V^B(Y)[c] reads the entry c, since it depends on an entry only there, with σ_c(x) the bound of a_{j(x)}|𝐒_c(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇_{c^B}𝐒_c(x)|, ∇_{c^B} the covariant gradient at c^B, and ϱ(x) the room of *The room at real points.*. The moves at x depend on σ_c at the other points of Y, so the room is well posed through this supremum over Y.
    - *The background entry U_{1,2} (derived near the cut, not reviewed).* The first-stage brackets read U_{1,2} as the background of 𝐇″_k in (1.54), (1.55), and (1.61) (p.370 l.39–p.371 l.24, p.373 l.2–3 and l.34–35), and (iii) compares run B's 𝐇″_k at the background ι_YU^A_{1,2} with run A's at U^A_{1,2} (the W-δ note's §12, *10.5's premise at 𝐇″_k*). So V^B(Y)[c] takes this background as an entry b, with b = U^B_{1,2} at t = 0 and b = ι_YU^A_{1,2} at t = 1, and the joint line needs 𝐒_{1,2} with ι_YU^A_{1,2} = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_{1,2}}U^B_{1,2} on Y, with its bound σ_{1,2} in r(Y). Taking b as the patch of the entries U_1 and U_2 instead gives at t = 1 the patch of ι_YU^A_1 and ι_YU^A_2, which differs from ι_YU^A_{1,2} near the cut, and that difference is the lift–patch commutator against (iii). Away from the cut 𝐒_{1,2} is 𝐒_{U_1} on Ω″^∼_{h+1} and 𝐒_{U_2} on its complement, up to the tail of ι_Y. Near the cut it is bounded directly, in four steps, and its current in a fifth.
      - *The band.* By the W-δ note's §12, *The cut.*, U_{1,2} = 1 on both finest strips of the cut in each run, with ∂U(p) = 1 and D*∂U = 0 there, so the patch has no crossing term. The cut runs in 𝐁″_k's layer h, the collar of 𝐁₁ lies inside the band of that layer, and by *The band regularity of U_{1,2}* (the W-δ note's §12) VAR (2) holds on the band at the index h, all data being 1 there. The background of 𝐇″_k is read in 𝐁″_k's layers, so ϱ = c_ρα_h at the points of the band, and n ≤ h (the W-δ note's §12, *The consumer.*). The gauge convention is the joint line's: ι_Y's axial gauge is taken relative to run B's base U_0, under which ι(Mc^B) = c^B holds with no gauge factor, up to the response to J_S.
      - *The coarse side.* The coarse move A′ of U^A_{1,2} against MU^B_{1,2} on run A's lattice vanishes on run A's finest strips of the cut, since run B's layer-1 data there are run A's bonds, all equal to 1, so A′ has no jump at the cut. Elsewhere it is bounded by *The profile.* of (b), rerun with π′(x) := (ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀}(a_{j(x)}/a_h)² on the collar layers j(x) < h of the band and π′ := π elsewhere; π′ is continuous at j = h and π′ ≤ π on the band. The band's sources satisfy a_{j′}²|r₁| ≤ C_rL³B₃(ε/a_h)ε₁(a_{j′}/a_h)² by the Cauchy note's (9.5) with VAR (2) at the index h, and with the Hölder factor 2(a_{j′}/ε)^{ε₀} ≤ 2(a_h/ε)^{ε₀} this is at most Cπ′(y′)ε₁; also a_{j′}³|r₀| ≤ C(ε/a_h)ε₁²(a_{j′}/a_h)³. The sources at coarser layers keep the propagator gains (a_j/a_{j′})² for D*r₁ and (a_j/a_{j′})³ for r₀, which compensate π′(y′)/π′(x) ≤ (a_h/a_j)². At finer layers the factor is L^{3n}L^{−2n} = L^n, below π's L^{(4−ε₀)n}. Between neighbouring layers π′ changes by at most L², so the kernel's rate η′ = 2 log L/(LM₁) is at most δ₀/16 when δ₀LM₁ ≥ 32 log L, which holds for M₁ large as in (J-W). The a priori inequality then closes as under (T-π), and on the band a_h|A′| + a_h²|∇A′| ≤ (a_h/a_j)²(a_j|A′| + a_j²|∇A′|) ≤ C(ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀}ε₁.
      - *The fine side.* ι_YU^A_{1,2} is critical for all variations preserving its data, and U^B_{1,2} for the M-preserving variations except on its strip bonds S on both sides of the cut, with no crossing term by *The band.* So U^B_{1,2} differs from ι_Y(MU^B_{1,2}) by the response to the current J_S on S, with |J_S| ≤ O(1)L³B₃ε₁a_h^{−3} by VAR (2) at the index h. PBG (3.42) at the one-step scale ε gives ε|resp| + ε²|∇resp| ≤ Cε³|J_S|e^{−δ₀d(x,S)/ε}, so a_h|resp| ≤ CL³B₃ε₁(ε/a_h)² and a_h²|∇resp| ≤ CL³B₃ε₁(ε/a_h), with the same decay.
      - *The move.* 𝐒_{1,2} is the lift of A′ through ι_Y plus the response, so the flow of *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain* (kernel e^{−½δ_f|x−y|/ε}) gives σ_{1,2}(x) ≤ C(ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀}ε₁ on the band, in the units of 𝐁″_k's layer h. Since n ≤ h, (ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀} ≤ (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}, so σ_{1,2}(x) ≤ d̄(x) for C_U(M) ≥ C. Off the band the profile π and the decay of the response apply, as away from the cut. The terms of the factor ε/a_j at the finest layers next to ∂Y_i are not of this kind; they belong to the near domains at ∂Y_i ((iv), *The near domains at ∂Y_i.*).
      - *The current (derived, not reviewed).* The current entry moves from J(U^B_{1,2}) to Q₁^{pl}(ι_YU^A_{1,2})*J(U^A_{1,2}). Away from the band this is *The Current at the Multiscale Chain.* at U_1 or U_2. On the band its multiplier representation does not apply, since U_{1,2} is critical only on each side of the cut, and the strip current |J_S| ≤ O(1)L³B₃ε₁a_h^{−3} carries no factor ε/a_h, so a_h³|ΔJ_{1,2}| ≤ Ĉ_Jd̄ fails pointwise there. The move has the divergence form of (δ) instead:
        ΔJ_{1,2} = [J(ι_YU^A_{1,2}) − J(U^B_{1,2})] − Q₁^{pl}*∇Δ(U^A_{1,2}).
        - The first bracket. J = D*F at both configurations, which differ by e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_{1,2}}. Along the line e^{it(ε/L)𝐒_{1,2}}U^B_{1,2} it is D*K₂(t) + s₂(t), with K₂(t) the transported curvature difference, a_h²|K₂(t)| ≤ O(1)|t|σ_{1,2}, and s₂(t) the change of D* acting on the curvature, a_h³|s₂(t)| ≤ O(1)C₀ε₁|t|σ_{1,2}, at complex t as well, since the line is explicit in t.
        - The second term is (δ)'s constraint identity J^B(ι_YW) = Q₁^{pl}*(J(W) + ∇Δ(W)) at W = U^A_{1,2}. That identity uses only that ι_YW minimises run B's action at the data W, not that W is a minimiser, so it holds here. At the real U^A_{1,2}, |Δ| is bounded through the oscillation as at the real decoupled W (*Real points.*), with VAR (2) at the index h on the band (*The band.*) in place of the class bounds, so a_h²|Δ| ≤ O(1)C_δ′(ε/a_h)^{β_o}.
        - Where it enters. U_{1,2} and J_{1,2} are read only as the background of 𝐇″_k, through the operators of VAR (179)–(180) at (𝐔, 𝐉) and through the source 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2} of (1.54) (*Where Bałaban puts 𝐉.*). So the move is read only through the smoothed functionals of *Where the current enters.*: H*∇*K = (∇H)*K, with the factor (ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀} from σ_{1,2} ≤ C(ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀}ε₁ (*The move.*) or (ε/a_h)^{β_o} from Δ, and the (3.117) pairing with the loss (a_h/ε)^{β″} of the crude Hölder bound. The source s₂ is pointwise of the size of *The Current at the Multiscale Chain.*, ≤ O(1)C₀ε₁σ_{1,2} ≤ Ĉ_Jd̄ for Ĉ_J ≥ O(1)C₀.
        - The disc. Cauchy's estimate of the joint line holds with any r ≥ r(Y), and it is taken with r̄(Y) := max(r(Y), max_x d̄(x)/ϱ(x)), the second maximum over the points of the band, which leaves its final bound unchanged, since that bound uses r(Y) only through σ_c ≤ C(ε₀)d̄. So |t| ≤ c_ρα_h/d̄(x) on the band, and d̄(x) ≥ C_U(M)(ε/a_h)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ there, by n ≤ h as in *The move.* Hence a_h²|K₂(t)| ≤ O(1)(C/C_U(M))c_ρα_h(ε/a_h)^{2(1−ε₀)/3}, and the K₂-part of the functionals moves by at most O(1)B₀C_{Q*}c_ρα_h(ε/a_h)^{2(1−ε₀)/3−β″}a_h^{−3}, against the room O(1)Mα_ha_h^{−3} of *The enlarged domain.* at the layer h, whose bounds are per layer (CRE (2.34)–(2.39)). Since 2(1−ε₀)/3 > ½ for ε₀ < 1/4, the exponent is positive for β″ ≤ ½, which the choice β″ ≤ 1/20 of (δ) (W-δ §8, stage 0) gives, and the move is within half the room for c_ρ ≤ cM(O(1)B₀C_{Q*})^{−1}, a condition of the kind of the choice of c_ρ in *The class at the entries.* The Δ-part is (δ)'s current part at the layer h, with the factor (ε/a_h)^{c_J}. *The enlarged domain.* uses only the divergence form and the per-layer bound of K, so the current stays in 𝒟_R along the whole disc of the joint line, and Bałaban's estimates hold there with twice the bounds.
    - *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain (Derived, Unreviewed).* Let c ∈ {U″_k, U^0_k, U_{k,Λ}, U_k}, at real data. The global one-step lift ι is treated first, and the localised lift ι_Y after it (*The Lift ι_Y.* below).
      - The coarse side. At every point of the member's determining set, which satisfies PRT2 (2.1)–(2.2) (*The profile.* of (b)), c^A = e^{iεA_c}Mc^B with a_{j(x)}|A_c(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A_c(x)| ≤ d̄(x) := C_U(M)(ε/a_{min(j(x),n)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, by the profile of (b) at each data, with *The kernel form of VAR Proposition 4.* (rate ⅛δ₀), and (c) through U_0. At the joins 𝐁₁ and 𝐁₂ ((2.14) [III]) the profile holds with RM replaced by LM₁ under the condition (J-W) of (b), *The joins.*, which holds for M₁ large. d̄ is non-increasing in the layer, and d̄(y) ≤ L^{(1−ε₀)m/3}d̄(x) for y in the layer j(x) − m. For U″_k both runs' members are taken with run B's coupling in exp ig_kB, and the coupling difference (A-iii) of §4(a) is carried with the coupling terms of (α), so d̄ need not cover it.
      - The one-step problem. By *The joint line.* run B's constraints at c factor through M, so c^B = ι(Mc^B) in a suitable gauge. ι's problem has a single layer, the blocks of size ε of T_ε, at every data. The Cauchy note's §9.7 rests on three statements of this problem: the reference configuration U₀′ := e^{i(ε/L)ℒA_c}c^B satisfies VAR (14) at the data c^A; J(U₀′) − J(c^B) = D*(DℒA_c) + O(|F||ℒA_c|) in weak form, with 𝔊_BJ(c^B) = 0 by the criticality of c^B; and PBG (3.42) and (3.44) for the Green operator 𝔊_B of the problem, transferred to 𝔊 by PBG Theorem 3.13 (p.426 l.29–32). The layers of c^A and c^B enter only through the sizes of A_c and of F(c^B). VAR Theorem 1 bounds these on each layer: on its cubes, in a gauge, |A| < B₃Mε₁a_j^{−1}, |∇A| < B₃Mε₁a_j^{−2}, and |∂*∂A| < B₃Mε₁a_j^{−3} (VAR (9)–(10), p.279 l.19–26). So the argument of §9.7 applies, with the sources bounded pointwise by the layer of their point.
      - The kernels. PBG (3.42) and (3.44) (p.397 l.37–46, p.398 l.11–17) bound the kernels pointwise by e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}, with δ₀ of PBG Theorem 3.1 and the distance d of PRT2 (2.46) (p.231 l.8–14), which at one step is the length of a fine path in units of ε. At one step ξ′ = L^{−1}, so the Hölder seminorm of exponent ε₀ in (3.44), taken on the ξ′-lattice (PBG p.397 l.17–18), of a bounded source is at most 2L^{ε₀} times its supremum, a constant of L. The correction of ℒ has the kernel of (T^{pl})^{−1}, which decays at the rate δ_Q^{pl} of the Cauchy note's §9.4. So every operator of the argument has a kernel bounded by a constant times e^{−δ_f|x−y|/ε}, δ_f := min(δ₀, δ_Q^{pl}), times the scale factor of its entry at the scale ε. The sources are split over the blocks as in §9.5.
      - The weights. Put Ŵ(x) := sup_y e^{−½δ_f|x−y|/ε}(a_{j(x)}/a_{j(y)})²d̄(y). A path from a point of the layer j to a point of the layer j − m, m ≥ 1, crosses the layers j − 1, …, j − m + 1. Each of these is wider than RMa_i ≥ RMε by PRT2 (2.2) (p.224 l.8–13), and at least LM₁ε wide at the joins (*The joins.* in (b)). With W this width bound, the term of y in Ŵ(x) is ≤ L^{7m/3}e^{−½δ_fW(m−1)}d̄(x) ≤ L^{7/3}d̄(x), under the condition
        (F-W) ½δ_fW ≥ (7/3)log L, for W = RM and W = LM₁.
        For y in the layer of x or a coarser one the term is ≤ d̄(x). So d̄(x) ≤ Ŵ(x) ≤ L^{7/3}d̄(x), and by the triangle inequality (a_{j(x)}/a_{j(y)})²Ŵ(y) ≤ e^{½δ_f|x−y|/ε}Ŵ(x). Hence a kernel bounded by e^{−δ_f|x−y|/ε} maps a source bounded by Ŵ(y)a_{j(y)}^{−2} to a field with a_{j(x)}|·| + a_{j(x)}²|∇·| ≤ 2c_{δ_f/2}Ŵ(x) at x. Here c_{δ_f/2} := sup_yΣ_{y′}e^{−½δ_fd(y,y′)} is a constant of d and δ_f, and the factors ε² and ε of the bounded-source entries of (3.42) give powers of ε/a_{j(x)} ≤ 1.
      - The bound. §9.7 is used at the linear level and integrated along the data, as §9.8 integrates δℋ/δB in *The decay.* (CRE (3.7)), so no contraction in a weighted norm is needed. Let V_t := e^{itεA_c}Mc^B and W_t := ι(V_t) for t ∈ [0, 1]. V_t is regular, as c^A and Mc^B are, so W_t is the minimal configuration of VAR Theorem 1 at one step, and W_t = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒^ι(t)}c^B with 𝐒^ι(0) = 0. The derivative of ι at V_t in the direction A_c is the first-order part of the fixed point of §9.7 at the critical background W_t: δ_t = ℒ_tA_c − 𝔊_t(D*Dℒ_tA_c + O(|F||ℒ_tA_c|)). Here ℒ_t and 𝔊_t are the operators of §9.4 and §9.7 at W_t; W_t is critical at its own data, so the derivative is the lift at W_t, with no background-change term, and 𝐒^ι(t) is taken in VAR's gauge of ι's problem at W_t, transported to c^B. Its sources are bounded by C_ℒ′Ŵ(y)a_{j(y)}^{−2}, C_ℒ′ the constant of ℒ's fine gradient (the Cauchy note's §10.4, *Regularity of the Lifts.*, its l.879). So a_{j(x)}|δ_t(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇δ_t(x)| ≤ C_δ(ε₀)Ŵ(x) uniformly in t, with C_δ(ε₀) a constant of d, L, ε₀, B₀, B₀′(ε₀), C_ℒ′, and c_{δ_f/2}. By the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula d𝐒^ι/dt = δ_t up to a pointwise correction of relative size O((ε/(La_{j(x)}))a_{j(x)}|𝐒^ι(t)(x)|) with its gradient. This closes by continuity in t: while a_{j(x)}|𝐒^ι(t)(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐒^ι(t)(x)| ≤ 2C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3}d̄(x), the correction is ≤ ½ for γ small, so the bound holds with room on [0, 1]. So ι(c^A) = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒^ι_c}c^B with 𝐒^ι_c := 𝐒^ι(1), and a_{j(x)}|𝐒^ι_c(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐒^ι_c(x)| ≤ 2C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3}d̄(x).
      - With the lift ι_Y. By *The Lift ι_Y.*, ι_Yc^A = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B on Y, where 𝐒_c − 𝐒^ι_c is ≤ 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} in the units of every layer. Since d̄(x) ≥ C_U(M)L^{−k/3}ε₁ and L^{k/3}e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} ≤ (2/(3eδ₀M₁))^{1/3}, this is ≤ C′d̄(x), with C′ := 2C_t(2/(3eδ₀M₁))^{1/3}/C_U(M). Hence σ_c(x) ≤ C(ε₀)d̄(x), with C(ε₀) := 2C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3} + 2C′, a constant independent of k, of the layer, of the volume, and of ε. C′ is exponentially large in RM₁, so every use of σ_c/ϱ small needs γ small after R, M, and M₁ are fixed, which is Bałaban's order (γ last). The callers below use the weaker consequence σ_c ≤ C(ε₀)(a_k/ε)^{ε₀}d̄(x), valid since a_k ≥ ε, at the non-deep layers, where k < 8n + j(C) and they absorb the factor (a_k/ε)^{ε₀} explicitly; that factor is not uniform in k elsewhere. This is the bound that *The room at real points.* and *At real points the ratio is small.* use.
      - (F-W) is an additional lower bound on Bałaban's R and M and on M₁, of the same kind as ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1 of (b) and (J-W): RM, LM₁ ≥ 14 log L/(3δ_f). δ_f depends only on d and L (δ_Q^{pl} by §9.4), so (F-W) is met by the same choices: R and M large, and M₁ a large power of L (CRE p.245 l.35–37), before γ.
      - The lift condition of (i). The per-term lift ι_X of (i) has the tail of the Cauchy note's §9.8 at the collar of X^∼. In the units a_j of the generation j of the term this tail is ≤ C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^j} at every point, by the computation of *The Lift ι_Y.* with L^j for L^k, which uses the worst case |F| ≤ C_Fε₁/ε². A term of the generation j reads a point x of a layer m ≥ j at the thresholds of the scale j, since its class Ũ^c_j(X) conditions Ω_j as one layer (CRE (2.34)–(2.39) at n = j, p.261 l.14–40). So its radius at x in the units a_j is r_{0,j}, the same at every layer m ≥ j. In these units the move of the pair at x is ≤ L^{j−m}σ_c(x) + C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^j}, the tail without the factor L^{j−m}. The quantities of the Cauchy note's *The lift condition.* (§5, at the real minimisers) are bounded as follows: the configuration's own quantities per layer, through Bałaban's class membership, and ε₁ only in the move and the tails. A bound of the own quantities through ε₁, as VAR (9)–(10) give it (VAR (7) takes one ε₁ for all layers, p.278), would not be uniform in K at a layer m far below k, since ε₁/α_{0,m} grows like (g_k/g_m)(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀+r}(log g_m^{−2})^{−q₀}.
        - The plaquettes, A′, and cube-gauge fields of c^B. c^B is a real member of run B, at which Bałaban evaluates run B's terms (U_k: CRE p.259 l.40–44, "sufficiently regular configurations U_k=U_k(V)", p.263 l.19; U″_k and U^0_k: LFR II (1.49) and p.375 l.17–35; U_{k,Λ}: LFR II p.372 l.39–p.373 l.6, where the first-stage terms read it; his usage, accepted). So with its current it lies in the spaces of the terms of the generation j, whose conditions (2.34)–(2.39) at x ∈ X ∩ Ω_j are stated at n = j in the units a_j. His construction does not involve C₀ and C₁, so this holds at the thresholds α̃_{·,j}/μ for μ = 32B₃²L³ and μ = 8B₃C_{Q*} (*Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*). With r = α̃/(8B₃) (*The room at real points.*), the plaquettes, A′, and cube-gauge fields of c^B at x (the last with the factor BCM of (2.38)) are therefore below α̃_{·,j}/(32B₃²L³) in the units a_j, at most half of the radius r_{·,j}/(2B₃) = α̃_{·,j}/(16B₃²) of the lift condition;
        - the move by e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_X};
        - (iv), by RG I (1.17);
        - the current of the real member that the pair lift carries, by the same membership in its run at μ = 8B₃C_{Q*}: |J| < α̃_{0,j}/(8B₃C_{Q*}) = r_{0,j}/C_{Q*} in the units a_j at x, so the pair-lift current, at most C_{Q*} times this, is below r_{0,j}.

        The configuration's own quantities take at most half of each radius (the first and the last item). The move's part with the factor L^{j−m} is below a quarter of it, since r_{0,j} ≥ r_{0,m}L^{j−m} and σ_c(x)/α_m tends to 0 (*At real points the ratio is small.*, whose power of L^{−n} absorbs its factors polynomial in k). The tail part of ι_X is ≤ C_te^{−½δ₀M₁L^j}(ε₁/α_j) times a constant of the radius, where ε₁/α_j ≤ C(M)2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)(k − j + 1)^{1/2}R_kε_j/α_j, as for ι_Y below, need not be small, but R_kε_j/α_j tends to 0 and the factor (k − j + 1)^{1/2} (with (7k + 1)^{1/2} where the ε₁ of §1 is the larger, as below) is absorbed by e^{−½δ₀M₁L^j}, since k < 8n + j(C) ≤ 16j + j(C) + 8 for the generations j > m/2 at a point of a layer m ≥ n − 1 of a history with the finest scale n > k_s/8, the only ones at which (i), and with it ι_X, is formed (the older generations are compared directly through Bałaban's per-run bound, (iv), *The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer*, *The old generations.*; the deep terms are bounded by the triangle inequality). The tail of ι_Y is ≤ 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} in the units of every layer (*With the lift ι_Y.*), so its part is ≤ (2C_tε₁/α_j)e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} times a constant of the radius at every generation j ≤ m, with ε₁/α_j ≤ C(M)2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)(k − j + 1)^{1/2}R_kε_j/α_j by α_j ≥ ε_j and CRE (2.6) as in *At real points the ratio is small.*; R_kε_j/α_j tends to 0 with ḡ there (q₀ > p₀ + r, log g_j^{−2} ≥ ½log g_k^{−2}), and sup_k(k + 1)^{1/2}e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} is finite, so no relation between k and j is needed. Where the ε₁ of §1 is the larger, the further factor C(1 + β₀)(k_s − k + 1)^{1/2}(1 + A₁/A₀) of that paragraph is ≤ C(1 + β₀)(7k + 1)^{1/2}(1 + A₁/A₀), since k_s − k < 7n ≤ 7k, and it is absorbed by e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} in the same way. The parts with the factor L^{j−m} are as above at every j ≤ m. So each is below its radius for γ small, and the lift condition holds at the real members U″_k, U^0_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_k, for ι_X at the generations j > m/2 and for ι_Y at every generation j ≤ m, which (ii) and the data of the old generations use.
    - *The Lift ι_Y (Derived, Unreviewed).* Y ∈ 𝐃_k is a union of M-cubes of the scale k (LFR II p.376 l.25–31). Let Y^∼ be Y with the adjacent M₁-cubes of the scale k, the operation ∼ for M₁-cubes of CRE p.256 l.57–63 at the scale k, so that dist(Y, (Y^∼)^c) ≥ M₁a_k. ι_Y is the localised lift of the Cauchy note's §9.8 with Y^∼ in place of X^∼: ι_YU := U(𝐁_1(Y^∼), M^·(Q_1^{s*}U)), with Ω₀ := Y^∼ and Ω₁ the union of the big blocks of size M₁ε of Y^∼ at distance > R_ιM₁ε/L from its complement, with R_ι := 4dL + 1 the parameter R of VAR (1) for this one-step problem, which meets VAR p.300's R_ι > 4dLR₁ at R₁ = 1 (the W-δ note's §12, *The lift's R_ι (checked).*). The proofs of its properties in §9.8 use only the one-step problem on Ω₀, so they hold as there: ι_Y is a function of U on Y^∼; Mι_YU = U on the coarse bonds of Y^∼, which contain □₀ and Y_4 = Y; ι_Y1 = 1; and ι_Y is gauge covariant.
      - The tail at the chain. Let c^A be a real member of 𝒞. As in §9.8, *The tail.*, ιc^A is the minimiser of the Y^∼-problem at its own data, so ι_Yc^A = exp(i(ε/L)T_Y)ιc^A. Here T_Y := ℋ(B_Y), ℋ is the solution map of VAR (179)–(181) at the background ιc^A, and B_Y := (1/i)log[Q_1^{s*}c^A(ιc^A)^{−1}] on the collar Λ₀, with B_Y = 0 on Ω₁. The plaquettes of c^A on T_ε are ≤ 2B₃Mε₁ by VAR (9) on its layers, which is at most a₁ for γ small. So VAR Theorem 1 at one step gives |F(ιc^A)| ≤ C_Fε₁/ε², the bound C_Fε₁/a² of §9.8 at its worst, a = ε. Hence |B_Y| ≤ LC_LC_Fε₁/ε per fine bond, and VAR (190) integrated along tB_Y gives |T_Y| ≤ C_ι(ε₁/ε)e^{−δ₀dist(·,Ω₁^c)/ε} and |∇T_Y| ≤ C_ι(ε₁/ε²)e^{−δ₀dist(·,Ω₁^c)/ε}, as in §9.8, *The decay.*
      - The size. On Y and within 2L fine spacings of it, dist(·, Ω₁^c) ≥ M₁a_k − R_ιM₁ε/L − M₁ε − 2ε, so e^{−δ₀dist/ε} ≤ e^{δ₀(R_ι/L+2)M₁}e^{−δ₀M₁L^k}. At a point of any layer m, a_m ≤ a_k, so a_m|T_Y| + a_m²|∇T_Y| ≤ 2C_ιe^{δ₀(R_ι/L+2)M₁}ε₁L^{2k}e^{−δ₀M₁L^k}. Since x²e^{−cx} ≤ (2/(ec))² for x ≥ 0, L^{2k}e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} ≤ (4/(eδ₀M₁))². So the tail is ≤ C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} in the units of every layer, with C_t := 2C_ιe^{δ₀(R_ι/L+2)M₁}(4/(eδ₀M₁))². For a term of the generation j ≤ k this is ≤ C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^j}, the form that the rate of (iv) needs, with c = ½. By the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula ι_Yc^A = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B on Y, with 𝐒_c − 𝐒^ι_c = T_Y + O((ε/L)|𝐒^ι_c||T_Y|) and 𝐒^ι_c the field of the global lift (*The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.*). So 𝐒_c − 𝐒^ι_c is ≤ 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} in these units, for γ small.
      - The moment defect. *The linearisation at 1.* of §9.8 on Y^∼ bounds d(ι_Y − ι)(1)h on Y by C_ιe^{−δ₀dist(·,Ω₁^c)/ε} ≤ C_ιe^{δ₀(R_ι/L+2)M₁}e^{−δ₀M₁L^k} times the constant curl of h, since the mismatch is sourced by the curl. Here δ₀ is the rate of §9.8, not that of PBG Theorem 3.1 in δ_f.
        - The argument of the Cauchy note's *The moment of the lifted term.* (§5, the localised-lift paragraph) has three inputs. The first is this linearisation bound on the support of the term, C″_Πe^{−δ₀M₁L^j} times the curl there. The second is RG I (4.5) at order 2 for the Hessian of the term, (8B₃/α_{2,j})²E₀exp(−κd_j(X)), at the term's own scale j. The third is the test of the quadratic form on affine block fields, whose growth enters only through exp(−κd_j(X)). The collar enters only through the first, by its distance: §9.8 derives C″_Π from C_ιe^{−δ₀dist(·,Ω₁^c)/ε} on the one-step problem on Ω₀, with no power of the collar's scale. With ι_Y that distance is ≥ M₁a_k − RM₁ε/L − M₁ε − 2ε, so C″_Πe^{−δ₀M₁L^j} becomes C_ιe^{δ₀(R_ι/L+2)M₁}e^{−δ₀M₁L^k}, and the Hessian and the test are those of the same term. So, for run B's generation-j term along ι_Y, |Π^{B∘ι_Y}_{μν,κλ} − β^B_{j+1}δ_{μν}δ_{κλ}| ≤ C_Π(j)E₀e^{−δ₀M₁L^k} ≤ C_Π(j)E₀e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}, with C_Π(j) of §5 and C″_Π := C_ιe^{δ₀(R_ι/L+2)M₁}. The coefficients of its plane-resolved part are ≤ 2C_Π(j)E₀e^{−δ₀M₁L^k}.
        - The defect (Π^{B∘ι_Y}_{22,11} − β^B_{j+1})A_ε(h_z, ·) + N_j is the coefficient times A_ε(h_z, ·), which is ≤ C ε₁² per point, so it is ≤ 2496C_XC₀²E₀C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j} per point as in §5. So the moment defect of ι_Y has the form C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−cδ₀M₁L^j} that the rate of (iv) needs, with c = 1, E₀ included in the constant. C_Π(j)ε₁² ≤ max(1, k − j) (§5), and the moment part of the rate of (iv) absorbs that factor.
    - *The Current at the Multiscale Chain (Derived, Unreviewed).* Let c ∈ {U″_k, U^0_k, U_{k,Λ}, U_k}, at real data, and Ũ_c := Mc^B. The current part of the joint line moves the current of c by ΔJ_c := Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A)*J(c^A) − J(c^B). The Cauchy note's *The current along the pair line.* is run at c, with the layer of the point in place of its single scale k and the multipliers of (d) in a localised form. In this block d is the distance of PRT2 (2.46), which measures each layer in its own units.

By *The joint line.* the layers of run B's set are at shared scales ≥ 1, so 𝐐(c^B) = 𝐐(Ũ_c)Q₁^{pl}(c^B) layer by layer, J(c^B) = Q₁^{pl}(c^B)*J′_c with J′_c := 𝐐(Ũ_c)*λ^B_c, and D*_{Ũ_c}J′_c = 0 as in *The intermediate current and the estimate.* With δ𝐉_c := J(c^A) − J′_c,

        ΔJ_c = Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A)*δ𝐉_c + [Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A) − Q₁^{pl}(c^B)]*J′_c.

      - The weights. d̄ is non-increasing in the layer, and d̄(y) ≤ L^{(1−ε₀)m/3}d̄(x) for y in the layer j(x) − m (*The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.*). Every layer is wider than W in its own units, W = RM by PRT2 (2.2) and W = LM₁ at the joins (*The joins.* in (b)), so a point of the layer j(x) − m, m ≥ 1, is at distance ≥ W(m − 1) from x, and the points at distance ≤ 8 from x lie in the layers ≥ j(x) − 1, since W > 8. Hence
        (LW₀) d̄(y) ≤ L^{1/3}d̄(x) for d(x, y) ≤ 8;

        and in the same way (ε/a_{j(p)})e^{−¼δ₀d(p,x)} ≤ L(ε/a_{j(x)}) for every p. The condition ¼δ₀W ≥ log L follows from ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1 of (b) and from M₁ ≥ M_J(d, L) of *The choice of M₁.* in (b). The localities below, the blocks of a bond and their neighbours, the reach of *The reach.*, and the columns of Q₁^{pl}(·)*, are within distance 8, except at the interface bonds z ∈ S_j, where the double-bar entry (VAR (154), p.302 l.4–5) reads the j-block B(x) of the outer endpoint, in the layer j − 1. There they are within r_Q := d(L − 1) + 2 of z, inside the layers ≥ j(z) − 1, since the layer j − 1 is wider than RM ≥ 12L > r_Q; so (LW₀) still holds, by the layer index.
      - The multipliers at a fixed right inverse. The Cauchy estimate of *The multipliers.* moves Ũ_c to c^A on one disc, whose radius the supremum of d̄ over the volume sets, and that supremum is set by the finest layer of the volume, older histories included. So the difference is taken at the fixed right inverse R₀ := H₀(Ũ_c) of *The right inverses.*, with Q_db(Ũ_c)R₀ = 1, |R₀e_y| ≤ B₀e^{−½δ₀d(·,y)}, and |∇R₀e_y| ≤ B₀a_{j(·)}^{−1}e^{−½δ₀d(·,y)}. Put E := (Q_db(c^A) − Q_db(Ũ_c))R₀.
        - The kernel of E. An entry of Q_db(U) at a data bond z is linear in the field on the bonds of the blocks of z and its neighbours, and it depends on U only there, through the transports along the paths of these blocks, the logarithms of (B1), and the twist (154). The contour holonomies of Ũ_c are within 2C₀ε₁ of 1, as in *The multipliers.* So for |t| ≤ T_z := c₃/(16dLd̄_z), under 4C₀ε₁ ≤ c₃, the logarithms stay in the domain of CMP 98 Proposition 3, Ad and dexp^{−1} stay bounded, and the entry is ≤ q_Q times the supremum of the field on these bonds, with q_Q a constant of d and L, the bound of a local average of (b)(iii) at real configurations. Cauchy's estimate at t = 1, with T_z ≥ 2, gives |(Q_db(c^A) − Q_db(Ũ_c))X(z)| ≤ 2q_QT_z^{−1}sup|X| = c_δQd̄_z sup|X|, c_δQ := 32dLq_Q/c₃. With the bound of R₀, |E(z, y)| ≤ c_Ed̄_ze^{−½δ₀d(z,y)}, c_E := c_δQB₀e^{½δ₀r_Q}, the factor e^{½δ₀r_Q} covering the distance from z to the bonds of its blocks, ≤ 2 away from the interfaces and ≤ r_Q at S_j. At S_j the field on the layer-(j − 1) bonds of B(x) carries the factor L more, |εA_c| ≤ εd̄/a_{j−1}, so c_δQ carries the factor L, and q_Q on the complex disc is taken at Ad ≤ e^{c₃/2} with dexp^{−1} bounded. The intermediate sums below run over data bonds, at most 2d per block point, so c_tot carries the factor 2d in (T-E), in C_w, and in (II).

Then a_{j′}³|(I)| ≤ x₁d̄(y), x₁ := (8c′_W + 20c_W)B₀L^{1/3}c̄′_e.

Their proofs use the weighted divergence conditions, which hold by D*J = 0 in each run, the condition of Ũ_c being run B's restricted to T_ε, and the plaquette bounds of the configurations.         - The first term. Mι_Yc^A = c^A on the coarse bonds of Y^∼ (*The Lift ι_Y.*), so the Cauchy note's *The Adjoint Averaging.* (ii) applies at W := ι_Yc^A with W̄ = c^A, with c_QL^{d+1}κ_W ≤ 1 by the plaquette bound of *The lift condition of (i).* D*_{c^A}J(c^A) = 0 and D*_{Ũ_c}J′_c = 0, so D*_{c^A}δ𝐉_c = −(D*_{c^A} − D*_{Ũ_c})J′_c. With (LW₀) for the coarse bonds of the column, a_{j(x)}³|(Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A)*δ𝐉_c)(x)| ≤ 2L^{1/3}x̂_Jd̄(x) + 2dc_brL^{d−1}L^{1/3}B̄′_Jε₁d̄(x).

As there, a_{j(x)}³ times the second term is ≤ c_Dc_vL^{d−1}(ε/a_{j(x)})σ_c^xB̄′_Jε₁, with σ_c^x ≤ C(ε₀)L^{1/3}d̄(x) the maximum of σ_c on these bonds (*The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.* and (LW₀)).
