      - The current part. At the fixed entries ι_Yc^A, which satisfy the configuration conditions by *The lift condition of (i).*, the line u ↦ (J(c^B) + uΔJ_c)_{c∈𝒞} moves only the currents. At u = 0 each current is below ½ of its threshold, at U^0_k by *The room at real points.* and at the other members with the conditions of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) there (*The conditions at the other configurations*, below, derived, not reviewed), and the thresholds at x are ≥ α_{j(x)}/(1 + β₀) times their scale factors. So for |u| < T_J(Y) := min_c min_xα_{j(x)}/(4(1 + β₀)Ĉ_Jd̄(x)), over the entries c and the points x of Y at which the entry c is read, as for r(Y) (the background entry of U_{1,2} with its current J_{1,2} among them, whose Ĉ_Jd̄ near the cut is derived with σ_{1,2} in *The background entry U_{1,2}.*, not reviewed), each current stays ≤ ½ϖθ + ¼θ ≤ ϖθ, θ its threshold, since ϖ ≥ ½, and the endpoint u = 1 lies in the domain by the lift condition. The function bounded along u is V^B(Y) at the fixed entries ι_Yc^A = c(1) of the joint line with the currents J(c^B) + uΔJ_c, which is not run B's chain at any background. The current is an independent coordinate of the terms' spaces (CRE p.261 l.5–10), so the term-by-term bound of *The bound of f on the disc* applies at these points as at c(1), with the same inputs. The two cases of the Cauchy note's *The current part.* then bound the current part by 4r_J(Y) times that bound, with r_J(Y) := 1/T_J(Y) ≤ 4(1 + β₀)Ĉ_J max_{x∈Y}d̄(x)/α_{j(x)}. The bound of *At real points the ratio is small.* is a bound of C(ε₀)(a_k/ε)^{ε₀}d̄(x)/α_m, so it bounds d̄(x)/α_m up to the factor 1/C(ε₀). With the count of the domains Y, the current part is therefore ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point at the layers ≥ n, with C_h enlarged by a constant of Ĉ_J, β₀, and C(ε₀).
      - At complex points. Let ΔJ_c(z) := Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A(z))*J(c^A(z)) − J(c^B(z)) on Y, with the members and the lifts of *The Fine Side at Complex Points.* For a point x and a complex-linear functional ℓ of norm 1, 𝔣_J(z) := ℓ(a_{j(x)}³ΔJ_c(z)(x)) is analytic on the disc, since J(W) is a polynomial in the bond variables of W and their inverses, and Q₁^{pl}(W)* one in the transports of W. At the real points of the disc, which are regular with 2ε₁′, |𝔣_J| ≤ 2Ĉ_Jd̄′(x) by the bound above with ε₁′ for ε₁, d̄′ as in *The Alignment Field at Complex Points.* On the disc each current is the covariant divergence of the field strength (RG I (1.8)), so VAR (9)–(10) at c_0 and (19) at ε₂ ≤ 8B₅ε₁′ for 𝐇_c bound it by C^c_Jε₁′a_m^{−3} on the layer m, C^c_J := O(1)B₃M + 16L³B₅, the factor L³ for run B's finest layer. The pair-lift current is at most 2C_{Q*} times this, the transports of ι_Yc^A(z) being G^c-valued with Ad within a factor 2 of isometric. So |𝔣_J| ≤ 𝔐_J := (2C_{Q*} + 1)C^c_Jε₁′ on the disc, 𝔐_J ≤ 1 for γ small, and the Lemma (Two Constants) gives a_{j(x)}³|ΔJ_c(x)| ≤ (2Ĉ_Jd̄′(x))^{15/16} ≤ 2Ĉ_J^{15/16}d̄′(x)^{15/16} at (𝐔, 𝐉).
      - The current part at complex points. At (𝐔, 𝐉) the current part is bounded as above with 2Ĉ_J^{15/16}d̄′(x)^{15/16} for Ĉ_Jd̄(x). At u = 0 each current is below ½ of its threshold, at U^0_k by *The Room.* at complex points and at the other members with LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) there (derived, not reviewed, in *The conditions at the other configurations* and, at complex points, *The current entries.*). For |u| < T^c_J(Y) := min_c min_{x∈Y}α_{j(x)}/(8(1 + β₀)Ĉ_J^{15/16}d̄′(x)^{15/16}) the currents stay ≤ ϖ times their thresholds, as at real points, and the entries ι_Yc^A satisfy the configuration conditions by *The lift condition at complex points.* (in *The Fine Side at Complex Points.*), so the current part is ≤ 4r^c_J(Y) times the term-by-term bound of *The bound of f on the disc* at these points, r^c_J(Y) := 1/T^c_J(Y). *The Ratio.* bounds 8C(ε₀)^{15/16}C_U(M)^{15/16}(a_k/ε)^{15ε₀/16}(ε/a_{min(m,n)})^{5(1−ε₀)/16}(ε₁′/α_m)ε₁′^{−1/16}, which is at least 8C(ε₀)^{15/16}d̄′(x)^{15/16}/α_m, so it bounds d̄′(x)^{15/16}/α_m up to the factor 1/(8C(ε₀)^{15/16}). With the count of the domains Y, the current part at (𝐔, 𝐉) is therefore ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point at the layers ≥ n, for n ≥ n₀′, with C_h enlarged by a constant of Ĉ_J, β₀, and C(ε₀).
      - (T-E) consists of smallness conditions on ε₁ and is listed in (T-Λ). C_ℒ′ of (III) is the constant of the Cauchy note's §10.4 (*Regularity of the Lifts.*), whose proof uses the sup, gradient, and Hölder bounds of H₁e_y at the receiving layer and the local |F|; for the multiscale R₀e_y these come from PBG (3.133), Theorem 3.12, and (b), so it holds with the same constant.
    - *The bounds of V(Y) (quoted).* Run B's V(Y) satisfies on the whole space Ũ^c_k(Y, α̃₀, α̃₁) the bound (1.68), |V(Y, (𝐔, 𝐉))| ≤ αexp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), for Y ∖ Z^∼ ≠ ∅, and the bound (1.69), O(1)Σ_{j≤k}|Γ_j⁰ ∩ Y|, for Y a component of Z^∼ (p.377 l.8–19). Its arguments are the background and the shared fields; run B's chain and its links are functions of these.
    - *The count (derived).* Y ∩ Z^∼ is a union of components of Z^∼ containing at least one (p.377 l.5–6), so Y is determined by Y ∖ Z, and the tree of (1.67) meets every M-cube of the components of Y ∖ Z (p.376 l.28–30), the collar Z^∼ ∖ Z of each included component among them. Anchoring the tree at a collar cube gives Σ_Yαe^{−(1+2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)} ≤ C(κ)α times the number of M-cubes of the collars of Z^∼, for κ large against the entropy of the trees. The collar is one layer of the cubes of which Z is a union (CRE p.245 l.40–43, p.250 l.26), so it has at most 3^d M-cubes per M-cube of Z, and each M-cube of Z contains a point of the component's history. (1.69) is per point of Γ_j⁰.
    - *The variation of the chain (derived, not reviewed).* It has two inputs.
      - The bound of f on the disc (derived, not reviewed). At t ≠ 0 the entries c(t) are not run B's chain at any background, so (1.68) does not apply to f as it stands. Its proof applies at the entries term by term for the unrenormalised terms, and for the renormalised terms through their data (*The entries*, below). Its inputs are the localisation of the entry differences in the link maps and the conditions of the next item, marked below.
        - The bound (1.68) is a sum of term bounds. Each term of the expansions (1.51), (1.54), and (1.57)–(1.64) receives the factor (1.66) from its own inductive bound and from the decay of 𝐇″_k, 𝐇_{𝐁₁}, 𝐇_k, and 𝐇(𝐁″_k(Y_1), ·). The sums over the Y_i′, X, j, and the types then follow (p.376 l.39–46), and (1.68) itself is p.377 l.10–13. The identities between the members produce the expansion; they do not bound its terms. The 𝐄- and 𝐑-terms enter as renormalised combinations, sums over X ∋ z with a counterterm (p.376 l.40–41), whose bound holds where Theorem 2 applies. V^B(Y)[c] is that expansion with the entries in place of the members (*The entries.*).
        - The inductive bounds are on spaces of configurations. The analyticity of the expanded old terms follows from "the assumed analyticity of 𝐄^{(j)}(X, 𝐔, 𝐉) on the space U_j^c(X, α₀, α₁)" (RG I p.277 l.41–43). So a term at a link image of the entries has its bound once that image lies in its space.
        - The renormalisation of the proof of Theorem 2 (CRE pp.279–280) runs through RG I (3.28)–(3.36). Bałaban sets up the analysis for the argument exp iη𝐀 exp iL^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1}, though CRE's proof of Theorem 2 then puts 𝐀 = 0 (p.280 l.18–19), so (3.67) at 𝐀 ≠ 0 is an extension of his proof, not a printed statement: a minimiser in its gauge times an arbitrary regular field 𝐀 of the class (3.31), |𝐀|, |∇^η𝐀|, ‖𝐀‖_{1,β} < α₂ for 0 ≤ β ≤ β_H, with β_H Bałaban's β₀ there, a different constant from the β₀ of the thresholds. This is so because "the function 𝐇_k(B′) will be transformed into many different functions" (RG I p.276 l.27–41). For multiscale configurations the bounds are in the scale of the layer (CRE p.280 l.20–23).
        - The renormalised terms at the entries. In RG I the field 𝐀 sits in the data of a local minimisation, (3.25) and (3.28)–(3.33), and the terms are evaluated at the local minimiser of that data ((3.18), p.273 l.42–46; CRE p.280 l.16–20, where U_k on Ω_n∖Ω_{n+1} is a U_n-type configuration). So RG I's set-up applies to the renormalised terms at run B's local minimisers of data built from the entries, the link images.
          - The entries (derived, unreviewed). The entry c(t) = e^{it(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B is not a local minimiser of its own data for t ≠ 0, so a renormalised combination is not evaluated at c(t) itself. By the rule of *The entries.*, the term and its counterterm both read U_j(□₀, M^·(c(t))), and this holds also for the completions at U_k of p.377 l.22–26, which are 𝐄-terms at U_k. At t = 0 this is the value at c^B, by U_k = U_{j,□₀}(M^·(U_k)) (CRE p.283), away from the near domains at the boundaries of the minimisation domains. For t ≠ 0 the data M^·(c(t)) = M^·(exp iη𝐀 c^B) with η𝐀 := t(ε/L)𝐒_c is of the form (3.30) of RG I, a minimiser times exp iη𝐀 with 𝐀 in place of (tζ̃_□ + t_□ζ_□)𝐇_k(B′) (p.276 l.27–41). Term and counterterm then read one configuration, so the matching of tr F² in CRE (3.57), (3.61), and (3.66) is Bałaban's, and (3.67) holds at U_j(□₀, M^·(c(t))) once 𝐀 is in the class (3.31) on □₀ (the next item and (F-H)). So no non-minimiser defect arises. The rule covers the domains X ⊂ □^{∼n−j} of CRE's second proof. The domains of its second class, X ∩ (□^{∼n−j})^c ≠ ∅, are bounded by 2E₀exp(−½κ(n − j))exp(−½κd_j(X)) and read the configuration itself (CRE p.283); at an entry they need c(t) ∈ U_j^c(X, α₀, α₁), which is the membership of the link images in their terms' spaces (*The class at the entries.* and the next item), not a consequence of the rule.
          - At the endpoint ι_Yc^A the combinations read run B's U^B_j(□₀, M^·(c^A)), since M^·(ι_Yc^A) = M^·(c^A), and run A's read U^A_j(□₀, M^·(c^A)) = c^A. Their difference is an equal-data run difference of the composite d ↦ 𝐄(X, U_j(□₀, d)), of the type of (α) and (β), with the status of (α).
        - The class at the entries. On the disc |t|σ_c ≤ c_ρα_m at the layer m. The class (3.31) needs |𝐀|, |∇^η𝐀| < α₂ on □₀, and Lemma 4 needs |B′| < α₃ (RG I p.279 l.47–49), in the layer scale of CRE p.280 l.20–23. c_ρ is taken with c_ρα_m < min(α₂, α₃) in that scale, which changes only constants through r(Y) ∝ 1/c_ρ.
        - The link maps are run B's maps and local minimisations at the entries. Their decay on the complex class is PBG Theorem 3.4 and VAR p.309 l.1–4, as for the lifts. The factor e^{−δd((Y_1^0)^c∩Y_1∩Y′,X)} of (1.66) also needs the 𝐇-functions small on the boundary layers, from the localisation of their arguments (LFR II p.375 l.1–2 and l.7–8). At the entries the arguments are differences of entry data, so their localisation needs 𝐒_c − 𝐒_{c′} localised as the members' differences are.
          - The localisation of the entry differences (derived, not reviewed, with (iii)). Put D_c := (ε/L)𝐒_c. The members whose differences enter the 𝐇-arguments are one minimiser map at two data, c = Ψ(d_c) and c′ = Ψ(d_{c′}), with d_c − d_{c′} supported in a region R (Λ, or the boundary layers of Y_1^0 and Y_2^0), which is how Bałaban localises them (p.375 l.1–8). Write d^A_c, d^B_c for the two runs' data of c and δd_c := d^A_c − d^B_c for the data run difference, ≤ d̄. Then D_c = Δ(d^A_c) + [Ψ^B(d^A_c) − Ψ^B(d^B_c)], with the equal-data run difference Δ(d) := ι_YΨ^A(d) − Ψ^B(d), and the bracket is ∫₀¹∂Ψ^B(d^B_c + sδd_c)δd_c ds. So D_c − D_{c′} has three parts.
            - The equal-data part Δ(d^A_c) − Δ(d^A_{c′}) = ∫₀¹ ∂Δ(d_s)(d^A_c − d^A_{c′}) ds, d_s := d^A_{c′} + s(d^A_c − d^A_{c′}). ∂Δ(d_s) is the run difference of the linearised minimiser map, the operator H(s) of (iii) at the background Ψ(d_s), and the relative walk-term bound of (W-δ) (`WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md` §3) gives its kernel at most C_Hδ_w times the kernel bound of H, with the decay of (1.7). So this part is ≤ C_Hδ_w|d^A_c − d^A_{c′}|e^{−δ′d(x,R)}, with δ′ the decay rate of (1.7).
            - The data-difference part ∫₀¹∂Ψ^B(·)(δd_c − δd_{c′}) ds. R is the same set in both runs (Λ, or the boundary layers, built from the shared variables), and in each run the data of c and c′ agree off R; the data that differ, V_Λ^{(A)}(M^k(𝐔)) for U_k against U_{k,Λ} included, are functions of the variables in R, though not local within it. So δd_c − δd_{c′} is supported in R, and it is ≤ 2d̄. ∂Ψ^B has the kernel decay of (190) (VAR p.308), so this part is ≤ O(1)d̄e^{−⅛δ₀d(x,R)}.
            - The background part ∫₀¹[∂Ψ^B(d^B_c + sδd_c) − ∂Ψ^B(d^B_{c′} + sδd_{c′})]δd_{c′} ds. The two arguments differ by Δd := d^B_c − d^B_{c′} + s(δd_c − δd_{c′}), supported in R, and the bracket pairs the mixed second derivative δ²Ψ^B(x)/δd(y′)δd(y″) with Δd(y′), y′ ∈ R, and δd_{c′}(y″), which is spread everywhere. A Cauchy estimate in the direction Δd alone would give decay from the support of δd_{c′}, not from R. So each variable is taken in turn. Ψ^B is taken in the Landau-gauge representation of Proposition 9 relative to its base, where (190) bounds δ𝐇/δB; in the axial gauge the derivative would also contain ∂u/∂B along the contours, which (190) does not cover. ∂Ψ^B is analytic on the class with the kernel bound (190) uniformly there, and the polydisc of radius ρ in each single variable about every point of the segment stays in the class, since the segment lies in the class at half its radii. So Cauchy's estimate in d(y′), with the radius ρ := min over the layers of R of ½r_{0,m}, gives |δ²Ψ^B(x)/δd(y′)δd(y″)| ≤ (K/ρ)e^{−⅛δ₀d(x,y″)}, and in d(y″), by the symmetry of mixed partials, ≤ (K/ρ)e^{−⅛δ₀d(x,y′)}, both with the scale weights of (190). The smaller of the two is at most their geometric mean, (K/ρ)e^{−(δ₀/16)(d(x,y′)+d(x,y″))}. Summing over y″ with |δd_{c′}| ≤ d̄ and over y′ ∈ R with half the rate, the part is ≤ C(d̄/ρ)sup|Δd|e^{−(δ₀/32)d(x,R)}.
            - *The size of the data differences (derived, not reviewed).* At real points d_c − d_{c′} is the real difference of Bałaban's localisation (p.375 l.1–8), O(ε_k) and supported in R. At complex points it is not O(ε_k): the members' data on R are different functions of 𝐔 (M^k(𝐔) against V_Λ^{(A)}(M^k(𝐔)) for U_k against U_{k,Λ}), and their difference is of the α₁ scale. It is bounded pointwise, with decay from Λ, and the decay pays it where the configurations are read, as for the complex members.
              - R = Λ. For U_k against U_{k,Λ} the difference on Λ is (e^{iX} − 1)d_{c′}, X := −(1/i)log V_k′⌈_Λ, and for U_{k,Λ} against U_1 on Z″_k ⊂ Λ it is (e^{iY} − 1)d_{c′}, with |X| ≤ C_Λ(r_{0,k} + r_{1,k}) and |Y| ≤ C_Y(r_{0,k} + r_{1,k}) at complex points (*The data angles.* of *The complex members through (1.57)–(1.58) about 𝐔.*). So s_R(y) := |d_c − d_{c′}|(y) ≤ 4δ^c on R, δ^c as there.
              - R = the boundary layers. There the data of the representations (1.61)–(1.62) are (u_k^0)_−^{−1}exp iQ̃(η𝐇″_k)R((u_k^0)_+)M^·(U_1) and u_{1,−}^{−1}exp iQ̃(η𝐇_{𝐁₁})R(u_{1,+})M^·(U_{k,Λ}), against M^·(U_1) and M^·(U_{k,Λ}). Bałaban states that u(x) "is an analytic function of 𝐇 restricted to this block of the set 𝐁, to which the point x belongs", given by an explicit formula (p.373 l.29–31). At 𝐇 = 0 the configuration is unchanged, (1.57) gives U_1 = U_{k,Λ}^{u_1^{−1}} = U_{k,Λ}, and the gauge fixing makes u_1 = 1; by the locality, u(x) = 1 whenever 𝐇 vanishes on the block of x. So on the ball |𝐇| ≤ ρ_u of its analyticity, in the units of the layer, |u(x) − 1| ≤ C_u|𝐇|_{B(x)} for |𝐇|_{B(x)} ≤ ½ρ_u, C_u := 2sup|u − 1|/ρ_u, and the same holds for exp iQ̃(η𝐇) − 1. For (1.62), |𝐇_{𝐁₁}|(y) ≤ O(1)B_Ve^{−δ_Vd(y,Λ)}δ^c at complex points (*The steps.*), so s_R(y) ≤ C_R e^{−δ_Vd(y,Λ)}δ^c, C_R := O(1)(1 + C_u)B_V. For (1.61), |𝐇″_k(g_kB)| ≤ C_fac(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}ε_m (*The factor.*), of the ε scale as at real points.
              - The localisation with pointwise sizes. The three parts of *The localisation of the entry differences* hold with |d^A_c − d^A_{c′}| and sup|Δd| replaced by the pointwise s_R(y′) + 2d̄ inside the sums over y′ ∈ R, each kernel keeping half its rate. Since d(x, Λ) ≤ d(x, y′) + d(y′, Λ) and Λ ⊂ R or s_R(y′) carries e^{−δ_Vd(y′,Λ)}, the α₁-scale part gives |D_c − D_{c′}|(x) ≤ C_E δ^c e^{−δ‴d(x,Λ)}, δ‴ := ½min(δ″, δ_V), δ″ := min(δ′, δ₀/32), with C_E := C(C_Hδ_w + 1 + d̄/ρ)C_R a constant of d, L, and M, the remaining sums over y′ being volume-free. The ε-scale parts, from 𝐇″_k and from d̄, are C(C_Hδ_w + 1 + d̄/ρ)(C_fac(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}ε_k + d̄)e^{−δ″d(x,R)}, as at real points.
              - Where they are needed. At the points read by the terms, X ⊂ Z^c and □₀ ∩ Z, d(x, Λ) has the lower bounds of *The decay.* and *□₀ ∩ Z*, so the α₁-scale part is below ⅛ of the thresholds of the layer of x, frozen ones included, under (D-Λ) at the rate δ_D (*The decay.*), with C_E among the constants of (D-Λ). On the boundary layers, where the 𝐇-functions must be small for the factor of (1.66), the smallness at complex points is Bałaban's statement, made for the functions of (𝐔, 𝐉) (p.374 l.50 – p.375 l.8: "very small, because of the localization of the argument, and the exponential decay"), accepted.
          - On the disc. The normaliser σ_c(x) of *The joint line.* is enlarged by the bound of |D_c − D_{c′}|(x) of *The size of the data differences.*, C_Eδ^ce^{−δ‴d(x,Λ)} + C(C_Hδ_w + 1 + d̄/ρ)(C_fac(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}ε_k + d̄)e^{−δ″d(x,R)}, in the units of the layer of x, keeping the decay factors. The α₁-scale part is below ⅛ of the thresholds at the points read, under (D-Λ) (*Where they are needed.*). In the ε-scale part, ε_k/α_{0,m} is not bounded uniformly at the fine layers m ≪ k (CRE (2.6)), and the factor (k − m + 1)^{1/2} of ε_k/ε_m is paid by e^{−δ″d(x,R)} as in *U_{k,Λ}, U_1, and U_{1,2}*, at the plaquettes read off Z, where d(x, R) ≥ k₀ + 1 − m in M₁-cubes of the scale m. With (N₀ + 1)^{1/2}ε_k/α_m and d̄/α_m → 0 and these payments, the enlargement of r(Y) is ≤ ½r(Y)'s bound for n ≥ n₀′ and keeps the disc inside the room; on □₀ ∩ Z only the layers m ≥ k₀ + 1 occur, where the factor is at most N₀^{1/2} and is paid as there (*The cubes □₀ at Z.*, derived, not reviewed). Then |t||D_c − D_{c′}|(x) ≤ Cϱ(x)e^{−δ′d(x,R)}/a_{j(x)}, localised at the rate of the members' differences, and the 𝐇-functions are small on the boundary layers as at p.375. The input is (iii)'s transfer, the form (c) for H, derived, not reviewed, with (iii); no further input enters. For a pair of members that are not one map at two data (U_1 against U_2 across ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1}) the entries are those of *The background entry U_{1,2}.*, derived, not reviewed.
        - The choice of 𝐀₀ through the entry of U^0_k makes 𝐀₀ the link image M^·(c_{U^0_k}). The same term bounds cover it.
        - (F-H) The Hölder part of (3.31) at the entries (derived, unreviewed). Bounding ‖𝐒_c‖_{1,β} itself doesn't work: at the Hölder index β_H ≥ 19/20 of the Cauchy note's §5, the lattice bound costs (a_n/ε)^{β_H}, which exceeds the gain (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} of d̄. The bound of f needs less, since f only has to be bounded on the disc and r(Y) supplies the smallness.
          - Where the Hölder norm enters. RG I uses it only through (4.18), |∂∂B| < α₁(L^jη)^{2+β} for B = Q(ηA) (p.285). (4.18) enters the renormalised terms in two places, first for the second-order Taylor remainder of B in ⟨𝐄^{(3)}, B, B, B^{(2)}⟩, where it gives the power 4 + β in place of 5 in (4.22), "hence this is an irrelevant term" (p.288 l.37–40). It is also used for the third-order terms of (5.43) (p.298 l.3–4). The analyticity (3.36) is a statement about the spaces U_j^c(X, α₀, α₁). Lemma 4 as printed assumes (3.31) in full, the Hölder norm included (p.280 l.25), but its proof does not use the Hölder norm (checked line by line, p.279 l.33–p.280 l.22): 𝐀 enters only through (3.48)–(3.49), |Q′| ≤ O(1)L^jη|𝐀| < O(1)α₂L^jη with Q′ analytic in 𝐀, and τQ′ is then replaced by B′ with |B′| < α₃, so the proof needs only |𝐀| < α₂ with O(1)α₂L^jη < α₃; neither |∇^η𝐀| nor ‖𝐀‖_{1,β} of (3.31) (p.276 l.39) appears in it. CRE (3.66) analyses A(h_z, U_k) "in the same way" (p.283 l.16–20), so the counterterm needs the same second-difference bound and has its own excess.
          - The split. By RG I (3.48)–(3.49) (p.279), B = Q(L^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1}) + Q′ with |Q′| ≤ O(1)L^jη|𝐀|. The first part is that of the minimiser c^B, which satisfies (4.18) at every β ≤ 1 by VAR (9). By the argument of (4.17) (p.285), Q′ has |∂Q′| ≤ O(1)(L^jη)²(|∇^η𝐀| + |𝐀||∇A₀|), and the lattice bound gives |∂∂Q′| ≤ 2 max|∂Q′|. In the layer scale of CRE p.280 l.20–23, L^jη is L^{j−m} at the layer m.
          - The excess. The Taylor remainder with ∂∂Q′ in place of B^{(2)} has the form of (4.22) with the power 4 in place of 4 + β, the bound α₁(L^jη)^{2+β} of the second differences being replaced by the bound O(1)(L^jη)²|∇𝐀| of *The split.*, where |∇𝐀| stands for |∇^η𝐀| + |𝐀||∇A₀| + |𝐀| here and below, the last term covering the commutators [B, Q′] and [Q′, Q′] in F. Its size is derived per domain in *The counterterm and (5.43) per domain.* (*The excess.*): per position of the layer m it is at most C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}(α + |∇𝐀|)|∇𝐀|(L^{j−m})⁴e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)}. A domain Y meets L^{d(m−j)} = L^{4(m−j)} positions (d = 4) of the scale j per cube of the layer m, against the factor (L^{j−m})⁴, so the excess is at most C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}(α + sup|∇𝐀|)sup|∇𝐀| per cube of the layer m and per generation, a γ-independent multiple of E₀ wherever α and |∇𝐀| are at most fixed multiples of α₃. It is not summable in the age, but there are at most m generations.
          - The counterterm and (5.43) per domain (derived, unreviewed). The counterterm −β_jA(h_z, ·) has no domain X of its own. Its coefficient is a sum over the domains: β_j = −½Σ_xΠ^{(j)}_{22}(x)x_1² (CRE (3.64)), and Π^{(j)} is (3.50) with 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U_j, z) replaced by 𝐄^{(j)}(U_j, z) on the whole lattice L^{−j}Z^d, the sum over the X ∋ z of the second derivatives at the trivial configuration (CRE p.281 l.46–59; (3.62)–(3.64) on p.282–283). By (2.27), Π(x, y, z) = Σ_{X∋z}Π(X; x, y, z), and β_j is linear in Π, and Π^{(j)}_{μν,κλ} is independent of z (p.281 l.56–60), so for each z, β_j = Σ_{X∋z}β_j(X), β_j(X) := Σ_{x,y}Π^{(j)}_{22}(X; x, y, z)(x_1 − z_1)(y_1 − z_1), the dependence of β_j(X) on z being suppressed, which equals −½Σ_{x,y}Π^{(j)}_{22}(X; x, y, z)(x_1 − y_1)², since Σ_xΠ^{(j)}_{22}(X; x, y, z) = 0 and Σ_yΠ^{(j)}_{22}(X; x, y, z) = 0: the first identity (I.4.15), which (3.54) uses per X, with λ(x) = x₂, and the symmetry of (3.50) for the second argument, so no translation invariance enters the per-X identity, unlike (3.62), beyond the z-independence of the total used for the split. These X ∋ z are infinite-volume domains at the trivial configuration, attached to z, not the localisation domains of the expansion; the attachment is legitimate because the counterterm piece depends on U only on supp h_z, and z ∈ X, so d(S, X) ≤ d(S, z) for every set S. For the actual domains X ⊂ □^{∼2} the piece stays with X, read on X^∼ ⊃ supp h_z (supp h_z = {x : |x_μ − z_μ| < 1 for all μ} on the lattice T_{L^{−j}}, CRE p.283 l.15 with (3.40)), those that meet Z included, so the per-X cancellation of *The second-order parts.* below is kept. This allocation is this note's construction; it goes with Bałaban's treatment of the terms with domains meeting Z, which he adds, subtracts, and resums into the V(Y) (LFR II p.377 l.22–26). Only the completing domains there, which need not be localisation domains of the scale k, have their pieces attached to Y_0, the smallest localisation domain of 𝐃_k (CRE p.254 l.47) containing □₀ = □^{∼5}, with d_k(Y_0) ≤ C(d), at the cost of the constant e^{(1+3β)κC(d)}, and summed over X by the bound of |β_j(X)| below. The kernels are not supported in X. By RG I (4.2)–(4.3) they are derivatives of 𝐄^{(j)}(X, exp iξ𝐇_j(□₀, B)), and the derivatives of 𝐇_j(□₀, ·) decay exponentially in the length of a shortest tree graph from their points to their final point (p.282 l.1–4), which lies in X, since 𝐄^{(j)}(X, ·) depends on 𝐀 only on X; in the norm (4.4) a factor B_i localised outside X brings exp(−δ₀dist^{(ξ)}(X, supp B_i)) (p.282 l.8–9). With the B_i at single bonds x_i in (4.3), this factor taken for the point farthest from X, and the constant of (4.5) (p.282 l.13–16), |𝐄^{(n)}(X; x_1, …, x_n, z)| ≤ (2n²B₃/α₂)^nE₀e^{−κd_j(X)}e^{−δ₀max_idist^{(ξ)}(X, x_i)} ≤ (2n²B₃/α₂)^nE₀e^{−κd_j(X)}e^{−(δ₀/n)Σ_idist^{(ξ)}(X, x_i)}, so only the one factor that Bałaban prints is used. The coefficients of the split are the whole-lattice kernels of (3.50), the derivatives at B = 0 of 𝐄^{(j)}(X, W(B), z) with W(B) := U_j(exp iB) the scale-j minimiser on L^{−j}Z^d (CRE p.281 l.56–59), for every X ∋ z; so β_j = Σ_{X∋z}β_j(X) and the cancellation Σ_{X∋z}D_{μν,κλ}(X, z) = 0 of *The second-order parts.* below hold exactly. Their decay is VAR's: Sect. G is set for the minimiser of the whole problem (VAR p.277 l.11–20, where Ω₀ = ⋯ = Ω_k is admissible), its first derivative decays at the rate ⅛δ₀ ((190), p.308), the higher derivatives are its tree expressions with the decay of RG I p.282 l.2–4, at a rate δ_t > 0, VAR's constants are absolute or depend on d, L, and M (VAR p.305 l.44), not on the volume, and the whole lattice is Bałaban's own (CRE p.281 l.57–59); so these kernels satisfy the bound above with δ_t in place of δ₀ and a constant C(n) in place of 2n²B₃. The actual terms with X ⊂ □^{∼2} are expanded through U_j(□₀, ·), □₀ = □̃⁵ (RG I (3.18)–(3.19), p.273 l.37–p.274 l.8), with the kernels of (4.2)–(4.3), and these differ from the whole-lattice ones by Δ(X) (derived, not reviewed). W(B) is a minimal configuration of the class for small real B, so W(B) = U_j(□₀, D(B)) with D(B) := M^·(W(B)), Bałaban's representation of a minimal configuration through its own data on □₀ (RG I (3.18) and (3.39), CRE p.283 l.48–49). On the scale-j bonds of □_j ⊃ □_{k+1} ⊃ □̃⁴ (RG I p.273 l.40–41) D(B) = exp iB, the constraint of W(B); on the finer layers of □₀'s sequence, which lie outside □̃⁴, D(B) consists of finer averages of W(B), and U_j(□₀, ·) has its data on all these bonds (RG I p.276 l.35–36). By the chain rule, the terms of a B-derivative of 𝐄^{(j)}(X, U_j(□₀, D(B)), z) at 0 in which every derivative falls on the scale-j data in □_j give the kernel of (4.2); each other term contains a derivative of U_j(□₀, ·) in a datum b on a finer layer, which brings exp(−δ₀dist^{(ξ)}(X, b)) (p.282 l.8–9) with dist^{(ξ)}(X, b) ≥ dist^{(ξ)}(□̃², (□̃⁴)^c) ≥ M(L^jL^{−m})^{−1} in CRE's translation to the L^{−m}-scale (CRE p.280 l.19–23), times derivatives of the finer averages at b, which are averages of derivatives of W and decay from b at the rate δ_t. Half of the first factor pays the sum over b, the number of finer bonds at the distance r from X being polynomial in r and L^{m−j}, so the moments of Δ(X) are at most C(n, p, δ₀, δ_t, M, L)(B₃/α₂)^nE₀P_M(d_j(X))e^{−κd_j(X)}L^{c(m−j)}e^{−½δ₀M(L^jL^{−m})^{−1}}, c a constant of d, and its part of the expansion of the actual term is irrelevant: with the field bounds below it is at most C_N(L^jL^{−m})^N times the bound of its X, for every N, and it stays with X. The positions z are those of Λ_j⁰, Λ_j less one layer of the MR_j-cubes (CRE p.259 l.30–31), as in (3.67), and □^{∼2} ⊂ Λ_j for them is derived except at one corner of an 𝐑-operation below, where the missing domains are bounded instead: Λ_j is a union of MR_j-cubes of the scale j (CRE p.256 l.21–24), so the points of Λ_j⁰ have the sup-distance ≥ MR_ja_j from Λ_j^c, while □ ∈ π_m has the side Ma_m and □^{∼2} lies within 3Ma_m of z (p.279 l.45–48), which MR_ja_j covers at m = j since R_j ≥ L ≥ 3 for γ small (CRE (2.5), l.630); for m > j and the domains of a transformation step it is derived: z ∈ Ω_m, Λ_{m−1} ⊂ Λ_j by (2.1) (p.254 l.47–49), Z_{m−1} = Λ_{m−1}^c (p.264 l.52), B^m(P¹_m) avoids Z′^{∼5}_{m−1} (p.265 l.18–19), and Ω_m keeps three further layers from B^m(P¹_m)^c ((3.5)), all of LMR_m-cubes of the step's frame, of the side MR_ma_m (p.265 l.12–14), so dist(Ω_m, Λ_j^c) ≥ 8MR_ma_m ≥ 3Ma_m. At the domains of an 𝐑-operation (LFR I (1.10)–(1.12), p.179) the integrations on Z contribute to the boundary terms only (p.189 l.14–16; (1.61)–(1.62), the renormalisation of the Wilson action included), the sum still runs over the old sequences {Ω_j^c, Z_j} (p.201 l.13–14), and the old terms stay connected with the old determining set (LFR II p.375 l.32–34); so the renormalised terms there are the old 𝐄^{(j)}(Λ_j, ·), Λ_j a union of MR_j-cubes with its old Λ_j⁰, while the layer m of z is that of the new sequence Ω″. Here Ω^{∼n} is the enlargement by n layers (Z″_k ⊃ Z″_{k−N₀+1} in (1.10), as Z_j = Λ_j^c increases with j), and R_j is a power of L that drops by at most one factor L per step (CRE (2.5); LFR I p.177 l.19–21), so the minimality of N₀ gives R_ja_j ≥ La_k for k − N₀ + 1 < j ≤ k, and MR_{k−N₀+1}a_{k−N₀+1} = Ma_k. On Z^c, Ω″_m = Ω_m and the bound of a transformation step applies. On Z (derived, not reviewed), z ∈ Λ_j⁰ lies in Ω_j ⊂ Ω″_j, so m ≥ j; at m = j the margin MR_ja_j ≥ 3Ma_m; for j < m ≤ k − N₀, z ∈ Ω″_m = Ω_m^{∼5} and dist(Ω_m, Λ_j^c) ≥ 8MR_ma_m, so the gap is ≥ 3MR_ma_m; for m ≥ k − N₀ + 1 and j ≤ k − N₀, Ω″_m ⊂ Ω^{∼7}_{k−N₀+1}, with Ω″_k = Ω^{∼5}_{k−N₀+1}, and dist(Ω_{k−N₀+1}, Λ_j^c) ≥ 8Ma_k, so the gap is ≥ Ma_k ≥ 3Ma_m for m ≤ k − 1 and ≥ 3Ma_k at m = k; for k − N₀ + 2 ≤ j < m the margin MR_ja_j ≥ LMa_k ≥ 3Ma_m; for j = k − N₀ + 1 < m ≤ k − 1 the margin Ma_k ≥ 3Ma_m; for j = k − N₀ + 1, m = k, and z ∈ Ω_{k−N₀+2}, dist(Ω_{k−N₀+2}, Λ_j^c) ≥ 8LMa_k. At the corner j = k − N₀ + 1, m = k, z ∈ Λ_j⁰ ∖ Ω_{k−N₀+2}, the margin is one M-cube of the scale k and □ ∈ π_k has that side, so □^{∼2} can leave Λ_j (CRE does not describe the 𝐑-operation and only assumes that its output has the properties of the inductive description, p.244 l.41–42); there the missing domains X ∋ z, X ⊂ □^{∼2}, X ⊄ Λ_j, are treated as the completing domains (derived, not reviewed). The expansion of the actual X ⊂ Λ_j ∩ □^{∼2} goes through as above, since □ ⊂ Λ_j ⊂ Ω_{k−N₀+1} (Λ_j is a union of scale-k M-cubes) gives □₀ = □^{∼5} ⊂ Ω^{∼5}_{k−N₀+1} = Ω″_k, inside the small-field layer k. The total over all X ∋ z of the marginal parts cancels against the counterterm as before, so what is left of the missing X is −β_j(X)[A(h_z, ·) − A(h_z, 1)] and their D-parts, ½tr F² and the anisotropic quadratic form at z with the coefficients β_j(X) and D_{μν,κλ}(X, z). In the scale j the field strength at a point of the layer m is at most O(1)(L^{j−m})²(α + |∇𝐀|) (CRE p.280 l.19–23), so each is at most C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}(α + |∇𝐀|)²(L^{j−m})⁴e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)} per position, by the bound of |β_j(X)| and the moments with n = 2. This is the form of the excess with α + |∇𝐀| in place of |∇𝐀|, again two ratios of field bounds to α₃, and *The sum over the terms* (l.656) counts at most three such ratios per term. These pieces are attached to Y_0 at the cost e^{(1+3β)κC(d)} and summed over X by the factor e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)}, like the completing domains. Since j = k − N₀ + 1 < k at the corner (N₀ = 1 makes it m = j), the factor (1 + 7β/2) of the generation k is not needed there. At most |X|(2r + 1)^d points lie at the distance r from X, and |x_i − z| ≤ diam(X) + dist(X, x_i) for z ∈ X, with |X| and diam(X) polynomial in d_j(X) with coefficients depending on M. So the moments Σ_{x_1,…,x_n}|𝐄^{(n)}(X; x_1, …, x_n, z)|Π_i|x_i − z|^{p_i} are at most C(n, p, δ₀, δ_t, M)(B₃/α₂)^nE₀P_M(d_j(X))e^{−κd_j(X)} ≤ C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−n}e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)}, with α₂ = 2B₃c₂L²α₃/β_I at each index (the W-δ note's l.178 and l.1092), C(M, β, κ) depending also on δ₀, B₃, c₂, L, and β_I, and P_M a polynomial whose coefficients depend on M. The same decay makes the sums over x and y absolutely convergent, so β_j = Σ_Xβ_j(X) may be summed in either order, and (I.4.15) applies with the unbounded λ(x) = x₂. In particular |β_j(X)| ≤ C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)}. The counterterm is assigned to the domains through this split, β_jA(h_z, ·) = Σ_{X}β_j(X)A(h_z, ·), each piece with the factor of its X. Bałaban analyses A(h_z, U_k) "in the same way as above" (CRE p.283 l.16–17), that is, by the analysis of pp.280–281 with A(h_z, ·) in place of 𝐄^{(j)}(X, ·, z) (derived, not reviewed): supp h_z = {|x_μ − z_μ| < 1} in the scale j (p.283 l.15) lies within a_j ≤ Ma_m of z, hence in □̃; A(h_z, ·) is the sum of the Wilson terms of the plaquettes p weighted by h_z(x(p)) (CRE p.253 l.27–29), so it depends on the plaquettes in supp h_z, is entire in them, is gauge invariant, and is bounded on the class by a constant of d times the square of the class radius; RG I's passage from U_k to U_j(□₀, ·), (3.3) (p.270 l.23–30, U_k = U_j(Ū^j_k) up to a gauge transformation, by VAR Sect. G and (97) of [12]) and (3.18)–(3.19) (p.273 l.42–47, p.274 l.1–8), uses of the function only its gauge invariance and its dependence on a domain in □̃², and CRE p.280 l.14–23 carries it to the layer m; the Ward–Takahashi identities hold for every analytic gauge-invariant function of small configurations (RG I p.282 l.42–46, (4.7)). So A(h_z, U_j(□₀, exp iB)) is expanded by its kernels at the trivial configuration, and the Ward identities move the factors B to z, which gives ½Σ_{μ<ν}tr F_{μν}²(z) plus Taylor remainders of B about z, which contain only second differences of B, and terms of higher order in B ((3.66)). With B = Q(L^{−1}η𝐇_{k+1}) + Q′ of *The split.*, the remainders with a second difference of Q′ are bounded through |∂∂Q′| ≤ 2max|∂Q′|, as for the 𝐄-terms in *The excess.* below, and are of the order (L^{j−m})⁴(α + |∇𝐀|)|∇𝐀| at a point of the layer m, with a constant set by the normalisation of A, the power 4 in place of 5 − β of (3.67). Times |β_j(X)| this is at most C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}(α + |∇𝐀|)|∇𝐀|(L^{j−m})⁴e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)} per position, of the size of the 𝐄-term excess (*The excess.* below), and so at most C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}(α + sup|∇𝐀|)sup|∇𝐀| per cube of the layer m and per generation. So the 𝐄-term excess and the counterterm excess have the form of RG I (4.22) attached to a domain X, with the power 4 and with C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}(α + |∇𝐀|)|∇𝐀| in place of the prefactor (32B₃(α₁/α₂)c₀(δ₁)c₁(δ₁))⁴ of (4.22).
            - The third-order terms per domain (derived, not reviewed). RG I's decomposition (5.37)–(5.38) acts on the total Π and uses its symmetries, so it has no per-X form. The proof of Theorem 2 in CRE expands per domain instead, "an approach to renormalization, which is alternative to the one described in Sects. 3–5 [I]" (CRE p.279), and p.376 l.39–46 names that form: the sum over X "includes renormalization in the form described in the proof of Theorem 2". In it the third-order terms of (5.43) are replaced by the irrelevant terms of each X ((3.56) holds per X). CRE then cancels the marginal term per position with the total Π^{(j)} ((3.57)). The per-domain cancellation, with which every step carries the factor of its X, is this note's construction (*The second-order parts.* below).
              - Per domain. Let z lie in the layer m, with □ ∈ π_m the cube containing z. For X ∋ z with X ⊂ □^{∼2}, the Ward–Takahashi identities (I.4.14)–(I.4.15) move the factors B to the point z, and (3.49) and (3.56) (CRE p.280–281) give Σ_{n≤4}(1/n!)⟨𝐄^{(n)}(X, z), ⊗ⁿB⟩ = ½Σ_{κ<μ, λ<ν}𝐄^{(2)}_{μν,κλ}(X, z)tr F_{κμ}(z)F_{λν}(z) + (the irrelevant terms), the irrelevant terms bounded by O((L^jL^{−m})^{5−β})exp(−κd_j(X)) for each X (p.281 l.44–46). The domains X ∋ z with X ⊄ □^{∼2} are bounded by (3.48), each with the factor exp(−κ(L^jL^{−m})^{−1})exp(−½κd_j(X)) of its own X.
              - The excess. The irrelevant terms that contain a second difference of B come from the Taylor remainders of B about z, B_μ(x) − B_μ(z) − Σ_κ(x_κ − z_κ)(∂_κB_μ)(z), each a sum of at most |x − z|² second differences along a lattice path in the cube hull of {x, z}, paired with 𝐄^{(2)}(X, x, y, z); the constant parts are annihilated in both arguments by (I.4.15). With ∂∂Q′ in place of ∂∂B they are bounded through |∂∂Q′| ≤ 2max|∂Q′| as the counterterm's, by O(1)(L^{j−m})⁴(α + |∇𝐀|)|∇𝐀| at the points of the layer m times the moment Σ_{x,y}|𝐄^{(2)}(X, x, y, z)||x − z|²(1 + |y − z|)² ≤ C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)}, by the kernel bound and the moments of *The counterterm and (5.43) per domain.* with n = 2. This is the form of the counterterm's excess above, attached to X. *The third-order remainder.* RG I expands the last factor of ⟨𝐄^{(3)}, B, B, B⟩ to the second order about the point by the lattice Taylor formula (4.30), whose remainder is a sum of at most ½|Γ_{x,x₃}|² second differences of B_{μ₃} (p.288 l.29–39). With ∂∂Q′ in place of ∂∂B, this remainder is at most O(1)|x₃ − z|²(L^{j−m})²|∇𝐀|, by |∂∂Q′| ≤ 2max|∂Q′| and the bound of ∂Q′ of *The split.* The two other factors satisfy |B| ≤ O(1)α₁L^jη = O(1)α₁L^{j−m} on the disc on □̃⁴ ⊃ □^{∼2}, the parts of the sums outside □̃⁴ being exponentially small by RG I (4.1)–(4.5) (p.280 l.44–46, p.282 l.13–19), the minimiser part by |B| < α₁L^jη and Q′ by |Q′| ≤ O(1)L^jη|𝐀| with |𝐀| < α₂ < α₁ (RG I p.280 l.44–45, with CRE's translation of RG I's η-scale to the L^{−n}-scale, p.280 l.19–23). The moment Σ_{x₁,x₂,x₃}|𝐄^{(3)}(X, x₁, x₂, x₃, z)||x₃ − z|² ≤ C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−3}e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)} follows from the kernel bound and the moments of *The counterterm and (5.43) per domain.* with n = 3. So this part is at most C(M, β, κ)(α₁/α₃)²E₀α₃^{−1}(L^{j−m})⁴|∇𝐀|e^{−(1−β)κd_j(X)}. At equal indices the ratio α₁/α₃ = 2B₃λ_RL²/β_I is a constant of M, L, B₃, and β_I (§8 of the W-δ note, stages 2 and 3). Here α₁ is the field bound at the points of the layer m, α_{1,m} of CRE (2.39) (p.261 l.35–38), a fixed multiple of α̃_{0,m} by q₁ = q₀ and C₁ = λ_RC₀ (the W-δ note's l.1101), and α₃ is at the term's index j, so α₁/α₃ ≤ C(M)2^{q₀}(1 + β₀)(m − j + 1)^{1/2} by CRE (2.6) as in *The result*, and the factor (α₁/α₃)² is at most C(M)4^{q₀}(1 + β₀)²(m − j + 1), which *The result* carries. This is the form of the 𝐄^{(2)} remainder above with α₃ in place of α + |∇𝐀|, attached to X.
              - The second-order parts. The coefficients 𝐄^{(2)}_{μν,κλ}(X, z) of a single domain need not be isotropic. For the infinite-volume domains, Σ_{X∋z}𝐄^{(2)}_{μν,κλ}(X, z) = Π^{(j)}_{μν,κλ} ((3.57), p.281), which by the covariance (3.58)–(3.60) vanishes for κ < μ, λ < ν unless μ = ν and κ = λ, and then equals β′_j = Π^{(j)}_{22,11} = β_j ((3.61), (3.64)). So 𝐄^{(2)}_{μν,κλ}(X, z) = β_j(X)δ_{μν}δ_{κλ} + D_{μν,κλ}(X, z) for κ < μ, λ < ν, with β_j(X) = 𝐄^{(2)}_{22,11}(X, z), the coefficient of the split above at the same z, and Σ_{X∋z}D_{μν,κλ}(X, z) = 0 for every z and all κ < μ, λ < ν. No excess arises at the order n = 1, the first-order term vanishing by RG I (4.14) for G semisimple (p.284 l.18–23), nor at the order n = 4, whose terms contain first differences only. The F(z) are those of B at z, the same for all X ∋ z, so the D-parts of all infinite-volume X ∋ z sum to 0 at each z and at every configuration. Over the actual domains they therefore sum to minus the D-parts of the completing domains below, which carry the completing factor and are attached to the domain Y_0 ⊇ □₀ of the completing counterterm pieces, which is sound because every X ∋ z of a cube reads the same □₀ in (1.59) (LFR II p.372 l.5–12). Each run applies this regrouping to its own terms; it rearranges absolutely convergent sums, so the totals are unchanged, and the regrouped parts enter the run comparison only through the per-X bounds above. The β_j(X)-parts, β_j(X)·½Σ_{μ<ν}tr F_{μν}²(z), cancel against the counterterm pieces β_j(X)A(h_z, ·) up to β_j(X) times the irrelevant terms of (3.66), which is the counterterm's excess above. The 𝐄-terms of the actual domains X ⊂ □^{∼2} are infinite-volume ones: their domains lie in Λ_j, and such terms do not depend on Λ_j (CRE p.279 l.18–20). Their coefficients in (3.49) are read through U_j(□₀, ·), and they differ from those of the whole-lattice expansion per domain by Δ(X) of *The counterterm and (5.43) per domain.*, whose part is irrelevant and stays with X (Bałaban resums (3.56) with the total Π^{(j)}, p.281 l.46–59, and does not state the per-domain comparison). The infinite-volume X ∋ z with X ⊄ □^{∼2} that complete the sum have d_j(X) ≥ 2(L^jL^{−m})^{−1}, since X contains z ∈ □ and a point off □^{∼2}, and with the inductive bound on such X (for X ⊄ Λ_j through Λ_j = the whole lattice: Bałaban's terms with X ⊂ Λ do not depend on Λ, CRE p.279 l.17–19, and satisfy (I.1.18), p.259 l.39–44) they carry the factor exp(−(1 − 2β)κ(L^jL^{−m})^{−1})exp(−½κd_j(X)) through (3.48) and the moments above, with exp(−(1 − 2β)κ(L^jL^{−m})^{−1}) ≤ C(β, κ)(L^jL^{−m})^5 (p.280), and their counterterm pieces are attached to the domain Y_0 ⊇ □₀ of *The counterterm and (5.43) per domain.*
              - At j = k. The counterterm pieces and the excess are bounded through moments of 𝐄^{(2)}(X, ·, ·, z) and 𝐄^{(3)}(X, ·, ·, ·, z) of order at most three, the same moments that bound Bałaban's irrelevant terms of (3.56). He states those with the factor exp(−κd_j(X)) of the inductive bound (p.281 l.44–46), and at j = k the renormalised terms carry the factor exp(−(1 + 4β)κd_k(X)) of the improved bounds after the kth transformation (p.376 l.9–10, with renormalisation in the form of Theorem 2's proof, l.39–46). For the counterterm pieces and the excess at j = k, the improved bound holds on the slightly larger spaces (CRE p.262 l.26), which contain the Cauchy circle, and with the kernel decay above it gives the moments with the factor e^{−(1+7β/2)κd_k(X)}, in particular |β_k(X)| ≤ C(M, β, κ)E₀α₃^{−2}e^{−(1+7β/2)κd_k(X)}. So (1 + 3β)κd_k(X) is extracted with Y_0 = X, and e^{−½βκd_k(X)} remains for the sum over X, summable over X ∋ z for κ large at fixed β (the condition is recorded under κ in CONSTANTS_AND_CONVENTIONS_WDELTA.md), Bałaban's order of the choices (CRE p.260 l.27–28: κ₀ "can be chosen arbitrarily large, similarly as κ, if the other parameters are fixed properly"): ½βκ > c_dom, c_dom the growth rate of the number of domains X ∋ z in d_k(X) (not the c_d of the W-δ note's l.184).
          - The sum over the positions (derived, not reviewed). Bałaban extracts (1.66) and the factor exp(−(1+3β)κd_{k,Z}(Y_4)) from the inductive bound exp(−κd_j(X)) of each term before any renormalisation (p.376 l.3–6, and after (1.67)). The remainder exp(−βκd_j(X) − ½δd((Y_1^0)^c∩Y_1∩Y′_{i_0}, X) − Σ_i ½(κ₁−1)M^{−d}|Y_i′|) controls the resummations, and for the 𝐄- and 𝐑-terms the sum over X "includes renormalization in the form described in the proof of Theorem 2" (p.376 l.39–46). That renormalisation produces his own irrelevant terms, which CRE states with the factor exp(−κd_j(X)) (p.281 l.44–46) and RG I (4.22) with exp(−⅓κd_j(X)) (p.286 l.20), applied to this remainder. The excess of the 𝐄-terms and of the counterterms has the form of (4.22), with the factor exp(−(1 − β)κd_j(X)) in place of its exp(−⅓κd_j(X)), attached to its X by *The counterterm and (5.43) per domain.*, with the power 4 in place of 4 + β. His resummation applies to it, as follows from p.376 l.2–9 and l.34–41. Bałaban's extraction is exp(−κd_j(X)) ≤ exp(−βκd_j(X))exp(−(1 − β)κ(L^jη)^{−1}d_k(Y_0)) ≤ exp(−βκd_j(X))exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_k(Y_0)) for j < k (p.376 l.3–7), with (L^jη)^{−1} ≥ L. The excess carries the rate (1 − β)κ, and the same two steps give exp(−(1 − β)κd_j(X)) ≤ exp(−βκd_j(X))exp(−(1 − 2β)κ(L^jη)^{−1}d_k(Y_0)) ≤ exp(−βκd_j(X))exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_k(Y_0)), since (1 − 2β)L ≥ 1 + 3β for L ≥ 2 and β ≤ 1/7, which the choice β = 1/8 (§4(d), *The choice β = 1/8, checked.*) meets. The per-X bounds of the excess hold at every configuration of the class, so they hold uniformly on the polydisc of the variables s of his factors of the Y′_i, as his bounds of the terms do. So the excess is a sum over the pairs (X, z ∈ X) of per-X bounds that carry his extracted factor and his remainder (p.376 l.37), and the sum over X ∋ z uses exp(−βκd_j(X)) as his does for j < k, and at j = k the remainder e^{−½βκd_k(X)} of *At j = k.*, which is likewise summable. A block of the layer m contains (L^{m−j})⁴ positions z of the scale j (the points of Λ_j⁰, CRE (3.67), p.283 l.28–31, counted in the scale j as in (2.43), p.263 l.21–22), against the factor (L^{j−m})⁴ of the power 4, which gives the factor 1 per j and, summed over j, the factor m, with the threshold factor (m − j + 1)^{3/2} per generation of *The result* below. At j = k, where Bałaban uses the improved factor (1 + 4β) (p.376 l.9–10), the counterterm pieces and the excess carry (1 + 7β/2), enough for the extraction of (1 + 3β), by *At j = k.* above. It carries the factor of its own X, so no volume factor arises for Y ∖ Z^∼ ≠ ∅. The only change is in the sum over j at p.376 l.41: the power 4 is marginal in the age, which gives the factor m ≤ k in place of CRE (2.45)'s (1 − L^{−β})^{−1}, as in *The result* below.
          - The result, given the localisation of the entry differences above. On the disc, |t|‖𝐒_c‖ ≤ ϱ = c_ρα_m at the layer m (*The joint line.*). Per cube of the layer m and per generation j ≤ m the excess is at most C(M, β, κ)E₀[α₃^{−2}(α_f + c_ρα_m)c_ρα_m + (α₁/α₃)²α₃^{−1}c_ρα_m], with α₃ at the term's index j (the W-δ note's l.178 and l.1092) and the field bounds α_f and α₁ at the points of the layer m, at most fixed multiples of α̃_{0,m} (CRE (2.38)–(2.39), p.261 l.30–38, with q₁ = q₀ and C₁ = λ_RC₀, the W-δ note's l.1101); α_f is the field bound written α in *The counterterm and (5.43) per domain.*, and α below is that of (1.68). By CRE (2.6) (p.255 l.44–46), g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)(m − j)^{1/2}g_j and g_j ≤ (1 + β₀)g_m for m > j, so log g_m^{−2} ≤ 2log g_j^{−2} for g small, and α_m ≤ α̃_{0,m} = C₀g_m(log g_m^{−2})^{q₀} ≤ 2^{q₀}(1 + β₀)(m − j + 1)^{1/2}α̃_{0,j}. So each ratio of a bound at the index m to α₃ is at most C(M)2^{q₀}(1 + β₀)(m − j + 1)^{1/2}, each term of the excess has at most three such ratios, and the excess is at most C(M, β, κ)E₀8^{q₀}(1 + β₀)³(m − j + 1)^{3/2}. Summed over the generations j ≤ m ≤ k it is at most C_Fk^{5/2}. So sup|f| ≤ (α + C_Fk^{5/2})e^{−(1+2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)} for Y ∖ Z^∼ ≠ ∅, with C_F a constant of E₀, c₂, λ_R, β_I, B₃, L, δ₀, β₀, q₀, M, β, κ, the absolute constant B of (2.38), and c_ρ, which are also the dependences of the constants C(M) of the ratios, not of γ; for the components of Z^∼, (1.69) gets the same additive term per point. The factor k^{5/2}, k < 8n + j(C), is polynomial in n, and the slack factors L^{−(1−97ε₀)n/12} at real points and L^{−(1−121ε₀)n/16} at complex points of the ratio absorb it, with a constant polynomial in j(C).
        - The other input is the next item, which puts each link image in its term's space.
      - The conditions at the other configurations that the terms read (derived at U^0_k, U_k, U″_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_1 = U_{1,2} on Z^c, and for the plaquettes and currents of the determining-set configurations, unreviewed; at the representation configuration of (1.61) at complex s by Bałaban's (1.65); with *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, unreviewed, for the halves of the thresholds). What a term needs is that its argument lies in its analyticity domain, at the thresholds α̃′ along the line.
        - U^0_k. The second-stage terms read U^0_k only, as 𝐄(X, U^0_k), and Ũ″^c_k(Y_1, α̃₀, α̃₁) "is contained in the analyticity domain of the term 𝐄(X) we consider, hence in the analyticity domains of all terms" (p.375 l.29–35). Its conditions are (1.65) (*The room at real points.*).
        - U_k. The real backgrounds of the support lie in Ũ^c_k(Y_4, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2) (§3(d), *The room at real points.*).
        - U″_k. The 𝐁-terms 𝐁_k(U″_k, A) + 𝐁″_k(U″_k, A) of (1.49) read U″_k. Their analyticity domain is the space of LFR I (1.70), Ũ_k^{(n)c}(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), defined by (1.64)–(1.69) (LFR I p.192 l.6–11). At real points U″_k lies in it, since it lies on the support of χ″_k = χ_k^{(N)} (LFR I (1.24) at n = N), and the spaces (1.64)–(1.69) are "modelled on the definition (1.24)" of the characteristic functions (p.190 l.9–11). The current and A′ conditions are Bałaban's: the 𝐁-terms of (1.49) are evaluated at U″_k, so with its current it lies in their space (his usage, accepted, as at U_k, CRE p.259 l.40–44). VAR (9)–(10) would not give them uniformly in K at a layer far below k, since VAR (7) takes one ε₁ for all layers (p.278). This holds at α̃′/2 (*Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*), since α′_{i,m}/(2ε_m) exceeds every constant for γ small. The space of (1.70) is stated for the new terms ℂ_k^{(n)}, and the old 𝐁_k(U″_k, A) lie in CRE's spaces, which p.375 l.32–34 covers.
        - The determining-set configurations. The space (1.64)–(1.69) at a configuration 𝕌 also bounds U_{p,X}(M^·(𝕌)) = U(𝔹_p(X) ∪ {Γ_i^{(n)}}_{i<p}, M^·(𝕌)) and its current (LFR I p.190 l.18–22), through |∂U_{p,X} − 1| and |J_{p,X}| in conditions (i)–(iii). No term takes U_{p,X} as its argument: the terms ℂ_k^{(n)}(X, ·) are analytic on the space at (𝕌, 𝕁) itself ((1.70), p.192), and the decomposition 𝕌 = U′U, the bounds of A′, and LFR I's cube-gauge conditions (1.65), (1.67), and (1.69) are conditions on 𝕌 and its own U, A′, and A (the definition of the space, LFR I p.190, and LFR I (1.69), p.191). So the determining-set configurations need only their plaquette and current bounds, which are those derived here. The representation configuration U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), ·) of (1.61) has mixed data, M^·(U_1) twisted by 𝐇″_k on (Y_1^0)^c∩Y_1, V″, and M^·(U_0^{(AL)}) (LFR II p.373 l.18–22), so it is not of the form U_{p,X}(M^·(one entry)). It is the extended U^0_k, and its conditions at run B's entries are Bałaban's LFR II (1.65) (p.375 l.17–21) in run B, given that run B's arguments lie in Ũ^c_k(Y_4, ·), which is the lift condition (Open Gaps (3)).
        - The line. At each of these entries the moves of *The room at real points.* (U(t) = U′U, the plaquettes, U_{p,X}, and J_{p,X}) keep the line inside the space at α̃′ for |t| ≤ 1/r(Y).
        - U_{k,Λ}, U_1, and U_{1,2} (derived, unreviewed).
          - Where they are read. The second-stage terms read U^0_k only (p.375 l.29–35). There U_1, U_{k,Λ}, and the extended (1.62) and (1.63) enter only inside the extended function U^0_k, built with the localised 𝐇_k(s(Y_4′)) (p.374 l.42–43), whose analyticity on Ũ^c_k(Y_4, ·) with values in Ũ″^c_k(Y_1, ·) is (1.65), at α̃′/2 by *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, and E(X, U^0_k) is analytic on Ũ^c_k(Y_4, ·) (p.375 l.35–37). The first-stage terms have X ⊂ Z^c and read U_{1,2}, U_1, and U_{k,Λ} in turn (p.372 l.39–p.373 l.6). On Z^c, U_{1,2} = U_1 (p.371 l.25–27). A term of the form (1.59) reads the configuration on the cube □₀ = □^{∼5}, and one of the form (1.60) on X ("the analyticity domain is restricted on the cube □₀, or on the domain X", p.372 l.34). So U_2 is not read where □₀ ⊂ Z^c. Where □₀ meets Z, of U_1 and U_2 it reads U_1 only, on its layers m ≥ k₀ + 1 only, and it does not meet the cut ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1} (*The cubes □₀ at Z.* below). Off □₀ and X the brackets of (1.59) and (1.60) read the configuration only through 𝐇″_k, 𝐇_{𝐁₁}, and 𝐇_k, whose analyticity Bałaban states: "quite large, restricted only by the analyticity properties of the function 𝐇″_k" off □₀ and X (p.372 l.34–36), 𝐇_{𝐁₁} "depends analytically on the field U_{k,Λ}" and is very small on the boundary layer (p.374 l.5–7, p.375 l.5–8), and the extended (1.63) with 𝐇_k has "a large domain of analyticity" (p.374 l.40–41); that the brackets read the configuration off □₀ and X only through these functions comes from their form, whose composites read their data on the blocks of □₀. So the conditions needed here are those of U_{k,Λ} and U_1 on □₀ and on X at real points, the line being moved as in *The line.*
          - *The cubes □₀ at Z (derived, not reviewed).* In (1.59) the cube □ ∈ π_n contains the point z of the term, X ⊂ □^{∼2}, and X ⊂ Z^c (p.372 l.1–3), so z ∈ Z^c, and □₀ = □^{∼5}, with ∼ taken in the M-cubes of the scale n (p.372 l.11), lies within 6Ma_n of z in the ℓ∞ distance and within 6dMa_n ≤ 6dMa_k in the ℓ¹ distance, since n ≤ k.
            - N₀ ≥ 2. R_k is the smallest power of L with R_k ≥ (log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5), p.255 l.37–40), so R_k ≥ L for γ small, and the defining equality L^{−N₀+1}MR_{k−N₀+1} = M (LFR I p.179 l.9–10) excludes N₀ = 1. So k₀ + 1 = k − N₀ + 1 ≤ k − 1.
            - The distance. Z is a union of components of the large-field region Λ_k^c (LFR I p.177 l.17–33), and Λ_k ⊂ Ω_k (CRE p.269 l.9). Let y ∈ Ω^c_{k₀+1} ∩ Z. The segment from z ∈ Z^c to y ∈ Z contains a point w of the closure of Λ_k (w = z if z ∈ Λ_k; otherwise z lies in another component of Λ_k^c, and the segment leaves it through the closure of Λ_k, since two components of Λ_k^c with a common boundary point would share a face and be one). So |z − y| ≥ |w − y| ≥ dist(Ω_k, Ω^c_{k₀+1}) ≥ dist(Ω_k, Ω^c_{k−1}) ≥ 8MR_ka_k, by CRE (3.5) as in *The middle layers.* in §9 (dist(Ω_{j+1}, Ω_j^c) ≥ 8MR_{j+1}a_{j+1}). Since 8MR_ka_k > 6dMa_k for R_k ≥ L > d, □₀ does not meet Ω^c_{k₀+1} ∩ Z, and its distance from that set is ≥ 8MR_ka_k − 6dMa_k ≥ 2MR_ka_k.
            - What lies in Ω^c_{k₀+1} ∩ Z. On Z the layer m of 𝐁″_k lies in (Ω″_{m+1})^c, which is Z″_{m+1} ⊂ Ω^c_{m+1} for m + 1 ≥ h + 1 and Ω^c_{m+1} otherwise (LFR I (1.11)–(1.12), p.179). So every layer m ≤ k₀ lies in Ω^c_{k₀+1}, and Z ∩ Ω_{k₀+1} ⊂ Ω″_{k₀+1} lies in the layers m ≥ k₀ + 1. The same holds for the layers Ω_m ∖ Ω_{m+1} of 𝐁_k, which lie in Ω^c_{m+1}. The part of Z where U_{1,2} = U_2 is (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Z ⊂ Z″_{h+1} ⊂ Ω^c_{h+1} ⊂ Ω^c_{k₀+1}, and so are the collar of the join, which lies in the band at the cut (the W-δ note's §12, *The band regularity of U_{1,2}.*, *The strips.*), and the cut itself.
            - So on □₀ ∩ Z the configuration read is U_1 = U_{1,2}, at its layers m ≥ k₀ + 1, where k − m + 1 ≤ N₀ and the factor (k − m + 1)^{1/2} below is paid by (N₀ + 1)^{1/2}. □₀ is inside the minimisation domain of U_1, away from its boundary, and on □₀ the determining set 𝐁₁ is 𝐁″_k (by (2.14) [III], 𝐁₁ = 𝐁″_k off the strip of the join).
          - The data. U_{k,Λ} = (exp iη𝐇_k(−(1/i)log V′_k⌈_Λ)U_k)^{u_{k,Λ}^{−1}} by (1.58). The factor χ(Λ) of LFR I (1.101) is present in LFR II (1.1) (the display of p.369 that begins (1.1) = χ_kχ_{k,Λ}δ_{G₀}(V′_k)χ), and on its support |(1/i)log V′_k| < M_0ε_k on Λ, with M_0 = O(1)B_5M^6 (p.378 l.14–17). U_1 = (exp iη𝐇_{𝐁₁}(−(1/i)log M^·(U_{k,Λ})⌈_{Z″_k})U_{k,Λ})^{u_1^{−1}} by (1.57), and M^·(U_{k,Λ}) satisfies LFR I (1.84) inside Λ (p.371 l.33–35), so the argument of 𝐇_{𝐁₁} is < O(1)B_3B_5M^6ε_k. The datum of 𝐇_k is a scale-k field on Λ. The datum of 𝐇_{𝐁₁} is supported in Z″_k ⊂ Λ, and its component on the layer j of 𝐁₁ is at the scale j, h ≤ j ≤ k − 1 (Γ″_{k−1}, …, Γ″_{h+1} and Γ_h ∩ Z″_{h+1}, LFR I p.180). Every component of either datum is an angle of size at most D_Wε_k, D_W := O(1)B_3B_5M^6.
          - The plaquettes. Gauge transformations leave |W(∂p) − 1| unchanged. For W = exp iη𝐇 W′, as in the first inequality of LFR I (1.46), |W(∂p) − 1| ≤ |W′(∂p) − 1|(1 + ½η|𝐇|(∂p)) + η²|(D^η_{W′}𝐇)(p)| + ½η²(|𝐇|(∂p))². For an angle datum of size δ localised in a domain Y_δ, the VAR function obeys the bound of LFR I (1.45), sup_{B^m(y)}L^mη|𝐇|, sup_{B^m(y)}(L^mη)²|∇_{W′}𝐇| ≤ B_Ve^{−δ_Vd(y, Y_δ)}δ, with the covariant gradient and the scaled distance d in M_1-cubes of the corresponding scales (derived, not reviewed). Bałaban prints (1.45) for 𝐇^{(n)}_{k,Z} only, and states the decay for 𝐇_{𝐁₁} in words below (1.57) ("the function is decaying exponentially off the domain Z_k″", LFR II p.371). Both functions of (1.57) and (1.58) are VAR functions: U_{𝐁₁}(·) and U_k(·) are minimal configurations U(V′V₀) for the determining sets 𝐁₁ and 𝐁_k, so by VAR Proposition 9 (p.309) U(V′V₀)U(V₀)^{−1} is exp iη𝐇(B) in the Landau gauge, B = (1/i) log V′, and the functional derivative obeys VAR (190) (p.308): |δ𝐇_μ(B, x)/δB_ν(y′)| and its gradient are ≤ O(1)[(L^jη)^{−1}, (L^jη)^{−2}](L^{j′}η)^{−d}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8} for x ∈ Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, at every B in the domain (172), which contains tB, 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, for δ small. Then 𝐇(B) = ∫₀¹ ⟨(δ𝐇/δB)(tB), B⟩ dt, the pairing summing over y′ ∈ Y_δ with the weight (L^{j′}η)^d, and e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8} ≤ e^{−δ₀d(y,Y_δ)/16}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/16}. The remaining sum over y′ is bounded by a constant, so the bound holds with δ_V := δ₀/16 and a constant B_V independent of the scales and of the volume. The covariant gradient ∇_{W′}𝐇 and |𝐇| are invariant under the gauge transformations u_1, u_{k,Λ} of (1.57)–(1.58), so the Landau gauge of Proposition 9 gives the bound in the gauge of (1.57)–(1.58). This is the step by which Bałaban obtains (1.45) ("therefore the following estimate holds", LFR I p.186). Since L^kη = 1, the second inequality of (1.46) then gives, at a plaquette p of the layer m, the added term at most 9B_Ve^{−δ_Vd(p, Y_δ)}δ(L^{k−m}η)², and the factor at most 1 + 8B_VL^{−m}δ ≤ 2. Here δ = D_Wε_k ≤ D_W(1 + β₀)(k − m + 1)^{1/2}ε_m by CRE (2.8), ε_n ≤ (1 + β₀)(n − m)^{1/2}ε_m. There is no gain in L^{−(k−m)}: the data are angles at their own layers. The factor (k − m + 1)^{1/2} is paid as follows.
            - On the layers m ≥ k₀ + 1 it is at most (N₀ + 1)^{1/2}, since k₀ = k − N₀ (LFR I p.181 l.40).
            - On the layers m ≤ k₀, the plaquettes read lie on X ⊂ Z^c, and Λ ⊃ Y_δ lies at a distance ≥ RMa_{k₀+1} from ∂Z (*The representation with the gain.* of the W-δ note's §12). In M_1-cubes of the scale m this is d(p, Λ) ≥ (RM/M_1)L^{k₀+1−m} ≥ k₀ + 1 − m. So e^{−δ_Vd(p,Λ)}(k − m + 1)^{1/2} ≤ (N₀ + 1)^{1/2}e^{−δ_V(k₀+1−m)}(1 + (k₀ + 1 − m)^{1/2}) ≤ c_V(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}, c_V := 1 + sup_{t≥0}t^{1/2}e^{−δ_Vt}.
            So each of the two steps (1.58) and (1.57) multiplies the bound by at most 2 and adds at most C_W(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}ε_m(L^{k−m}η)², C_W := 9B_VD_W(1 + β₀)c_V. At a real point U_k satisfies the plaquette conditions at c_Uε_m(L^{k−m}η)² on the layer m, with c_U the constant of the support (§3(d), the item on (1.64)–(1.69)). So U_{k,Λ} satisfies them at (2c_U + C_W(N₀ + 1)^{1/2})ε_m(L^{k−m}η)², and U_1 at (4c_U + 3C_W(N₀ + 1)^{1/2})ε_m(L^{k−m}η)². These are below half the thresholds of (1.66) and of (1.68) for the spaces with n < N; at n = N (j = k), the space of LFR II (1.65), (1.68)–(1.69) are vacuous and the non-frozen thresholds are those of (i). The prefactors of (1.64)–(1.68) exceed 1 − 4β, since the sum over i is < 3 and the sum over q is < 1 (LFR I p.190), so they are ≥ ½ under the choice β ≤ 1/8, within Bałaban's β ≤ 1/4 (p.191 l.36–37), and "below half the thresholds" holds when the bounds are below α_{0,m}/4 times the scale factors. α_{0,m}/ε_m = (C₀/A₀)(log g_m^{−2})^{q₀−p₀} (CRE (2.4) and (2.28), pp.255 and 259), while N₀ = O(log R_k/log L) with R_k ≤ L(log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5), p.255), and g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k for m ≤ k (CRE (2.6), second line), so log g_m^{−2} ≥ log g_k^{−2} − 2 log(1 + β₀) and (N₀ + 1)^{1/2}ε_m/α_{0,m} → 0 as γ → 0, since q₀ > p₀ (the choice of *The room at real points.*), on the layers whose thresholds are taken at their own index. On the frozen layers k₀ + 1 ≤ m < j of (1.66) the threshold is the frozen one, and against it the increment loses (L/L₀)^{2(j−m)} ≤ L^{2(N₀−1)} ≤ (L + 1)²(N₀ + 1)^{2β₀}R_k² (LFR I p.192 l.18–19, L^{N₀−1} = R_{k−N₀+1} ≤ (L + 1)(N₀ + 1)^{β₀}R_k), which q₀ > p₀ alone does not pay. With R_k = O((log g_k^{−2})^r) and N₀ = O(log R_k/log L), the loss times (N₀ + 1)^{1/2} is at most a constant times (log g_k^{−2})^{2r} times a power of log log g_k^{−2}. The frozen threshold carries α_{0,j}, so the ratio is ε_m/α_{0,j}, and ε_m/α_{0,j} ≤ (1 + β₀)²N₀^{β₀}ε_j/α_{0,j} by CRE (2.7)–(2.8), with α_{0,j}/ε_j ≥ (C₀/A₀)(log g_k^{−2} − 2 log(1 + β₀))^{q₀−p₀}; the factor N₀^{β₀} is a power of log log g_k^{−2}. So the frozen conditions hold at U_{k,Λ} and U_1, below half the frozen thresholds for γ small, under the choice q₀ > p₀ + 3r (derived, not reviewed); the 3r rather than 2r pays the current entries of (1.66), at U_{k,Λ}, U_1, and the U_{p,X}, which lose L^{3(j−m)}L₀^{−2(j−m)}, at most a constant times R_k³ times powers of N₀. CRE (2.28) bounds q₀ below only ("integers greater than 1", p.259 l.50), as for the choice q₀ > p₀ + r of the Cauchy note's §5; no upper bound on q₀ or q₁ is printed in CRE, LFR I–II, RG I–II, PBG, or VAR (a search of the transcriptions), and q₀ enters LFR II only through bounds such as p.379 l.31, which carry g_k and stay small.
          - The cube-gauge conditions. (1.65), (1.67), and (1.69) [IV] bound the field A of U^u = exp iηA in a gauge u on each cube (LFR I p.190 l.16–17). At a real point U_k satisfies them with c_Uε_m in place of BCMα_{0,m}, c_U being also the constant of the support for these conditions. For W = (exp iη𝐇 W′)^{u_W^{−1}} the gauge u_W composed with W′'s gauge u gives W^{u_Wu} = exp(iηAd_u𝐇)exp iηA′′, A′′ the field of W′, so the field of W differs from A′′ by Ad_u𝐇 plus terms of order η|𝐇||A′′|, and its gradient by the corresponding terms with the covariant gradient ∇_{W′}𝐇 that (1.45) bounds. With these bounds of 𝐇 and ∇_{W′}𝐇, as in the plaquette bullet, U_{k,Λ} and U_1 satisfy (1.65) and (1.69) with (2c_U + C_W(N₀ + 1)^{1/2})ε_m and (4c_U + 3C_W(N₀ + 1)^{1/2})ε_m in place of BCMα_{0,m}, below half of it for γ small. On the frozen layers (1.67) bounds L^jη|A| on the CML^jη-cubes by L₀^{2(j−m)}BCMα_{0,j} (LFR I p.190 l.49–53). There U_k satisfies it at the frozen index j, by the regularity of the support at that index (LFR I p.182), with c_Uε_j in place of BCMα_{0,j}, For U_{k,Λ} and U_1 the increments of 𝐇 are bounded at the layer m, not at the frozen index, and the conversion to the frozen threshold costs (L/L₀)^{2(j−m)}, as for (1.66) above; so (1.67) at U_{k,Λ} and U_1 on the frozen layers holds under the same choice q₀ > p₀ + 3r, by the same count (derived, not reviewed).
          - The other conditions. U_{k,Λ} and U_1 are the configurations of the determining sets 𝐁_k and 𝐁₁ at data that equal those of U_k off Λ and differ from 1 by at most D_Wε_k on Λ (LFR I (1.83)–(1.84), p.371 l.33–35). Their currents, the configurations U_{p,X}(M^·(W)), and these configurations' currents therefore satisfy the remaining entries of (1.66) and of (1.68) (for n < N; of (1.66) and (i) at n = N) by VAR (9)–(10), as for U″_k above, with the data contributions bounded as in the plaquette bullet, through (1.45) with the factor (N₀ + 1)^{1/2}, below half the thresholds for γ small. At real points A′ = 0.
          - The representation configurations. At a real point the extended (1.62) and (1.63) equal U_1 and U_{k,Λ} (p.373 l.39, p.374 l.17), so they have the same conditions. The representation configuration U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), D(s)) of (1.61) at complex s is the extended U^0_k itself: (1.61) is a representation of U^0_k ("U_k^0 = U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), M^·(U_k^0)) = …", p.373 l.18–22), into which the extended (1.62) and (1.63) are substituted, with the variables (𝐔, 𝐉) introduced into the localised functions 𝐇_k(s(Y_4′)), 𝐇_{𝐁₁}, and 𝐇″_k (p.374 l.37–43, p.375 l.5–13). Bałaban's (1.65) states that this extended function maps Ũ^c_k(Y_4, α̃₀, α̃₁) into Ũ″^c_k(Y_1, α̃₀, α̃₁), "the space defined by (1.64)–(1.69) [IV] for n = N" (p.375 l.14–25). So the conditions of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) at (1.61)'s configuration at D(s) are (1.65), used as he states it, and at the halved thresholds by *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, as for U^0_k above.

The cubes and the localisation domains, and so these factors, are the same in both runs, since they are scale-k objects (RG II p.3 l.14–17 and l.27–33). So at complex s the two runs' transferred propagators differ by the sum over the walks of these factors times the run differences of the transferred walk terms. The difference is defined only after the transfer, and it is bounded per walk term, not per factor: (1.7) bounds whole walk terms, and PBG gives no bound per factor (`WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md` §3(a)). If each intertwining defect of a walk term's constituents is at most δ_c times the bound of its constituent, the walk term's difference, telescoped over its at most N_c(|ω| + 1) terms, is at most N_c(|ω| + 1)max_cδ_c(1 + max_cδ_c)^{N_c(|ω|+1)−1} times its bound. Its status is that of the Cauchy note's §5, where (5.1v) is derived and unreviewed: derived given its inputs at the composites, the tail of the localised lift, the lift condition, the current along the pair line, (R-Δ^c-T), and, where □₀ meets more than one layer, the fine side. At real data these inputs are derived as at the members of 𝒞 up to two strips of the local determining sets at ∂□₀, run B's finest strip and the strip where the two runs' layers differ, whose contributions at the points read are at most constant multiples of d̄, in the current along the pair line through the term (IV) (*The inputs at the composites.* below, *The strips at ∂□₀.*, *The current at the strips.*, unreviewed), with the composites' layers at PBG's block size M′ (*The block size M′.*, *R₁ = 1 (a renaming of the product).*, derived, not reviewed) and the composites' run difference taken at the scale of their generation (*The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer*, *The configuration part.*). (R-Δ^c-T) is derived, not reviewed, in the Cauchy note's §5. At the complex arguments of RG I's Lemma 4 and of the decoupled data D(s), the fine side and the move of the lift condition are derived through VAR's Proposition 9 (*The Fine Side at Complex Points.*, *The composites and the representation configurations*, not reviewed), and the own quantities per layer through run B's own composite and the Lemma (Two Constants) (*The inputs at the composites.*) and through (1.65) at the representation configurations (*The Room.*), and the current along the pair line through the Lemma (Two Constants) from its real bound (*The current along the pair line at complex data.* below, not reviewed). The piece (α) of (ii) is the same group at the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63), with the same status away from the near domains that meet ∂Y_i, through the response to run B's finest strip at ∂Y_i (*The representation configurations.* below, not reviewed), the conditions of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) there given the lift condition (derived, not reviewed), and at those near domains with the age factor and the run difference of *The near domains at ∂Y_i.* below (derived, not reviewed).
    - *The inputs at the composites (derived, unreviewed).* The composites U^A_j(□₀, D) and U^B_{j+1}(□₀, D) minimise the action under the multiscale constraints of the determining set of U_j(□₀), "(⋃_{n=0}^{j−1}Λ_n) ∪ □_j^{(j)}" (RG I p.276), at the data D, each run's averages of the same entry at its own blocks, which agree at the blocks of the shared scales. The layers are those of CRE's 𝐁_j(□₀) (p.256 l.57–62), all within the distance 2M₁ of ∂□₀ in the units of the scale j (□₀^{∼−2} ⊂ Ω_j, l.62), and they satisfy PRT2 (2.1)–(2.2) with RM := R′M′, R′ := LK − 1 as in *The separation.*, so that (J-W) at the width R′M′ gives the conditions of the profile, (2.59) at α = 1/128 and ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1 (*The kernel form of VAR Proposition 4.*), while the counts at α = ¼ below are taken at the smaller common width M′, as the count at a smaller width of (b) allows; M′ is PBG's block size of *The block size M′.* at R₁ = 1 (*R₁ = 1 (a renaming of the product).*; the W-δ note's l.930, which records that this is not Bałaban's literal usage; derived, not reviewed), since CRE's layer widths LM₁ = LKM′ in their own units (p.256 l.57–62) exceed R′M′ (*The composites over 𝒮_z.* below), and D lies in the data class of RG I (3.30)–(3.31), so VAR Theorem 1 gives (9)–(10) on the cubes of its class in each layer and the class (8), 𝔘_k({Ω_j}, B₃ε₁) (VAR p.279 l.12, by the conditions (2) of p.278 l.5–6), on all of □₀, up to ∂□₀. At real data D = M^·(c^A) is the data of a real member, regular at the scale O(B₃)ε₁ by VAR (9)–(10) on its layers (*The representation.* in (d)), so (9)–(10) hold at that scale, which the strip constants below use through B₃ and C_F; at complex data the scale is ε₁′, relative to d̄′.
      - The strips at ∂□₀. Run B's local determining set is CRE's 𝐁_{j+1}(□₀) of p.256 l.57–62, the U_j(𝐁_j(□₀), ·) of (1.59) (LFR II p.372 l.7), built from Ω_0 = □₀, so its finest layer is not empty: it is the strip S_□ := □₀ ∖ Ω^B_1 at ∂□₀, of the ℓ∞ width at least M₁ε and at most 2M₁ε, the domains of p.256 l.59–61 being maximal (only the global sequences of the members have Ω_1 = Λ_1 = T, *The fluctuation fields A*). The count of the W-δ note's §12, *The collar's layers.*, run with Ω = □₀, shows that the two runs' layers agree at the shared scales p ≥ 2 of run A and differ only on the strip T_□ of the points of □₀ within the ℓ∞ distance 2M₁a_2 of ∂□₀, which contains S_□ and Ω_0 ∖ Ω^A_2 (and, for j ≤ 1, the whole part of □₀ where the runs' layers 0, 1 and 0, 1, 2 differ, within 2c_1 of ∂□₀ by the same count, c_p := L^pεM₁ the cube sides of that count, the W-δ note's l.922; □₀ and its nested domains are boxes, so CRE's lattice ℓ¹ distance to their complements is the distance to the nearest face, and the ℓ∞ widths here are those distances). Off T_□ run B's constraints are run A's composed with M, and both runs' data are averages of the same entry at the same blocks. On T_□ ∖ S_□ run B's constraints are at scales ≥ 1 and still factor through M. So c^B := U^B_{j+1}(□₀, D) is critical for every variation that preserves its ε-block averages except at the bonds of S_□, and ι_Y(Mc^B) = c^B up to gauge up to the response of the one-step problem of ι_Y to the current J_S of c^B on S_□, as at the representation configurations (*The representation configurations.* below). In the coarse comparison, VAR (112) about Mc^B, two sources at T_□ are added to those of the members. The first is the data ratio B on the constraints of run A in T_□ that Mc^B need not satisfy, those at which run B fixes only a coarser average. Where run B fixes the finer average, Mc^B satisfies run A's constraint, since M^{p+1} = M∘M^p. At such a constraint the datum of run A and the average of Mc^B are averages of the entry ι_Yc^A and of c^B with equal averages at the next coarser block of run B, of the side a_{j′+1} ≤ L³ε. VAR's cubes of (9)–(10) do not reach ∂□₀, so the bounds there are those of the class (8) (l.662): its plaquette bound at run B's finest spacing ε/L gives |F(c^B)| ≤ L²B₃ε₁ε^{−2}, and |F(ι_Yc^A)| ≤ C_Fε₁ε^{−2} (*The tail at the chain.*, VAR Theorem 1 at one step). In the axial gauge of that block both fields are at most dL³(C_F + L²B₃)ε₁/ε, so a_{j′}|B| ≤ O(1)dL⁵(C_F + L²B₃)ε₁ at the layers j′ ≤ 2. The second is the current of Mc^B at those layers. By the chain rule of the Cauchy note's §10.3 before (10.3a), dA_ε(Mc^B)[X] = dA_{ε/L}(c^B)[ℒX] + db(c^B)[ℒX], it is ℒ* of the current of c^B plus the blocking artifact ℒ*(D*r₁ + r₀), which is among the members' sources. Half of the rate pays e^{−¼δ₀RM(j−3)^+} ≤ L^{−8(j−3)^+}, and the other half the count (2.58) of (b) over the blocks of T_□, at α = ¼, whose condition (2.59), ¼αδ₀RM > 2d log c₀(½α) + 1, reads (1/16)δ₀M′ > 2d log c₀(⅛) + 1 at RM = M′, a lower bound on M′ imposed in *The block size M′.*; at the joins RM is LM₁ under (J-W) of (b), *The joins.* So a source at the layers j′ ≤ 2 of the size s, in a_{j′}³|·| for a current and in a_{j′}|·| for a datum, contributes at a point of the layer j at most O(1)B₀c₀(⅛)^dL^{3j}L^{−8(j−3)^+}s ≤ O(1)B₀c₀(⅛)^dL^{24}(ε/a_j)s in a_j|·| + a_j²|∇·|, by the bound of *The propagator.* of (b), a_j|𝔊f(x)| + a_j²|∇𝔊f(x)| ≤ 2B₀Σ_{y′}(a_j/a_{j′})³e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}sup_{Δ(y′)}a_{j′}³|f|, the factor L^{3j} bounding (a_j/a_{j′})³ and 6c₀(⅛)^d being the sum (2.58) at α = ¼, c₀(α) := Σ_{z∈ℤ}e^{−αδ₀|z|}. With s ≤ C_sε₁ the two strip sources contribute at most C_T(ε/a_{j(x)})ε₁ ≤ C_T(ε/a_{min(j(x),n)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, C_T := O(1)B₀c₀(⅛)^dL^{24}C_s, which C_U(M) contains (*The alignment.* of (d)), and the composition of the strip, the data-difference, and the equal-data displacements is that of *The alignment.* applied twice, under C_U(M)ε₁ ≤ 1/6 with C_T in C_U(M). The run-B identity of *The three parts.* holds for y off T_□ up to the term (IV) := a_{j′}^{−d}[Σ_{r∈ℛ_T}λ^B_{c,r}(𝐐(c^B)ℒR₀e_y)_r + ⟨J_S, ℒR₀e_y⟩], ℛ_T the rows of 𝐐′ on T_□ that are not run A's rows composed with M (the fine-bond rows of S_□ enter only through ⟨J_S, ℒR₀e_y⟩, so no row is counted twice), since every other row pairs with ℒR₀e_y as at the members. In these units the move at a point x′ of □₀ is L^{j−j(x′)}σ_c(x′) plus the tail of ι_Y, j(x′) the layer of x′ in the member's sequence and σ_c(x′) ≤ C(ε₀)d̄(x′) the bound of *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.* in the units of that layer, uniform in k, d̄(x′) = C_U(M)(ε/a_{min(j(x′),n_b)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ with n_b the history's finest scale of d̄, n ≥ n_b − 1 (*The alignment.*; the n of *The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer*, where the layer of z is called m). □₀ meets only the member's layers ≥ n − 1, since the distance from Ω_n to Ω_{n−1}^c is at least 8R_n M-cubes of the scale n and □₀ spans 11 of them, with 8R_n > 11d^{1/2} for g small (CRE (3.5), (3.20); the Cauchy note's §5, *The near domains at a finer layer.*), and j ≤ n, so j(x′) ≥ j − 1 and min(j(x′), n_b) ≥ min(j, n_b) − 1. Hence the move in the units a_j is at most L^{4/3}C(ε₀)d̄_j + 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} at every point of □₀, d̄_j := C_U(M)(ε/a_{min(j,n_b)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, the tail being of that size in the units of every layer (*With the lift ι_Y.*). The composite's own plaquettes, A′, cube-gauge fields, and current are bounded per layer, not through the class (8), whose B₃ε₁ is not uniform in K at the layer j, and not through RG I (3.27) (p.275 l.30–37), whose B₃O(1)Mα₀ on □̃⁴ is above the threshold. Run B's own composite U^B_{j+1}(□₀, M^·(c^B_Y)), at the data of the member c^B_Y with ι_Yc^A_Y = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B_Y (*With the lift ι_Y.*, where the members are called c^A and c^B; the subscript Y separates them from the composites c^A and c^B of this bullet and of *The strips at ∂□₀.*), is the configuration at which Bałaban evaluates run B's bracket of (1.59), U_j(𝐁_j(□₀), exp iQ(η𝐇″_k)M^·(U^0_k)) and U_j(𝐁_j(□₀), M^·(U^0_k)) (LFR II p.372 l.7–8; at the other members in CRE's reading at the layer, as for (iv)). So with its current it lies in the spaces of the terms of the generation j at α̃_{·,j}/μ, μ = 32B₃²L³ and 8B₃C_{Q*} (*Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*). At U^0_k this is his usage at the composites of (1.59), accepted as at the members (l.536), his construction not involving C₀ and C₁.
