So the composite moves by at most B₀c_tot(¼)(L^{4/3}C(ε₀)d̄_j + 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k}), and the plaquettes and cube-gauge fields of c^B = U^B_{j+1}(□₀, M^·(ι_Yc^A_Y)) exceed those of run B's own composite by less than an eighth of the radius for j > n/2, n ≥ n₀″, and γ small, n₀″ the threshold of the count of *At real points the ratio is small.* redone at the index j (the power of L^{−min(j,n_b)} being (1 − ε₀)/3 − 16ε₀ = (1 − 49ε₀)/3, with min(j, n_b) ≥ (n_b − 1)/2 and k < 16min(j, n_b) + j(C) + 8, below), by that count for the first part and as for the tail of ι_Y in *The lift condition of (i).* for the second. For n < n₀″ no lift is formed: the two runs' renormalised terms of every generation are bounded separately, each at its own evaluation points by Bałaban's per-run bound (accepted), at most E₁L^{−(1−β_E)(m−j)} per point of the layer m, so the pair's difference is at most 2E₁(1 − L^{−(1−β_E)})^{−1} per point; n₀′ of *Small n.* is taken ≥ max(n₀″, n₀‴) + 1, so such a history has n_b ≤ n + 1 < n₀′, its layers being ≥ n_b − 1 (*The result.*), and its whole run difference, (ii) with its endpoint V^B(Y)[ι_Yc^A] included, is bounded by *Small n.*, with no lift formed, the factor θ̃^{−n₀′} being absorbed in C_h as θ̃^{−n₀} is in *At real points the ratio is small.* At complex data the difference of the two composites is analytic on the disc, bounded at its real points by this bound and on the disc by Proposition 9's C_Hε₁′ ≤ 1 (*The composites and the representation configurations*). The Lemma (Two Constants) then bounds it by the 15/16 power of the real bound, which the count of *The Ratio.* makes small at the index j in place of m: with k < 16min(j, n_b) + j(C) + 8 (below) and K − k < 8n_b + j(C), the power of L^{−min(j,n_b)} is 5(1 − ε₀)/16 − 15ε₀ = 5(1 − 49ε₀)/16 > 0 under ε₀ < 1/125, which absorbs ε₁′^{−1/16} ≤ (ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C)^{1/32} and the factors polynomial in k, the tail part being small as at real data; so for n ≥ n₀′. Among the own quantities, Proposition 9 enters only through this difference. The current that the pair lift carries is that of run A's composite, at M^·(c^A_Y), which is c^A_Y itself (l.427), or, for the first composite of (1.59), at exp iQ(η𝐇″_k)M^·(c^A_Y); both are configurations at which Bałaban evaluates run A's bracket of (1.59), so |J| < r_{0,j}/C_{Q*} by the same membership in run A, with no data move. So only the move needs the bound of *At real points the ratio is small.* at the index j in place of m, and its power of L^{−min(j,n_b)} absorbs its factors polynomial in k for the generations j > n/2, the only ones at which (i) and (iv) are formed, since there min(j, n_b) ≥ (n_b − 1)/2 and k < 8n_b + j(C) ≤ 16min(j, n_b) + j(C) + 8; the generations j ≤ n/2 are compared directly through Bałaban's per-run bound, without the composites' lift condition (*The old generations.* in *The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer*), and the deep terms are bounded by the triangle inequality, as in *The lift condition of (i).*. ι_Y acts on the composite as the localised lift of *The Lift ι_Y.* with □₀ in place of Y^∼; at the points read, at the distance ≥ 3Ma_n ≥ M₁a_n from ∂□₀ (M ≥ 2dM₁), its tail is that of *The tail at the chain.* with that distance in place of M₁a_k, with C_F, and so C_t, carrying the composites' factor O(B₃) of *The strips at ∂□₀.*, ≤ 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^n} ≤ 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^j} in the units a_j of the generation, as in *The lift condition of (i).*, the form the tail part of the rate uses with the factor L^{m−j} in the units a_m.
      - *The composites over 𝒮_z (derived, not reviewed).* The near domain of z is □₀ := □̃⁵ (the Cauchy note's §5), the cube of (1.59) at the same z. The composite of (1.59) is RG I's U_j(□₀) at the term's generation j ≤ n, with the data of the scale j on □_j ⊃ □̃⁴ (RG I p.273 l.39–42), while 𝒮_z asks for 𝐔 = U_n(□₀, M^·(𝐔)) with the data of the scale n there (the Cauchy note's §5, *The regular subclass.*). A minimiser under the finer level-j constraints need not minimise under the coarser level-n ones, so the composites are not placed in 𝒮_z itself. Each is a composite over 𝒮_z, c(W, 𝐀) = U_j(□₀, exp iQ(η𝐀)M^·(W)) with W ∈ 𝒮_z, RG I's configuration of (3.28) with 𝐀 in place of the fluctuation part (p.276 l.26–36), and the Cauchy note's (5.1c𝐀) (§5, *The composites over 𝒮_z.*) gives its age factor where 𝐀 satisfies (I.3.31); where □₀ meets the layer n − 1, with (R-Δ^c-T) (the Cauchy note's §5, *The near domains at a finer layer.*), whose membership at the radii of the layer n carries over to the composites, since Lemma 4 reads 𝐀 only through (I.3.48)–(I.3.49). At the generation j = m of the layer m of z, this is the case n = j of (5.1c𝐀) (its n the layer), which holds wherever the composite lies in the norm class 𝒫_j(X; r) of each near domain X (the Cauchy note's §5, *Complex domain (R-Δ^c).*: run A's restriction in U^c_j(X, r_{0,j}, r_{1,j}) with its current at most r_{0,j}/C_{Q*}, the lift in run B's configuration conditions). That membership is the lift condition of *The inputs at the composites.* at the index j: run A's composite is its own evaluation point, with |J| < r_{0,j}/C_{Q*}, and the lift of run A's composite lies within the fine-side move σ_c of c^B, which lies within an eighth of the radius of run B's own composite, the two together inside the radius by the count of l.664 at the index j (derived, not reviewed). At 𝐀 = 0 the composite is W on □₀, since the level-j constraints imply the level-n ones (M^n = M^{n−j}∘M^j). The data are of this form in each case. *Run A's composites.* W is run A's member c^A_Y, or U^0_k for the composites of (1.59), a minimiser at real or complex data, which lies in 𝒮_z (the Cauchy note's §5). 𝐀 is the fluctuation part (tζ̃_□ + t_□ζ_□)𝐇″_k at the interpolated data of RG I (3.4) and (3.25) (p.270 l.34–42, p.275 l.15–17), decoupled at the data D(s), with exp iQ(η𝐀)M^·(W) = M^·(exp iη𝐀W) by RG I (3.3) (p.270 l.27–28), and 𝐀 = 0 at the data M^·(W). Bałaban places it in (I.3.31): the decoupled 𝐇_k(s(Y₀), B′) has its bound (1.21) "for all admissible norms", and "this function multiplied by tζ̃_□+t_□ζ_□ has to satisfy the bounds (I.3.31)", which he meets with ½α₂ for the tζ̃_□ part and on his t_□ circle (RG II p.7 l.4–11); in LFR II with B_T and e^{2·6^dκ₁} (*On Bałaban's t_□ circle*, l.653). In CRE's reading at the layer the radius is the layer's α_{2,n}, as in the Cauchy note's (5.1c𝐀). *Run B's composites at the lifted entries.* Their data M^·(ι_Yc^A_Y) are those of e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B_Y (*With the lift ι_Y.*), so W = c^B_Y, run B's member, and exp iη𝐀 = exp iη𝐀_fl exp i(ε/L)𝐒_c, 𝐀_fl run B's fluctuation part as in the first case. The move has the sup and gradient bounds of *The inputs at the composites.* but no Hölder bound within the radius ((F-H)). Lemma 4 needs only |𝐀| < α_{2,n} (the Cauchy note's §5, *The composites over 𝒮_z.*, the last sentence), which holds since |𝐀_fl| ≤ ¾α₂ on the circle (l.653) and the move, in the units of the layer at most L²σ_c(x′) ≤ L²C(ε₀)d̄(x′) at the points x′ of □₀ (*The inputs at the composites.*; L² converts the points of the layer n − 1; the ι_Y tail 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} is inside σ_c through the 2C′ of C(ε₀), l.533, as for the members), is below ¼α_{2,n} = 2B₃c₂r_{0,n} for n ≥ n₀‴, the index from which the count of *At real points the ratio is small.* gives L²σ_c ≤ B₃c₂r_{0,n} (at complex data relative to d̄′); for n < n₀‴ no lift is formed and the history falls under *Small n.*, as for n < n₀″ (*The inputs at the composites.*, n₀′ ≥ max(n₀″, n₀‴) + 1). The second differences of Q′ give the excess of (F-H) (*The excess.*), counted in *The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer*, *The excess part.* CRE's local set 𝐁_j(□₀) of the cube □₀ (p.256 l.57–62) has nested cubes as its domains, separated as in *The separation.* of (b), so it is a sequence of the kind of REG (1.3)–(1.6) that RG I's U_j(□₀) uses (RG I p.273 l.39–42), and the composite of (1.59) is RG I's U_j(□₀) under the re-basing of REG's sizes to the block size M′ (*R₁ = 1 (a renaming of the product).*, derived, not reviewed): RG I asks for R = R₁ at REG's M₁, that is widths > R₁M₁ (REG (1.4)), which CRE's widths LM₁ meet literally only if L > R₁. The profile near ∂□₀ (derived, unreviewed). RG I constructs U_j(□₀) for "a sequence of cubes {□_n}, n = 0, 1, …, k + 1, satisfying the conditions (1.3)–(1.6) [14] (with k + 1, L^{−1}η instead of k, η, R = R₁)" (RG I p.273 l.40–44), [14] being REG. REG's sequences admit Ω_0 ≠ T_η (REG (1.3)–(1.6), p.077), here Ω_0 = □_0, while PRT2's (2.1)–(2.2) live on the torus. PRT2 covers that case: the variational problem with Dirichlet operators outside a neighbourhood Ω of Ω₁ has the same solution (PRT2 p.228 l.18–33), and its Theorem "holds for the operators G(Ω) with Dirichlet boundary conditions on Ω^c, Ω ⊃ Ω₁" (p.248 l.4), with the data cubes adjacent to the Dirichlet boundary treated in `WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md` §11. The profile of (b) is built from these propagator bounds through *The kernel form of VAR Proposition 4.*, so it holds for the local sequence up to ∂□₀, with M′ for RM (*The inputs at the composites.*). The nonlinear problem on □₀ needs no transfer from PRT2: VAR poses its variational problem on a domain Ω₀ ⊂ T_η, "a sequence of domains Ω_j, j = 0, 1, …, k, … Ω_j ⊂ T_η, Ω₀ ⊃ Ω₁ ⊃ ⋯ ⊃ Ω_k" (VAR p.277 l.11–12), on configurations on Ω₀ with the action (5) summed over the plaquettes p ⊂ Ω₀ and data on every block of Ω₀ (p.278 l.2–30), so Ω₀ = □₀ is VAR's own setting, and its results, Proposition 4 included, hold there as VAR states them, under its condition (1) with R ≥ R₁, met through *R₁ = 1 (a renaming of the product).* (R-Δ^c-T) is derived, not reviewed, in the Cauchy note's §5, *The near domains at a finer layer.* The complex data D(s) at complex decoupling parameters are covered by *The Fine Side at Complex Points* (*The composites and the representation configurations*).
      - The current along the pair line at complex data (derived, not reviewed). It is bounded as at the members (*The Current at the Multiscale Chain.*, *At complex points.*). For a point x and a complex-linear functional ℓ of norm 1, 𝔣_J := ℓ(a_{j(x)}³ΔJ(x)) is analytic on the disc, J(W) being a polynomial in the bond variables of W and their inverses and Q₁^{pl}(W)* one in the transports of W. At the real points of the disc the composites are the real minimisers at real data, regular with 2ε₁′, and the current input above gives |𝔣_J| ≤ 2Ĉ_J′d̄′(x), Ĉ_J′ the members' Ĉ_J (*The Current at the Multiscale Chain.*, ΔJ_c) with x̂_ν + x₄ in place of x̂_ν (*The current at the strips.*). On the disc each composite is e^{iη𝐇(z)}c_0 with VAR (9)–(10) at c_0 and (19)–(21) for 𝐇 (*The composites and the representation configurations* in *The Fine Side at Complex Points*), so its current is ≤ C^c_Jε₁′a_m^{−3} on the layer m, as at the members, the pair-lift current is ≤ 2C_{Q*} times this, and |𝔣_J| ≤ 𝔐_J ≤ 1 for γ small. The Lemma (Two Constants) gives a_{j(x)}³|ΔJ(x)| ≤ 2Ĉ_J′^{15/16}d̄′(x)^{15/16}, the input of (5.1v) at the complex data, in the form that *The current part at complex points.* uses at the members.
      - The representation configurations (derived, not reviewed, away from the near domains that meet ∂Y_i). U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), ·), U(𝐁_1(Y_2), ·), and U(𝐁_k(Y_3), ·) minimise the action under determining sets whose boundary layers are CRE's 𝐁 ∪ 𝐁(Y_i) (p.257 l.22–26). The two runs' layers agree at the shared scales n ≥ 2 of run A and differ only on a strip at ∂Y_i (the W-δ note's §12, *The collar's layers.*), where run B's layers 1 and 2 are still at scales ≥ 1 and its layer 0, the strip S of the ℓ∞ width at least c_0 = M₁ε and at most 2c_0 (as S_□ in *The strips at ∂□₀.*), fixes the fine bonds. So run B's constraints factor through M except on S, and, as for U^B_1 in *The joint line.*, c^B is critical for every variation that preserves its ε-block averages except at the bonds of S, and ι(Mc^B) = c^B up to gauge up to the response of ι's one-step problem to the current J_S of c^B on S. The class (8) of VAR Theorem 1, which holds up to ∂Y_i (p.278 l.5–6, p.279 l.12), gives |J_S| ≤ B₃ε₁(L/ε)³ on run B's finest layer. Let x lie in a domain read at z ∈ Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1} whose near domain □₀ does not meet ∂Y_i. Then x ∈ □̃² is at the distance ≥ 3Ma_n from ∂Y_i, since □₀ = □̃⁵ adds three layers of M-cubes of the side Ma_n to □̃². S lies within the ℓ∞ distance 2c_0 of ∂Y_i, so within the ℓ¹ distance 2dM₁ε, and d(x, S) ≥ 2Ma_n under M ≥ 2dM₁, the lower bound of the W-δ note's §8, stage 3 (its l.185). The computation for U^B_1, with 2Ma_n in place of 2MR_ka_k and the crude |J_S|, gives the response ≤ O(1)ε₁P(L^{n+1})e^{−2δ₀ML^n} in the units of the layer n + 1, P a polynomial. □̃² does not reach the layer n + 2, since Ω_{n+2} lies at least 8R_{n+2} M-cubes of the scale n + 2 inside Ω_{n+1} ∌ z (CRE (3.5), (3.20)), so j(x) ≤ n + 1, and in the units of the layer j(x) the response is ≤ C_r′ε₁e^{−δ₀ML^n}, C_r′ := O(B₃)sup_{x≥1}P(Lx)e^{−δ₀Mx}, the factor O(B₃) being that of the members' data in VAR (7) (VAR p.278; *The strips at ∂□₀.*), which |J_S| carries as at the composites. Since d̄(x) ≥ C_U(M)L^{−(n+1)/3}ε₁ (min(j(x), n_b) ≤ n + 1, n_b the history's finest scale of d̄), it is ≤ C″d̄(x) with C″ := C_r′sup_{n≥0}L^{(n+1)/3}e^{−δ₀ML^n}/C_U(M), a constant of d, L, M, δ₀, and the constants of C_U(M), and it enters σ_c as the tail of ι_Y does. The coarse side holds by the profile of (b), which covers each run's collars of the joins ((b), *The joins.*), with the cross-run sources of the strip of the width 2M₁a_2 at ∂Y_i where the two runs' layers differ (the W-δ note's §12, *The collar's layers.*) added and bounded as at the composites (*The strips at ∂□₀.*), at most C_T(ε/a_{j(x)})ε₁, which C_U(M) covers in d̄(x). At complex data J_S and those sources are bounded through the representation e^{iη𝐇(z)}c_0 as at the composites. The current along the pair line carries the term (IV) of the strip at ∂Y_i as at the composites (*The current at the strips.*), the points x read lying at the distance ≥ 2Ma_n from that strip under M ≥ 2(d + 1)L²M₁. So the inputs of (5.1v) follow as at the composites, at real and at complex data. The near domains that meet ∂Y_i are the next bullet.
      - *The near domains at ∂Y_i (derived, not reviewed).* Let □₀ meet ∂Y_i, and let h be the layer of 𝐁″_k that contains the piece of ∂Y_i met (CRE's assumption ∂Ω ⊂ Ω_j ∖ Ω_{j+1} of (2.14), with a piece at each layer, p.257 l.7–9 and l.22–26). At the data of a member c^A is that member, since LFR II prints (1.61) as an identity, U_k^0 = U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), M^·(U_k^0)) (p.373 l.20), and at the complex data of the members' continuation U_k(𝐕) it is the member at 𝐕, both sides being analytic in 𝐕; there c^A lies in 𝒮_z (the Cauchy note's §5, *The regular subclass.*, □₀ meeting only the member's layers ≥ m − 1 by *The near domains at a finer layer.* there). At the decoupled data D(s), where the localisation of 𝐇″_k replaces the data of the boundary layer Y_1 ∖ Y_1^0 in (1.61) (p.373 l.31–34), c^A is not shown to be the local minimiser of its own data on □₀, since it is critical only for the variations that preserve the fine data of the collar (at s = 1 it is the member), so it is not shown to be in 𝒮_z, and (5.1c) is replaced by the direct comparison of *The age factor.* below. The bound has four parts.
        - The collar regularity. Y_i ∖ Y_i^0 carries CRE's 𝐁_h(Y_i), whose layers run from the scale 0 at ∂Y_i to h, all within 2M₁a_h of ∂Y_i (CRE p.256 l.57–62; LFR II p.373 l.26–27). Its data are averages of configurations regular at the index h there. At the real data of a member they are that member's own averages (LFR II (1.61)), and the member satisfies VAR (2) at its layer h by VAR Theorem 1. At the decoupled data D(s) they are the averages of the exponentials of the averaged η𝐇(s) of (1.61)–(1.63) applied to such a member, whose plaquettes at the index h are bounded through the bounds of 𝐇(s) at real s (RG II's norms of Theorems 3.1–3.10, PBG (3.43), as in (δ), *Real points.*). So the step of VAR's Sect. F in *The band regularity of U_{1,2}* (the W-δ note's §12) applies on the collar, with p^{loc} the data plaquette bound at the index h in place of 0: one step gives (2) on Δ₀ with max{B₃p^{loc}, θe^{old}}, and the contraction θ ≤ L^{−3} gives VAR (2) at the index h on the collar, at B₃ times the larger of p^{loc} and the ε₁ of the configuration's problem, in both runs.
        - The coarse side. The coarse move A′ of c^A against Mc^B vanishes on run A's finest layer at ∂Y_i, since run B's layer-1 data there are run A's layer-0 data, the bonds themselves, the same in both runs (M^·(ι_Yc^A) = M^·(c^A)). Elsewhere on the collar it is bounded as in (ii), *The background entry U_{1,2}.*, *The coarse side.*, with the profile π′ = (ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀}(a_{j(x)}/a_h)² on the collar layers j(x) < h and the band sources replaced by the collar's, bounded through the collar regularity. So the collar's own sources give a displacement with a_h|·| + a_h²|∇·| ≤ C(ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀}ε₁ on the collar. The cross-run sources of the strip of the width 2M₁a_2 at ∂Y_i, where the two runs' layers differ (the W-δ note's §12, *The collar's layers.*), are the data ratio and the current of *The strips at ∂□₀.*, of the sizes there by the class (8), and they give at most C_T(ε/a_{j(x)})ε₁ in a_{j(x)}|·| + a_{j(x)}²|∇·| by *The size of the strip sources.* The two displacements compose as in *The alignment.* under C_U(M)ε₁ ≤ 1. On the collar a_{j(x)} ≤ a_h, (ε/a_h)^{1−ε₀} ≤ (ε/a_h)^{(1−ε₀)/3}, and min(j(x), n) ≤ h, so d̄(x) ≥ C_U(M)(ε/a_h)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, and C_T(ε/a_{j(x)})ε₁ ≤ (C_T/C_U(M))d̄(x) (*The strips at ∂□₀.*). Hence a_{j(x)}|A′| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A′| ≤ c_∂d̄(x) on the collar, c_∂ := 2(C + C_T)/C_U(M).
        - The fine side. c^B is critical for the M-preserving variations except on the strip S (the bullet above), the second line of VAR (2) at the index h gives |J_S| ≤ O(1)L³B₃ε₁a_h^{−3}, and PBG (3.42) at the scale ε gives the response ≤ CL³B₃ε₁(ε/a_h)² in a_h|·| and ≤ CL³B₃ε₁(ε/a_h) in a_h²|∇·|, with the decay e^{−δ₀d(x,S)/ε}; in the units of the layer j(x) ≤ h it is ≤ CL³B₃(ε/a_h)ε₁ ≤ (CL³B₃/C_U(M))d̄(x), by the lower bound of d̄ in the bullet above. The flow of *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain* is linear in the coarse move: δ_t is linear in the direction A_c, with its operators at the regular backgrounds W_t, and its sources are bounded through the weight Ŵ built from the coarse bound (*The bound.* there). So the coarse bound c_∂d̄ on the collar and d̄ off it give σ_c ≤ max(1, c_∂)C(ε₀)d̄(x) up to the response, the continuity argument of *The bound.* closing for γ small as there, and σ_c ≤ C″_∂d̄(x) with C″_∂ := max(1, c_∂)C(ε₀) + CL³B₃/C_U(M), a constant independent of k, of the layer, of the volume, and of ε, at real and at complex data as away from ∂Y_i.
        - The age factor. Each run's renormalised 𝐄-terms at its representation configurations carry the irrelevant factor of CRE's proof of Theorem 2: Bałaban's summation over X at the representation configurations "includes renormalization in the form described in the proof of Theorem 2" (LFR II p.376 l.40–41), with the bound O((L^jL^{−n})^{5−β_E})exp(−κd_j(X)) per X for the irrelevant parts, the second-order parts of a single X being cancelled only in the sum over X ∋ z (CRE p.281 l.44–50, "where β is a positive number", the small exponent of (3.67) that the Cauchy note's §5 writes β_E and fixes ≤ 1/20; not the W-δ note's Hölder exponent β), on his spaces of configurations (his presented claim, accepted). This covers irr^A at c^A and irr^B at run B's own representation configuration at the data M^·(c^B_Y), his evaluation points. It does not cover run B's configuration U^B(𝐁_k(Y_i), M^·(c^A)) at the lifted data, the configuration of (α), nor the frozen difference Δ = G − 𝐓^A, which contains G and is not one of his terms. For Δ at the data of a member and at their continuation c^A lies in 𝒮_z (the first sentences of this bullet), so (5.1c) holds with Δ as the term and (5.1v) follows as at the near domains under (N), with σ_c ≤ C″_∂d̄. For Δ at the decoupled data D(s), and for run B's configuration at the lifted data, CRE's printed proof of Theorem 2 does not apply: it runs at configurations whose restriction to □₀ is U_j(□₀, M^·(U)) (CRE p.280 l.15–17, p.283 l.47–48; RG I (3.25) and Lemma 4), the collar layers of the scales < j inside □₀ make c ≠ U_j(□₀, M^·(c)), and Bałaban does not print the proof that LFR II p.376 l.40–41 applies at his representation configurations. There the age factor is derived directly, by comparing c with a member of 𝒮_z that has the same curvature at z (derived, not reviewed). Let c be either configuration, T the term (Δ, or run B's term), X ∋ z a domain of T of the generation j, m′ ∈ {m − 1, m} the finest layer of the member's sequence that □₀ meets, h ≥ m′ the layer of 𝐁″_k that contains the piece of ∂Y_i met, β_H ≥ 19/20 the Hölder index of the Cauchy note's l.106, and irr(T)(c) := Σ_{X∋z}T(X, c) − T(Λ_j, 1, z) − ΔβA(h_z, c) − N(c) as in the Cauchy note's (5.1c) (l.104–128). *(1) The comparison member.* In the complete axial gauge of c centred at z, let ℓ be the linear field of the constant curvature F_ℓ, χ a cut-off equal to 1 on B(z, 2Ma_{m′}) and to 0 outside □̃⁴, and c̃ := U_{m′}(□₀, M^·(exp(iχℓ)c)), extended as c outside □₀. Linear fields have vanishing second differences, so they are critical for the linearised problem at their own averages, and F_ℓ ↦ F(c̃)(z) equals F(U_{m′}(□₀, M^·(c)))(z) + (I + K)F_ℓ + O(|F_ℓ|²), with |K| ≤ C(e^{−2δ₀M} + σ) ≤ ½ (PBG (3.42) for the cut-off's tail, σ the field size of c in the units a_{m′}, small for γ small). The contraction gives F_ℓ with F(c̃)(z) = F(c)(z) and |F_ℓ| ≤ 4sup_{□₀}|F(c)|. c̃ is the composite of its own data, extended as a configuration of the class outside □₀, and its data lie in the class for γ small, since the move is at most 11Mσ in the units of the layer; so c̃ lies in 𝒮_z (the Cauchy note's l.124), and (5.1c) holds at c̃, with (R-Δ^c-T) where m′ = m − 1. *(2) The terms.* Let 𝐀_c and 𝐀_c̃ be the fields of c and c̃ in that gauge, δ := 𝐀_c − 𝐀_c̃, and f(σ′, τ) := T(X, exp i(σ′𝐀_c̃ + τδ)). f is analytic for |σ′| ≤ r/‖𝐀_c̃‖_X and |τ| ≤ r/‖δ‖_X, with ‖·‖_X the norm of the term's domain 𝒫_j(X; r), current included, and r its radius. ∂_τf(0, 0) = T₁(δ) = 0: by global gauge invariance T₁(Ad_gδ) = T₁(δ) for every g ∈ G, so T₁'s coefficient at each bond is an Ad-invariant element of 𝔤, which is 0 since 𝔤 is semisimple (I.4.14). Cauchy's estimate in both variables then gives |T(X, c) − T(X, c̃)| ≤ C sup|T(X, ·)|(‖δ‖_X² + ‖δ‖_X‖𝐀_c̃‖_X)/r². Since F(c)(z) = F(c̃)(z), the axial gauge gives a_j|δ| + a_j²|∇δ| ≤ C[F]_{β_H}a_j^{2+β_H}(d_j(X) + 1)^{1+β_H} on X, [F]_{β_H} the larger Hölder seminorm of F(c) and F(c̃) on B(z, θa_{m′}), θ := ⅛, and a_j|𝐀_c̃| + a_j²|∇𝐀_c̃| ≤ C sup|F|a_j²(d_j(X) + 1). Relative to the radius, by (4), these are of the orders (L^{j−m′})^{2+β_H} and (L^{j−m′})², the current parts being of the order (L^{j−m′})³. So the difference is ≤ C sup|T(X, ·)|(L^{j−m′})^{4+β_H}(d_j(X) + 1)^{2+β_H} for d_j(X) < θL^{m′−j}. For d_j(X) ≥ θL^{m′−j} the sup bounds give e^{−½κθL^{m′−j}}e^{−½κd_j(X)} ≤ C(L^{j−m′})⁵e^{−½κd_j(X)}. Summed over X ∋ z with Bałaban's e^{−κd_j(X)}, the terms differ by ≤ CE₁(L^{j−m′})^{4+β_H}. *(3) The counterterms.* h_z(x) = Π_μmax{1 − |x_μ − z_μ|, 0} in the units a_j (CRE p.283 l.12–16), so ΔβA(h_z, ·) and N read only the plaquettes within a_j of z and are quadratic in them. F(c) − F(c̃) = O([F]_{β_H}a_j^{β_H}) on supp h_z gives (L^{j−m′})^{4+β_H} again. *(4) The regularity of c.* Steps (1)–(3) need F(c) Hölder of the index β_H on B(z, θa_{m′}), with [F]_{β_H} ≤ Cσa_{m′}^{−2−β_H}, σ ≤ O(1)B₃Mα_{0,m} the amplitude of (I.3.27) at the layer, against which the Cauchy note's l.165 measures B, ∂B, and ∂∂B; c̃ inherits this through its data, moved by exp(iχℓ). The member W₀ ∈ 𝒮_z has it by (I.3.27) at the index m (the Cauchy note's l.124), and a_m ≥ a_{m′}. VAR's class (8) and LFR I (1.65), (1.67) bound only |A| and |∇A|, so for c it is derived, as W₀'s bound plus that of the moves below at the scale a_h ≥ a_{m′}, each of an amplitude ≤ σ for γ small. *At D(s).* u_k^0 = u(𝐇) of CMP98 (106) is fixed by (77) and (81) as a function of the field alone (p.30), and M^·((e^{iA}U)^{u(A)^{−1}}) = u(A)_−^{−1}e^{iQ̃(A)}R(u(A)_+)M^·(U) holds for every field A by (88)–(92), with Q̃ the logarithm of CMP98's average (LFR II p.366 l.28). So the boundary-layer data at D(s) are the averages of (exp(iη𝐇″_k(s))U_{1,2})^{u(s)^{−1}} (LFR II p.370 l.41, p.373 l.29–34), a gauge transform of exp(iη𝐇″_k(s))U_{1,2}, which differs from the member's exp(iη𝐇″_k)U_{1,2} by the factor exp(iηδ𝐇(s)), δ𝐇(s) := 𝐇″_k(s) − 𝐇″_k, up to Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff terms. The jumps of u at the faces of the sub-blocks are gauge; the only mismatch that is not gauge lies at ∂Y_1^0, on blocks of the layer h, of the size O(L)|ηδ𝐇(s)| (LFR I (1.37)). 𝐇″_k(s) is a finite combination of walk terms of the operators of 𝐁″_k, whose layer on the collar is h, multiplied by the decoupling parameters of cubes of the size M (RG II p.3 l.5–39). So 𝐇″_k(s) and δ𝐇(s) are Hölder at the scale a_h, with RG II's bounds (1.18) and (1.21) "for all admissible norms" and RG I (3.31). In the background gauge c = W₀ + δ𝐇(s) + w, and w is the response to the residual of δ𝐇(s) for the collar-refined problem. The part of that residual in the range of Q_h* lies in that of Q_q* for the finer collar layers q, since Q_h factors through Q_q, so the multipliers absorb it. The rest, the decoupling commutators [D*D, h_□] (PBG (3.104)) and the mismatch at ∂Y_1^0, is bounded and smooth at the scale a_h, so PBG (3.42)–(3.43) at the layer p(x) of the point give |∇w(x)| ≤ B₀a_{p(x)}|λ|, λ the size of that source, and chains of cubes of the sizes a_p join these into the Hölder bound at the scale a_h. *At the lifted data.* c is compared with ι_Yc^A, which has run B's data exactly (M^·(ι_Yc^A) = M^·(c^A), *The coarse side.*), so no data source arises. ι_Yc^A is critical for run B's constraints except at run A's constraints that run B drops on the strip of the width 2M₁a_2 at ∂Y_i, with the source Q^{A*}λ^A, and at the current J_S of the lift on S. By the collar regularity both sources are ≤ O(1)L³B₃ε₁a_h^{−3}. The response r to a source at the scale a_s ≤ a_2 has |∇²r| ≤ Cε₁a_h^{−3} and |∇r| ≤ Cε₁a_h^{−3}a_s, so [∇r]_{β_H} ≤ Cε₁a_h^{−2−β_H}(a_s/a_h)^{1−β_H}; ι_Yc^A is the one-step lift of c^A, which is Hölder at a_h by the case D(s) or as the member, and the lift's own correction is a response at the scale a_s = ε to sources bounded by the curvature of c^A, so it obeys the same bound. *Complex data.* irr(T) at these configurations is analytic on the disc of the decoupling parameters or of the data. It is bounded at the real points of the disc by (1)–(3) and on the disc by C(1 + d)E₁ (Bałaban's bound of the term and the counterterm on the class). So the Lemma (Two Constants) gives the 15/16 power of the real bound, with (15/16)(4 + β_H) ≥ 4.64 > 4 + β′ for β_H ≥ 19/20 and β′ ≤ ½. With L^{j−m′} ≤ L·L^{j−m}, |irr^G(c^A)| ≤ C_D(1 + d)E₁(L^{j−m})^{4+β′} at these near domains, C_D a constant of d, L, G, and the stage-2 constants. The interpolation of (5.1v) between the class bound, now with σ_c ≤ C″_∂d̄, and this bound gives (5.1v) at these near domains, since (4 + β′)(1 − θ_V) = 4 + β′/2 (the Cauchy note's l.106). As everywhere in (iv), G and this bound are formed only for the generations m/2 < j ≤ m at a point of the layer m; the older generations at these near domains are compared directly (*The old generations.*). The 𝐑-terms are not in this group; they are the Cauchy note's *The 𝐑-terms' variation group* and §10.5, with their status there.
    - *The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer (derived given the inputs of (iv)).* In this bullet n is the birth scale of the history, m ≥ n the layer of z (called n in the bullet above), and j the generation of a term, as in (5.1v), not the index of the composites above. Counting the L^{4(m−j)} scale-j points per scale-m point, as in the Cauchy note's §5 after (5.1v) but without the stopping-lattice factor N_s⁴, (5.1v) at the layer m gives per point |(iv)| ≤ E_VΣ_{m/2<j≤m}[(σ̂_{m,j}/r_{0,m})^{θ_V} + (C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j})^{θ_V}]L^{−(β′/2)(m−j)} for the generations m/2 < j ≤ m, the only ones at which (i) and (iv) are formed, with θ_V = β′/(2(4 + β′)), about 0.053 at β′ = 19/40; in this bullet β′ is the Cauchy note's age exponent (β′_C at l.657), not the walk-factor note's §11 exponent β′ ≤ 1/60 of l.652–657. For them j > m/2 ≥ n/2, so min(j, n) ≥ n/2 and k < 8n + j(C) ≤ 16min(j, n) + j(C), which is what the lift condition at the index j uses (*The inputs at the composites.*, *The lift condition of (i).*). *The old generations.* At a point of the layer m the terms of the generations j ≤ m/2 are not split into (i) and (iv), and neither the per-term lift ι_X, nor G, nor the current along the pair line is used for them. They stay in (ii), whose Cauchy estimate needs only the lift condition of ι_Y, which holds at every generation (*The lift condition of (i).*). After (ii) the telescoping reads them at run B's composites and representation configurations at the data M^·(ι_Yc^A) (*The entries.* in (ii); l.659), local minimisers of data in run B's class, and at run A's at M^·(c^A). At the composites this is derived (not reviewed) at every generation j ≤ m as in *The inputs at the composites.*, with the data move measured against the layer m instead of the index j: the own quantities are those of run B's own composite at its member's data, Bałaban's evaluation point of (1.59); on □₀ the member's layers are ≥ m − 1 (the Cauchy note's §5, *The near domains at a finer layer.*), so the move in the units a_j is ≤ L^{j−m+1}σ_c(x′) + 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} with σ_c(x′) ≤ L^{1/3}C(ε₀)d̄_n, d̄_n := C_U(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, and the composite moves by at most B₀c_tot(¼)(L^{j−m}L^{4/3}C(ε₀)d̄_n + 2C_tε₁e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k}); against r_{0,j} ≥ r_{0,m}L^{j−m} the first part is small by *At real points the ratio is small.* at the layer m, and at complex data by *The Ratio.* at the layer m through the Lemma (Two Constants), and the second part as for ι_Y in *The lift condition of (i).* At the representation configurations the own quantities go through (1.65) (*The Room.*). At these configurations each run's renormalised terms of the generation j are bounded by Bałaban's Theorem 2. These are the terms minus their vacuum values 𝐄^{(j)}(Λ_j, 1, z) and their counterterms, the irr_j of the Cauchy note's l.62; the vacuum values do not depend on the field and enter the constant E_k (the Cauchy note's l.926), and the β_j are absorbed by CRE (2.24): into g_stop at the all-small-field term (its l.62), and into the coupling g″_k(·) with the functions φ_m in the large-field case (l.377). Theorem 2 gives the bound (2.43), E₁Σ_n(L^{j−n})^β|Γ_n ∩ Ω|, used per point in the per-z form of the Cauchy note's l.106 (CRE (3.67), p.283 l.31) and per Y through Bałaban's resummation into the V(Y) (LFR II p.376 l.39–46), with β = 1 − β_E (the Cauchy note's l.106), "for β<1, and sufficiently regular configurations U_k=U_k(V)" (CRE p.263 l.14–20). His example of "sufficiently regular" is "V restricted by the characteristic functions in (2.18)" (p.263 l.19–20), χ_k(Ω_k) of (2.17), plaquettes below ε_kη² (p.257 l.38–40), together with the characteristic functions inside 𝐓_k.
