Since min(a + x, s) ≤ a^{1−θ_V}s^{θ_V} + x, (iv) is bounded by the summands above, with the Cauchy note's 6⁵C_α in place of C_α in E_V, plus Σ_{m/2<j≤m}X_j. In the units of the layer the move is at most σ_c(x′) ≤ C(ε₀)(a_k/ε)^{ε₀}d̄(x′) plus the tail of ι_Y (*The inputs at the composites.*, *The configuration part.*), so s_j ≤ 3L²σ̂_m with σ̂_m := sup_{□₀}σ_c, the factor 3 covering the terms |𝐀| and |𝐀||∇A₀| of (F-H)'s |∇𝐀| under |∇A₀| ≤ 1, and L² the conversion of the points of the layer m − 1 in □₀ to the units of the layer m (a_m|A| ≤ La_{m−1}|A|, a_m²|∇A| ≤ L²a_{m−1}²|∇A|), and s_j/α_{3,j} ≤ 3L²(σ̂_m/r_{0,m})(r_{0,m}/α_{3,j}), r_{0,m}/α_{3,j} = L²α_{0,m}/(4β_Iα_{0,j}) ≤ L²(1+β₀)²max(1, m − j)^{1/2}/(4β_I) by the Cauchy note's *The constant.* and α_{3,j} of its l.163. Here α is the background amplitude O(1)B₃Mα_{0,m} of CRE p.280 l.19–23, so α/α_{3,j} ≤ C(M)max(1, m − j)^{1/2} in the same way. *The configuration part.* bounds σ̂_m/r_{0,m} ≤ σ̂_m/ε_m by L^{−(1−25ε₀)n/3} times R_k, (k − m + 1)^{1/2}, L^{j(C)ε₀}, and constants (at complex data relative to d̄′). With (α₁/α₃)² ≤ C(M)(m − j + 1) (l.615), each X_j is then at most C(M, β, κ)E₀ times max(1, m − j)^{3/2} times the ratio σ̂_m/r_{0,m} and these constants, the square of the ratio being at most the ratio where it is ≤ 1. So Σ_{m/2<j≤m}X_j ≤ C_xE₀m^{5/2}L^{−(1−25ε₀)n/3} times these factors. Since θ̃ ≥ L^{−θ_V/8} with θ_V/8 < 1/100 < (1 − 25ε₀)/3, and m ≤ k < 8n + j(C), the slack absorbs m^{5/2}, R_k, and (k − m + 1)^{1/2}, and Σ_jX_j ≤ C_xθ̃^n per point, C_x carrying L^{j(C)ε₀} as C_{iv} does. The tail of ι_Y enters σ̂_m with the factor e^{−½δ₀M₁L^m} (*The inputs at the composites.*) and is absorbed as in the tail part above.
      - The result. For n > k_s/8, so that k < 8n + j(C), |(iv)| ≤ C_{iv}θ̃^n per point at the layers ≥ n. C_{iv} depends on M, ḡ, β₀, p₀, L, r, β′, β_E, ε₀, δ₀M₁, E_V, E₁, E_o, and C_x of *The excess part.*, the constants C_t and c of the tail, and Bałaban's constants. It depends on j(C) through L^{θ_Vε₀j(C)} times a polynomial in j(C) and in log(ḡ^{−2} + j(C)): the first factor comes from (a_k/ε)^{ε₀} ≤ L^{j(C)ε₀}L^{8nε₀} in σ_c, which the slack in n cannot remove, and the second from log g_k^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) (BETA_FUNCTION_WILSON_LOOPS.md Lemma 2.1(i)) and K − k < 8n + j(C). It does not depend on k, K, ε, or the volume, and the callers use it at fixed j(C). C_h's enlargement 2θ̃^{−n₀′} of *Small n.* depends on j(C) through n₀′ ≥ max(n₀″, n₀‴) + 1: each of these thresholds is the least n at which a power L^{−cn}, c > 0, beats factors polynomial in K − k < 8n + j(C) and in log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) and at most L^{Cε₀j(C)}, so n₀′ grows at most linearly in j(C), and θ̃^{−n₀′} ≤ L^{θ_Vn₀′/8} is exponential in j(C), of the same kind as L^{θ_Vε₀j(C)}. This is the form h_n = C_hθ̃^n of (LF-2), per point of the history at every scale (the Cauchy note's §8.6), with C_h enlarged by C_{iv}. The history's finest layer, at the scale n − 1 (the Cauchy note's §8.6), gets the same bound with C_{iv} multiplied by L^{β′}: there d̄ carries the factor L^{(1−ε₀)/3} more, which costs L^{θ_V(1−ε₀)/3} ≤ L^{β′}, the old generations' L^{−β′m/2} the factor L^{β′/2} more, and the double-exponential summands cost θ̃^{−1} ≤ L^{θ_V/8} ≤ L^{β′}; at m = n − 1 the generations j > m/2 have min(j, n) ≥ (n − 1)/2, so the lift condition holds with j(C) + 8 in place of j(C). A domain at the layers > k_s/8 that is not deep gets ≤ C_{iv}θ̃^{k_s/8} per point in the same way. (α) is the same group at the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63) and has the same bound. Both rest, for the generations m/2 < j ≤ m, on the inputs of (iv), and for the older generations on Bałaban's per-run bound at run A's data (his claim) and on (5.1c), (R-Δ^c-T), and (5.1c𝐀) with the excess of (F-H) at run B's lifted data, real and complex (*The old generations.*, derived, not reviewed), and at the near domains at ∂Y_i, where run B's term at the lifted data is bounded by the direct comparison of *The age factor.* (derived, not reviewed), at data placed in run B's class by the lift condition of ι_Y at every generation (*The lift condition of (i).*, unreviewed); at the composites (5.1v) holds through the Cauchy note's (5.1c𝐀), the composites being composites over 𝒮_z, with run B's move through (F-H) and its excess (*The composites over 𝒮_z.*, *The excess part.*, derived, not reviewed). The inputs are derived at real and at complex data (unreviewed; at the composites and the representation configurations with the strips of *The strips at ∂□₀.* and the term (IV) of *The current at the strips.*, the composites' summands at the scale of their generation, *The configuration part.*), at the near domains that meet the boundaries ∂Y_i of the representation configurations through *The near domains at ∂Y_i.* (derived, not reviewed), where the age factor of the frozen difference at the decoupled data D(s) and of run B's terms at the lifted data is derived directly (*The age factor.*, derived, not reviewed); at the data of a member the frozen difference's is (5.1c), c^A being the member. The conditions of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) at the representation configurations, which the terms read only through the extended U^0_k of (1.61) and which are then Bałaban's (1.65), hold given the lift condition (derived, not reviewed), and (R-Δ^c-T) is derived, not reviewed. (5.1v) itself is unreviewed.
  - The room at real points (derived, on three premises: §3(d)'s item on (1.64)–(1.69) for the membership of the real backgrounds, *The multipliers.* for J_{p,X}, and *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, unreviewed; the current is moved by *The Current at the Multiscale Chain.*, derived, not reviewed). Bałaban's C₀ and C₁ of (2.28) are "sufficiently large" (p.259 l.50), that is, bounded below only, so they are taken at least λ_Q := max{1, 4B₃, 8B₃, 32B₃²L³, 8B₃C_{Q*}, C_mem} times his lower bounds, C_mem that of *The conditions.* in §4(d), C_{Q*} the constant of the Cauchy note's pair lift (§5, *Complex domain*). Then his statements hold at the thresholds α̃′ := α̃/(4B₃) of the Cauchy note's common polydisc and at their halves α̃′/2, the radii r of its norm class, as well (*The room at complex points.*, *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*). In particular (1.65) holds with α̃₀′/2 and α̃₁′/2 in both of its spaces, its proof being that of (1.89) [IV] with ε_j replaced by the thresholds (p.375 l.25–27). The integers q₀ and q₁ of (2.28) are bounded below only ("greater than 1", p.259 l.48–50), so q₁ ≥ q₀ > p₀ is a choice that (2.28) and RG I p.277 l.54 and p.285 l.17 allow, and it is made here. The real backgrounds of the support lie in Ũ^c_k(Y₄, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2), as they lie in the spaces (1.64)–(1.69) of LFR I (§3(d), the item on (1.64)–(1.69)), since α′_{i,m}/(2ε_m) = (C_i/(8B₃A₀))(log g_m^{−2})^{q_i−p₀} also exceeds every constant for γ small (CRE (2.4), (2.28)). So at a real point U^0_k, with its current, satisfies the conditions of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) for n = N (p.375 l.24–25) with the halves α̃′/2 of the thresholds, by (1.65), which is a statement about U^0_k (p.375 l.17–21). For the other configurations at which the terms are read, the other members of 𝒞 and the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63), Bałaban states only that the extended functions (1.63) and (1.62) have regularity "almost the same" as U(𝐁_k(Y_3), M^·(𝐔)) and U(𝐁_1(Y_2), M^·(𝐔)) (p.375 l.1–13). Their conditions at real points are derived in *The conditions at the other configurations*, except the representation configuration of (1.61) at complex decoupling parameters. The composites are treated in (iv). Each conditioned quantity is below ½ϖ times its threshold, where ϖ ≥ 1 − 4β is the prefactor of LFR I (1.64)–(1.68) at the point (pp.190–191). β := 1/8 is taken, in CRE's class (2.34)–(2.39) and in LFR I's tests alike, which Bałaban's β ≤ 1/4 (p.191 l.35) allows, so ϖ ≥ ½ and the room in each condition is at least ¼ of its threshold. CRE p.261 l.42 asks only that β be "a small positive constant, but not too small, e.g., we can take β = 1/4"; *The choice β = 1/8, checked.* below checks that no step needs more. The old terms are analytic on Ũ″^c_k(Y₁, α̃₀, α̃₁) with Bałaban's bounds (p.375 l.29–35), so for the joint line of (ii), which starts at run B's chain c^B, it suffices that each of its configurations stays in the space at the thresholds α̃′. At a point x of the layer m the thresholds of that space carry α′_{i,j} with j ≥ m and factors ≥ 1. Here the layer of x is the index of the scale factors of its region. In (1.66) the region (Ω_l ∖ Ω_{l+1}) ∩ X, l = k₀ + 1, …, j, carries the factors (L^jη)^{−p} of the scale j with L_0^{2(j−l)} (p.190 l.38–48), and after the j − h preliminary integrations the determining set 𝔹_k^{(j−h)} of LFR I (1.16) (p.180 l.19–26) contains (Ω_{j+1}^c ∖ Z_j″)^{(j)}, whose variables are at the scale j. So the data of the configuration there is at the scale j, and its layer there is j. The cascade test (1.24) with the thresholds ε_j there (pp.181–182) is a test on these variables and does not by itself fix the layer. (1.66) imposes the same scale-j thresholds on the determining-set configurations U_{p,X}(M^·(𝕌)) = U(𝐁_p(X) ∪ {Γ_i^{(n)}}_{i<p}, M^·(𝕌)) and their currents there (p.190 l.18, l.38–48), so the layer that governs d̄ and σ_c there is j as well. In the units of a layer l < j these thresholds would be smaller by (L_0²/L^p)^{j−l} < 1, since L_0² ≤ L/3 (LFR I p.191 l.35). And α′_{i,j} ≥ α′_{i,m}/(1 + β₀), since g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)g_j and g(log g^{−2})^{q_i} increases for g small. With α_m := min(α′_{0,m}, α′_{1,m}), every threshold at x is therefore at least α_m/(1 + β₀) times its scale factor. Along the joint line, at |t|σ_c(x) ≤ c_ρα_m, inside the analyticity domains of the maps below, the moves of each configuration c(t) are as follows.
    - In the decomposition c(t) = U′U with U := c^B and A′ := t𝐒_c on the lattice T_{ε/L}, the G-valued part and the cube-gauge regularity condition (1.69) are unchanged, |A′| ≤ |t|σ_c(x)/a_m, and |∇_UA′| ≤ |t|σ_c(x)/a_m².
    - The plaquettes of c(t) move by ≤ 4|t|σ_c(x)(ε/(La_m))². Run B's U_{p,X}(M^·(c(t))) and J_{p,X}(M^·(c(t))) are the configuration and current of the determining set 𝐁_p(X) ∪ {Γ_i}_{i<p} at the data M^·(c(t)) (p.190 l.17–18), so they move with the data: U_{p,X} by (3.133) with the count of (b), and J_{p,X} = 𝐐*λ through its multipliers by Cauchy's estimate, as in *The multipliers.* So each of these quantities moves by ≤ C_p|t|σ_c(x) times its scale factor, with C_p ≥ 4 a constant of B₀, c_K, and the constants c̄_e, c_W, C_r, and C_ℒ′ of *The multipliers.*
    - The currents are frozen along the configuration line of (ii) and move only in its current part, by ΔJ_c, whose room and bound at the real members are those of *The Current at the Multiscale Chain.* in (ii).

    Each move other than the current is therefore ≤ C_p|t|σ_c(x) times its scale factor. So, the currents being moved separately, ϱ(x) ≥ c_ρα_m at the real points x of the layer m, with c_ρ := 1/(4(1 + β₀)C_p). Then each move is at most ¼ of its threshold, and the joint line stays in the space at the thresholds α̃′ for |t| ≤ 1/r(Y).
  - *The choice β = 1/8, checked.* Every bare β of the transcriptions of CRE, LFR I, and LFR II was read (119 occurrences on 28 pages: 29 on 10 pages of CRE, 59 on 11 of LFR I, and 31 on 7 of LFR II).
    - The upper bounds hold at 1/8: LFR I p.182 l.17 (0 < β ≤ ½), p.191 l.34–35 and p.198 l.16 (β ≤ 1/4), p.199 l.13 (¾ < 1 − β), p.200 l.22–23 (21/8 ≤ 3(1 − β/2) and ≤ 4(1 − β/2)), p.200 l.28 and l.39 (room 1 − β), p.187 l.25–31 (β ≤ ½), and LFR II p.376 l.5–6 ((1 − β)L ≥ 1 + 3β at L ≥ 2).
    - RG I lies outside this count. Its class parameter, written β_I as in the Cauchy note, enters the margins of its Lemma 4 (pp.275–280), and the Cauchy note's §5 takes β_I ≤ 1/4. Its upper bounds hold there at L ≥ 2: (1 + 7β_I)L^{−2} ≤ 1 (p.279 l.28, "for β not too large") and (1 + 8β_I)L^{−2} ≤ 1 (p.280 l.18), which are 11/16 and ¾ at β_I = 1/4 and L = 2. Its other restrictions, O(1)Mα₁ ≤ β_I (p.278 l.46) and (4B₃²O(1)M)²α₀ ≤ β_I (p.278 l.64), are choices of α₀ and α₁ made after β_I.
    - The steps that need β not too small need only β > 0 with another constant chosen after it: κ large at LFR II p.389 l.12 (βκ ≥ 6·2^d), and LFR II p.389 l.39 and p.388 l.34; R large at LFR I p.184 l.4 (B₃e^{−2δM₂R_j}22d² < β/10); the gains (1 + cβ)κ of LFR I p.192 l.11, CRE p.262 l.27–28, and LFR II pp.376–391; and the margin α₃ of RG I p.280, proportional to β, through the thresholds of kind γ small. These choices are made after β.
    - The steps homogeneous in β hold for every β: LFR I (1.31) (p.184 l.8–9, at L² > 2), and p.186 l.42–p.187 l.13, where the small factors are chosen as αβ (p.186 l.42) and the step needs 8α ≤ 1 (p.187 l.13).
    - CRE p.276 l.30 enlarges the class by 1 + β, and the definitions of CRE p.261 l.15–37 and LFR I pp.181–182 and pp.186–191 carry β as a parameter.
    - The other letters β of these pages are other constants: the exponent of Theorem 2 (CRE p.263 l.16–42, p.281 l.45, p.283 l.28–32, p.284 l.15), the β-function (CRE p.255 l.52, p.282 l.43), the coupling parameter of CRE p.246 l.17–18, β′ of CRE (2.6)–(2.9), and the exponent of LFR II p.357 l.10, where any β > 0 holds.
  - The room at complex points (derived, not reviewed). The class norms of the Cauchy note's §8.2 and §8.6 are taken on its norm classes at the radii r = α′/2 = α/(8B₃): d_j is the smallest number with the bound of §8.2 on U^c_j(X, r_{0,j}, r_{1,j}), and the same holds for the norms of the 𝐑- and 𝐁-terms and for (LF-2). A bound derived on a class holds on its norm class, and the real backgrounds of the support lie in the norm classes (*The room at real points.*), so every use at real configurations is unchanged. The sub-bullets derive the coarse alignment at the points of the norm class and the joint line of (ii) there, through the complex lift. The class norms are evaluated only at run A's configurations, in (i). *The Room* puts U^0_k and U_k in run A's half of the norm class; the lift half at the members is *The lift condition at complex points.* for n ≥ n₀′, and at the decoupled configurations *The lift half at W.* (derived, not reviewed); the composites have theirs in *The composites and the representation configurations* (derived, not reviewed). The joint line also uses (1) the inputs of (ii) at the real points of the line, which *The Fine Side at Complex Points* carries to the complex points, and (2) the inequality (W-δ) of (iii), for the split of each V(Y), both derived, not reviewed. The class bound (5.1c) at run A's configurations is derived in the Cauchy note's §5 at the near domains satisfying (N), unreviewed, at the others through (R-Δ^c-T), derived, not reviewed, and at the near domains at ∂Y_i through Bałaban's proof of Theorem 2 run with Δ as the term, derived, not reviewed, given his presented claim that the proof applies at his representation configurations ((iv), *The near domains at ∂Y_i.*).
    - *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds (Derived, Unreviewed).* Bałaban's statements hold at the thresholds α_{i,j}/μ for the seven values μ ∈ {½, 1, 4B₃, 8B₃, 32B₃²L³, 8B₃C_{Q*}, C_mem} that the notes use, once C₀ and C₁ are at least λ_Q = max{1, 4B₃, 8B₃, 32B₃²L³, 8B₃C_{Q*}, C_mem} times his lower bounds, as *The room at real points.* takes them. At μ = ½, used for the margin of the X_s representation (`WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md`, §12, *The domain.*), the lower bounds hold a fortiori and the upper bounds hold for γ small. The construction does not involve C₀ and C₁, and each of his restrictions on them is of one of three kinds, each of which holds at C₀/μ and C₁/μ. This rests on every use of C₀, C₁, and the thresholds in CRE, LFR I, LFR II, RG I, and RG II.
      - *The construction.* In CRE, C₀ and C₁ occur, directly or through the thresholds, only in (2.28) (p.259 l.40–50), in the spaces of p.261 (l.5–37, l.60), in (3.43) (p.276 l.24–31), and in the remark of p.262 l.43–46. In LFR I the thresholds occur only in the spaces (1.64)–(1.69) (p.190 l.13–p.191 l.21, p.195 l.22–24) and in the remarks of p.191 l.34–43 and p.192 l.16–22. The C₀ of LFR I (1.70) (p.192 l.11) is the constant of a bound, which at the full thresholds restricts to the smaller spaces, so it needs no identification. The α of LFR I pp.186–187 and pp.198–200 is an absolute constant, and the α₀ of p.200 l.35 is the exponent log L₀²/log L. In LFR II the thresholds occur only in the extensions of p.358 l.27–29, p.366 l.25–28, and p.367 l.46–48, in the spaces of (1.65) (p.375 l.17–35), and in the bounds (1.47) (p.368 l.23–27) and p.379 l.31. The α of LFR II p.377 l.11–14 and pp.388–391 is the constant of a bound. The cutoffs are ε_j = g_jA₀(log g_j^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.4)), δ_k = g_k(A₁/A₀)p₀(g_k) (CRE p.265 l.39) in (3.16), and ϑ_k (LFR I p.193 l.21–23). The number of usual steps is N = R_j (LFR II p.384 l.17, LFR I p.177 l.25–33, N > N₀ by p.179 l.10), with R_j the power of L of CRE (2.5). None of these involves C₀ or C₁. So the effective actions, the expansions, and the R-operation are the same for all values of C₀ and C₁. The extensions are given by explicit substitution formulas, which do not involve C₀ or C₁ either: RG I (3.13) replaces J_{k+1} by 𝐉 and U_{k+1} by 𝐔 in (3.11)–(3.12) (p.272), (3.15) defines 𝐇_j and 𝐇_k by VAR (179)–(180) with 𝐔 and 𝐉 in their operators (p.273), and LFR II constructs its extensions "by replacing" the real arguments by the complex ones (p.358, p.366), with 𝐀₀ = 𝐇_{k,Λ}((1/i)log M(U₀(𝐔))) by VAR Sect. G (p.367). So the extensions at smaller thresholds are the restrictions of those at larger ones. The real configurations alone would fix an extension only on the graph 𝐉 = J(𝐔), since 𝐉 is an independent variable; the formulas fix it everywhere.
      - *Upper bounds.* The following are upper bounds on the thresholds:
        - RG I's restrictions O(1)Mα₁ ≤ β (p.278 l.46), (4B₃²O(1)M)²α₀ ≤ β (p.278 l.64), Mα₀ and Mα₁ small (p.275 l.28), α₀ and α₁ much smaller than the α′ of the domain of 𝐏^{(k)} (p.270 l.2–3), and α₂ and α₃ sufficiently small (p.279 l.47–49, p.280 l.31);
        - RG II's (LM)⁴α₀, (LM)⁴α₁ ≤ 1 (p.20 l.23), its α′ much bigger than α (p.6, p.10, p.15), its R₁ with the factor O(α₀ + α₁) (p.16), and α₅‖C‖ < ½ (p.17);
        - the smallness of LFR II (1.47) and of the bound of p.379 l.31;
        - the parts α²ε of the step statement of LFR I p.191 and of LFR II (1.65) (*Mixed statements.* below).

        At α_{i,j} = g_jC_i(log g_j^{−2})^{q_i} they hold for γ small at fixed C₀ and C₁, γ depending on all other constants (RG I Theorem 3, p.264 l.39–41), and they are weaker at smaller thresholds. In (1.47) the smallness comes from L^{−N} with N = R_j, and L^{−R_j} ≤ (log g_j^{−2})^{−ν} for every ν when γ is small (CRE (2.5)). So (1.47) bounds neither C₁ nor q₁. The bound of p.379 l.31 carries the factor g_k. Apart from the order relation below, no printed restriction bounds q₀ or q₁ above. The one printed relation between them is RG I's B₃²O(1)Mα₀ < ½α₁ (p.277 l.54) and α₂ + B₃O(1)Mα₀ < α₁ in (4.17) (p.285 l.17), which force q₁ ≥ q₀ for g small. So the choice is q₁ ≥ q₀ > p₀ + 3r, as at l.15, and within it q₁ = q₀ is taken, with C₁/C₀ large enough for these two relations, as in W-δ §8, stage 0, and its l.1101 (C₁ = λ_RC₀); §4(d)'s *The factor.* uses q₁ = q₀.
      - *Homogeneous relations.* The following are unchanged when α₀, α₁, α₂, and α₃ are divided by the same μ:
        - RG I's B₃²O(1)Mα₀ < ½α₁ (p.277 l.54), B₃α₃ < ½α₁ (p.280 l.6), 2B₃α₃ ≤ βL^{−2}α₀ (p.280 l.16), and O(1)(α₂ + B₃O(1)Mα₀) < α₁ (p.285 l.17);
        - the ratio α₁/α₃ of the Cauchy factors (RG I p.280 l.39–44, RG II p.7 l.27);
        - RG I's α′ < α and 2B₃α₀′ against α₀ (p.263), and the factor (2n²B₃/α₂)^n of (4.5) (p.282), spent in (4.21) as (8B₃α₁/α₂)⁴ (p.286);
        - α₂ itself: RG I takes it "depending on α₀ and on some absolute constants" (p.272), fixed by room conditions linear in 𝐀 up to second order, so α₂ := c₂min(α₀, α₁) with c₂ absolute, which divides by μ with α₀ and α₁;
        - the parts e^{−R}ε < αε of the step statement of LFR I p.191 and of LFR II (1.65) (*Mixed statements.* below);
        - the factors L₀^{2N₀} < L(L + 1)N₀^{−1}R_k of LFR I p.192, which involve no threshold and are the same at every μ.
      - *Mixed statements.* The step statement of LFR I p.191 l.39–43 is proved as (1.44)–(1.52) (p.191 l.42–44), where each step spends a part αβ2^{−(j−i)} of the margin of the space against the shift of the fluctuation field (p.186 l.42–p.187 l.31), and LFR II (1.65) is proved as LFR I (1.89) with ε_j replaced by α_{0,j} (p.375 l.24–27). Read with ε replaced by α₀ on LFR I pp.198–200, each splits into restrictions of the three kinds: e^{−R}ε < αε is homogeneous (p.198, p.199), B₃δ′_k < αε is a lower bound (p.198, p.199), and the terms α²ε are upper bounds (p.199, p.200). Each part is listed under its kind.
      - *Lower bounds against sizes free of C₀ and C₁.* The following are lower bounds on the thresholds against sizes that do not involve C₀ and C₁:
        - the parts B₃δ′_k < αε of the step statement of LFR I p.191 and of LFR II (1.65) (*Mixed statements.*);
        - RG I's ε₀ small (p.263), and ε₁ small against α₂ (p.273 l.13, p.280 l.49), with the radius α₂/3 and the factor α₂^{−1} of (3.17) against ε₁ (p.273);
        - RG II's 4B₀C₁e^{16κ₁}ε₁ ≤ ¼α₂ (p.7 l.9) and α₂^{−1}ε₁ (p.8), with C₁ there an absolute constant (RG II p.9 l.48); the constant C₃ of RG II p.20 l.24–25 carries RG II's own α₄ and α₆ (p.16, p.18), not the thresholds;
        - CRE (3.43) (p.276), which asks |A| < C₁p₁(g_k) with p₁(g) = (log g^{−2})^{p₁} and p₁ < p₀ (LFR I p.183): if this C₁ is the threshold constant, it is a lower bound covered by λ_Q, and otherwise it does not involve the thresholds;
        - the membership of the real backgrounds in the spaces, derived at the reduced thresholds in *The room at real points.* from §3(d)'s item on (1.64)–(1.69).

        The sizes are ε_j, δ_k, ϑ_k, and the fluctuation shifts. These restrictions are what "C₀, C₁ are sufficiently large" (CRE p.259 l.50) provides: each holds for all C_i above a lower bound K_i that is fixed by the constants chosen before C₀ and C₁.
      - *The order of the constants.* B₃ depends on d and L only (VAR Theorem 1, p.279 l.28), C_{Q*} = 2(1 + (2d + 1)L^{d−1}) (Cauchy note l.112), and C_mem = 4O(1)(1+β₀)²B₃(1 + C_m) with C_m a constant of δ₀(d, L) (*The conditions.*). So λ_Q is fixed before C₀ and C₁, and C_i ≥ λ_QK_i is a choice that "sufficiently large" allows. Each of the three kinds then holds at C_i/μ:
        - the lower bounds, since C_i/μ ≥ K_i, as λ_Q ≥ μ for each of the six values μ ≥ 1 by its definition as their maximum;
        - the homogeneous relations, as at C_i;
        - the upper bounds, for γ at most the least of the seven values γ(C₀/μ, C₁/μ).

        N = R_j and the cutoffs are the same in all five runs. So his statements hold at the thresholds α_{i,j}/μ, with α₂ and α₃ divided by μ with them, and his bounds at his thresholds restrict to these smaller classes.
      - *What it covers.* This covers the common polydisc α′_{i,j} = α_{i,j}/(4B₃) of the Cauchy note's §5, its norm class r_{i,j} = α_{i,j}/(8B₃), the current bound r_{0,j}/C_{Q*} of the norm class, and the radii r_{0,j}/(4B₃L³) = α_{0,j}/(32B₃²L³) and r_{1,j}/(2L³) = α_{1,j}/(16B₃L³) of its class (b). In particular the inclusions that the Cauchy note's norms use hold there: RG I Lemma 4 (p.280, (3.53)) with α₃/μ, CRE (3.43), the class at the next scale (p.262 l.26–30, p.279 l.22–23), and (1.65) with α̃₀′/2 and α̃₁′/2 in both of its spaces.
    - *The Margin.* The Cauchy note's §8.2 runs R-Δ^c at the radii r of the norm class and takes the margin m_k := α_{3,k}/2 of that run, and places the cutoff ball of (3.16) inside it with the ratio T₀ := m_k/ϑ_k ≥ 2. By ϑ_k = (A₁/A₀)ε_k, ε_k = g_kA₀(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.4)), and α_{3,k} ≥ c_mg_k(log g_k^{−2})^{min(q₀,q₁)}, α_{3,k}/ϑ_k ≥ (c_m/A₁)(log g_k^{−2})^{min(q₀,q₁)−p₀}. Since q₀, q₁ > p₀ by the choice of *The room at real points.*, α_{3,k}/ϑ_k ≥ 4 for γ small, so T₀ ≥ 2.
    - *The Room.* At a point (𝐔, 𝐉) of Ũ^c_k(Y₄, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2), (1.65) at these thresholds puts U^0_k, with its current, in Ũ″^c_k(Y₁, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2); the other members of 𝒞 are as at real points (*The conditions at the other configurations*, derived, not reviewed, on LFR I (1.64)–(1.69)). The old terms are analytic on Ũ″^c_k(Y₁, α̃₀, α̃₁) with Bałaban's bounds (p.375 l.29–35). So each conditioned quantity of LFR I (1.64)–(1.68) is below ½ϖ times its threshold, and the room in each condition is at least ¼ of its threshold, as at real points. For U_k itself, the point (𝐔, 𝐉), this holds directly, since Ũ^c_k(Y₄, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2) imposes the conditions at the points of Y.
    - *The Alignment Field at Complex Points.* Let z ↦ (𝐔(z), 𝐉(z)) be the line of *The complex class.* through (𝐔, 𝐉), and A(z) the alignment field of run A's configuration of the chain at the point z of the line against Ũ(z). For a point x and a complex-linear functional ℓ of norm 1, f(z) := ℓ(a_{j(x)}A(z)(x)) is analytic for |z| < 25 (*The disc.*). Let d̄′(x) be the coarse-side bound d̄(x) of (ii) with ε₁′ for ε₁. The real points x with |x| < 25 are regular with 2ε₁′ for ε₁, by the count of *The real points.* with 26 for 25 and (T-c), so |f(x)| ≤ 2d̄′(x) ≤ 2C_U(M)ε₁′ there. The line's data at z and at Re z differ only through i Im(z)A′_I, by ≤ 2C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) ≤ 2ε₁′ times their scale factors, since C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) ≤ ε′_k ≤ ε₁′. So each run's configuration of the chain at z differs from that at Re z by ≤ 2C_pε₁′, as U_{p,X} moves with its data in *The room at real points.* Writing U^A(z)Ũ(z)^{−1} = [U^A(z)U^A(Re z)^{−1}][U^A(Re z)Ũ(Re z)^{−1}][Ũ(Re z)Ũ(z)^{−1}] gives |f| ≤ M_c := (2C_U(M) + 5C_p)ε₁′ on the disc, the fifth C_pε₁′ covering the commutators of the three factors. M_c ≤ 1 for γ small. The Lemma (Two Constants), with 𝔪 = 2d̄′(x) and 𝔐 = 1, gives |f(i)| ≤ (2d̄′(x))^{15/16} ≤ 2d̄′(x)^{15/16}, and the same holds for a_{j(x)}²∇A. So at (𝐔, 𝐉), a_{j(x)}|A(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A(x)| ≤ 𝔡(x) := 4d̄′(x)^{15/16}. This is the coarse input of the fine side at complex points.
    - *The Fine Side at Complex Points (Derived, Unreviewed).* The joint line of (ii) at the point z of the line needs ι_Yc^A(z) = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c(z)}c^B(z) with a bound on 𝐒_c(z). The construction of *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.* is a flow in t, and it continues analytically to the disc |z| < 25 of *The disc.*, where its operators keep their bounds. Here ‖𝐒‖ := sup_x(a_{j(x)}|𝐒(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐒(x)|), with ∇ the covariant difference at c^B(z), as in r(Y).
      - The members. Under (T-c), Proposition 9 (p.309 l.8–14) gives each member at the data V′V₀ of the disc, with V₀ real and |V′ − 1| < C_Hε₁′, as c(z) = e^{iη𝐇_c(z)}c_0 in the Landau gauge, with c_0 the member at V₀ and 𝐇_c analytic in z with VAR (19)–(21) (p.281 l.5–19) at ε₂ ≤ 8B₅ε₁′. Run A's member is c^A(z) = e^{iεA_c(z)}Ũ_c(z), Ũ_c(z) := Mc^B(z), with the alignment field A_c(z) of *The Alignment Field at Complex Points.* against this Ũ_c(z), which is analytic on the disc with a_{j(x)}|A_c(z)(x)| ≤ M_c and a_{j(x)}²|∇A_c(z)(x)| ≤ M_c there.
      - The complex class of ι's problem. ι's problem has the single layer of the blocks of size ε (*The one-step problem.*), so PBG's class (3.37)–(3.38) (p.396 l.38–49) for its operators consists of the configurations W = e^{i(ε/L)A′}U with U G-valued, satisfying (3.35)–(3.36) on its cubes, and |A′| < α₁ε^{−1}, |∇_UA′| < α₁ε^{−2}, and |D*_UD_UA′| < α₁ε^{−3}. For α₁ ≤ a₁^{PBG}, the constant a₁ of PBG Theorem 3.4 (p.400 l.7–10), that theorem extends G′, (Q′G′²Q′*)^{−1}, R, and G to this class as analytic functions of A′ with all the inequalities of Theorems 3.1–3.3. VAR p.309 l.1–4 extends the operators of VAR (174)–(180), among them 𝔊 and H₁ (VAR (175)), to regular complex configurations as analytic functions with the properties and bounds of the real case, and Proposition 9 states the solution map ℋ of (179)–(180) analytic in the external configuration on (3.35)–(3.38), with (190); RG I p.273 l.1–3 substitutes complex configurations into these operators in the same way. T^{pl} of ℒ is a polynomial in the transports of W, so on the class it is a perturbation of relative size O(α₁) of its value at U, and (T^{pl})^{−1} decays at the rate δ_Q^{pl} with twice the constant for α₁ small, by the Neumann series.
      - The lifted backgrounds in the class. c^B_0 satisfies (3.35)–(3.36) on the cubes of ι's problem by VAR (9)–(10), as in *The one-step problem.* Let 𝔯 := 8C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3}M_c, and for ‖𝐒‖ ≤ 2𝔯 let W(𝐒, z) := e^{i(ε/L)𝐒}c^B(z) = e^{i(ε/L)A′}c^B_0, with A′ the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff composition of 𝐒 and 𝐇_{c^B}(z). Run B's finest layer has the spacing ε/L, so (19) gives ε|𝐇_{c^B}| ≤ Lε₂, and ε|A′| ≤ α′ := 2(2𝔯 + Lε₂), the factor 2 covering the BCH terms for γ small. On the fine lattice each difference costs at most 2L/ε, so ε²|∇_{c^B_0}A′| ≤ 2Lα′ and ε³|D*_{c^B_0}D_{c^B_0}A′| ≤ 8dL²α′. So W(𝐒, z) lies in the class with α₁ := 8dL²α′ under
        (T-ι) 16dL²(16C_δ(ε₀)L^{7/3}M_c + 8LB₅ε₁′) ≤ a₁^{PBG}.
        The field strength of W(𝐒, z) is that of c^B(z) up to 2|∇𝐒| ≤ 4𝔯a_{j(x)}^{−2}, and that of c^B(z) is that of c^B_0 up to 2|∇𝐇_{c^B}| ≤ 2L²ε₂a_{j(x)}^{−2} by (19), so the sizes of F that *The one-step problem.* uses keep their layered form, with a constant multiple of ε₁′ for ε₁.
      - The flow. Let Φ(W)A be the derivative at B = 0 of the solution map ℋ of ι's problem at the external configuration W, in the direction εA. At the real points of the disc, δ_t = Φ(W_t)A_c is the derivative of *The bound.*, which decomposes it into ℒ_t, 𝔊_t, and b_t. On the class Φ is analytic in W (Proposition 9). The flow is taken along critical backgrounds only, as at real points (derived, not reviewed). Let V_t(z) := e^{itεA_c(z)}Mc^B(z) and W_t(z) := ι(V_t(z)), with ι continued by Proposition 9 in the axial gauge of ι's problem: VAR p.307 l.1–9 transforms the Landau-gauge function back to the axial gauge by a transformation analytic in ℋ, and the result "is an analytic function of V′ … and for V′ with values in G it coincides with the minimal configuration". The data is V′V₀ with V₀ the real data of c^B_0 and |V′ − 1| ≤ C_Hε₁′ + M_c, since ε|A_c(z)| ≤ (ε/a_{j(x)})M_c ≤ M_c, so W_t(z) is defined and analytic in (t, z) on [0, 1] × {|z| < 25} under the added smallness C_Hε₁′ + M_c ≤ a_V, a_V the threshold of VAR's domain (172), which (T-ι) and (T-c) meet for γ small. Put c^B(z) := W_0(z), the continuation of c^B = ι(Mc^B) in the gauge of the real construction, and W_t(z) = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒(t,z)}c^B(z), with 𝐒 analytic and 𝐒(0, z) = 0; its relation to Proposition 9's Landau representative of run B's member is *The base of the flow.* below. At real z, 𝐒(t, z) is the field 𝐒^ι(t) of *The bound.* by construction, so the agreement with the real lift holds with no identity to prove. The critical-point equations of ι's problem and its constraints at W_t(z) are analytic in z and hold at real z, so they hold on the disc: every W_t(z) is critical at its own data. The derivative ∂_tW_t(z) and the expression of *The bound.*, δ_t = ℒ_tA_c − 𝔊_t(D*Dℒ_tA_c + O(|F||ℒ_tA_c|)) with its gauge fixing, are both analytic in z wherever W_t(z) lies in the class, since ℒ_t, (T^{pl})^{−1}, and 𝔊_t are analytic there (PBG Theorem 3.4, VAR p.309 l.1–4), and they agree at real z. Fix r < 25 and let T* be the supremum of the t ≤ 1 with ‖𝐒(s, z)‖ < 2𝔯 for all s ≤ t and |z| ≤ r; T* > 0, since 𝐒(0, ·) = 0 and 𝐒 is continuous on the compact set [0, 1] × {|z| ≤ r}. For s < T*, W_s(z) = W(𝐒(s, z), z) lies in the class at every |z| ≤ r by *The lifted backgrounds in the class.*, so the two sides above are analytic on the connected disc |z| < r and agree on its real segment, hence on the disc by the identity theorem. There the field strength of W_s(z) keeps the layered sizes of *The one-step problem.* with a constant multiple of ε₁′, and the kernels of ℒ_s and 𝔊_s keep the pointwise bounds of *The kernels.* with twice the constants (PBG (3.42) and (3.44) are part of Theorem 3.1, so Theorem 3.4 carries them to the class; 𝔊 by VAR p.309 l.1–4; (T^{pl})^{−1} by the Neumann series). So the argument of *The weights.* and *The bound.* applies at W_s(z), with no non-critical background, since every W_s(z) is critical. It bounds δ_s(z) at x by 2C_δ(ε₀)Ŵ_c(x), with Ŵ_c the weight of *The weights.* for the input 2M_c in place of d̄, and the factor 2 covering ℒ on the class. For y in the layer j(x) − m, (a_{j(x)}/a_{j(y)})² = L^{2m} ≤ L^{7m/3}, so (F-W) gives Ŵ_c ≤ 2L^{7/3}M_c, as for d̄, and ‖δ_s(z)‖ ≤ ½𝔯. With the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff factor β of *The bound.*, which is ≤ 2 for ‖𝐒‖ ≤ 2𝔯 and γ small, ‖∂_s𝐒(s, z)‖ ≤ 𝔯, so ‖𝐒(t, z)‖ ≤ T*𝔯 ≤ 𝔯 for t < T* and |z| ≤ r. If T* < 1, uniform continuity of 𝐒 on the compact set gives ‖𝐒‖ < 2𝔯 on [0, T* + τ] × {|z| ≤ r} for some τ > 0, against the definition of T*. So T* = 1, and ‖𝐒(1, z)‖ ≤ 𝔯 for |z| ≤ r, for every r < 25: the solution 𝐒^ι(t, z) := 𝐒(t, z) is analytic on the disc with ‖𝐒^ι(1, z)‖ ≤ 𝔯 there, and it is the real construction at real z. Let 𝐒^ι_c(z) := 𝐒^ι(1, z) and ιc^A(z) := W(𝐒^ι_c(z), z). The critical-point equations of ι's problem at the data e^{itεA_c(z)}Ũ_c(z) hold at real z and are analytic in z, so they hold on the disc.
      - The tail at complex points. ιc^A(z) lies in the class, so the solution map ℋ of the Y^∼-problem at the background ιc^A(z) is analytic, with (190) (Proposition 9), and ι_Yc^A(z) := exp(i(ε/L)T_Y(z))ιc^A(z), T_Y(z) := ℋ(B_Y(z)), is analytic on the disc; at real z this is the identity of *The tail at the chain.* The computation there holds at z with |F(ιc^A(z))| ≤ C^c_Fε₁′ε^{−2}, C^c_F := C_F + 8Lα′/ε₁′, since the curl of A′ is ≤ 2|∇A′| and the quadratic terms are below the linear ones for γ small, and with the block-axial gauge of ιc^A(z), whose transformation has a complex part within a factor 2 of isometric in Ad. So the tail is ≤ C^c_tε₁′e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} in the units of every layer, C^c_t := 2C_tC^c_F/C_F. The data of the Y^∼-problem differs from that of its background on the collar by e^{iB_Y(z)}, with |e^{iB_Y(z)} − 1| ≤ 2C_LC^c_Fε₁′, so Proposition 9 there needs VAR's thresholds of (T-c) with 2C_LC^c_F for C_H.
      - The bound. By the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula ι_Yc^A(z) = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c(z)}c^B(z) on Y, with 𝐒_c(z) analytic on the disc and 𝐒_c − 𝐒^ι_c ≤ 2C^c_tε₁′e^{−½δ₀M₁L^k} in the units of every layer, as at real points. So ‖𝐒_c(z)‖ ≤ 𝔐_S := 𝔯 + 4C^c_tε₁′ on the disc, and 𝔐_S ≤ 1 for γ small. At the real points s of the disc, which are regular with 2ε₁′, 𝐒_c(s) is, given the identity at real z of *The flow.*, the field of *The Fine Side at the Multiscale Chain.* for the real representative c^B(s), so a_{j(x)}|𝐒_c(s)(x)| and a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐒_c(s)(x)| are ≤ 2σ_c′(x) at every lattice point x, with σ_c′(x) the bound σ_c(x) of (ii) with ε₁′ for ε₁. For a lattice point x and a complex-linear functional ℓ of norm 1, the Lemma (Two Constants) applies to ℓ(a_{j(x)}𝐒_c(z)(x)) and to ℓ(a_{j(x)}²∇𝐒_c(z)(x)), with 𝔪 = 2σ_c′(x) and 𝔐 = 1, giving (2σ_c′(x))^{15/16} ≤ 2σ_c′(x)^{15/16} for a_{j(x)}|𝐒_c(i)(x)| and for a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐒_c(i)(x)| at (𝐔, 𝐉), and 𝔰(x) := 8σ_c′(x)^{15/16}, the sum of the two bounds doubled. The moves of *The room at real points.* then hold at (𝐔, 𝐉) with 𝔰(x) for σ_c(x), the factor 2 covering the commutator of e^{it(ε/L)𝐒_c} with e^{iξA′}, and the plaquettes and U_{p,X} move through PBG (3.133) (p.422), and J_{p,X} = 𝐐*λ by Cauchy's estimate on its analytic dependence on the complex data, as in *The multipliers*, on a domain of analyticity that contains the moved data.
      - The lift condition at complex points. With 𝔰 for σ_c, the moves of *The room at real points.* keep every condition of run B's class at the thresholds α̃′ along c(t) for |t| < 1/r(Y), from the room of *The Room.*, and 1/r(Y) ≥ 2 for n ≥ n₀′ (*The Ratio.*). So the lifts ι_Yc^A = c(1) satisfy run B's configuration conditions at (𝐔, 𝐉), at U^0_k and U_k from *The Room.*, for n ≥ n₀′. Its three further inputs at complex points are the following (derived, not reviewed).
        - The representative (derived, not reviewed). *The Room.* certifies Bałaban's extension U^0_k(𝐔), while c^B(i) is Proposition 9's representative in the Landau gauge relative to c_0, and the class conditions are not invariant under complex gauge transformations at thresholds α ≪ ε₁′. The two are related by a small gauge transformation, as follows.
          - The same function. At s = 1 the representation (1.61) is the identity U^0_k = U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), M^·(U^0_k)) (LFR II p.373), so Bałaban's extended U^0_k along the joint line is the minimiser function at the line's data, continued analytically in z. VAR's axial-gauge function U(V′V₀) (VAR p.307 l.1–9, "an analytic function of V′ … for V′ with values in G it coincides with the minimal configuration") is analytic in z along the same data and agrees with it at real z, where both are the real minimiser in the axial gauge Ax_k(𝔅_k, U₀). So they agree on the disc by the identity theorem.
          - The transformation. Proposition 9's representative is c^B(z) = U(V′V₀)^{u(z)}, with u the transformation of VAR Proposition 2 (p.281 l.1–3), R̄₀u^j = 1 on Λ_j, continued analytically (p.307 l.1–3). U(V′V₀) and U(V₀) = c_0 both satisfy the axial conditions relative to U₀, so on a block B^j(y) of the layer j u is fixed by the transports of e^{iη𝐇_c} along the axial contours from y, each of at most dL^j bonds of the η-lattice, and by the normalisation R̄₀u^j = 1. By VAR (19) η|𝐇_c| ≤ ε₂L^{−j}, so |log u| ≤ 4c_Gdε₂ for ε₂ small, of order ε₁′ with ε₂ ≤ 8B₅ε₁′ (*The members.*). Only this bound is used: the gradient of u does not enter the covariant quantities, since plaquettes, currents, averages, and covariant derivatives transform pointwise, ∇_{W^u}(Ad(u)S)(x) = Ad(u(x))(∇_WS)(x) and J(W^u) = Ad(u)J(W). The A′ conditions of the decomposition 𝐔 = U′U are not G^c-covariant: a complex u changes A′ by the gradient of Im log u. So the transport acts on the line's moves, with the G^c-invariance of the terms (RG I (1.19)); it does not place c^B(i) itself in the space. (u is not smooth on the scale of the blocks: the axial contours are hierarchical (REG (1.19), p.079 l.5–10), and a_j|∇log u| is only O(d²)L^jε₂.)
          - The base of the flow. Three representatives of run B's member occur: W_0(z), the base of the flow, the continuation of ι(Mc^B) in the gauge of the real construction; Proposition 9's Landau representative U(V′V₀)^{u(z)}; and U^0_k(𝐔), the base of *The Room.* The second and the third are related by u above. The first is ι applied to the averages of the second: by (181), ι(M(W^u)) = ι(MW)^{ū}, with ū the block-constant transformation given by u at the block centres (VAR p.307 l.10–20), so |log ū| ≤ sup|log u| ≤ 4c_Gdε₂. So W_0(i) and U^0_k(𝐔) differ by a G^c-valued gauge transformation with |log| ≤ 8c_Gdε₂, and the transport below covers it with the factor ‖Ad‖ ≤ 1 + O(c_Gdε₂).
          - The transport. The continued V^B(Y) is invariant under complex gauge transformations in the neighbourhood of G where the identity (181) extends (VAR p.307 l.10–20), so the joint line and the current line are transported by u^{−1}, with Ad(u^{±1}) within 1 + O(c_Gdε₂) of isometric, and J(c^B(i)) = Ad(u)J(U^0_k(𝐔)), since J(W^u) = Ad(u)J(W). The room of *The Room.* is at least ¼ of each threshold, and the transport changes each conditioned quantity pointwise by a factor 1 + O(c_Gdε₂), within that room for γ small.
        - Small n (derived, not reviewed). For n < n₀′, 𝔰/α_m is not small, so the lift is not used there. The split into (i)–(iii) is a decomposition of the run difference V^A(Y) − V^B(Y) at the members, and for these finitely many n the difference is bounded as a whole by the triangle inequality: run A's V(Y) at its member (𝐔, 𝐉) of the norm class and run B's at its own member c^B(i) = U^0_k(𝐔)^u are each within Bałaban's bound per point, run A's by (1.65) and p.375 l.29–35, run B's by *The Room.* at U^0_k(𝐔) and the transport by u of *The representative.* So the difference is at most twice Bałaban's bound per point, which is ≤ C_hθ̃^n with C_h enlarged by 2θ̃^{−n₀′}, as the real fallback absorbs θ̃^{−n₀}. n₀′ depends on M, ḡ, β₀, p₀, c_ρ, L, r, ε₀, j(C), β, β′, C_H, the constants of n_R, and Bałaban's constants only (*The Ratio.*, n_H of (iii), and n_R of (δ), *The factor.*), so the enlargement is a constant of these. n₀′ is also taken ≥ max(n₀″, n₀‴) + 1 (*The inputs at the composites.*, *The composites over 𝒮_z.*), so that the composites of (iv) at layers below those thresholds occur only in the histories of this bullet; this adds the constants of those two counts (c₂, B₃, C″_∂, and C(ε₀) of σ_c) to the list above.
        - The per-term lift ι_X (derived, not reviewed). ι_Xc^A(z) is built as ι_Yc^A(z) above, with X^∼ for Y^∼: the flow of *The flow.* is the same global ι, and the tail at complex points is ≤ C^c_tε₁′e^{−½δ₀M₁L^j} in the units a_j of the generation j, by *The tail at complex points.* with L^j for L^k, as at real points (*The lift condition of (i).*). So the move of the pair at x in the units a_j is ≤ L^{j−m}𝔰(x) + C^c_tε₁′e^{−½δ₀M₁L^j}, the real move of *The lift condition of (i).* with 𝔰 for σ_c and C^c_tε₁′ for C_tε₁. Since r_{0,j} ≥ r_{0,m}L^{j−m}, the first part relative to the radius r_{0,j} is ≤ 𝔰(x)/r_{0,m} ≤ r(Y) ≤ ½ for n ≥ n₀′ (*The Ratio.*), and the tail enters as at real points with C^c_t ≤ 2C_tC^c_F/C_F. So the lift condition of (i) holds at (𝐔, 𝐉) for n ≥ n₀′, and for n < n₀′ the difference is bounded as a whole (*Small n.*).
        - The other members (derived, not reviewed). U″_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_1 = U_{1,2} on Z^c are reached from U_k = (𝐔, 𝐉) and from U^0_k(𝐔) through the four links (1.51), (1.54), (1.57), and (1.58), which hold on the disc as identities of analytic functions. The bound 26C_𝐇ε̂′ of *The disc.* on their fields does not place them below the thresholds: ε̂′ contains C(M)R_kC_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁), and the thresholds of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) are the class radii α_{·,m} themselves (LFR I p.190 l.18–p.191 l.30). The members are bounded instead through the complex factor of their data, at the point (𝐔, 𝐉) itself.
          - The data. Write 𝐔 = e^{iξA′}U as in CRE (2.39), U G-valued. Each member c is the minimiser of its VAR determining-set problem at data D = D′D₀, where D₀ is the datum of the same problem at U, with the same real integration variables V′ on Λ, B, and V″, and D′ := D D₀^{−1} carries A′ on the averages of 𝐔. An average over a block of the layer q runs over paths of at most O(1)L^qη/ξ bonds, so |log D′| ≤ O(1)L^qη·sup|A′| ≤ O(1)α_{1,q} at the blocks of the layer q, by (2.39). D₀ is real. Its plaquettes at the layer q are at most O(1) times those of U, ≤ O(1)α_{0,q} by (2.34), on the shared part (the argument of RG I (1.17), p.263 l.10–17), and those of the real integration variables, which are O(ε_k) by the characteristic functions (LFR I (1.75), as in (c)). The factor R_k of ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k comes only from Sect. F's gauge field on Z, |A₀| < O(1)M⁶R_kε_k (LFR II p.357 l.46–48), not from the plaquettes, so it does not enter the regularity of D₀.
          - The real part (derived, not reviewed). c₀, the minimiser at D₀, is the member evaluated at the real configuration U of the class, since D₀ is the datum of the same problem at U. It is bounded directly through its data, not through the links: the link fields at a general real U carry the angles of the data in Λ, of the size of the class radii times a constant of M, but the plaquettes of c₀ depend on the data only through their plaquettes. VAR Theorem 1 with a single regularity constant would give O(1)B₃max_qα_{0,q} at every layer, which exceeds α_{0,m} by up to (1+β₀)²(k − m + 1)^{1/2}. Its Sect. F gives the layered form, since its estimates are local.
            - The layered data. Let p_q be the bound of D₀'s plaquettes at the layer q in the units of that layer, in the form of VAR (7): p_q ≤ O(1)α_{0,q} + O(ε_k) by *The data.* above. By CRE (2.28), α_{0,q} = g_qC₀(log g_q^{−2})^{q₀} tends to 0 with γ, so the global ε₁ := max_qp_q satisfies VAR's ε₁ ≤ a₁ for γ small, and Theorem 1 (Sects. A–E) gives c₀ in the space (2) at the global ε₀ = O(1)C₁B₃ε₁ (VAR p.302 l.38–39, with C₁ = L³ for the regularity, l.39).
            - The local step. Sect. F proves the inequalities (2) at a plaquette or bond of a unit cube Δ₀ ⊂ B^j(Λ_j) through a big cube □ ⊃ Δ₀ of the size 2R₁M₁L^jη. Bałaban's "They are local" (VAR p.302 l.44) refers to the inequalities (2), which are proved cube by cube; the locality of the step's inputs is read from the proof of pp.300–304 and checked there: U_k′ with (2) on □̃ (p.300 l.39–40, (145)–(146), (151), (155)), REG's Theorem 2 on the pair U_k′, 1 on ℭ_k ⊂ □̃ ((152)), the critical equation (158) on ℭ_k, the data V₁′ on □_k only ((160)), and the uniform constant B₃ of (162). So its inputs are c₀'s bounds (2) on □̃, which lies in B^{j−1}(Λ_{j−1}) ∪ B^j(Λ_j) (p.300 l.37), and the data plaquettes on □_k, |V₁′(∂p) − 1| < ε₁ on its layer-j part and < 2L²ε₁ on its layer-(j−1) part (p.303 l.11–20). Its output is (2) on Δ₀ with max{B₃ε₁, ½ε₀} (p.304 l.40–41), with the conditions 36dL²B₁Mε₀ ≤ a₃, 40dL²B₁Mε₀ ≤ a₄, 9dL²Mε₀ ≤ c₁, and M′ε₀ ≤ a₅ on the input (p.302 l.20, p.301 l.50, p.302 l.37–38, (166)), all monotone in ε₀. The ε₁ and ε₀ of the step are used only through the data on □_k and the bounds on □̃. So the step holds with ε₁ replaced by p^{loc}_j := max(p_{j−1}, p_j) and ε₀ by the largest layer-scaled bound of c₀ on the layers j − 1 and j.
            - The iteration. Bałaban repeats the step with ½ε₀ until B₃ε₁ is reached (p.304 l.41–43). Run layer by layer, with e^{(0)}_j := ε₀ and e^{(n+1)}_j := max{B₃p^{loc}_j, ½max(e^{(n)}_{j−1}, e^{(n)}_j)}, the bounds only decrease, so the conditions of the step hold at every n. Induction on n gives e^{(n)}_j ≤ max(max_{0≤i<n}2^{−i}B₃p^{loc}_{j−i}, 2^{−n}ε₀), since each step reaches one layer finer and halves. The lattice is finite, so for n large the last term is below B₃min_qp_q. p^{loc}_{j−i} involves the layers j − i − 1 and j − i, finer than j or equal. For q < j, α_{0,q} ≤ (1+β₀)²(j − q)^{β₀}α_{0,j}, by CRE (2.6) for g and (2.7) for the logarithm in (2.28) (n = j, m = q there). With β₀ ≤ ½ and 2^{−i}(i + 1)^{1/2} ≤ 1, c₀'s plaquettes at the layer j are ≤ (1+β₀)²B₃·O(1)(α_{0,j} + ε_k) in the units of that layer.
            - The cube conditions. VAR's (9)–(10) on the cubes of the size 2M_VL^jη follow from (167) with ε₀ = B₃ε₁ (p.304 l.57–p.305 l.6), an estimate on the cube and its □̃, which lie in the layers j − 1 and j (p.279 l.1–4). Run with the layered bounds, they hold with B₃M_V·(1+β₀)²O(1)(α_{0,j} + ε_k). This gives LFR I's cube conditions (1.65), (1.67), and (1.69), |A|, |∇A| < BCMα_{0,m} with B = B₃ (LFR I p.191 l.17–25), at the thresholds once the radii of the norm class are below α̃′/C_mem with C_mem containing (1+β₀)²O(1).
            - The rest. Criticality gives only 𝔓*(c₀)J = 0 (VAR (170)–(171), (178)), not J = 0. The currents of c₀ are bounded by the second inequality of VAR (2), |D_U^{η*}∂U| < ε₀L^{−2j}(L^jη)^{−1}, which is part of the same output of Sect. F ((168): "|D*_{U₁}∂U₁| < 2ε′ on Δ₀"), so the layered rerun gives them the same layered bound (1+β₀)²B₃O(1)(α_{0,j} + ε_k), with the same payment off Z below. The composites U_{p,X}(M^·(c₀)) are real minimisers of their determining sets at the data of c₀, and the same layered rerun applies to them, with the plaquettes and currents of LFR I p.190 l.18–22. The O(ε_k) part of p_q sits only where the real integration variables are, on Λ, and ε_k/α_{0,m} is not bounded uniformly at the fine layers m ≪ k (CRE (2.6)). It is paid by the spatial reach of the iteration: each step reads only □̃, so a data plaquette at the ℓ¹ distance D·4dR₁M₁a_m, D in units of the reach of □̃ at the layer m, enters e^{(n)} only for n ≥ D, with the factor 2^{−D}. On □₀ ∩ Z^c and on X ⊂ Z^c, where the terms read the members, Λ lies at a distance ≥ RMa_{k₀+1} from the plaquettes read (*U_{k,Λ}, U_1, and U_{1,2}*), so D ≥ (RM/(4dR₁M₁))L^{k₀+1−m} ≥ k₀ + 1 − m under RM ≥ 4dR₁M₁, and 2^{−D}(k − m + 1)^{1/2} ≤ c(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}, paid there as in that item by q₀ > p₀. On □₀ ∩ Z no such distance is needed, since there only the layers m ≥ k₀ + 1 of U_1 occur, where (k − m + 1)^{1/2} ≤ N₀^{1/2} (*The cubes □₀ at Z.*, derived, not reviewed), paid as on the layers m ≥ k₀ + 1 in *U_{k,Λ}, U_1, and U_{1,2}*, the frozen layers included. So c₀ satisfies LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) at half the thresholds α̃′ on □₀ and on X ⊂ Z^c, for every real U of the class.
          - The complex part. By Proposition 9, c = (e^{iη𝐇(B′)}c₀)^{u^{−1}} with B′ := (1/i)log D′, and 𝐇 is analytic in B′ on the domain of (172) with 𝐇(0) = 0. Proposition 9 asks V₀ to satisfy (7) at some ε₁ and |V′ − 1| < C₁ε₁, |B′| < 2C₁ε₁ with C₁ absolute (VAR p.305 l.31–44). It is applied at ε₁^P := max(ε_D, 2e·sup|B′|/C₁) = O(1)(max_qα_{0,q} + max_qα_{1,q}) + O(ε_k): V₀ = D₀ satisfies (7) at ε_D ≤ ε₁^P a fortiori, |B′| ≤ C₁ε₁^P/(2e) < 2C₁ε₁^P and |e^{iB′} − 1| ≤ |B′|e^{|B′|} < C₁ε₁^P for |B′| ≤ 1, and ε₁^P ≤ a₁ with Proposition 9's other smallness conditions holds for γ small by CRE (2.28). The constants of (190) do not depend on ε₁^P, and the regularity of c₀ is not taken from Proposition 9 at ε₁^P but from *The real part.* above. Its functional derivative obeys (190) on that domain (VAR p.308 l.38–48): |(δ/δB_ν(y′))𝐇_μ(B, x)| ≤ O(1)(L^jη)^{−1}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}exp(−⅛δ₀d(y, y′)) for x ∈ Δ(y), y′ ∈ Λ_{j′}, and the same with (L^jη)^{−2} for the first differences. So 𝐇(B′)(x) = ∫₀¹ (δ𝐇/δB)(sB′)B′ ds is bounded, at a point x of the layer m, by O(1)(L^mη)^{−1}Σ_{q}e^{−(1/16)δ₀d(x, Λ_q)}O(1)α_{1,q}; the sum over y′ ∈ Λ_q against (L^{j′}η)^{−d} costs half the rate. For |q − m| ≤ 1, α_{1,q} ≤ (1+β₀)²α_{1,m} by CRE (2.6)–(2.7) applied to (2.28). For q ≥ m + 2 the layers m + 1, …, q − 1 lie between. R_j decreases in j (CRE (2.5), (2.9)), so no width R_{m+1} is claimed for them; each crossed layer is at least one M-cube of its own scale wide (LFR I p.179 l.22–24, "separated by one layer of M-cubes"; the joins are LM₁ wide), that is at least one unit in the units of d (PRT2 (2.46)), and gives the factor e^{−(1/16)δ₀}. So e^{−(1/16)δ₀d(x,Λ_q)} ≤ e^{−(1/16)δ₀(q−m−1)}, and the sum Σ_{t≥0}e^{−(1/16)δ₀t}(1+β₀)²(t + 2)^{1/2} against α_{1,q}/α_{1,m} ≤ (1+β₀)²(q−m)^{1/2} is a constant of δ₀. For q ≤ m − 2 the layers m − 1, …, q + 1 lie between, each at least one M-cube of its scale wide, so e^{−(1/16)δ₀d(x,Λ_q)} ≤ e^{−(1/16)δ₀(m−q−1)} absorbs α_{1,q}/α_{1,m} ≤ (1+β₀)²(m − q)^{β₀} in the same way. Hence a_m|𝐇(x)| + a_m²|∇𝐇(x)| ≤ C_mα_{1,m} at the layer m, with C_m a constant of d and L (the O(1) of (190), of the averages, and of the sum, which depends on δ₀(d, L)).
          - The conditions. In the gauge u, c^u = U′c₀ with c₀ G-valued and U′ = e^{iη𝐇}, so a G^c-gauge representative of c has LFR I's decomposition with A′ = 𝐇 and U = c₀. LFR I's spaces are invariant under G-valued gauge transformations only (p.191 l.32), so this places c in the space through the G^c-invariance of the terms (RG I (1.19)), which is how it is used.
            - Derived, not reviewed, for the members whose data on Λ are averages of 𝐔: the A′ conditions, |A′| and |∇_UA′| ≤ C_mα_{1,m} in the scale of the layer m, below a quarter of the α̃′_{1,m}-thresholds once the radii of the norm class are below α̃′/C_mem, C_mem := 4O(1)(1+β₀)²B₃(1 + C_m) a constant of d and L, one of the values whose maximum is λ_Q (*Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*); and the conditions at c₀ (*The real part.*), on □₀ and on X ⊂ Z^c. For U″_k and U_{k,Λ}, whose data on Λ are nonlinear in 𝐔, and for U_1, whose data on Z″_k ⊂ Λ are M^·(U_{k,Λ}), the A′ conditions come from the route about (𝐔, 𝐉) (*The complex members through (1.57)–(1.58) about 𝐔.*, derived, not reviewed), which does not use the complex factor log D′ of their data (*Not needed on the route about 𝐔* below).
            - *The complex members through (1.57)–(1.58) about 𝐔 (derived, not reviewed).* At complex points U_k is replaced by 𝐔 and the data V″, M^·(U_k), V_Λ^{(A)} by M^·(𝐔) and V_Λ^{(A)}(M^k(𝐔)) (LFR II p.374 l.37–41). (1.58) and (1.57) (p.371) then hold as identities of analytic functions: U_{k,Λ} = (exp iη𝐇_k(X)𝐔)^{u_{k,Λ}^{−1}}, X := −(1/i)log V_k′⌈_Λ, and U_1 = (exp iη𝐇_{𝐁₁}(Y)U_{k,Λ})^{u_1^{−1}}, Y := −(1/i)log M^·(U_{k,Λ})⌈_{Z″_k}. This is the base that Bałaban names: on (Λ^∼)^c the regularity of (1.63) is "almost the same" as that of U(𝐁_k(Y₃), M^·(𝐔)), "because of the localization of the argument, and the exponential decay" (p.374 l.42–p.375 l.4). The steps below make this quantitative on X ⊂ Z^c, where the first-stage terms read U_{k,Λ} and U_1, and on □₀ ∩ Z, where they read U_1.
              - The base. 𝐔 = U′U, U′ = e^{iξA′}, lies in the norm class, so |∂𝐔 − 1| and |∂U − 1| are below r_{0,m} times the scale factors of CRE (2.34), U satisfies the cube conditions (2.38), and A′ satisfies (2.39) at r_{1,m} (CRE p.261). These are first-order conditions: 𝐔 satisfies PBG (3.35) and (3.37), but not the second-order conditions (3.36) and (3.38), which the class does not impose. The second-order derivative of a configuration enters the operators and the action only through its current (RG I p.261 l.35–42: "it appears only in the functions J_j … They appear in expansions of the action, and in expressions defining propagators"), and Bałaban replaces it by the variable 𝐉, "the first [variable] satisfying milder regularity conditions involving first order derivatives at most" (l.42). PBG's (3.38) serves to control the current of the complex configuration: (3.37)–(3.38) give (3.35)–(3.36) at O(1)(α₀ + α₁) (PBG p.396 l.51 – p.397 l.2). In VAR's field the current enters in two places: through Δ₁ = Δ_π − Δ_π^{(2)} (VAR (79), p.290), with ⟨A, Δ^{(2)}A⟩ = 2⟨HC^{(2)}(A), J⟩ linear in H*J (PBG (3.134)–(3.136)), hence through G₁, 𝔊, and H₁; and through V(A′) = −⟨HD₃(A′), J⟩ + … (VAR (80)). LFR II introduces (𝐔, 𝐉) "in the usual way into the localized function 𝐇_k" (p.374 l.37–39), so at complex points J is 𝐉 in both places, and at U_{k,Λ} it is U_{k,Λ}'s current entry (*The current entries.* below). PBG uses (3.36) in Theorem 3.12 through |H*J| ≤ O(1)Mα₀(L^jη)^{−3} in Δ^{(2)} (p.422 l.47–51), which at 𝐉 follows from CRE (2.36) and the decay of H's kernel. So, by Bałaban's statement, the operators of 𝐇_k and 𝐇_{𝐁₁} at (𝐔, 𝐉), and at U_{k,Λ} with its current entry, satisfy the bounds of PBG Theorems 3.4 and 3.12–3.13 and VAR p.309 l.1–4, and VAR (190) holds there (Bałaban's claim, accepted).
              - The data angles. At complex points |X| ≤ C_Λ(α̃_{0,k} + α̃_{1,k}) on Λ (the W-δ note's §12, *The absolute bound at complex points.*, from LFR II p.358–359 with ε_k replaced by O(α_{0,k} + α_{1,k})), and |Y| ≤ C_Y(α̃_{0,k} + α̃_{1,k}) on Z″_k ⊂ Λ (*The data X and X_s.* there), both derived, not reviewed, with C_Λ and C_Y constants of d, L, and M. Both bounds are linear in the radii of the class, so at the class radii r they read C_Λ(r_{0,k} + r_{1,k}) and C_Y(r_{0,k} + r_{1,k}). These are of the α₁ scale, not of the scale ε_k of the real case, so q₀ > p₀ does not pay them; the decay from Λ does.
              - The steps. As in *The plaquettes.* (the first inequality of LFR I (1.46) and VAR (190)), each step multiplies the plaquette bound by at most 2 and adds at most 9B_Ve^{−δ_Vd(p,Λ)}δ^c(L^{k−m}η)² at a plaquette p of the layer m, δ^c := max(C_Λ, C_Y)(r_{0,k} + r_{1,k}), with δ_V = δ₀/16. The gauge factors u_{k,Λ} and u_1 are G^c-valued with imaginary parts of the size of 𝐇_k(X) and 𝐇_{𝐁₁}(Y), so their Ad changes |·(∂p) − 1| by a factor ≤ 2 for γ small. The A′ of the decomposition c^u = e^{iηA″}U, with U the real part of 𝐔, is A″ = A′ + 𝐇 + commutators, by the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula, so |A″| and |∇_UA″| exceed the (2.39) bounds of A′ by at most O(1)B_Ve^{−δ_Vd(p,Λ)}δ^c in the scale of the layer m. The real part and its cube conditions are those of 𝐔.
              - The decay. Λ = (Ω^{∼4}_{k₀+1})^c ∩ Z ⊂ Ω^c_{k₀+1} ∩ Z (the W-δ note's §12, *The representation with the gain.*), while the points of Z^c next to Z lie in Λ_k ⊂ Ω_k ⊂ Ω_{k₀+2}, since Z is a component of Λ_k^c (CRE p.255 l.25) and k₀ + 2 ≤ k (N₀ ≥ 2, *The cubes □₀ at Z.*). A path from a point p of X ⊂ Z^c at the layer m to Λ therefore crosses the layers m + 1, …, k − 1, each at least one M-cube of its own scale wide (LFR I p.179 l.22–24), and it crosses the layer k₀ + 1 from Ω_{k₀+2} to Ω^c_{k₀+1}, a length ≥ dist(Ω_{k₀+2}, Ω^c_{k₀+1}) ≥ 8MR_{k₀+2}a_{k₀+2} (CRE (3.5), as in *The cubes □₀ at Z.*) at the spacing a_{k₀+1}, that is at least 8LMR_{k₀+2}/M₁ ≥ 8LMR_k/M₁ M₁-cubes of that scale, since R_j decreases in j (CRE (2.5), (2.9)). So d(p, Λ) ≥ max(k − m − 1, 8LMR_k/M₁) ≥ ½(k − m − 1) + 2MR_k/M₁ + 2M/M₁, and for every rate δ ≤ δ_V, e^{−δd(p,Λ)} ≤ e^{−½δ(k−m−1)}e^{−δMR_k/M₁}e^{−δM/M₁}. Against the threshold α̃′_{0,m} ≥ r_{0,m}, r_{0,k} + r_{1,k} ≤ (1 + λ_R)(1 + β₀)²(k − m + 1)^{1/2}r_{0,m} (CRE (2.6)–(2.7) applied to (2.28), with C₁ = λ_RC₀), and (k − m + 1)^{1/2}e^{−½δ_D(k−m−1)} ≤ c_V′ := sup_{t≥0}(t + 2)^{1/2}e^{−½δ_Dt}, where δ_D := ½min(δ″, δ_V) ≤ ½δ_V is the smallest rate used (*The size of the data differences.*). On the frozen layers m ≥ k₀ + 1 of (1.66) the conversion to the frozen threshold costs at most L^{3(N₀−1)}, for the plaquettes and for the currents (*U_{k,Λ}, U_1, and U_{1,2}*, *The plaquettes.*), and L^{N₀−1} ≤ (L + 1)(N₀ + 1)^{β₀}R_k (LFR I p.192 l.18–19) gives, with β₀ ≤ ½, N₀ + 1 ≤ C_L(1 + log R_k), C_L a constant of L. So, with M ≥ M₁ and R_k ≥ 1, L^{3(N₀−1)}(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}e^{−δ_DMR_k/M₁} ≤ c_Z := sup_{R≥1}(L + 1)³(C_L(1 + log R))^{1/2+3β₀}R³e^{−δ_DR}, a constant of δ_D and L. The rate δ_D covers the composites (rate ½δ_V, *The current entries.*, U_1 and the composites) and the entry differences (rate δ_D). So the added terms, of the plaquettes, the A′ entries, and the currents, and the α₁-scale part of the entry differences, are at most ⅛ of the thresholds, the frozen ones included, once
                (D-Λ) 72c_DB_Vc_V′c_Z(1 + λ_R)(1 + β₀)²max(C_Λ, C_Y)e^{−δ_DM/M₁} ≤ 1,
                with c_D ≥ 1 covering the O(1) of the current entries' bound and C_E/B_V of *The size of the data differences.*, a lower bound on M/M₁ after M₁ and δ_V, since C_Λ, C_Y, and λ_R grow polynomially in M (C_Λ = O(1)γ₀^{−1}d(100M)⁵B₃², λ_R = K_RM + 1). It is imposed with the other lower bounds on M, before γ.
              - The conclusion. The base is at the radii r = α̃′/C_mem, and the two steps with their gauge factors multiply its bounds by at most 16. With C_mem enlarged to at least 64 where it is smaller (it is one of the values whose maximum is λ_Q), U_{k,Λ} and U_1 satisfy at complex points, on X ⊂ Z^c, the plaquette conditions of (i) of LFR I p.190 for 𝕌, the conditions on the real part U, and the A′ conditions, below half the thresholds. The member's U_{p,X}(M^·(c)) plaquette conditions follow in the same way about the base U_{p,X}(M^·(𝐔)) of CRE (2.35), the argument being the averages of the field of (1.57)–(1.58), with the same decay. U″_k is read through (1.51) about U^0_k, whose conditions are Bałaban's (1.65) (accepted, p.375 l.14–21), taken at the thresholds of the class radii by *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, with the factor 𝐇″_k(s)(g_kB) bounded as in *The lift half at W.* (*The factor.*), at most C_fac(N₀ + 1)^{1/2}ε_m in the units of the layer m, which is o(1) times α_{0,m} since q₀ > p₀. This gives its plaquette, A′, and current conditions at complex points within a factor 2 of those of U^0_k, the current through the Laplace-type entries D*D and Δ of the factor, as in *The current entries.* below, with RG II's bounds of the linear operators at s in all norms (p.5 l.18–19). The value μ_r of μ used here, the ratio of Bałaban's radii α to the class radii r, is covered by the argument of *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds* as are the seven values listed there, and μ_r joins λ_Q.

The current entries of U_{k,Λ}, U_1, and the U_{p,X}(M^·(c)) at complex points are the next item.

                - The field. At a minimal configuration U_k the current satisfies 𝔓*(U_k)J = 0 (VAR (170)–(171), p.305), not J = 0, so the expansion about U_k has no source term: 𝐇 = 𝒜₁ + H₁B − HD(𝒜₁ + H₁B) with 𝒜₁ + 𝔊(δV/δA′)(𝒜₁ + H₁B) = 0 (VAR (174)–(175)), or the form (179)–(180). The form A₁ = −𝔊(J + δV/δA′) of VAR (111)–(112) is the expansion about a general U₀; at the real members it agrees with (175), since 𝔊J = G₁𝔓*J = 0 there, and the source-free form is the one VAR extends, with 𝐇(0) = 0. The current of U_k still enters these equations, through Δ₁ (hence G₁, 𝔊, and H₁), through V (VAR (79)–(80)), and through Δ^{(2)} in (179)–(180) (*The base.* above). The functions and operators of the equations extend to arbitrary regular configurations, and the equations then determine an analytic 𝐇 with the same properties (VAR p.308 l.49 – p.309 l.4). The regularity it needs is that of *The base.*

VAR (190) bounds D*_{U_k}D_{U_k} and Δ_{U_k} of the kernel δ𝐇/δB(y′) at x ∈ Δ(y) by O(1)(L^jη)^{−3}(L^{j′}η)^{−d}e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)/8}, and these bounds hold at the regular complex backgrounds (p.309 l.2–4), here at (𝐔, 𝐉) by *The base.* With 𝐇(X) = ∫₀¹(δ𝐇/δB)(tX)X dt, X supported in Λ, and PRT2's Lemma 2.1 (cited as [4] in PBG), |D*D𝐇_k(X)| and |Δ𝐇_k(X)| at a bond p of the layer m are at most O(1)B_Ve^{−δ_Vd(p,Λ)}δ^c(L^mη)^{−3}: the decay of the plaquette step of *The steps.*, at the scale (L^mη)^{−3} of the current in place of the curvature's (L^mη)^{−2}, since δ_V = δ₀/16 ≤ δ₀/8.

This is an algebraic identity at any background, the lattice form of D*_{A+a}F_{A+a} = D*_AF_A + D*_AD_Aa + [a ⌟ F_A] + D*_A[a ∧ a] + [a ⌟ D_Aa] + O(a³). The member's current entry is introduced in the usual way (RG I (1.8)–(1.9)): J(U_k) is replaced by 𝐉, here as in the field 𝐇_k(X; 𝐔, 𝐉), and the entry R(u^{−1})[𝐉 + D*D𝐇 + R_J] is analytic on the class and equals J(c) at the real members. At p of the layer m its size is at most |𝐉| + O(1)B_Ve^{−δ_Vd(p,Λ)}δ^c(L^mη)^{−3}, the terms of R_J being of the size of products of the plaquette bounds with the decaying bounds of 𝐇 and ∇𝐇. The near domains at ∂Y_i are the near-domain item.

    - *The Ratio.* 𝔰(x)/α_m ≤ 8C(ε₀)^{15/16}C_U(M)^{15/16}(a_k/ε)^{15ε₀/16}(ε/a_{min(m,n)})^{5(1−ε₀)/16}(ε₁′/α_m)ε₁′^{−1/16}. As in *At real points the ratio is small.*, ε₁′/α_m is at most C(M)(k − m + 1)^{1/2} times a power of log g_k^{−2}, by (2.4)–(2.6) and (2.28). Since ε₁′ ≥ ε_k ≥ g_k and g_k^{−2} ≤ ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C (BETA_FUNCTION_WILSON_LOOPS.md Lemma 2.1(i)), ε₁′^{−1/16} ≤ (ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C)^{1/32}. With k < 8n + j(C), which is k < 8n up to the stopping scale and k ≤ K = k_s + j(C) < 8n + j(C) beyond it, and K − k < 8n + j(C), (a_k/ε)^{15ε₀/16} = L^{15kε₀/16} ≤ L^{15j(C)ε₀/16}L^{15nε₀/2}, so the power of L^{−n} is 5(1 − ε₀)/16 − 15ε₀/2 = 5(1 − 25ε₀)/16. The domains that meet a layer m < n are treated as at real points, with ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} per point if they are not deep.
  - At real points the ratio is small. α_m ≥ ε_m, since q₀, q₁ > p₀ and C₀, C₁ ≥ 4B₃A₀ (CRE (2.4), (2.28)), choices that (2.28) allows because it bounds q₀, q₁, C₀, and C₁ below only (p.259 l.48–50). By (2.6) (p.255 l.42–50), g_k ≤ (1 + β₀)(k − m + 1)^{1/2}g_m and g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k, so log g_m^{−2} ≥ ½log g_k^{−2} for g small and ε_k ≤ 2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)(k − m + 1)^{1/2}ε_m. Hence, with σ_c ≤ C(ε₀)(a_k/ε)^{ε₀}d̄ of (ii), σ_c(x)/ϱ(x) ≤ c_ρ^{−1}C(ε₀)(a_k/ε)^{ε₀}C_U(M)C(M)2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)R_k(k − m + 1)^{1/2}(ε/a_{min(m,n)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}. This takes ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k of (c). Where the ε₁ of §1 is the larger, ε₁/ε_k is at most C(1 + β₀)(k_s − k + 1)^{1/2}(1 + A₁/A₀) instead, as in the result for W_ext, polynomial in n since k_s − k < 7n, and it is absorbed with the other polynomial factors below. With k < 8n + j(C) (*The Ratio.* above), (a_k/ε)^{ε₀} = L^{kε₀} ≤ L^{j(C)ε₀}L^{8nε₀}, so the power of L^{−n} is (1 − ε₀)/3 − 8ε₀ = (1 − 25ε₀)/3. R_k ≤ L(log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)), the factor (k − m + 1)^{1/2} ≤ (1 + j(C))^{1/2}(8n + 1)^{1/2}, and the factor 1 + C_Fk^{5/2}/α of the additive term of §4(d), *The result*, are absorbed by L^{−(1−97ε₀)n/24}. For the domains at the layers ≥ n the variation of (ii), 2r(Y) times the bound of V(Y), is therefore ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point, since L^{−(1−25ε₀)n/3} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−97ε₀)n/12} for θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4}, with 1 − 97ε₀ > 0 under ε₀ < 1/125, once r(Y) ≤ ½, which holds for n ≥ n₀, with n₀ depending on M, ḡ, β₀, p₀, c_ρ, L, r, ε₀, j(C), and Bałaban's constants; j(C) enters since log g_k^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) (BETA_FUNCTION_WILSON_LOOPS.md Lemma 2.1(i)) and K − k < 8n + j(C), and no dependence on k, K, ε, or the volume arises.
