A domain that meets a layer m < n has the variation ≤ C_hθ̃^m ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} per point if it is not deep, and is bounded in (e), *Deep V(Y) in a Young Polymer*, if it is. At real points the age bound of (i) at run A's configurations is Bałaban's at the regular configurations. The bound rests on the inputs of (ii) and, for the per-Y split, on (iii), derived, not reviewed. At the complex points of the class the variation rests also on the room at complex points.
  - The boundary terms generated at this step (p.372 l.31–33) are such re-summed terms, so (i)–(ii) apply to them at their generation.
- *The complex class.* (LF-2) needs the comparisons of (b)–(d) at the complex configurations of the inductive class, since the Cauchy note's §8.6 bounds class norms. For the minimisers, their lifts, the actions, and the forms, they follow from the real comparisons through the lemma below (derived). The actions and the forms are gauge invariant. For the minimisers and their lifts, which depend on the gauge, the lemma is applied to the representative given by the fixed point of VAR's contraction at the reference Ũ(z), which is analytic in z and at real z is the representative of the real bounds. The old terms of V(Y) are treated in "The room at complex points" above.
  - *Lemma (Two Constants).* Let 1 < 𝔯 < 𝔯₁, let 𝔣 be analytic on |z| < 𝔯₁ with |𝔣| ≤ 𝔐 there, and let |𝔣| ≤ 𝔪 on the real segment (−𝔯, 𝔯), with 0 < 𝔪 ≤ 𝔐. Then |𝔣(z)| ≤ 𝔪^{𝔢(z)}𝔐^{1−𝔢(z)} for |z| < 𝔯, with 𝔢(z) := 1 − (2/π)|arg((𝔯 + z)/(𝔯 − z))|. For 𝔯 := 24 and 𝔯₁ := 25, |𝔣(z)| ≤ 𝔪^{15/16}𝔐^{1/16} for |z| ≤ 1.
  - *Proof.* On the upper half-disc D₊ := {|z| < 𝔯, Im z > 0}, 𝔴 := (𝔯 + z)/(𝔯 − z) maps D₊ onto the open first quadrant, the segment onto the positive real axis, and the semicircle onto the positive imaginary axis. So 𝔥 := 1 + (2i/π)Log 𝔴 is analytic on D₊, and Re 𝔥 = 𝔢 is 1 on the segment and 0 on the semicircle. Let 𝔉 := 𝔣𝔪^{−𝔥}𝔐^{𝔥−1}. Then |𝔉| = |𝔣|𝔪^{−𝔢}𝔐^{𝔢−1} is bounded on D₊, continuous on its closure except at ±𝔯, and ≤ 1 on its boundary there. For η > 0 let 𝔉_η := 𝔉·((𝔯² − z²)/(4𝔯²))^η, with the principal power, since Re(𝔯² − z²) > 0 on D₊. Then |𝔉_η| ≤ 1 on the boundary and 𝔉_η → 0 at ±𝔯, so |𝔉_η| ≤ 1 on D₊ by the maximum modulus principle, and η → 0 gives |𝔣| ≤ 𝔪^𝔢𝔐^{1−𝔢} on D₊. On the lower half-disc the same follows for z ↦ conj(𝔣(z̄)), since 𝔢(z̄) = 𝔢(z), and on the segment it is the hypothesis. For |z| ≤ 1, |arg(𝔯 ± z)| ≤ arcsin(1/𝔯), so 𝔢(z) ≥ 1 − (4/π)arcsin(1/𝔯), which exceeds 15/16 at 𝔯 = 24, since (4/π)arcsin(1/24) < 0.054. Since 𝔪 ≤ 𝔐, 𝔪^𝔢𝔐^{1−𝔢} = 𝔐(𝔪/𝔐)^𝔢 ≤ 𝔪^{15/16}𝔐^{1/16}. For 𝔪 > 𝔐 the bound 𝔪^{15/16}𝔐^{1/16} ≥ 𝔐 holds trivially, so the Lemma applies with any real-segment bound 𝔪.
  - *The lines.* Let (𝐔, 𝐉) be in the class, with 𝐔 = e^{iξA′}U as in CRE (2.39), U G-valued, A′ = A′_R + iA′_I, and 𝐉 = 𝐉_R + i𝐉_I, the four fields real. Let 𝐔(z) := e^{iξ(A′_R + zA′_I)}U and 𝐉(z) := 𝐉_R + z𝐉_I. Then (𝐔(i), 𝐉(i)) = (𝐔, 𝐉), and the line is real for real z. Since ∇_U is a real operator, |A′_R| and |A′_I| are ≤ |A′|, and |∇_UA′_R| and |∇_UA′_I| are ≤ |∇_UA′|. Both runs are evaluated at the common data D(z) := M^·(𝐔(z)) at the shared scales, since Bałaban's extension replaces the variables V_k in U_0 by the averages M^k(𝐔) (LFR II p.358 l.27–29). 𝐔 lives on one run's lattice (CRE p.256 l.58), and only its averages at the shared scales enter, which are data of both runs. The integration variables (V′ on Λ, B, and V″) and the couplings are fixed and equal in both runs, with B real and |B| ≤ b_k, as in (b)–(d). The line 𝐉(z) plays no role in (b)–(d), whose minimisers carry their own currents, or J′ at the alignment, and whose forms sit at these minimisers; 𝐉(z) enters only the old terms of V(Y), in "The room at complex points". The class of the norms of (LF-2) is the multiscale class Ũ^c of CRE (2.39); Bałaban's extension uses the single-scale class U_k^c(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}).
  - *The real points.* For real z with |z| < 24, ξ|A′_R + zA′_I| ≤ 25ξ|A′| and ξ²|∇_U(A′_R + zA′_I)| ≤ 25ξ²|∇_UA′|, with the bounds of (2.39). With (2.34)–(2.37), the plaquettes, currents, and averages of the line at a point of the layer m are therefore ≤ C_c(α_{0,m} + α_{1,m}) times the scale factors of (2.34)–(2.37), with C_c := 60: the linear terms give at most 2·25, and the quadratic ones, which carry a further factor α_{1,m}, at most 10 for γ small. So the real points of the line are real configurations whose data are regular with ε₁′ := C(M)R_kε′_k, where ε′_k := ε_k + C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) + 25g_kb_k and α̂_i := max_{m≤k}α_{i,m}. The quantities of VAR's classes at these points, the plaquette and D*∂ conditions of VAR (2) and the plaquette condition (7) of the data (VAR p.278 l.3–8 and l.36), are local and gauge invariant, and they are at most ε₁″ := C(M)ε′_k, the data's by Proposition 2 of [4] as VAR uses it (p.278 l.46), with C(M) at least its constant; here the currents are those of the local minimisers of the data, bounded by CRE (2.37), and the plaquettes those of (2.34)–(2.35), which make VAR's space (6) non-empty, as Proposition 2 needs (CRE's (2.36) bounds the variable 𝐉, which VAR's classes do not read). The factor R_k of ε₁′ enters only through the representation exp iξA₀ on the domain Z after "a proper gauge transformation" of the construction of Sect. F [15] (LFR II p.357 l.46–48, |A₀|, |∇^ηA₀| < O(1)M⁶R_kε_k; [15] is VAR in LFR II's list, p.392, and VAR's Sect. F puts the configuration "in the generalized axial gauge" by a gauge transformation, p.300 l.38–40). The bound is used as printed: O(1)M⁶R_kε_k ≤ C(M)R_kε′_k for C(M) ≥ O(1)M⁶, and the line's own displacement, at most 25ξ|A′| and 25ξ²|∇_UA′| by the first sentence, adds at most C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) ≤ ε′_k ≤ R_kε′_k (R_k ≥ 1) to the logarithm, for γ small; no reading of what R_k measures enters. (b)–(d) read this representation through (T-Λ). Hence (b)–(d) hold there with ε₁′ in place of ε₁, under the following condition.
    - (T-c): the conditions of (T-Λ) hold with 26ε₁′ in place of ε₁, 26C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) ≤ min(α₁, α_J), with α₁ and α_J of the pair class, and the thresholds of VAR Sects. A–E hold for the pairs of VAR (172)–(175) (p.305) with ε₁′ for ε₁. There C₁ := C_H is a constant with |V′ − 1| < C_Hε₁′ for the data of the disc; for ι's problem the data deviation also contains M_c = (2C_U(M) + 5C_p)ε₁′, so there C₁ := C_H + 2C_U(M) + 5C_p, and B₅ and ε₂ of (T-ι) depend on M, which is fixed before γ: the line moves the data by ≤ 26ε₁′ and the chain's arguments are ≤ 26C_Ψε̂′ ≤ 52C_Ψε₁′, so C_H := 2(26 + 52C_Ψ) serves, by |e^{iX} − 1| ≤ 2|X| for |X| ≤ 1. With B₅ := 6B₁B₃C_H (173), ε₂ := 8B₅ε₁′ (below (175), p.305), and ε₃ := 4ε₂ (113) (p.294 l.42–45), the thresholds are 32B₅ε₁′ ≤ min(a₃, ½c₄, (18C₂B₀dc₁(½))^{−1}) (Proposition 4, p.292 l.50, and p.289 l.31–32) and 3B₀C_HB₃ε₁′ ≤ a₄ (Proposition 6, p.295 l.45–49), with Bałaban's absolute constants a₃, a₄, c₄, B₀, B₁, B₃, and C₂. Under them Proposition 9 (p.309 l.8–14) gives 𝐇 with (19)–(21) at ε₂ = B₅ε₁′.

    This follows the pattern of Bałaban's extension of p.358 l.27–31, whose bounds "hold with ε_k replaced by O(α_{0,k} + α_{1,k})". The line's real points carry up to 25 times the class bounds and lie outside his class U_k^c(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), so their regularity with ε₁′ is the derivation above, not his statement; (b)–(d) use only that regularity. (T-c) holds for γ small, uniformly in K. Indeed g(log g^{−2})^{q_i} increases for g small and g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k for m ≤ k (CRE (2.6)), so α̂_i ≤ (1 + β₀)C_ig_k(log g_k^{−2})^{q_i}, and ε₁′ is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}, up to constants. Every threshold of (T-c) bounds ε₁′ or α̂₀ + α̂₁ by a constant independent of k and K, so all of them hold for γ small.
  - *The disc.* For |z| < 25 the line moves A′ and ∇_UA′ by at most 26 times their class bounds. Under (T-c) the line therefore lies in the analyticity domains of VAR Sect. G, as in (c), "The remainder", and of the pair class 𝒟̄. So the minimisers, their lifts, the actions, and the forms of (b)–(d) are analytic in z there. Each run's per-point action density is ≤ C_actg_{j(x)}^{−2}ε₁′² there, with C_act := d²(52C₀)², by |F| ≤ 52C₀ε₁′/a_j². Each run's forms are ≤ c_4N_k per point, as on the alignment's disc.
  - *The results.* At the point (𝐔, 𝐉), z = i, the Lemma gives the following bounds per point of the history.
    - The actions. At the real points, the per-point densities d_y = [a_y(U^A) − a_y(Ũ)] + b_y(U^B) of (c), "The comparison", at each configuration of (b)–(d) with its alignment field, are ≤ C_y(M)g_{j(x)}^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁′². This follows from the first- and second-order bounds 4d²C₀ε₁′d̄′ and 4d²d̄′² of a block density under the alignment, d̄′ := C_U(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁′, and from |b_y| ≤ C_b(ε/a_n)ε₁′², with C_y(M) := 4d²(C₀ + C_U(M))C_U(M) + C_b. On the disc they are ≤ 2C_actg_{j(x)}^{−2}ε₁′². So on the class |d_y| ≤ C_dc(M)g_{j(x)}^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{5(1−ε₀)/16}ε₁′², with C_dc(M) := max(C_y(M), 2C_act).
    - The forms. At the real points they differ by ≤ r̄_nN_k, by *The result for the forms.* with ε₁′ ≤ 1. On the disc they differ by ≤ 2c_4N_k. So on the class they differ by ≤ max(C_F(M), 2c_4)(ε/a_n)^{5(1−ε₀)/16}N_k per point.
    - The couplings. Through the weights they enter linearly, as A(w^B − w^A, ·), so on the class they are bounded directly by the weight difference times the disc bound of a density. Through the data exp(ig_kB) they enter through a data derivative times |g^A_k − g^B_k||B|, which Cauchy's estimate on the data disc of (c), "The remainder", bounds on the class with ε₁′ for ε₁. Both bounds keep the form of the real ones, linear in the coupling differences.
    - The absorption. g_{j(x)}^{−2}ε₁′² and N_k carry the same factors as in the result of (c): linear in k and powers of log g_k^{−2}. Since 5/16 exceeds 1/4, θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4} and k < 8n give L^{−5(1−ε₀)n/16} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−ε₀)n/16}. These absorb the factors, so the changes on the class are ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point, with θ̃ unchanged.

    So the per-point bounds of (b) and (c) on the class, which the complex version of the Lemma of the Cauchy note's §8.6 needs for d, and the bounds of the four actions and of the form in 𝐂_k − W_ext, are derived on the class. The exception is the variation (ii) of V(Y) at the complex points.
- *The lift difference as a Taylor coefficient.* A bound of E without the Cauchy note's (10.6c), as the first Taylor coefficient of the alignment field along the data tg_kB, does not work, since the real bound of that field holds in the alignment's gauge, its first coefficient is computed in the Landau gauges of VAR Sect. G, and the first-order gauge mismatch between the two is what (10.6c) controls.

(e) *The Carried Boundary Terms (Derived; the R-Operation's Own Terms Derived, Not Reviewed).* In (e), k is the fluctuation step, and k_s := K − j(C) is the stopping scale of the Cauchy note's §8.2, the k of §1, which sets the thresholds k_s/8 and k_s/4.
- *The objects.* A boundary term 𝐁^{(i)} of CRE (2.40)–(2.42) is localised in X ∈ 𝐃_i with X ∩ Ω_i ≠ ∅ and X ∩ Z_i^∼ ≠ ∅, and is bounded by B₀exp(−κd_i(X)) on its class (p.261 l.47–66). After the step that creates it, it is a fixed function, evaluated at the later backgrounds: the form 𝐁^{(i)}(U_k, …) of (2.40), with 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) remaining in the action (3.25). The class norm of its two-run difference is b_i, the smallest number with |𝐁^{B,(i)}(X) − 𝐁^{A,(i)}(X)| ≤ b_iB₀exp(−κd_i(X)) on the class: the norm of the Cauchy note's §8.2 with B₀ in place of E₀. The class is the intersection of the two runs' classes Ũ^c_i(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), on which both runs' terms are analytic and bounded. The thresholds (2.28) carry each run's own g_i, and g^A_i and g^B_i differ relatively by ≤ Cg_i²|δ_i|, so the intersection contains either run's class with α̃₀ and α̃₁ scaled by 1 − C′g_i²|δ_i|, C′ a constant of q₀ and q₁. Here Z_i = Λ_i^c (p.255 l.25), and Γ_m = Ω_m^{(m)} ∖ Ω_{m+1}^{(m)} is the layer of the scale m (p.255 l.15–16). By (2.47) (p.263 l.50–p.264 l.8), the part 𝐁^{(i,m)} of the generation i on the layer Γ_m, m < i, carries the factor 2^{−(i−m)}, from exp(−d_i(X)). So the generations i ≥ m sum to ≤ 2B₁ per point of Γ_m, and their differences to ≤ 2B₁max_{i≥m}b_i. The generations i < m on Γ_m are not in (2.47); they carry the inward weight of "The weights of the ages" below.
- *Where they enter.* At a fluctuation step k, 𝐁_k enters CRE (3.26) only through its first expression: as the integrand 𝐁_k(U_k(…)) − 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) of the t-integral, and, multiplied by t, in the measure (3.27) of that integral (p.271 l.7–8, l.22). The output of that expression goes into 𝐁^{(k+1)} only (p.271 l.29–31, p.279 l.16). The s-integral and the logarithm in (3.26) are taken at t = 0 (p.271 l.14–15), so the 𝐁-terms do not enter the step's 𝐄- and 𝐑-outputs; 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) remains in the action as a frozen function. Two more expressions of the t-integrand go into the boundary terms only (p.277 l.10–17): the coupling term A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·), localised in Z_k, and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}), the expressions above expanded in the perturbative terms of (1.22), each with a localisation in S_{k+1}. At an R-operation, the old terms whose domains meet a component Z belong to the first part of (1.49), which is integrated with Z (LFR II p.369 l.28–30). The terms disjoint from Z are expanded around the new background U_k. There 𝐓(X, U_k) stays as a frozen function (taken at U_k, p.373 l.5–6), and the brackets 𝐓(X, exp iη𝐇″_k(g_kB)U^0_k) − 𝐓(X, U^0_k) of (1.59)–(1.60), with their analogues along the chain of (d), give "a term which is small, and satisfies the exponential bound (2.42) [III] in d_k(Y₀)". Bałaban takes these "together with other terms of the first part of the effective action" (p.372 l.27–33), so they too are integrated with Z. So the R-operation carries the frozen boundary terms disjoint from the components with the factor 1. The boundary terms that it creates itself are those of the preliminary integrations of LFR I §1, localised in X ∈ 𝔻_m, for some m ≥ j + 1, with X ⊂ Ω_{m+1}^c, X ∩ Ω_m ≠ ∅, and X ∩ (Z_{j+1} ∖ Z″_{j+1}) ≠ ∅ (LFR I (1.63), p.189 l.40–47), and bounded by C₀exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_m(X)) on the spaces Ũ_k^{(n)c} (LFR I (1.70), pp.190–191). They are 𝔹_k^{(n)} = Σ_{i≤n}ℂ_k^{(i)}, with ℂ_k^{(n+1)} the logarithm of the integral (1.60) plus the coupling term A(1/(g_k^{(n+1)})² − 1/(g_k^{(n)})², U_k^{(n+1)}) (LFR I (1.61)–(1.62), pp.188–189). Z_{j+1} is identified with Z_{j+1} ∩ Z (LFR I p.177 l.33–35), so these terms meet the components renormalised at the step. Where the integrations of a component end at some n, Bałaban leaves them "as a part of all boundary terms" (LFR I p.191 l.44–47), and from then on they are carried like the others. So their two-run difference at creation is a source of the recursion below. It is derived in *The Creation Difference of 𝔹_k^{(n)}* below (not reviewed). The old 𝐁-terms enter the quotients of the Cauchy note's §8.6 through V(Y) of (d).
- *Deep terms.* A term is deep at a step i > k_s/8 if its domain contains a point of a layer ≤ k_s/8 at that step.
  - A boundary term of the generation i that is still carried at a later step contains a point of a layer ≤ i. Its domain X meets Z_i^∼, one layer of MR_i-cubes around Z_i (p.245 l.40–43), so X has a point y in an MR_i-cube touching Z_i. By (2.9), MR_ia_i ≤ (1 + L^{−1})MR_{i+1}a_{i+1} ≤ 2MR_{i+1}a_{i+1}, so y lies within three layers of LMR_{i+1}-cubes around Z′_i, the union of the LMR_{i+1}-cubes meeting Z_i (p.264 l.52–55). By (3.2)–(3.5), Ω_{i+1}^c ⊇ (B^{i+1}(P^1_{i+1})^c)^{∼3} ⊇ Z′_i^{∼8} (p.265 l.2, l.17–22, l.43), so y ∉ Ω_{i+1}. The layer of a point changes only when a component containing the point is renormalised, since an R-operation acts inside the renormalised components: its domain Λ = (Ω_{k₀+1}^{∼4})^c ∩ Z and the sets Z″_k ⊂ Λ lie in Z (LFR I p.192 l.43, p.195 l.42); then X meets that component, so the term belongs to the first part of (1.49) and is integrated with it (LFR II p.369 l.28–30). So y stays at a layer ≤ i while the term is carried.
  - The output domains of a step contain the domains of their inputs: at a fluctuation step the polymers of the cluster expansion are unions of the inputs' domains with further cubes (RG II p.12 l.9–11, l.23, (2.9), (2.13); CRE p.278 l.16–31), and at an R-operation the same holds for the expansions of LFR II p.387 l.40–42, p.388 l.2, l.13–14. So a term built from a deep input is deep, a non-deep term is built from non-deep inputs only, and by the first item a non-deep boundary term at a step > k_s/8 has a generation > k_s/8, as have its boundary inputs.
  - The norms D, S, and b of the Cauchy note's §8 at the steps > k_s/8 are taken over the non-deep terms. A deep term at a step j > k_s/8 has a point y at a layer ≤ k_s/8, and y ∈ Λ_j^c, since Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j ⊂ Ω_{k_s/8+1}. Let Z′ be the component of Λ_j^c containing y (LFR I p.177 l.16–17); its history reaches a layer ≤ k_s/8.
    - Let the term, of the generation i and with the domain X, meet a component Z ≠ Z′ renormalised at the step j > k_s/4. X is connected (RG I p.257 l.7–9), so it passes from y through Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j to Z, and crosses every layer Γ_l, k_s/8 < l < j. Bałaban's bound of exp(−d_i(X)) for a generation-i term with a point in Γ_n, ≤ 2^{−(i−1−n)} (p.264 l.1–3), gives exp(−d_i(X)) ≤ 2^{−(i−1−k_s/8)} for i > k_s/8 + 1. The layers i ≤ l < j cost ≥ R(j − i) π_i-cubes, and the layer j − 1 alone ≥ RL^{j−1−i}, by (a) of "The weights of the ages", so exp(−½δκd_i(X)) ≤ exp(−½δκRL^{j−1−i}), and with ½δκR ≥ σ ≥ (d + 1)log L + 1 this pays the L^{d(j−i)} points of the scale i in a point of the scale j up to a factor L^de^{−(j−1−i)}. Since j − k_s/8 > k_s/8, one of the two factors is ≤ L^de²2^{−k_s/16} at every generation. So the deep terms meeting Z are ≤ C_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16} per point of Z at the scale j, with k̄ := max(k_s, j) ≥ j bounding the number of generations i ≤ j, Ē₀ := max(E₀, B₀, R₁) bounding the frozen 𝐄-, 𝐑-, and 𝐁-terms as in the Cauchy note's §8.6, and C_D a constant of C_X, L, and d, the rest of the exponent, exp(−(κ − 1 − δκ)d_i(X)), controlling the sum over X as exp(−(κ − 1)d_j(X)) does in Bałaban's (2.48) (p.263 l.50–52), with C_X the constant of that sum at the rate ½(κ − 1), which is ≤ κ − 1 − δκ for δ ≤ ¼ and κ ≥ 2; their two-run difference is ≤ twice this, by the triangle inequality. They enter ν_j of the Cauchy note's §8.6.
    - Otherwise the term meets no component renormalised at the step j other than Z′. If Z′ is renormalised, the term belongs to its first part and is bounded in the Cauchy note's §8.6, "Fine scales and old histories". If Z′ is of the second class, the term enters 𝐓′_k(Z′), an ingredient of Z′'s large-field operation, and stays deep with Z′ (LFR II p.378 l.8, l.20–22). If Z′ is not among the components renormalised at the step j (LFR I p.177 l.33), the term is carried with Z′ and stays deep. If Z′ is alive at the stopping scale, the pair is old and is bounded by the triangle inequality in the Cauchy note's §6.2. A deep term that meets a component renormalised at a step j with k_s/8 < j ≤ k_s/4 is bounded by the triangle inequality in the Cauchy note's §8.6, "Fine scales and old histories", which covers every component renormalised at a step ≤ k_s/4.
  - *Deep V(Y) in a Young Polymer (Derived; the Exceptional Constituents and the Completions Unreviewed).* Let a component be renormalised at the step j > k_s/4, Bałaban's k in LFR II (1.61)–(1.68), and let V(Y) be a factor of LFR II (1.91) in a polymer X′ whose components of Bałaban's Z, those that X′ contains, are all young. By LFR II p.377 l.5–8, Y ∩ Z^∼ is a union of components of Z^∼, and these lie in X′, so every point of Y ∩ Z lies at a layer > k_s/8. Let Y be deep, with a point y at a layer ≤ k_s/8. Then y ∉ Z, and y ∈ Λ_j^c, since Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j ⊂ Ω_{k_s/8+1}. The component W of Λ_j^c containing y is not one of the components that make up Z (LFR I p.177 l.33), so W ∩ Z = ∅. In this item the old terms' generation is i, Bałaban's j in (1.66).
    - *The Constituents.* V(Y) re-sums, at fixed Y = Y₄, the terms of the expansions (1.61)–(1.64) of the terms of p.373 l.10–13: the terms of the first part of (1.49) other than its first two, and the terms with the localisation domains Y₀ of (1.59)–(1.60), which contain a component of Λ^∼ (p.372 l.17–18). Each of them has a localisation domain X that meets Z (p.373 l.13–14). Y₄ = Y₁ ∪ Y′₂ ∪ Y′₃ ∪ Y′₄, with Y₁ the smallest 𝐃_k-domain containing X and the components of Z that meet X (p.373 l.15–16), and Y₀ in (1.66) is the smallest 𝐃_k-domain containing X (p.376 l.9). Each constituent has the factor (1.66) (p.376 l.19), and Y₄ ∖ Z = (Y₀ ∖ Z) ∪ ⋃_{i=2}^4(Y′_i ∖ Z) (p.376 l.23). So every constituent of a deep V(Y) has y in Y₀ ∖ Z or in some Y′_i ∖ Z. The V(Y) of (1.70) and (1.91) is this re-summation minus the completions of p.377 l.22–26, which *The Completions.* below bounds.
    - *The Crossing.* Let a constituent have some Y′_i nonempty, i₀ the first such index. Each Y′_i connects Y′_i ∖ Z with Λ (p.376 l.24–25), and Λ = (Ω^{∼4}_{k₀+1})^c ∩ Z ⊂ Z (LFR I p.192 l.43). If y ∈ Y′_i, a path in Y′_i joins y to Λ. If y ∈ Y₀, X has a point y′ within one M-cube of the scale of y's layer, so at a layer ≤ k_s/8 + 1, since the layers are wider than RM in their own units (PRT2 (2.2), p.224 l.10–13). A path in X, which is connected (RG I p.257 l.7–9), joins y′ to a point of X, a contour realising the scaled distance d((Y₁⁰)^c ∩ Y₁ ∩ Y′_{i₀}, X) of the third term of (1.66) (PRT2 (2.46), p.231 l.9–13, cited at LFR II p.376 l.13–16) joins that point to Y′_{i₀}, and a path in Y′_{i₀} joins its end to Λ. The path leaves W only through Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j, before it reaches Λ ⊂ Z, so it crosses each layer Γ_l with k_s/8 + 1 < l < j inside W, outside Z: N := j − 2 − ⌊k_s/8⌋ layers at least. A layer is crossed by at most three consecutive pieces, in X, along the contour, and in Y′_{i₀}. The tree lengths and volumes are counted in the scale of each layer. CRE states this for the operation ∼ (p.263 l.6–8) and for volumes (l.21–22); for tree lengths Bałaban's bound of exp(−d_i(X)) by 2^{−(i−1−n)} (p.264 l.1–3) forces it, since that bound fails under single-scale counting. For an old term of the generation i, d_i(X) is taken in the layers of the step i. A layer Γ_l with l ≥ i is measured there in the finer units a_i ≤ a_l, so its crossing costs ≥ RL^{l−i}. A layer with l < i is measured at the step i in units no coarser than its own at the step j, since a point's layer index never decreases over the history. A fluctuation step k adds Ω_{k+1} inside the current top layer Ω_k and keeps Ω_1, …, Ω_k (CRE (3.5), p.265 l.42–44). An R-operation on a component Z replaces Ω_l by Ω″_l = (Z″_l^c ∩ Z) ∪ (Ω_l ∩ Z^c) ⊇ Ω_l, since Z″_l ⊂ Z_l, and keeps Ω_l for l ≤ h (LFR I (1.10)–(1.12), p.179 l.12–38); LFR II works with the same Ω″_l (p.362 l.10). So a piece of extent t in the units of its layer at the step j has an extent ≥ t in the units of the step i, for the old terms integrated with Z and for the completions of *The Completions.* alike. So pieces of extents t₁, t₂, and t₃ in the layer's units, with t₁ + t₂ + t₃ > RM, cost ≥ t₁/M − 1 of d_i(X), ≥ t₂ of the scaled distance, and ≥ t₃/M M-cubes of Y′_{i₀}. Half of the exponential that remains after Bałaban's extraction (p.376 l.37), exp(−½βκd_i(X) − ¼δd((Y₁⁰)^c ∩ Y₁ ∩ Y′_{i₀}, X) − Σ_i¼(κ₁ − 1)M^{−d}|Y′_i|), therefore gives e^{−σ_V} per crossed layer, with σ_V := c_VR − ½βκ and c_V := min(½βκ, ¼δM, ¼(κ₁ − 1)). The order of choice is κ before R. κ_B and the condition (κ-½) of *The Bound.* do not involve R, since Bałaban's resummations hold at every step k with one κ while the widths MR_k grow with k (CRE (2.5)). R is then fixed so large that σ_V ≥ 1.
    - *The Bound.* The other half of that exponential controls Bałaban's resummations of p.376 l.39–42, over the Y′_i, the X, and the generations, as the whole does. Those sums need the rates only above the constants of the counting of trees, domains, and points (Sects. 6, 7 [I] and Sects. 2, 3 [III], p.376 l.42), which Bałaban's large κ and κ₁ also exceed at half the rates. For κ this is the condition (κ-½): κ ≥ 2κ_B, with κ_B the threshold on κ of Bałaban's resummations of p.376 l.39–46 and of the small constant of p.377 l.4–5, and ½βκ ≥ 1, for the generation sums of the boundary terms (CRE p.264 l.1–3). κ can be chosen arbitrarily large if the other parameters are fixed accordingly (CRE p.260 l.27–28), at fixed β, as Bałaban does when he takes βκ ≥ 6·2^d "for κ large enough" (LFR II p.389 l.12). The δ-sum costs a factor of the type δ^{−d}, absorbed with the other constants in α, "arbitrarily small for γ small" (p.377 l.14). So the constituents of a deep V(Y) with a nonempty Y′_i sum to ≤ C_Vα e^{−σ_V max(N, 0)}exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), with C_V a constant of the halved resummations. Under the condition σ_V ≥ 1 on Bałaban's R, of the kind of (⋆), and with j > k_s/4, N ≥ k_s/8 − 2, so e^{−σ_V max(N, 0)} ≤ e²e^{−k_s/8} ≤ e²2^{−k_s/16}. The two runs' sums of these constituents therefore differ by ≤ r_Dα exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), r_D := 2e²C_V2^{−k_s/16}, by the triangle inequality.
    - *The Exceptional Constituents (Derived, Unreviewed).* The constituents with all Y′_i empty have only the first two terms of (1.66), with Y = Y₁ = Y₄ and Y₀ ∖ Z = Y₁ ∖ Z (p.376 l.43–46).
      - *The crossing through X.* The domain X of the term meets Z (p.373 l.13–14) and is connected (RG I p.257 l.7–9). y lies in Y₀ ∖ Z, so X has a point y′ within one M-cube of the scale of y's layer, at a layer ≤ k_s/8 + 1, as in *The Crossing.* A path in X joins y′ to Z. It leaves W only through Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j, so it crosses each layer Γ_l with k_s/8 + 1 < l < j inside W, N layers at least, each with an extent > RM in the layer's units. Each crossing costs ≥ R − 1 of d_i(X): the piece t₁ of *The Crossing.*, with t₂ = t₃ = 0.
      - *The bound.* Bałaban extracts exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)) from the second term of (1.66), since d_k(Y₀) ≥ d_{k,Z}(Y) (p.376 l.44–45), and exp(−βκd_i(X)) remains. Half of it gives exp(−½βκ(R − 1)) ≤ e^{−σ_V} per crossed layer, since c_V ≤ ½βκ. The other half controls Bałaban's resummations, which the exponential remaining after his extraction controls (p.376 l.37–42), over the generations (l.41) and over the X with Y₀ ∖ Z fixed (l.45–46), under (κ-½) of *The Bound.*
      - So the exceptional constituents of a deep V(Y) sum to ≤ C_Eαe^{−σ_V max(N, 0)}exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), with C_E the constant of that halved resummation. With N ≥ k_s/8 − 2 and σ_V ≥ 1, the two runs' sums differ by ≤ r_Eα exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), r_E := 2e²C_E2^{−k_s/16}, by the triangle inequality.
    - *The Completions (Derived, Unreviewed).* At p.377 l.22–26 Bałaban adds to the action the missing terms with localisation domains X meeting Z, the 𝐄-terms of the new action and the 𝐑-terms and boundary terms of the first k − 1 operations RT, at U_k. He re-sums them in the domains Y of p.377 l.5–7 and subtracts them from V(Y), and the result is the V(Y) of (1.70) and (1.91). A completion of the generation i ≤ k has its inductive bound exp(−κd_i(X)) on its class, which p.376 l.5–9 turns into the first two terms of (1.66): exp(−βκd_i(X) − (1 + 3β)κd_k(Y₀)), Y₀ the smallest 𝐃_k-domain containing X, for i < k, and exp(−(1 + 4β)κd_k(X)), with Y₀ = X, for i = k. Bałaban says that they "may be resummed in domains Y described above" (p.377 l.24–25), the Y ∈ 𝐃_k whose intersection with Z^∼ is a union of components of Z^∼ (l.6–7), so the assignment of a completion to a domain Y is a choice of the construction, the same in both runs. The completions are re-summed by the rule of the exceptional constituents (p.376 l.43–46, p.377 l.1–7): X goes to Y₁(X), the smallest domain of 𝐃_k containing X and the components of Z that meet X (p.373 l.15–16). Then Y ∖ Z = Y₀ ∖ Z, which Bałaban states for these domains at p.376 l.44 and which depends only on X and Z, so d_k(Y₀) ≥ d_{k,Z}(Y), and this is how the first two terms of (1.66) give (1.68) there.
      - *The bound.* With this rule the completions of a deep V(Y) are bounded as the exceptional constituents. y lies in Y₀ ∖ Z, X meets Z by definition and is connected, and the layers of the step i are no coarser than those of the step j (*The Crossing.*), so *The crossing through X.* applies. Half of exp(−βκd_i(X)) gives e^{−σ_V} per crossed layer, and the other half controls the re-summation over the X, the generations, and the three types, under (κ-½). So the completions of a deep V(Y) sum to ≤ C_Cαe^{−σ_V max(N, 0)}exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), with C_C the constant of that halved re-summation, and the two runs' completions differ by ≤ r_Cα exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), r_C := 2e²C_C2^{−k_s/16}.
- *The step map.* At the fluctuation step k the boundary terms are among the activities of the expansion of the Cauchy note's (8.1), and its marking "R-mark" applies: the two-run difference of an output is a sum of terms, each linear in one marked activity, with the other activities at run B or at run A. So the two-run difference of 𝐁^{(k+1)} has three parts. (i) The terms with the frozen 𝐁-difference of one input marked. (ii) The terms with the step's own two-run difference marked, applied to run B's boundary terms: the measure difference of (8.1), which contains 𝐁_k through (3.27), and the variation of the old terms at the two runs' backgrounds. (iii) The 𝐁-parts of the output differences generated by the other marked activities. The t-integral is a product of the integrand 𝐁_k(U_k(…)) − 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) and the measure (3.27), which contains t𝐁_k. Its two-run difference telescopes into the integrand's difference against run A's measure plus run B's integrand against the measures' difference, and the measures' difference is, by the interpolation of the Cauchy note's (8.1), a sum of terms each linear in one marked activity, t times a frozen 𝐁-difference among them. So R-mark applies with two insertions, one in the integrand and one in the measure, and every term is linear in one marked activity.
  - For (i): Bałaban bounds the expansion of the t-integrand 𝐁_k(U_k(…)) − 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) as in (2.42) with the additional factor O(ε_k), "arising from the bound of the fluctuation field" (p.277 l.7–9), through the analyticity of the term in the fluctuation (RG I (3.10)–(3.14), p.272 l.6–44). The frozen difference of a term of the generation i is analytic on the same domain, being the difference of two such terms, with the bound b_iB₀exp(−κd_i(X)) there, so the same estimate gives C_εε_k times that bound, C_ε being Bałaban's constant in O(ε_k). Its place in the measure (3.27), multiplied by t, has the same bound. Bałaban's treatment of the 𝐁-terms by RG I (3.6)–(3.7) with the localisation over the Y₀ ∈ 𝐃_k meeting Λ_{k+1} (CRE p.276 l.42–46), the cluster expansion (3.44)–(3.47) (pp.277–278), and the three facts of the Cauchy note's §8.2 then give an output of the form (2.42), and by §8.5 Bałaban's derivation of (2.47) applies to it. So (i) is ≤ ε_k^{*B}w_{k+1−i}L^{d(k+1−i)}b_i from the input generation i > k_s/8, with ε_k^{*B} := 2C_XC_KPC_εε_k, which tends to 0 with g_k, w_r the weight of the age r below, and L^{d(k+1−i)} the number of points of the scale i in a point of the scale k + 1.
  - (ii) and (iii) are ≤ s^B_{k+1} := C_B(𝒟^∗_{k+1} + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}) on the non-deep domains, at real and at complex backgrounds (the variation part of (ii) at complex backgrounds by *Complex Backgrounds.* below, under (L-V), unreviewed). Here 𝒟^∗_i := max_{k_s/8<i′≤i}𝒟^B_{i′}, with 𝒟^B_{i′} := D_{i′−1} + S_{i′−1} + g_{i′−1}²|δ_{i′−1}| + e_{i′} + max_{k_s/8<l<i′}d_l in the notation of the Cauchy note's (8.3), and C_B is a constant of C_KP, C_g, C_β/b′, E₀/B₀, B₀, and W, the last through the measure difference acting on run B's boundary terms of all ages. (iii) and the measure difference are the 𝐁-part of the marked expansion of (8.1) with the marked activities of §8.2, ≤ C_KP(E₀/B₀) times the right side of (8.3), which is ≤ C𝒟^B_{k+1}.
  - The coupling term A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·) of p.277 l.10–14. By (2.24) (p.259 l.14–21), φ_l ∈ C_0^∞(Λ_l) with φ_l = 1 on Λ_l^{∼−1}, and Ω_{l+1} ⊂ Λ_l^{∼−1} by (3.5), so at a point x of a layer Γ_m, g_k^{−2}(x) − g_k^{−2} = β_m(1 − φ_m(x)) + Σ_{m<l≤k}β_l. This is ≤ b′(k − m + 1) + C by the window bound (W) of `BETA_FUNCTION_WILSON_LOOPS.md` §2, and Bałaban's bound of the term rests on this growth against the exponential decay of 𝐇_k (p.277 l.13–14). The two runs' functions differ by Δβ_m(1 − φ_m(x)) + Σ_{m<l≤k}Δβ_l, with |Δβ_l| ≤ C_βd_l (the Cauchy note's §8.3), so by ≤ C_β(k − m + 1)max_{m≤l≤k}d_l, the same growth. So the coupling part of the term's difference is ≤ (C_β/b′)max_{k_s/8<l≤k}d_l times Bałaban's bound of the term on the non-deep domains, inside C𝒟^B_{k+1}; the rest of the difference, at the two runs' configurations, is a variation.
  - V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}) consists of the expressions above expanded in the perturbative terms of (1.22) (p.277 l.14–17), so its differences are of the kinds (i)–(iii).
  - *The Variation at the Fluctuation Step (Derived; at Complex Backgrounds Unreviewed).* The variation is one run's old term 𝐁^{(i)}(X, ·), i ≤ k, at the two runs' configurations of the t-integrand, U_k(…) = exp(iη𝐇_k(B′))U_{k+1} (RG I (3.2)–(3.3); CRE p.271 l.7–8), with the fluctuation A_f in Bałaban's domain (3.43), complex, with |A_f| < C₁p₁(g_k) (p.276 l.29–31). As in (d)(ii), let A be the alignment field of run A's configuration U^A of the t-integrand against Ũ := MU^B, let 𝐔(s) := e^{−isεA}U^A be the path, with the current moved as in (d)(ii), let ϱ^F(x) be the room of the path at x in the term's class Ũ^c_i(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) (p.261 l.57–66), let d̄^F(x) bound a_{j(x)}|A(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A(x)|, and let r^F(X) := sup_{x∈X}d̄^F(x)/ϱ^F(x). For r^F(X) ≤ ½, Cauchy's estimate on the class bounds the variation by 2r^F(X)B₀exp(−κd_i(X)). At the points of the current layer the two runs' configurations differ by the classical depth, inside e_{k+1}.
    - *The Room at Real Backgrounds (Derived).* Let U_{k+1} be real, G-valued with its own current. At a point x of the layer m, each quantity that (2.34)–(2.39) condition at x is, for the configuration of the t-integrand, at most the background's quantity plus the move of the shift exp(iη𝐇_k(B′)). The background coincides on Ω_m ∖ Ω_{m+1} with a U_m-type configuration and "has the same regularity properties and bounds" (p.275 l.36–39), so its quantities are ≤ a constant times ε_m times their scale factors, while α_{c,m}/ε_m = (C_c/A₀)(log g_m^{−2})^{q_c−p₀}, c = 0, 1, exceeds every constant for γ small (CRE (2.4), (2.28)). On Ω_{k+1}^c the shift uses the part of the old terms' domains that is "much greater than bounds on this function" (p.277 l.1–7). At the layer m that part is the difference β2^{−(k+1−m)}α_{c,m} of the prefactors of (2.36)–(2.39) at the steps k and k + 1, times the scale factors, so the shift moves each quantity by ≤ ½β2^{−(k+1−m)}α_{c,m} ≤ ¼βα_{c,m} times its scale factor. On Ω_{k+1} the shift is ≤ a constant times g_kC₁p₁(g_k) times the scale factors of the scale k, against α_{0,k} and α_{1,k} = g_kC₁(log g_k^{−2})^{q₁}, ratios of a constant times (log g_k^{−2})^{p₁−q_c}, which tend to 0, since p₁ < p₀ (LFR I p.183 l.2) and q₀, q₁ > p₀ ((d)(ii), "At real points the ratio is small"). The term's thresholds at x are, for i > m, the prefactor times α_{c,m}, ≥ (1 − β)α_{c,m}, and for i ≤ m those of the scale i, which are ≥ α_{c,m}/(1 + β₀) times the scale-m factors, since g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)(m − i + 1)^{1/2}g_i (CRE (2.6)), g(log g^{−2})^{q_c} increases for g small, and the scale factors of the scale i exceed those of the scale m by L^{m−i} ≥ (m − i + 1)^{1/2}. So every threshold at x is ≥ τ_m := (1 − β)α_{c,m}/(1 + β₀) times its scale-m factor. With β ≤ 1/8, as in (d)(ii), and β₀ ≤ ½, ¼βα_{c,m} ≤ ⅛τ_m, and for γ small the background's quantities are ≤ ⅛τ_m as well. So each conditioned quantity of the configuration of the t-integrand is ≤ ¼ of its threshold, and the room in each condition is ≥ ¾τ_m ≥ ½α_m/(1 + β₀) times its scale factor, α_m := min(α_{0,m}, α_{1,m}). Each run's configuration of the t-integrand is the minimiser U_k at its data, U_k(exp iB′V^{(k)}) = exp(iη𝐇_k(B′))U_{k+1} (RG I (3.3), p.270 l.24–28), so it has the multiplier representation of *The Current at the Multiscale Chain.* in (d)(ii), with the determining set of U_k, and that item bounds the move of its current. The moves of the path are those of the list of (d)(ii), "The room at real points", with (2.34)–(2.39) in place of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69), each ≤ (C_p + x̃_J(1 + 1/C_U(M)) + C_J)|s|d̄^F(x) times its scale factor. So ϱ^F(x) ≥ c_ρα_m at real backgrounds, with the c_ρ of (d)(ii).

Both classes use that sequence, and the fluctuation step keeps it, adding Ω_{k+1} (CRE (3.5)).
