        - On the disc |z| < 25 the alignment field and the current difference are analytic, as the configurations are. They are ≤ M_c^F := (2C_U(M) + 5C_p)ε₁^c + 52C_shg_kC₁p₁(g_k) times their scale factors, by the factorisation of *The Alignment Field at Complex Points.* with the shifts of *The Alignment at Complex Fluctuations.* M_c^F ≤ 1 for γ small.
        - The Lemma (Two Constants) at z = i gives a_{j(x)}|A(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A(x)| ≤ 4d̄_c(x)^{15/16}, and the current difference ≤ 2(C_J″d̄_c(x))^{15/16} times its scale factor.
        - The moves of the path are those of the list of (d)(ii), "The room at real points". The plaquettes, U_{p,X}, and 𝐉_{p,X} move through PBG (3.133) and Cauchy's estimate on the multipliers, as in *The Fine Side at Complex Points.* So the moves are ≤ C_mv|s|𝔡_c(x) times their scale factors, with 𝔡_c(x) := 4(1 + C_J″)d̄_c(x)^{15/16} and C_mv := C_p + 1.
        - This needs (T-c), and (T-F) with ε₁^c for ε₁^F, which hold for γ small, as those do.
      - The Cauchy estimate. Let r^F_c(X) := sup_{x∈X}C_mv𝔡_c(x)/ϱ^F_c(x) ≤ (4C_mv/β)sup_{x∈X}2^{(i−m(x))₊}𝔡_c(x)/α_{m(x)}. For |s| < 1/r^F_c(X) the path stays in the term's class Ũ^c_i(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), so for r^F_c(X) ≤ ½ Cauchy's estimate bounds the variation by 2r^F_c(X)B₀exp(−κd_i(X)), as at real backgrounds.
      - The ratio. At a point of the layer m, 𝔡_c(x)/α_m ≤ C_c′P(k)(ε/a_{min(m,n)})^{5(1−ε₀)/16}, as in *The Ratio* above. The factor ε₁^{c(−1/16)} of the power 15/16 is bounded as in the ratio of (d)(ii) at complex points, by (ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C)^{1/32}. Here P(k) collects the polynomial factors in k, in log g_k^{−2}, and in K − k, the last two with coefficients depending on ḡ and b′, and C_c′ is a constant of M, β₀, p₀, c_ρ, L, r, ḡ, b′, and Bałaban's constants.
        - For a non-deep X, min(m, n) > k_s/8 as there, and i − m < K − k_s/8 = 7k_s/8 + j(C). So r^F_c(X) ≤ (4C_mv/β)C_c′2^{j(C)}P(k)2^{7k_s/8}L^{−5(1−ε₀)k_s/128}.
        - Under the condition
          (L-V) L^{5(1−ε₀)/128} ≥ 4,
          a lower bound on Bałaban's L, which RG I p.251 l.14 bounds below only ("an odd, positive integer > 11"), this is ≤ (4C_mv/β)C_c′2^{j(C)}P(k)2^{−k_s}2^{−k_s/8}.
        - θ̃ ≥ (1 + θ)/2 ≥ ½ (the Cauchy note's §8.7), and P(k)2^{−k_s} is bounded by a constant of j(C), ḡ, and b′, since k and K − k are ≤ K = k_s + j(C). So r^F_c(X) ≤ C_rθ̃^{k_s/8}, with C_r a constant of the above, of j(C), of ḡ, and of b′.
        - Let n₀ be the larger of the real threshold and the least k_s with C_rθ̃^{k_s/8} ≤ ½. For k_s ≥ n₀ the variation is therefore ≤ 2C_rθ̃^{k_s/8}B₀exp(−κd_i(X)). For k_s < n₀ each run's term is ≤ its bound, as at real backgrounds, so the variation is ≤ 2B₀exp(−κd_i(X)) ≤ 2θ̃^{−n₀/8}θ̃^{k_s/8}B₀exp(−κd_i(X)). So the variation is ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}B₀exp(−κd_i(X)) for all k_s, with C_h ≥ max(2C_r, 2θ̃^{−n₀/8}) added to its real-case value, and Bałaban's κ unchanged. This is the C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} of s^B_{k+1} at the complex backgrounds.
        - The order of choice is ε₀ first, then L under (L-V), then the constants that depend on L, with κ before R as in *The Crossing.*, and n₀ and C_h last.
- *The Creation Difference of 𝔹_k^{(n)} (Derived, Not Reviewed).* In this item k_R denotes Bałaban's k of LFR I §1, the step of the R-operation on a component Z, and ν denotes his n, the number of preliminary integrations done, so that the (ν + 1)st integration, (1.60), is at the scale j and ν = j − h (p.190 l.36, p.191 l.33). The birth scale of the history is n, as in (b).
  - *Which components.* A component renormalised at a step k_R ≤ k_s/4 is bounded by the triangle inequality in the Cauchy note's §8.6, "Fine scales and old histories", together with all of its terms, the 𝔹-terms included. So let k_R > k_s/4. Then h ≥ k_R/2 − 1 (§1), and every integration scale j ≥ h (the integrations run over h, …, k − 1, (1.71)) and every index m ≥ j + 1 of (1.63) exceed k_s/8 once k_R > k_s/4 + 4; the threshold of the triangle-inequality case is raised from k_s/4 to k_s/4 + 4, which changes only its constants by a factor L^{O(1)}. The 𝔹-terms whose domains contain a point of a layer ≤ k_s/8 are deep, and they are bounded by "Deep terms" with the generation i read as the index m of (1.63). Their bound C₀exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_m(X)) is stronger than the inductive one used there. If the deep point lies in another component Z′, the crossing bound of that item applies; if it lies in Z, the history of Z reaches a layer ≤ k_s/8 and Z is old. Below, all norms are over the non-deep terms.
  - *The norm.* Let c_ν be the smallest number with |ℂ^{B,(ν)}_k(X) − ℂ^{A,(ν)}_k(X)| ≤ c_νC₀exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_m(X)) on the intersection of the two runs' spaces Ũ_k^{(ν)c}(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), the bound of (1.70) (p.192). As in *The objects.*, the intersection contains either run's space with α̃₀ and α̃₁ scaled by 1 − C′g_j²|δ_j|. The domains X of (1.63) are physical sets of the layers of the matched pair, the same in both runs, as are the sets Z_{j+1} ∩ Ω_{j+1} and Ω_{j+1}^c ∖ Z″_{j+1} of the integration variables (§1).
  - *The identity.* (1.60) is a fluctuation integral "of the same type as the integral studied in Sect. 3 [III]" (p.189 l.36–38). The variables A_j and B_j are block variables at the shared scale j, the same in both runs. So the interpolation of the Cauchy note's (8.1) applies verbatim, with 𝐏_t built from the exponent of (1.60) and the Gaussian forms of A_j and B_j interpolated as Δ_t there, at run A's cutoffs. The logarithm of the integral, the constant E^{(j)}_0, and the coupling term of (1.62) together make up ℂ^{(ν+1)}_k, so ℂ^{B,(ν+1)}_k − ℂ^{A,(ν+1)}_k is the right side of (8.1), the cutoff shells, and the coupling term's difference. The measure ⟨·⟩_t is of Bałaban's kind, since 𝐏_t is a convex combination of two admissible exponents. §8.5 (R-mark) then gives localised terms in the domains of (1.63), each linear in one marked activity, bounded by C_KP[ι_G + a_jι] times exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_m(X)). Bałaban's resummation into the smallest 𝔻_m-domain (p.189 l.43–46) is a fixed rule of the domains, the same in both runs.
  - *The marked activities.* They are the parts of 𝐏^B − 𝐏^A, with these bounds per point:
    - the fluctuation parts of the frozen 𝐄-, 𝐑-, and 𝐁-differences, at U_k^{(ν)}(exp i[g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j)]V^{(j)}) minus U_k^{(ν+1)}. These are the norms d, d^R, and b of the Cauchy note's §8.2 and of the recursion below, ≤ C𝒟^B_{k_R} with the factor O(δ′_j) of the fluctuation (p.191 l.46–p.192 l.6);
    - the fluctuation parts of the earlier differences ℂ^{B,(i)}_k − ℂ^{A,(i)}_k, i ≤ ν. By (1.70) and the inclusion of the shifted configuration in Ũ_k^{(ν)c} ⊂ Ũ_k^{(i)c} (p.191 l.33–42), each is analytic there with the bound c_iC₀exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_m(X)). As in (i) of "The step map", its fluctuation part carries O(δ′_j). At a point at most N₀ of these terms share a localisation region (the common domain Ω_{k₀+1}^c ∖ Ω″_{k_R}, p.191 l.48–49), and the others are on disjoint regions Z_{h+i} ∖ Z″_{h+i}. So their sum per point is ≤ C_cN₀δ′_jC₀max_{i≤ν}c_i, with C_c the constant of Bałaban's count. With his N₀δ′_j < 4N₀²δ′_{k_R} (p.192 l.1–4) this is ≤ 4C_cN₀²δ′_{k_R}C₀max_{i≤ν}c_i;
    - the perturbative part 𝐏^{(j)}(g_j, A_j, B_j) and the fine actions at the fluctuated configurations. Their difference at equal coupling is the classical depth of U_k^{(ν)} at the fluctuated data, ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point by (b) at the history's birth scale. Their coupling part is ≤ C_gg_j²|δ_j|, as in the Cauchy note's §8.2(b);
    - the coupling terms A(1/(g_k^{(ν)}(·))² − 1/g_j², ·) of (1.60) and A(1/(g_k^{(ν+1)}(·))² − 1/(g_k^{(ν)}(·))², U_k^{(ν+1)}) of (1.62). The functions g_k^{(ν)}(·) are built from the β_l of the history, so the two runs' functions differ by ≤ C_β(k_R − m + 1)max_{m≤l≤k_R}d_l at a point of the layer m, with the growth that Bałaban's bound of the terms already absorbs, as in "The coupling term" of "The step map". So this part is ≤ (C_β/b′)max_{k_s/8<l≤k_R}d_l times Bałaban's bound, and the rest is a variation at the two runs' configurations;
    - V^{(j)} = exp(iℍ_Z^{(j)})V_Z^{(j)} (1.58). ℍ_Z^{(j)} is an analytic function of U_k^{(ν+1)} on Z, ≤ O(1)e^{−R_j}ε_j on (Ω_{j+1}^c ∖ Z″_j)^{(j)} (p.188 l.31–34). Its run difference at the two runs' configurations is a variation, ≤ O(1)e^{−R_j} times the depth by Cauchy's estimate on its domain;
    - the Gaussian forms ⟨A_j, C*Δ^{(j)}_{Λ_{j+1}^c}CA_j⟩ and ⟨B_j, C*Δ^{(j)}CB_j⟩. Their difference is the per-layer (10.6) of §4(d), which rests on (W-δ) (derived, not reviewed), ≤ e times the form's bound, and its insertion is a marked quadratic activity of §8.5 with Gaussian mean ≤ C_G times it, as in the Cauchy note's §10.5;
    - the cutoffs. χ^{(j)} and χ′^{(j)} are soft tests, and their shells are bounded pointwise by δ^s_j/κ_s times a run's own term (§3(d)). The test (1.59) on B_j does not depend on the background, so its shell is the coupling shell of δ′_j, ≤ Cg_j²|δ_j|e^{−cp₁(g_j)²}, as in the Cauchy note's §8.2(b).
  - *The variation on Ũ_k^{(ν)c}.* Each run's term of the generations i ≤ ν, and each run's 𝐄-, 𝐑-, and 𝐁-terms, at the two runs' fluctuated configurations, is a variation. It is bounded as in *The Variation at the Fluctuation Step.*, with the room of the descending spaces in place of CRE's prefactors.
    - The room. At a point of the layer m, the prefactor of the space Ũ_k^{(i)c} is P_i(m) := 1 − βΣ_{i′=h+1}^{h+i}2^{−|m−i′|} − βΣ_{q>m}2^{−(q−m)} in (1.64) and (1.68) ((1.66) has m = j). So P_i(m) − P_{ν+1}(m) ≥ β2^{−|m−j|}, with j = h + ν + 1, the sum over i′ containing i′ = j. This includes i = 0, the space Ũ^c_k(X, α̃₀, α̃₁) of the carried terms (p.191 l.32–33). This per-m form holds on the layers of (i), where the factor L₀^{2max{0,m−k₀}} is the same for both spaces (p.190 l.26–31), and on the layers m ≥ j + 1 of (1.68). On Ω_{k₀+1} ∖ Ω_{j+1}, (1.66)–(1.67) freeze the prefactor and the scale factors at the space's own index (p.190 l.42–55), so the comparison of prefactors does not apply there, and the room is derived as follows. Write j_i := h + i < j, P_i^{fr} for the frozen prefactor of Ũ_k^{(i)c}, and f(m) for the list of scale factors of (1.66) at the index m, with the α's included.
      - The scale factors. For m < m′, entry by entry, f(m′) ≤ (α_{m′}/α_m)L^{−(m′−m)}f(m): the plaquette, gradient, and p-entries gain L^{−2(m′−m)} or more (for the p-entry of the current with m < p ≤ m′ the ratio is L^{−2m′−p+3m} ≤ L^{−2(m′−m)}), the current entry L^{−3(m′−m)}, and the A′ entries L^{−(m′−m)}, the weakest, which is why Bałaban needs L₀² ≤ L/3 (p.191 l.35). With α_{c,m} = g_mC_c(log g_m^{−2})^{q_c} and x := g^{−2}, T := x_{m′}/x_m, the ratio is T^{−1/2}(log x_{m′}/log x_m)^{q_c}. For T ≥ 1 it is ≤ C_α := sup_{T≥1}T^{−1/2}(1 + log T)^{q_c} (log x_m ≥ 1), and for T < 1 it is ≤ (1 + β₀)(m′ − m + 1)^{1/2} by CRE (2.6). So α_{m′}/α_m ≤ C_α(1 + β₀)(m′ − m + 1)^{1/2}, and the same holds for the α₀'s.
      - The number of frozen scales. The frozen layers have k₀ + 1 ≤ m ≤ j ≤ k with k₀ = k − N₀, so all the index differences below are ≤ N₀ − 1.
      - The prefactors. The prefactors used below are the frozen ones, at m equal to the space's index, where the sum over i′ is < 2, and those of (1.68) at m above the space's index, where it is < 1; the sum over q is ≤ 1. So each lies in [1 − 3β, 1] ⊂ [¼, 1] for β ≤ 1/4, and the ratio of two of them is ≤ 4. (At a layer strictly inside [h + 1, j] the sum over i′ of (1.64) approaches 3; those prefactors are not used here.)
      - Layers j_i < m ≤ j. Ũ_k^{(i)c} uses (1.68) there, b_i(m) = P_i(m)f(m), and Ũ_k^{(ν+1)c} uses (1.66), b(m) = P^{fr}L₀^{2(j−m)}f(j). So b(m)/b_i(m) ≤ 4C_α(1 + β₀)(j − m + 1)^{1/2}(L₀²/L)^{j−m}.
      - Layers k₀ + 1 ≤ m ≤ j_i. Both spaces use (1.66): b_i(m) = P_i^{fr}L₀^{2(j_i−m)}f(j_i) and b(m) = P^{fr}L₀^{2(j−m)}f(j), so b(m)/b_i(m) ≤ 4C_α(1 + β₀)(j − j_i + 1)^{1/2}(L₀²/L)^{j−j_i}.
      - The collars of (i). For a space of index j_i < j, the clause (i) at m = j_i covers all of Ω″_{j_i} ∖ Ω_{k₀+1} (p.190 l.31), so on the collars Ω″_m ∖ Ω″_{m+1}, j_i < m < j, outside Ω_{k₀+1} (LFR I (1.10)–(1.12), p.179), Ũ_k^{(i)c} is frozen at j_i while Ũ_k^{(ν+1)c} uses (i) at the index m. There b_i(m) = P_iL₀^{2max{0,j_i−k₀}}f(j_i), b(m) = P_{ν+1}(m)L₀^{2max{0,m−k₀}}f(m), and f(j_i) ≥ (α_{j_i}/α_m)L^{m−j_i}f(m) for m > j_i by the scale factors above. The factors L₀^{2max{0,·−k₀}} depend on the index (LFR I p.190 l.25 and l.30), not on the location, and their ratio is at most L₀^{2(m−j_i)}, so b(m)/b_i(m) ≤ 4C_α(1 + β₀)(m − j_i + 1)^{1/2}(L₀²/L)^{m−j_i} ≤ 2^{1/2}/8 < 0.18 under the condition below, and the room is ≥ 0.82b_i(m) ≥ 0.2(α_{j_i}/α_m)L^{m−j_i}f(m) ≥ f(m), which exceeds ¾β2^{−|m−j|}f(m).
      - The condition. Bałaban's L₀ is free in 2 ≤ L₀ < L/2 (LFR I p.182 l.18–19); his further condition O(1)B₃B₅M⁵L₀^{−2(N₀−1)} ≤ ¼ (p.200 l.30–35) holds for γ small at any fixed L₀ ≥ 2. Imposing L₀² ≤ L/(32C_α(1 + β₀)), met for example by L₀ = 2 and L ≥ 128C_α(1 + β₀), gives b(m)/b_i(m) ≤ 2^{1/2}/8 < 0.18 in both cases where the exponent is ≥ 1, that is, except at m = j in the first case. So the room b_i(m) − b(m) is ≥ 0.82b_i(m) there. On the layers j_i < m < j this is ≥ 0.82·¼f(m) > ¾β2^{−|m−j|}f(m) for β ≤ 1/4, as on the per-m layers. At m = j both bounds carry f(j), with the prefactors P_i(j) of (1.68) and the frozen prefactor of Ũ_k^{(ν+1)c}, which at m = j is P_{ν+1}(j); so the room is (P_i(j) − P_{ν+1}(j))f(j) ≥ βf(j) > ¾βf(j), by the per-m difference above. On Ω″_j ∖ Ω_{k₀+1}, clause (i) of Ũ_k^{(ν+1)c} at the index j faces Ũ_k^{(i)c} frozen at j_i; this is the collar comparison below with m = j, m − j_i ≥ 1. On the layers m ≤ j_i the room is ≥ 0.82·¼L₀^{2(j_i−m)}f(j_i) ≥ 0.2f(j_i), in the scale factors of the index j_i. In the scale-m factors, f(j_i) ≥ (α_{j_i}/α_m)L^{−3(j_i−m)}f(m) and α_m/α_{j_i} ≤ C_α(1 + β₀)N₀^{1/2}, so the room is ≥ c_Nf(m) with c_N := L^{−3N₀}/(5C_α(1 + β₀)N₀^{1/2}). N₀ is not a fixed constant: N₀ = O(log g_{k_R}^{−2}) (LFR I p.192 l.4–5, *The recursion over ν.*), so c_N^{−1} is a polynomial in g_{k_R}^{−2} of degree O(log L), with g_{k_R}^{−2} ≤ ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k_R) + C, a polynomial factor of the kind collected in P(k) (*Complex Backgrounds.*, *The ratio.*). The cube bounds (1.65), (1.67), and (1.69) carry no prefactor, and at m = j and on the per-m layers the two spaces' cube bounds coincide, so they have no room from the comparison of the spaces. They need none: they bound the field A of the real factor U only, U^u = exp iηA (p.190 l.14–17), and the variation's disc moves only U′ (*The room at real points.*), so they are needed at the real points of the path only, with the room taken at real points. There the real factor is a configuration U(𝐁, data) with data of the plaquette size c_Fε_m(L^{k−m}η)² of the support, c_F the constant of Bałaban's (1.44)–(1.52) (p.186–187), and VAR Theorem 1 bounds its field in the cube gauge by B₃c_F times the corresponding ε-scale factors, which is ≤ ¼BCMα_{0,m} for γ small, since α_{0,m}/ε_m exceeds every constant. On the frozen layers the comparison is with (1.67), L^jη|A| ≤ L₀^{2(j−m)}BCMα_{0,j} on the CML^jη-cubes (LFR I p.190 l.49–53), and uses the regularity of the support at the frozen index j (LFR I p.182): there the ε-scale factor is ε_j on those cubes, not ε_m, which would cost (L/L₀)^{2(j−m)}. VAR Theorem 1's cube-gauge bound is taken from VAR as the note's §3(d) uses it; its statement is not quoted here. The moves of the real path points are those of *The shift.* and of the depth, each ≤ ¼ of the plaquette room, and they move the cube field by the same factors. On the frozen layers the cube bounds of the two spaces differ by the scale factors as above, with BCM in place of the prefactor, the gain per scale being that of the A′ entries. The two spaces take these bounds on cube families of different sizes (CML^jη against CML^{j_i}η or CML^mη), and the passage between the families, a smaller cube inside a larger one of the other family with the larger cube's gauge transformation, is Bałaban's descending statement, used as he states it (p.191 l.33–38).
      This is the quantitative form of Bałaban's descending property (p.191 l.33–38) on these layers. The shift below is compared with this room.
    - The shift. Only B_j enters the configuration of (1.60), through exp iη𝐇_k^{(ν)}(g_jCB_j − hD̃(g_jCB_j)) (p.191 l.38–41), with |exp ig_jB_j − 1| < 2δ′_j by (1.59). B_j lives on the bonds of the scale j in Ω_{j+1}^c ∖ Z″_{j+1}, which contains points of the layers m ≤ j. There the shift is a field of the amplitude ≤ C_Hδ′_j, smooth at the scale j, so each conditioned quantity, measured in the scale-m factors of (1.64), is ≤ C_Hδ′_jL^{−(j−m)}: the field, its gradient, the plaquettes, and the current carry L^{−(j−m)}, L^{−2(j−m)}, L^{−2(j−m)}, and L^{−3(j−m)}. At the layers m > j the shift decays at the rate δ₀ of 𝐇_k^{(ν)} (RG I p.271 l.33–34) from Ω_{j+1}^c, across the layers j + 1, …, m − 1, each wider than RM in its own units (PRT2 (2.2)), and the scale-j factors exceed the scale-m ones by ≤ L^{3(m−j)}. So the shift is ≤ C_Hδ′_jL^{3(m−j)}e^{−δ₀RM(m−j−1)} times the scale-m factors there. Under (⋆), which gives δ₀RM ≥ 16 log L + 16, both cases give ≤ C_HL^{16}e^{16}δ′_j2^{−|m−j|}L^{−|m−j|/2}. Against the room β2^{−|m−j|}α_{c,m} it is ≤ (C_HL^{16}e^{16}/β)L^{−|m−j|/2}δ′_j/α_{c,m}. Here δ′_j = g_jA₁p₁(g_j) (LFR I p.183 l.3) and α_{c,m} = g_mC_c(log g_m^{−2})^{q_c}, and CRE (2.6) gives g_m ≥ g_j/((1 + β₀)(|m − j| + 1)^{1/2}), as in *The Room at Real Backgrounds.* For m < j this gives δ′_j/α_{c,m} ≤ (1 + β₀)(A₁/C_c)(|m − j| + 1)^{1/2}(log g_j^{−2})^{p₁−q_c}, and L^{−|m−j|/2} absorbs the root for L ≥ 4. For m > j the step log g_m^{−2} ≥ ½log g_j^{−2} can fail when g_j ≪ g_m; instead, with x := g^{−2} and T := x_j/x_m ≥ 1, g_j/g_m = T^{−1/2}, so δ′_j/α_{c,m} ≤ (A₁/C_c)sup_{T≥1}T^{−1/2}(1 + log T)^{p₁}·(log g_m^{−2})^{p₁−q_c}, which tends to 0 since q_c > p₁. Since q_c > p₀ > p₁, for γ small the shift is ≤ ¼ of the room. On the frozen layers m ≤ j_i the shift is compared in the scale factors of the index j_i, with no conversion. It is a field of amplitude ≤ C_Hδ′_j, smooth at the scale j, so each entry is ≤ C_Hδ′_j times the scale-j factor, and the scale-j factor is ≤ L^{−(j−j_i)} times the scale-j_i one (the weakest entries, A′, as in *The room.*). Against the room ≥ 0.2α_{j_i} times the scale-j_i factors, the shift is ≤ 5C_HL^{−(j−j_i)}δ′_j/α_{c,j_i}, and δ′_j/α_{c,j_i} ≤ (1 + β₀)(A₁/C_c)(j − j_i + 1)^{1/2}(log g_j^{−2})^{p₁−q_c} as for m < j above, the root absorbed by L^{−(j−j_i)}. So the shift is ≤ ¼ of the room for γ small, with no factor of N₀. This is the quantitative form of Bałaban's inclusion at p.191 l.38–42.
    - The ratio. So the path between the two runs' fluctuated configurations has the room ≥ ¾β2^{−|m−j|}α_m times the scale factors, except on the frozen layers m ≤ j_i, where it is ≥ ¾c_N times them, and the ratio is that of *Complex Backgrounds.* with 2^{|m−j|} for 2^{(i−m)₊}, and with the extra factor β2^{−|m−j|}/c_N ≤ (4c_N)^{−1} on the frozen layers, where the depth is measured in the scale-m factors as in *Complex Backgrounds.* That factor is polynomial in g_{k_R}^{−2} (*The room.*), so it joins P(k), and P(k)2^{−k_s} stays bounded by a constant of j(C), ḡ, b′, and L, since K = k_s + j(C). Both m and j lie between k_s/8 and K, so |m − j| < 7k_s/8 + j(C), and (L-V) gives the variation ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} times the term's bound, with C_h enlarged as there.
  - *The recursion over ν.* Collecting, with the three facts of the Cauchy note's §8.2 for the frozen inputs and §8.5 for the expansion,
    c_{ν+1} ≤ η^ℂ_{k_R}max_{i≤ν}c_i + s^ℂ_{k_R}, η^ℂ_{k_R} := 4C_XC_KPC_cN₀²δ′_{k_R}, s^ℂ_{k_R} := C_ℂ(𝒟^∗_{k_R} + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}),
    with C_ℂ a constant of C_KP, C_g, C_β/b′, C_G, C₀/B₀, and E₀/C₀. The source collects the frozen differences (𝒟^B), the depth, the per-layer (10.6), and the shells (inside 𝒟^∗ through e and |δ|), the coupling terms (C_β/b′ times max d_l), and the variation (C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}). c₀ = 0: for ν = 0 there is no ℂ-term. N₀ = O(log g_{k_R}^{−2}) by Bałaban's assumption (p.192 l.4–5), and δ′_{k_R} = g_{k_R}A₁p₁(g_{k_R}), so η^ℂ_{k_R} → 0 with γ. If η^ℂ_{k_R} ≤ ½, a threshold of the kind of (T1), then c_ν ≤ 2s^ℂ_{k_R} for all ν ≤ N, by induction: c_{ν+1} ≤ ½·2s^ℂ + s^ℂ. The same max form covers Bałaban's cumulation, since the N₀ overlapping terms are inside η^ℂ.
  - *Into the recursion of the boundary terms.* 𝔹^{(ν)}_k = Σ_{i≤ν}ℂ^{(i)}_k. Where the integrations of Z end, Bałaban leaves 𝔹^{(ν)}_k "as a part of all boundary terms" (p.191 l.44–47), and from then on they are carried as the 𝐁^{(i)} are. Per point at most C_cN₀ of the ℂ-terms overlap, so the difference of the carried sum is ≤ 2C_cN₀C₀s^ℂ_{k_R} per point. Outside the integrals there is no factor δ′_j, so the count N₀ stays. N₀ = O(log g_{k_R}^{−2}) is a power of log g^{−2}, of the kind of the factor (100MR_j)^d in c^B_j of the Cauchy note's (8.4). So the creation enters s^B_{k_R} as C_ℂ′N₀(𝒟^∗_{k_R} + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}), C_ℂ′ := 2C_cC_ℂC₀/B₀, and in (8.4) it adds N₀ to the polylogarithmic factor of c^B_j. C_Σ of (8.5) stays finite, since g_j^{κ₀} with κ₀ > 2 is summable against any power of log g_j^{−2}.
- *The weights of the ages.* An input of the generation i > k_s/8 enters the step k + 1 in two ways: through a domain X that reaches the region Ω_{k+1} of the fluctuation, and through the dependence of the fluctuation-dependent configuration on the fluctuation outside X. By the first item of "Deep terms", X has a point y at a layer ≤ i, so in both ways the layers Γ_{i+1}, …, Γ_k lie between y and Ω_{k+1}, and the age r = k + 1 − i pays a factor for each of these r − 1 layers.
  - (a) Through X. The layer Γ_l separates Ω_l^c from Ω_{l+1} by ≥ 8LMR_{l+1} ≥ 6MR_l in the units of the scale l, by (3.5) (Ω_l^c ⊂ Λ_l^c ⊂ Z′_l) and (2.9), at the layers built by fluctuation steps, and by > RM in its own units at every layer, those changed by R-operations included (PRT2 (2.2), p.224 l.10–13). That is ≥ RL^{l−i} ≥ R π_i-cubes for l ≥ i, with R that of (b); and d_i(X) is the tree length in π_i-cubes (RG I p.257 l.14–16). Bałaban obtains the factor exp(−δκ(L^jη)^{−1}) of a step from (1.18), with δ "a small, positive number", and "the remaining factor exp(−(1−δ)κd_j(X))" is used for the new terms (RG I p.272 l.2–4). His δ is taken as ½δ there, and the other ½δκ, spent on the r − 1 layers, gives e^{−½δκR(r−1)}; (1 − δ)κ remains for the new terms, as in the print.
  - (b) Through the configuration. RG I (3.9) bounds δ𝐇_j through the decay from X to the cube □ at which ζ_□𝐇_k(B′) is evaluated (RG I p.271 l.33–45). In CRE the fluctuation lives in Ω_{k+1}, so a cube next to X is reached only through the decay of 𝐇_k, the data map of the current multiscale determining set (p.276 l.13–15). The derivative of 𝐇_j decays at the rate δ₀ (RG I p.271 l.33–34), and (3.9) keeps ½δ₀dist + ½δ₀M(L^jη)^{−1} of it, for dist ≥ M(L^jη)^{−1} (p.271 l.39–45); Bałaban uses these factors for the bound (2.42) of the output (CRE p.276 l.13–16). The rest, ½δ₀(dist − M(L^jη)^{−1}), is ≥ ¼δ₀dist once dist ≥ 2M(L^jη)^{−1}, which holds when a layer is crossed. By the count of (b), PRT2 (2.58) at α = ¼, this rest gives e^{−(1/16)δ₀RM} per layer crossed, under (2.59) at α = ¼, and Bałaban's factors are untouched. Where X covers some of the layers and the decay of 𝐇_k bridges the others, each layer is paid by one of the two at the smaller rate, except the one layer they share, whose factor e^{σ} is absorbed in C_W.
  - So w_r ≤ C_We^{−σ(r−1)}, with σ := min(½δκR, (1/16)δ₀RM) and C_W a constant of B₃, c₀(⅛), and e^{σ}. Under the condition
    (⋆) σ ≥ (d + 1)log L + 1,
    W := Σ_{r≥1}w_rL^{dr} ≤ C_WL^dΣ_{s≥0}(eL)^{−s} ≤ 2C_WL^d. (⋆) is a condition on Bałaban's R and M, as (2.59) is, and implies the condition ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1 of §4(b) (it gives ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 2(d + 1)log L + 2). Further conditions on R and M of the same kind are (2.59) at α = ¼, used in (b) here, and (2.59) at α = 1/128, used by the kernel form of VAR Proposition 4 in §4(b). (⋆) is compatible with 2R_l > R, since R_l ≥ (log g_l^{−2})^r grows as the coupling falls (CRE (2.5)).
  - The same count gives a term of the generation i < m on a layer Γ_m the inward weight e^{−σ(m−1−i)}: from a point at a layer ≤ i it crosses Γ_{i+1}, …, Γ_{m−1} only. So C_w := Σ_{r≥1}L^{dr}e^{−σ(r−1)} ≤ L^d(1 − (eL)^{−1})^{−1} ≤ 2L^d under (⋆).
- *The recursion and its solution.* With η_k := ε_k^{*B} and b taken over the non-deep terms,
  b_{k+1} ≤ η_kΣ_{k_s/8<i≤k}w_{k+1−i}L^{d(k+1−i)}b_i + s^B_{k+1}.
  The source s^B_{k+1} is one number at each step, since the non-deep domains have no points at the layers ≤ k_s/8. If 2η_kW ≤ 1, a threshold on the coupling of the kind of (T1), then b_i ≤ 2s^B_i for every i > k_s/8, by induction. At the first generation above k_s/8 the sum is empty: by "Deep terms", the boundary inputs of a non-deep term have generations > k_s/8. So no initial data is needed. If the bound holds below k + 1, then b_i ≤ 2s^B_i ≤ 2s^B_{k+1}, since 𝒟^∗ is nondecreasing, and b_{k+1} ≤ 2η_kWs^B_{k+1} + s^B_{k+1} ≤ 2s^B_{k+1}. The b_i are class norms, and the source s^B_{k+1} contains the variation of (ii), derived at real and at complex backgrounds ("The step map", *Complex Backgrounds.*, unreviewed), and, at the steps of R-operations, the creation of the R-operation's own boundary terms (*The Creation Difference of 𝔹_k^{(n)}*, not reviewed), which replaces C_B by C_B + C_ℂ′N₀ in s^B at those steps. So b_i ≤ 2s^B_i on the class is derived, the complex variation and the creation not reviewed.
- *Into the quotients.* Take a component Z renormalised at the step j > k_s/4, with history from the birth scale n > k_s/8, and the non-deep terms of its integrand; the deep ones are those of "Deep terms".
  - On a layer Γ_m of the history the generations i ≥ m contribute ≤ 2B₁max_{i≥m}b_i by (2.47), and the generations k_s/8 < i < m contribute ≤ C_XB₀C_wmax_ib_i by the inward weight, C_X counting the domains at a point. With b_i ≤ 2s^B_i ≤ 2s^B_j, the frozen 𝐁-differences are ≤ 2(2B₁ + C_XB₀C_w)C_B(𝒟^∗_j + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}) per point of a layer, with no age factor.
  - The history has Σ_mp_m(Z) ≤ (1 + L^d)P_j(Z) points (the Cauchy note's §8.6). So the frozen 𝐁-differences enter φ + ψ there as C^B_n(𝒟^∗_j + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8})P_j(Z), with C^B_n := 2(1 + L^d)(2B₁ + C_XB₀C_w)C_B. With P_je^{−C_PP_j} ≤ 1, |q^B − q^A| gains 2(e + 1)C^B_n(𝒟^∗_j + C_hθ̃^{k_s/8})q̄.
  - The variation of V(Y) at the points of Y outside the history is ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} per point, by the profile of (b), since a non-deep Y has no points at the layers ≤ k_s/8; at complex points this rests on the room at complex points of (d)(ii), derived, not reviewed. A factor V(Y) of LFR II (1.91) in a polymer X′ whose components of Bałaban's Z are all young can be deep, through points of Y outside Z; its constituents are bounded in "Deep terms", *Deep V(Y) in a Young Polymer*, with the run difference ≤ r_D + r_E + r_C times the factor's bound.
- *What changes in the Cauchy note.*
  - In §8.2(a), the frozen 𝐁-differences become marked activities, feeding only 𝐁^{(k+1)}.
  - In §8, the norms D, S, and b at the steps > k_s/8 are taken over the non-deep terms.
  - In §8.6, the boundary terms leave (γ) of the Cauchy note's (LF) line, with the bound above: the carried ones, and the R-operation's own terms 𝔹_k^{(n)} through *The Creation Difference of 𝔹_k^{(n)}* (not reviewed). The bullet *No deep V(Y) in a young polymer.* is replaced by the bound of "Deep terms", *Deep V(Y) in a Young Polymer*, which adds 2(r_D + r_E + r_C) = 4e²(C_V + C_E + C_C)2^{−k_s/16} to the bracket. ν_j gains the terms in θ̃^{k_s/8} and k̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16} times R₁′(100MR_j)^dg_j^{κ₀}, and 4e²(C_V + C_E + C_C)L⁴R₁′2^{−k_s/16}(100MR_j)^dg_j^{κ₀} from the deep V(Y), and (8.4) gains C^B_R(100MR_j)^d𝒟^∗_jg_j^{κ₀}, with C^B_R := 2(e + 1)C^B_nL⁴R₁′.
  - In §8.7, each part of 𝒟^B_i is ≤ a constant times m_{i−1} in the weighted norm m_i = iθ̃^i + w_K, w_K := (K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}) + μ_K, the coupling part g²|δ| by g_i²(K − i) ≤ b^{−1} as there. Every index l ≥ k_s/8 − 1 has lθ̃^l ≤ C_μ(k_s + 1)θ̃^{k_s/8} ≤ C_μw_K, with C_μ := θ̃^{−1}max(1, 1/(e log(1/θ̃))), so max_{k_s/8−1≤l≤j}m_l ≤ (1 + C_μ)m_j. So 𝒟^∗_j is ≤ a constant times m_j there.
  - (T1) includes 2η_kW ≤ 1 and (T-F) of "The step map".

The Cauchy note derives (b)–(d) for single-scale data at the stopping scale; (b)–(d) above are the multiscale versions. (e) has no single-scale counterpart there.

## 5. The Large-Field Dressings of the Loop (Derived; the R-Operations With the Loop Not Reviewed)

Here 𝒲^A[τ] and 𝒲^B[τ⁺] are the normalised dressed loops of the Cauchy note's l.39 on a matched pair τ⁺, τ of its §6.2, each with its own κ divided out. They have the form of the map's l.261: e^{𝓔_k} times the leading word f_k plus the words tr(Y_{W₁}U_{(1)} ⋯ Y_{W_m}U_{(m)}) with the weights Q_k(𝔅). They are functions of the integration variables of 𝐓, which are the same in both runs (the Cauchy note's l.114; map l.263, l.326) and are written V below. a_j := εL^j, j(x), C₀, and the profile ρ̄(x) := 2C_G(ε₀)c″_K(ε/a_{j(x)})^{1−ε₀}ε₁ are those of §4(b), and m_W := ⌊k/8⌋. |·| and ‖·‖ are the norms of the Cauchy note, and ‖dπ‖ := sup_{|Y|=1}‖dπ(Y)‖_op for the representation π of the loop.

(a) *What the Main Proof Uses (Derived).*
- (LF-W) enters the main proof only at the Cauchy note's l.140, in the second term of ∫|𝒲^Bρ^B − 𝒲^Aρ^A| dV ≤ B_W∫|ρ^B − ρ^A| dV + ∫|𝒲^B − 𝒲^A|ρ^A dV, for the young matched pairs, k − j₀ < ⌊k/2⌋. The unmatched set (§6.1) and the old pairs use only |𝒲| ≤ B_W pointwise in V (l.110, l.116), and the all-small-field pair has no large-field dressing. The young pairs need the second term, summed over them, to be ≤ B_Ws^{W,tot}_Kδ_LFZ^A_{ℓ₀} plus the dev part that l.140 places inside s′_K, with s^{W,tot}_K summable in K. The young pairs then change F by ≤ B_Wδ_LF(2s_K + s^{W,tot}_K) in place of 2B_Wδ_LFs_K.
- *The Pairs With m_W ≤ k − j₀ < ⌊k/2⌋.* By the argument of the Cauchy note's l.116 with m_W in place of m, the oldest component X of such a pair meets the large-field region at every scale from j₀ to k, so P(X) ≥ m_W + 1. The bounds |𝒲^B|, |𝒲^A| ≤ B_W hold pointwise in V (l.110). Also ρ^A ≥ 0 on the real integration region (LFR I p.176 l.20) and Z^A_τ ≤ M^A_τ. With these and (11.3) with the weight ω(X) := e^{C_PP(X)},

(5.1) Σ_{(M), m_W ≤ k−j₀ < ⌊k/2⌋}∫|𝒲^B[τ⁺] − 𝒲^A[τ]|ρ^A[τ] dV ≤ 2B_We^{−C_P(m_W+1)}δ_LFZ^A_{ℓ₀}.

- *The Pairs With k − j₀ < m_W.* Their components are born at scales ≥ j₀ > k − m_W, and the ingredients at the scales i < j₀ are the all-small-field pair's (the Cauchy note's l.118). Since Ω_i = T for i < j₀ (§1), the nonempty layers Ω_i ∖ Ω_{i+1} of every step have i ≥ j₀ − 1 ≥ k − m_W ≥ 7k/8. For these pairs (b)–(e) derive, at every V of the real integration region of τ,

(W) |𝒲^B[τ⁺] − 𝒲^A[τ]| ≤ [e^{D^{dev}}(exp(Σ_{X⊂Z(τ)}C_Ms^W_KP(X) + s^{W,0}_K) − 1) + (e^{D^{dev}} − 1)]·𝒲̄[τ].

  Here 𝒲̄[τ] ≤ B_W is the paid majorant of run A's loop on τ: the sum over the dressing's terms of the products of its factors' bounds, with the attached part paid from the reserve as in the continuum note's (4.5) and the D2.6 closure 7.2. D^{dev} is the weighted difference mass of the dev insertions, which l.140 bounds by the recursion of the Cauchy note's §8.3 inside s′_K, and (d) supplies its depth term at the large-field backgrounds. s^W_K and s^{W,0}_K are defined in (f). (W) is the form (LF-1) of the Cauchy note's l.5 for the loop, and (5.2) is its integrated form.

(b) *The Run-Dependent Factors and the Assembly (Derived).*
- On a young pair with k − j₀ < m_W, four kinds of factors of 𝒲 depend on the run.
  - The transports of f_k and the U_{(i)}, read on the run's background (map l.265).
  - The insertions Y_W of the species dev, att(j, Z), att(j, coll), and wrap(j) (map l.276). There is no lw, since lw is made at step 1 only and j₀ > k − m_W ≥ 2.
  - The cluster weights φ_k(Γ) of Q_k and the local terms of 𝓔_k. These are numbers given the labels in each run (map l.324), so they do not depend on V.
  - The parts produced by the R-operations of τ's components (the D2.7 closure 15.3–15.6).
- E_const,k is the same number on every labelled term of a run (map l.268, l.312). It is a factor of the run's whole numerator, the same on τ and on ℓ₀, so it is not a large-field dressing and is not compared here.
- *The Assembly.* Let a product have factors with |x^A_i| ≤ x̄_i and |x^B_i − x^A_i| ≤ Δ_ix̄_i. The telescoping identity gives |Π_ix^B_i − Π_ix^A_i| ≤ (Π_i(1 + Δ_i) − 1)Π_ix̄_i ≤ (e^{Σ_iΔ_i} − 1)Π_ix̄_i. The products Π_W(1 + e^{θ(W)}ȳ_W) majorise the sums over 𝔅, since |Q_k(𝔅)| ≤ Π_{W∈𝔅}e^{θ(W)} (map l.310) and the dressing is multilinear in the species. Here ȳ_W is the bound of run A's insertion and d_W the bound of the two runs' difference. Then Π_W(1 + e^{θ(W)}(ȳ_W + d_W)) − Π_W(1 + e^{θ(W)}ȳ_W) ≤ (exp(Σ_We^{θ(W)}d_W) − 1)Π_W(1 + e^{θ(W)}ȳ_W), and |e^{𝓔^B} − e^{𝓔^A}| ≤ (e^{|𝓔^B − 𝓔^A|} − 1)|e^{𝓔^A}|. So (W) follows once the factors' relative differences sum to ≤ Σ_XC_Ms^W_KP(X) + s^{W,0}_K + D^{dev}, by (e^{a+b} − 1) ≤ e^b(e^a − 1) + (e^b − 1).

(c) *The Transports (Derived).*
- *The Alignment of the Backgrounds.* The transports are read on the term's background. This background is a VAR minimiser with the term's multiscale determining set, and its data are the term's integration variables, the same in both runs (map l.265; by the D2.7 closure 15.5 the words are functions of U^{old} through the multiscale minimiser CRE (2.12)). At equal data the profile of §4(b) gives U^A = e^{iεA′*}MU^B in the gauge of the Cauchy note's l.324, with A′* = A₁* − HD(A₁*). Then VAR (55) and (57) (p.286 l.24 and l.42, with |·|_{(−1)} the supremum over the layers) and the gradient bound of HD used at the Cauchy note's l.312 give

(P′) a_{j(x)}|A′*(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A′*(x)| ≤ ρ̄(x) + 8B₀C₂ρ̄_n² ≤ 2ρ̄(x),  ρ̄_n := sup_xρ̄(x).

  The second inequality holds under 16B₀C₂C_G(ε₀)c″_Kε₁ ≤ 1, since ρ̄_n²/ρ̄(x) ≤ 2C_G(ε₀)c″_Kε₁L^{−(1−ε₀)(2n−j(x))} and the finest layer n ≥ k − m_W has 2n ≥ k ≥ j(x).
- *The Coarse Links.* For a bond c of T_ε, CMP 98 (15) gives MU^B(c) = e^{iY_c}W(c). Here W(c) is the transport of U^B along the L fine bonds of c, Y_c := L^{−d}Σ_x(1/i)log h_{c,x}, and h_{c,x} := W(γ_{c,x})W(c)^{−1} (the Cauchy note's l.405). In the axial gauge of the Cauchy note's l.464, each fine bond within 4dε of the block's centre is within 4dε(ε/L)|F| of 1, and h_{c,x} is a closed path of at most (3d + 1)L fine bonds there. So |h_{c,x} − 1| ≤ 4d(3d + 1)ε²|F|. For run B's background, |F| ≤ C₀ε₁/a_{j′}² (§4(b), *Regularity*), where a_{j′} ≥ a_{j(c)}/L is the finest layer within 4dε of c. With |log h| ≤ 2|h − 1| for |h − 1| ≤ ½, this gives

(Y) |Y_c| ≤ C_Y(ε/a_{j(c)})²ε₁,  C_Y := 8d(3d + 1)L²C₀,  under 4d(3d + 1)L²C₀ε₁ ≤ ½.

- *The Leading Word and the Arcs.* Hol_C(U^B) = Π_cW(c), the ordered product over the bonds c of C in T_ε. Hol_C(U^A) = Π_ce^{iεA′*(c)}e^{iY_c}W(c) up to a gauge transformation, which leaves the closed trace unchanged. Since π(W(c)) is unitary and ‖π(e^{iX}) − 1‖_op ≤ ‖dπ‖|X|, (P′) and (Y) give

(T) |f^B_k − f^A_k| ≤ Σ_{c⊂C}δ_c,  δ_c := ‖dπ‖(ε|A′*(c)| + |Y_c|) ≤ ‖dπ‖(4C_G(ε₀)c″_K + C_Y)(ε/a_{j(c)})^{2−ε₀}ε₁.

  The transports U_{(i)} between the slots of a word obey the same bound with the insertions' norms as the factor, since the insertions transform covariantly at their slots (map l.266). So in every term of the dressing the transports differ relatively by ≤ Σ_{c⊂C}δ_c.
- *The Count.* A layer-j block of side a_j holds the initial points of at most 4a_j/ε bonds of the square C, at most a_j/ε on each side. There are at most L^d layer-j blocks per scale-(j + 1) point of Z_{j+1} ∩ X (the Cauchy note's §8.6, p_m(Z) ≤ L^d|Z_{m+1} ∩ Z|_{m+1}), and L^{−(1−ε₀)} ≤ θ̃. Put C^{tr}_W := 4‖dπ‖(4C_G(ε₀)c″_K + C_Y).
  - The bonds of C in the layers j < k contribute ≤ L^dC^{tr}_Wε₁θ̃^j per point of Z_{j+1} ∩ X, so ≤ L^dC^{tr}_Wε₁θ̃^{k−m_W}P(X) per component.
  - The at most 4ℓ_Ca_k/ε bonds of C in the stopping layer contribute ≤ s^{W,tr}_K := ℓ_CC^{tr}_Wε₁L^{−(1−ε₀)k}, where ℓ_C is the side of C in stopping-scale bonds (the continuum note's l.24).

(d) *The Insertions, the Weights Q_k, and the Local Terms (Derived).*
- *The Variation.* Each insertion of run A is analytic in (𝐔, 𝐉) on the sub-domain 𝒟^{sub} of the SW closure §3(b1) about (U^A, J(U^A)), and it obeys its bound there (D(k)(iii); the SW closure §3(b2); for the attached species, the D2.6 closure 5.1). Put Ũ := MU^B, J′ := 𝐐(Ũ)*λ^B, and δ𝐉 := J(U^A) − J′. Then (𝐔(t), 𝐉(t)) := (e^{i(t−1)εA′*}U^A, J(U^A) + (t − 1)δ𝐉) is the alignment path of §4(d) (*The Intermediate Current and the Estimate*). It joins (Ũ, J′) at t = 0 to (U^A, J(U^A)) at t = 1. At equal data its current bound reads a_{j(x)}³|δ𝐉(x)| ≤ x̄_J(2ρ̄_n + (ε/a_n)ε₁), with the alignment field bounded by 2ρ̄_n by (P′).
  - At a step i ≥ j₀ − 1, at a point of a layer l ≤ i, 𝒟^{sub} allows the phase (1 − β)α_{1,i}ε/a_i per fine bond, the gradient a_i²|∇A′| < (1 − β)α_{1,i}, and |𝐉 − J_i| < (1 − β)α_{0,i}a_i^{−3}. These are the top-layer radii with the layer's factor ≥ 1 − β.
  - On these layers i − l ≤ m_W, l ≥ k − m_W, and ρ̄ ≤ 2C_G(ε₀)c″_KL^{−(1−ε₀)(k−m_W)}ε₁. The phase gives the factor L^{i−l}, the gradient L^{2(i−l)}, and the current L^{3(i−l)}. So the path stays in 𝒟^{sub} for |t − 1| ≤ R_i, with

(R) 1/R_i ≤ c_W(ε₁/((1 − β)min(α_{0,i}, α_{1,i})))L^{3m_W−(1−ε₀)(k−m_W)},  c_W := x̄_J(4C_G(ε₀)c″_K + 1) + 4C_G(ε₀)c″_K.

  - By CRE (2.28) and (2.4), with q₀ = q₁ > p₀ (the SW closure §2) and C₀, C₁ ≥ A₀, α_{0,i}, α_{1,i} ≥ ε_i. As in §4(d)(ii), ε₁/ε_i ≤ C_αR_k(m_W + 1)^{1/2} with C_α := C(M)2^{p₀}(1 + β₀). Also 3m_W − (1 − ε₀)(k − m_W) ≤ −(4 − 7ε₀)k/8.
  - For R_i ≥ 2, Cauchy's estimate on |t − 1| ≤ R_i shows that every insertion of run A changes along the path by ≤ v_i := 2/R_i times its bound, with

(V) v_i ≤ r̄_K := 2c_WC_α(1 − β)^{−1}R_k(m_W + 1)^{1/2}L^{−(4−7ε₀)k/8}.

  - This holds under ε₀ < 4/7 and the conditions of §4(d)'s alignment. No function of run B is evaluated on run A's lattice. Run B's insertion at (U^B, J(U^B)) is compared with run A's at (Ũ, J′), the lifts of the same multiplier λ^B (as §4(d) and the Cauchy note's §10.4 use them), and run A's change along the path is added.
- *The Generated Part.* At aligned arguments the two runs' insertions differ by what the two step maps generate from their ingredients. The loop's words are marked activities of the fluctuation measure, species-blind (the Cauchy note's l.180), and the attached members are fixed factors of the same measure ((T0) of the D2.6 closure). So (8.2)–(8.3) bound the generated difference at the step i + 1, per unit of an insertion's weighted mass, by g_{i+1} ≤ (1 + C_g)𝒟^B_{i+1}. Here C_g is that of the Cauchy note's §8.2(b) and ε^* ≤ 1, with the depth term of 𝒟^B at the small-field points and (V) at the history's backgrounds. By §4(e), 𝒟^B_{i+1} ≤ C_𝒟Bm_i, with m_i := iθ̃^i + w_K and w_K := (K + 1)(θ̃^{k_s/8} + 2^{−k_s/16}) + μ_K of the Cauchy note's §8.6, and C_𝒟B the constant of §4(e) (not the C_m of the Cauchy note's §8.7). Since iθ̃^i ≤ kθ̃^{k−m_W−1} for k − m_W − 1 ≤ i ≤ k, g_{i+1} ≤ ḡ_K := (1 + C_g)C_𝒟B[(k + 1)θ̃^{k−m_W−1} + w_K] for these i.
- *The Masses.* Let Ȳ_X := Σ_ZE_{j(Z)}(Z), the sum over the components Z created in X's history, at their creation steps j(Z), of the lifetime totals E_j(Z) of the D2.6 closure 7.2. These totals contain Z's carried attached species with the carry factor 2, the species att(n, Z_n) of the later steps, the collar parts assigned to Z (map l.303), and the exposed segments that pay e^{E^∂}. Then E_j(Z) ≤ ε_A(j)(d′_j(Z) + 1) (closure 7.2), d′_j(Z) + 1 ≤ 2|Z|_j, and the components created at one scale are disjoint. So Ȳ_X ≤ 2ε̄_AP(X), with ε̄_A := sup_{j>k−m_W}ε_A(j). The wrap species have no component, and their weighted mass b_wrap is uniform in K, since it is part of the bound B_W of the continuum note's (8.1).
- *The Carry.* Let D_i be the weighted difference mass, at aligned arguments, of the attached and wrap species present at the step i, and M_i their weighted mass, with w(W) of the D2.6 closure §5.0. The carry lemma (closure 5.1–5.3) applies to the differences as to the insertions. Evaluation at the new background does not grow sup bounds, and the touched parts become descendants with the factor 1 + δ_i. This factor is doubled for a descendant of several parents, since the difference of a product replaces one parent at a time. So D_{i+1} ≤ (1 + 2δ_i)(D_i + v_iM_i) + g_{i+1}M_{i+1}, the last step's v_kM_k being the change along the path at the stopping scale. There are no attached species before j₀, at most m_W + 1 steps run from j₀ − 1 to k, Π_i(1 + 2δ_i) ≤ e^{2log 2} = 4, and M_i ≤ Σ_XȲ_X + b_wrap. Hence

(D) D_k ≤ c̄^W_K(Σ_XȲ_X + b_wrap),  c̄^W_K := 4(m_W + 1)(r̄_K + ḡ_K).

- *The Weights Q_k and the Local Terms.* φ_k(Γ) and the attached local terms of 𝓔_k are numbers given the labels (map l.324), so they do not change along the path. They are cluster terms of each run's expansions at the reference point (map l.322; the D2.6 closure 7.4(a)). Their activities differ by ≤ c̄^W_K relative, by the same recursion at the reference point, so the marked-cluster bound (G′) of the Cauchy note's §8.5 gives Σ_{Γ hits W}|φ^B_k(Γ) − φ^A_k(Γ)| ≤ C_KPc̄^W_Kθ(W). The attached local terms satisfy the same bound relative to their part of E_j(Z). Use θ(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16 (map l.310), C_KPc̄^W_Kν/16 ≤ ν_r/2, and σe^{−ν_rσ/2} ≤ 2/(eν_r). Then the Q-part of an attached or wrap member W is e^{θ(W)}(e^{C_KPc̄^W_Kθ(W)} − 1)ȳ_W ≤ C_Qc̄^W_Kw(W), with C_Q := C_KPν/(8eν_r). For the dev members the Q-part is in D^{dev}.
- *The Dev Insertions.* The same variation acts on the dev insertions at the steps ≥ j₀ − 1, and it is ≤ r̄_K per unit of their class norm. For ε₀ < 2/5, r̄_K ≤ C′_eθ̃^k with C′_e := sup_Kr̄_Kθ̃^{−k} < ∞, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4} and R_k is a power of log g_k^{−2} (CRE (2.5)). So it is a depth term of the Cauchy note's kind, e_i ≤ C_eθ̃^i with C_e enlarged by C′_e. D^{dev} is then bounded by the recursion of the Cauchy note's §8.3 (l.180) inside s′_K, as l.140 states.

(e) *The R-Operations With the Loop (Reduced to the Points (3), (6), (7), and (10) of (LF-A); Derived, Not Reviewed).*
- *The Objects.* At a step at which a component of τ is renormalised, the loop-linked part of LFR II (1.72)'s bracket is the ratio of gases Ξ^C/Ξ of the D2.7 closure 15.3. Its activities are Bałaban's (1.91), with the loop's slots. Its flat part P_{k−1}(C)μ^R_k + 4ζ^R_k is subtracted into κ_k (15.4), which each run divides out (the Cauchy note's l.39). The remainder e^{E°^R_k − Ψ(S)} and the deviation insertions D_g of 15.6 are cluster terms of the same gas. Every word is a function of U^{old} (15.5), so its two-run difference is that of (c)–(d). In a pair with k − j₀ < m_W every R-operation is at a step > k − m_W, so the fine scales and old histories of the Cauchy note's §8.6 do not occur.
- *The Comparison.* The activities are built from the renormalised components' operations 𝐓′, the factors V(Y) of (1.91), and the slot words.
  - The interpolation of the Cauchy note's §8.6 (*From the Quotients to the R-Terms*), 𝐓′_t := 𝐓′^A + t(𝐓′^B − 𝐓′^A) and V_t(Y), is applied with the slot words interpolated alongside, w_t := w^A + t(w^B − w^A). Every interpolated factor obeys the bound that both runs' factors obey, since those bounds are bounds of moduli on the class and so convex. The gas converges for every t by (1.92)–(1.97).
  - The derivative in t replaces one activity by its two-run difference. For 𝐓′ and V this difference is ≤ b̄_K times the activity's bound per point, with b̄_K := sup_{k−m_W<j≤k}{2(e + 1)[C_n𝒟_{[j]} + C^B_n𝒟^∗_j + C^θ_nθ̃^{k_s/8} + 2L^dC_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16}] + 4C_hθ̃^{k_s/8}}, with k_s, k̄, and Ē₀ as there. For a slot word it is the relative difference of (c)–(d), already counted in (W).
  - By (G′) of §8.5, the exponent E°^R_k − Ψ(S) and the D_g change by ≤ C_KPb̄_K per point of the renormalised component, beyond the words' part. The count of the activities in a cluster is absorbed by the weight of (1.93), as §8.6 absorbs q (q ≤ e^q). Per point of the component's history this is the part s^{W,R}_K := C_KPL^db̄_K/C_M of s^W_K.
- *What It Rests On.* 𝒟_{[j]} contains h_n, the (LF-2) bounds of the fine-lattice objects of §8.6: U₀, H_{1,j}, Δ₁(ζ₀), Im∂U₀, and the argument of V. These objects are (LF-A), whose π-part vanishes at single-scale data by the Cauchy note's β_π ≡ 0 and is derived, not reviewed, at the interfaces as the point (6) of (LF-A). So the R-part of (W) reduces to the points (3), (6), (7), and (10) of (LF-A), derived and not reviewed, as they stand in the Open Gaps and needs nothing else, and the loop adds no object to (LF-A). The exterior part W_ext of 𝐂_k enters the loop's R-operations only as a part of these objects, and it is derived in §4(d), *The exterior part W_ext.*

(f) *The Result (Derived; s^{W,R}_K Rests on the Points of (LF-A), Not Reviewed).*
- *The Constants.* Put

(S) s^{W,X}_K := [L^dC^{tr}_Wε₁θ̃^{k−m_W} + 2(1 + C_Q)c̄^W_Kε̄_A]/C_M,  s^W_K := s^{W,X}_K + s^{W,R}_K,  s^{W,0}_K := s^{W,tr}_K + (1 + C_Q)c̄^W_Kb_wrap.

  By (b)–(e), (W) holds with these at every V of the real integration region of a pair with k − j₀ < m_W, for k ≥ k_W. Here k_W is the least k from which R_i ≥ 2 (r̄_K ≤ 1), C_KPc̄^W_Kν/16 ≤ ν_r/2, s^W_K ≤ 1, s^{W,0}_K ≤ 1, and D^{dev} ≤ 1 (the last by the recursion of the Cauchy note's §8.3, whose bound tends to 0). The transports give the first term of s^{W,X}_K and s^{W,tr}_K, (D) and the Q-part give the rest of s^{W,X}_K and s^{W,0}_K, and (e) gives s^{W,R}_K.
- *The Integration.* Use the following facts.
  - (e^{a+b} − 1) ≤ e^b(e^a − 1) + (e^b − 1), e^{Σφ} − 1 ≤ (Σφ)e^{Σφ}, e^x − 1 ≤ (e − 1)x ≤ 2x for 0 ≤ x ≤ 1, and Σ_XC_MP(X) ≤ Π_X(1 + C_MP(X)).
  - (1 + C_MP)e^{C_Ms^W_KP} ≤ e^{C_PP} for C_P ≥ 2C_M and s^W_K ≤ 1.
  - The bracket of (W) depends on τ's labels only, 𝒲̄ ≤ B_W, and D^{dev} ≤ 1 for k ≥ k_W.
  - (11.3) with ω(X) := e^{C_PP(X)}.

  With these, (W) gives

(5.2) Σ_{(M), k−j₀<m_W}∫|𝒲^B[τ⁺] − 𝒲^A[τ]|ρ^A[τ] dV ≤ B_W(e²s^W_K + 2e·s^{W,0}_K)δ_LFZ^A_{ℓ₀} + Σ_τ(e^{D^{dev}} − 1)B_WM^A_τ,

  and the last sum is the dev part inside s′_K.
- *The Total.* Let s^{W,tot}_K := e²s^W_K + 2e·s^{W,0}_K + 2e^{−C_P(m_W+1)} for k ≥ k_W. For k < k_W let s^{W,tot}_K := 2, by the triangle inequality on all young pairs as in (5.1). By (5.1) and (5.2), Σ_{young}∫|𝒲^B − 𝒲^A|ρ^A dV ≤ B_Ws^{W,tot}_Kδ_LFZ^A_{ℓ₀} plus the dev part inside s′_K, which is what (a) asks. So (6.3) holds for the young pairs' numerators with B_W(s_K + s^{W,tot}_K) in place of B_Ws_K, the young pairs change F by ≤ B_Wδ_LF(2s_K + s^{W,tot}_K), and b^{(M)}_K of the Cauchy note's l.140 becomes 3e^{−C_P⌊k/2⌋} + 2s_K + s^{W,tot}_K.
- *Summability.* With k = K − j(C), the terms of s^{W,tot}_K are summable in K.
  - θ̃^{k−m_W} ≤ θ̃^{7k/8}, L^{−(1−ε₀)k}, e^{−C_P(m_W+1)}, and r̄_K are geometric in k up to powers of log g_k^{−2} (R_k and ε̄_A, CRE (2.5)).
  - c̄^W_K ≤ C[k²θ̃^{7k/8−1} + kμ_K], ε̄_A grows at most as a power of log g_k^{−2}, which is a power of log k, and Kμ_K = K^{2−κ₀/2}ℓ_K is summable for κ₀ ≥ 7 also after multiplication by any power of log K, as at the Cauchy note's l.140.
  - b̄_K is summable given (LF), by the Cauchy note's (8.4) and §8.7 (𝒟_{[j]} and 𝒟^∗_j are ≤ a constant times m_j, with j > 7k/8).
- *Conditions.* ε₀ < 2/5 (which covers (V)'s ε₀ < 4/7), 16B₀C₂C_G(ε₀)c″_Kε₁ ≤ 1, 4d(3d + 1)L²C₀ε₁ ≤ ½, the conditions of §4(d)'s alignment, C_P ≥ 2C_M as in the Cauchy note, and k ≥ k_W; the finitely many k < k_W change only the constants. The h_n of (e) rests on the points (3), (6), (7), and (10) of (LF-A), derived, not reviewed.
